# CITAR 0.1.6, Phase 3: the features (design)

**Status.** Design, 2026-10-11, for Phase 3 of `ops/plan-0.1.6.md` §7, written against `v0.1.6` at `d93345a` (Phase 2 complete: the Rust engine, the Rust bot, the bindings and saves v2 are the only engine; the Python engine is deleted and tagged `python-engine-0.1.6`). Revised the same day by the design review's fix round (P3.13). Sections are numbered P3.x so they cannot be confused with DESIGN.md's Phase 1 (§1-§12) and Phase 2 (P2.x). Appendix D is the work breakdown: nineteen packages, run strictly one after another, one agent at a time (owner's instruction, 2026-10-11). Each package appends an "As built in 3-xx" note to the section it implements, as Phase 2's packages did, and records its wall-clock time so the owner can compare single-agent runs with Phase 2's two lanes.

This file lives in `design/`, outside `docs/`, so mkdocs and the wiki generator never see it. It names code paths in backticks rather than linking them, so `scripts/check_links.py` (which scans `docs/`, `packaging/`, `crates/` and the root documents) needs no change.

**Sources:**
- `ops/plan-0.1.6.md` §1 (decisions R1-G and the defaults under them), §6.1-6.6 (feature designs), §7 Phase 3; §6.7 for the hooks Phase 4 needs.
- `ops/review-0.1.6/{auth-db-sharing, pool-queue-workers, agents-llm, web-game-client, web-site-pages, engine-diplomacy-interface, jobs-bench-lab-reports, server-runtime}.json`. They were written against `main` at `8290e01`, before Phase 0; every finding used here was checked again against the tree at `d93345a`, and the ones that no longer hold are listed in P3.1.5.
- `ops/phase0-spec.md` Stage A (the engine and bot hooks the features need), `ops/p2-notes.md` (2-11, 2-12 and 2-13 sections), `crates/citar-engine/DESIGN.md` P2.3.8, P2.6, P2.7 and the as-built notes of 2-05, 2-09, 2-11 and 2-13.
- The code each feature touches (P3.0 cites it line by line).

---

## P3.0 Facts the design leans on (checked against the tree at `d93345a`)

**The engine and the bot already carry the hooks** (Phase 0 Stage A, ported in Phases 1-2):
- **Negotiations are the chat model.** Every history entry has a `seq` and a message; `open` requires a message and refuses a second open chat between the same pair and more than the ruleset's opens per pair per turn (`crates/citar-engine/src/game/diplomacy/negotiation.rs:279-326`). Opening is an own-turn action (`any_time: false`, `api/tools/registry.rs:899-916`); answering is any-time. `accept/counter/reply/reject` with `decline/withdraw/end` read as `reject` (`negotiation.rs:400-409`); an empty counter is refused (`:556-564`); the message cap (`max_chat_messages`, the game's `config.diplomacy` or the ruleset's 30) closes a chat as `expired` instead of taking the message (`:568-571, 630-645`). The `end_turn` tool refuses while any negotiation the player is in is open, naming the one to answer first (`end_turn_refusal`, `:800-824`); `Game::end_turn` keeps the safety net that expires the initiator's chats (stage E0, `:776-793`). `close_negotiation` is exposed (`EngineGame.close_negotiation`, `citar/_facade_rust.py:874-879`).
- **The model-facing text was rewritten in Phase 0.** The registry no longer promises an immediate reply (`registry.rs:899-940, 1024-1031`) and `turn_progress` already lists the open negotiations and says `end_turn` waits for them (`api/briefing/mod.rs:742-756`). One gap remains: `respond_negotiation`'s schema lists only `negotiation_id` and `action` as required (`registry.rs:928`) although the engine refuses an entry without a message.
- **Seats.** `Controller` has `Hybrid`; `Handicap` (`human|ai`) and `AutoDecisions` (`un_vote`, `conquest`, `free_picks`) are separate, with defaults derived from the controller (`state/players.rs:59-129`). `EngineGame.set_controller(pid, controller, handicap, auto)` (`_facade_rust.py:951-955`) and the lobby's per-seat `handicap`/`auto` (`citar/server/session.py:1489-1492`) reach them.
- **The drive knows hybrid seats.** `Game::drive` stops with `Stop::HybridDiplomat(pid)` once a hybrid seat's driver has played, and the next drive ends the turn (`game/turn/drive.rs:152-155, 324-329`); a driver's `respond` may return `DriverOutcome::Deferred`, which leaves the chat to the host and makes that seat's end of turn wait (`Stop::AwaitingReply`, `:59-75, 449-468`); "the next drive asks the driver again, so a host whose wait has run out has the bot decide by driving with a driver that answers this time" (`:67-73`). `Game::answer(pid, nid, driver)` puts one chat to a driver (`:352-381`).
- **The bot's switches and advice.** `citar_bot::Owners` maps the ten categories (`trades, agreements, peace, war, denounce, un, city_states, espionage, captured_cities, chat`; `game/diplomacy/category.rs`) to `bot|llm` and decides `owns(negotiation)` (`crates/citar-bot/src/owners.rs`). `citar_bot::advice()` returns `{deal_value, war_readiness, spare_luxuries, wants}` (`lib.rs:334`); `citar_bot::evaluate()` values arbitrary terms (`lib.rs:350`) but is **not bound to Python**. The facade has `bot_instance`, `bot_set_diplomacy`, `bot_owns_negotiation`, `EngineGame.drive`, `.answer`, `.bot_advice` (`_facade_rust.py:344-390, 962-1028`).
- **Scenarios** list `hybrid` among their seat types (`api/scenario/editor.rs:24`), but `normalize_seats` keeps only the `llm` and `bot` sub-objects of a seat (`editor.rs:203-213`).
- **The client view** has no explored-region, era-stage or home-continent block (`api/views/client.rs:118-139`); the map keeps a continent per tile (`state/map.rs:41, 58`).

**The server today:**
- `SEAT_TYPES = ("human", "mcp", "llm", "bot")` (`session.py:23`). `BotAgent.play_turn` drives a `hybrid_diplomat` stop straight on, since no session has hybrid seats yet (`citar/agents/bot_agent.py:70-73`), and waits at most `REPLY_WAIT_SECONDS = 90` per turn for other seats' answers (`:25, 65-91`). `LLMAgent` gives each message it sends `negotiation_wait_seconds = 240` (`citar/agents/llm_agent.py:123, 394-467`).
- **Responders:** after every action, `_dispatch_negotiation_interrupts` starts one daemon thread per `(negotiation, entries)` for bot and LLM seats; the `_responding` set is never pruned (`session.py:362-402`); an unanswered chat is rejected with "(no response)" (`:404-417`). `LLMAgent.respond_negotiation` opens a fresh conversation per exchange, with the full system prompt, 15 tools and no deadline (`llm_agent.py:634-691`).
- **Model calls:** a worker's `busy` refusal makes `_step` sleep and retry forever, metering a request each time (`llm_agent.py:204-222, 259-266`); `WorkerConversation` ignores the helper's `Done.duration` and its `close()` does nothing (`citar/agents/providers/worker_provider.py:91-142`); `WorkerHub.submit` refuses at once when the connection's `in_flight >= max_concurrent` (`citar/server/workers.py:212-247`). Report narratives call the SDKs directly, outside `make_conversation`, and poll a busy worker every 5 s for up to 30 minutes (`citar/reports/__init__.py:387-444`).
- **Occupancy is per activity:** `pool.seats.occupied()` counts every live game with a living LLM seat on a machine for the whole game, plus named claims (`citar/pool/seats.py:36-63`); probe runs and reports claim a machine for their whole run (`citar/probes.py:631`, `reports/__init__.py:309`); benchmarks count `occupied` against `max_parallel` (`citar/server/benchmarks.py:776-826`); quiet hours and make-way pauses look only at `seat.type == "llm"` (`session.py:599-704`).
- **Admission** runs only at game creation and tolerates `concurrency` (`pool/seats.py:200-218`); `update_seat` skips it (`citar/server/app.py:569-582`); `PURPOSES = game, benchmark, probe, scenario, lab` (`citar/pool/admission.py:32`); `budgets.reserve/settle/release/refresh_from_ledger` have no caller outside `tests/test_pool.py`.
- **Two registries:** `config/servers.json` (`citar/servers.py`), dialled directly, edited by admin-only routes (`citar/server/admin_api.py:45-293`) and the `#/servers` page (`citar/web/js/servers.js`); and DB `servers` rows reached through helpers (`citar/server/pool_api.py`, `#/pool`, `citar/web/js/pool.js`). Three concurrency numbers: `Server.max_concurrent`, `config.connection.max_parallel`, the helper's `hello.max_concurrent`. A helper's hello overwrites `config.hardware` and replaces the model list (`workers.py:329-361`).
- **The usage ledger** is JSONL with no user (`citar/usage.py:1-17`); `session_servers` charges every LLM seat's machine as held for the whole game (`usage.py:277-295`).
- **The database** has the 16 tables of revision `f064316a6b34` plus `767c824c2fcb` (`citar/db/models.py`, `citar/migrations/versions/`). Visibility is `private|allowlist|link|public` and the string is compared in `models.py:276`, `auth/access.py:21-37, 164-183, 280-293, 310`, `server/app.py:514`, `pool/__init__.py:43`, `server/ownership.py:166`, `server/share_api.py:60-62`. `User.public()` exposes `role` and `status` (`models.py:171-183`). Users have no bio, links or field visibility. `ownership.sync` is never called and the engine's phase is `playing|over`, so a finished game keeps counting against `max_concurrent_games` (`app.py:185-202`, `ownership.py:58-72`). Nothing writes a `reports` row. Benchmark suites and runs, probes, lab experiments, scenarios and maps have no owner, and their routes need only `require_user` (`app.py:785-1424`); any member can change any queue priority (`app.py:1389-1410`); bot profiles and lab experiments are admin-only (`citar/server/bots_api.py`). Model scores read every save whatever its `data_sharing` (`citar/server/scoring.py:87-115`).
- **The web client:** Diplomacy is a modal rebuilt from `get_diplomacy` on every negotiation event, which wipes the textarea and the deal builder (`citar/web/js/panels.js:704-824`, `game.js:248-257, 315-324`); the minimap is a 200x130 canvas of the whole map, not aspect-correct, with the explored tiles drawn where they are (`game.js:58-72, 998-1030`); the nav has no people or profile pages (`nav.js:6-9`).
- **Tests and checks:** 665 Python tests (141 s); `scripts/audit_routes.py --strict` counts 190 routes, 0 unguarded; test.yml's front-end job parses every module as ESM; `scripts/dev_server.py` defaults to port 8799 (the owner's) and `scripts/server_soak.py` shows how a script refuses it. `tests/__init__.py` writes `TEST_REGISTRY` (a host, a dry-run machine, a dry-run "GPU box", an Anthropic API row, an electricity plan) as the suite's `config/servers.json`, which the benchmark, costing, pool and server-drive suites read through `servers.py`.

**Where the server acts for whoever asks** (found by the design review, each checked at `d93345a`):
- **A seat's model block is trusted.** Without a `server_id`, `servers.resolve_llm` returns the seat's `llm` block unchanged (`citar/servers.py:750-751`) and `pool_seats.authorize` skips it (`pool/seats.py:211`); the OpenAI and Anthropic providers then dial its `base_url` with the key named by its `api_key_env`, or with the server's own `OPENAI_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (`agents/providers/openai_provider.py:34-38`, `anthropic_provider.py:24-32`). `create_game` and `update_seat` take the block from any member (`app.py:505-529, 570-582`; `update_seat` runs no admission at all), and a scenario's seats keep it minus `api_key` (`api/scenario/editor.rs:203-212`).
- **Some machine routes act on the host, not the row.** `collect` runs `hwinfo.collect()` on the CITAR host (`admin_api.py:192-197`); `state`, `load`, `unload`, `loaded-config` and `estimate` drive the host's LM Studio (the `lms` CLI or the SDK) whenever the row's base URL is local (`:105-189`), and `normalize_server` defaults an LM Studio row to `http://localhost:1234/v1`; `detect` fetches the row's base URL (`:99-102`); the key route's `env` backend names any of the server's environment variables (`:238-256`); `servers-settings`, `lmstudio/devices`, `keys/unlock` and `keys/lock` are host-wide (`:92-96, 225-228, 273-284`).
- **Revoking a worker token** only sets `revoked_at` (`pool_api.py:473-487`, despite its docstring), and `authenticate` reads it at hello only (`workers.py:299-320`); disabling or deleting a machine leaves its helper connected.
- **The hub forgets a request once it is resolved.** `submit`'s timeout resolves the request and sends `CANCEL` (`workers.py:237-243`), and a frame for a resolved id is dropped (`:128-135`), so `in_flight` falls while a v1 helper is still generating.
- **Moderators** get `VIEW` and `MANAGE` on `public` objects, and on `link` objects with the slug (`auth/access.py:162-168`).
- **`_game_limit`** counts every `games` row in `setup` or `playing`, whatever its `kind` (`app.py:185-202`).
- **`pool.import_registry`** gives each imported server a new `sv_` id, an "Imported" group and `reach = direct` (`pool/__init__.py:358-397`); `servers.owner_id` and `server_groups.owner_id` are NOT NULL with `ondelete CASCADE` (`db/models.py:283, 307`).
- **A cancelled responder declines.** `_run_responder` rejects an unanswered chat with "(no response)" after any cancel short of a stop or a crash (`session.py:386-417`); `suspend`, `update_seat` and `_crashed` cancel agents with the session lock held (`:278-300, 484-529, 531-552`).
- **`LLMAgent`'s clock** leaves reconnects and pauses out of the turn's time (`_waited`) but counts chat waits against it (`llm_agent.py:401-467, 523`), and sets the request's timeout before the call goes out (`:185-188`).
- **Admission** refuses `concurrency` when the `in_flight` it is given reaches the grant's `concurrency` or the machine's slots (`pool/admission.py:240-253`).
- **The game's debug route** takes named actions only (`meet_all`, `reveal`, `gold`; `app.py:688-705`).
- **Phase 2 handed three items to Phase 3:** `replay.js` reading the `Delta` replay format, after which `Full` is retired ("Replay data" and the 8.2 table's `replay_data` row in `crates/citar-engine/DESIGN.md`); ETags on views; and trade options leaving the per-refresh view (P2.1.2; plan §6.8). Every seat's players view still builds `trade_options` for each met civilization (`api/views/players.rs:370`).
- **The web client:** `mobile.js` reads `/api/shared/reports` (`:190`); End Turn's `.turn-box` sits at the bottom right at z-index 6 (`style.css:110`).

## P3.1 Goals, non-goals and exit

### P3.1.1 What Phase 3 delivers

1. **3A Accounts.** A fresh Alembic baseline; the visibility ladder `private | allowlist | link | server | global`; an owner and a visibility for every job type; profiles with per-field visibility and avatars; a members-only directory; an admin users page with the audit log; the legacy server registry merged into the Servers page.
2. **3C The prompt scheduler.** One scheduler per machine for every provider: full leases (a model playing every turn) and partial tickets (one prompt), priority, FIFO and round-robin across members, loaded-model-only pooled work, cancellation, generation-time metering. Task classes in admission; the Queue page's pooled prompts.
3. **3B The compute ledger.** `compute_events`, credits per hardware class, hourly rollups, given and used per member with self-use excluded, budgets that finally reserve and settle, the ratio on profiles.
4. **3E Trade chat.** Lobby timeouts enforced by the session, status events for the two parties, one model conversation per chat, the late-reply fix; a docked chat window per civilization with the deal builder inside.
5. **3D Hybrid seats.** A bot plays the turns and a model does the diplomacy categories the seat hands it; triggers, a 3-minute diplomat phase, the bot's veto on mixed deals, fallback to the bot; lobby, scenario, benchmark and probe support; the diplomacy leaderboard.
6. **3F The minimap.** It follows explored land only, by era, keeps its size, never shrinks, and is aspect-correct.
7. **3G Machine settings.** A dialog per machine: capabilities (LLM slots, model swaps, CPU limits for Phase 4), auto/manual hardware, models and power with the last scan kept, the hardware class, the trust tier.
8. **Phase 2's handoffs** (P3.5.8): `replay.js` decodes the `Delta` replay format and `Full` is retired; views answer with an ETag; trade options are fetched when the deal builder opens instead of riding in every view.
9. **Closing the paths where the server acts for whoever asks** (P3.0's last list): members' model blocks name a machine row (P3.2.9), the machine routes that touch the host stay admin-only (P3.2.8), and a revoked or disabled machine is disconnected.

### P3.1.2 Owner decisions that bind this phase

From the plan (§1) and the owner's instructions for Phase 3:
- **Hybrid:** every diplomacy category is a per-seat bot/LLM toggle; the model is prompted on events plus every N turns (default 10); difficulty is chosen per seat; an optional advisor, off in benchmarks; a separate diplomacy leaderboard from paired seeds.
- **Pooling:** full and partial task classes; pooled prompts may run between a lobby game's turns, never while a benchmark game holds the machine; pooled prompts only on loaded models unless the machine's owner allows swaps; speed metrics use generation time, never queue wait.
- **Chat:** a docked, non-modal window per civilization that pops up at once; strict turn-taking with a thinking/queued indicator; End Turn waits for a reply with a timeout (a model's reply 10 min including queue time, an AI waiting on a person 5 min, both lobby settings), then a hybrid seat falls back to its bot and a full model seat declines; a cap of 30 messages; bots answer with canned lines.
- **Minimap:** human seats only; explored land only; at most 44x28 early, the explored home continent from the Renaissance, the full map from the Information era; keeps its size, never zooms back in.
- **Accounts:** visibility `global` means the public web, keeping allowlist and link sharing; profiles with per-field visibility, a 256 KB avatar upload, a members-only directory with an opt-out, an admin users page; future restrictions are capability flags.
- **Ratio:** GPU generation-seconds plus CPU core-seconds plus a credit priced per hardware class; self-use excluded; the member chooses who sees it, admins always can.
- **General:** there are no users yet, so schema and data may break (a fresh Alembic baseline); stability and speed first; fix bugs rather than carry them; one agent at a time; never port 8799, `C:/dev/citar-play`, a push or the VPS.

### P3.1.3 Non-goals

- **Phase 4:** the Rust helper and protocol v2 (raw completions, a real cancel, settings and catalog frames, power reports), CPU jobs and their limits, proof of work, the automatic trust tiers, the Rust hardware scan. Phase 3 must not depend on any of it, and leaves these hooks (named in each section): the machine's `capabilities.cpu` and settings, `compute_events.kind = 'cpu'` with `verification`, hardware classes for CPUs, `trust_tier` on users and machines, scheduler capacity read from one settings function, and the cancel path `Conversation.close() -> hub.cancel()`.
- **Phase 5-6:** packaging, deploy, the release docs pass, the real-model hybrid soak (plan §4.8).
- Federation between CITAR servers; streaming completions (protocol v1's unused `CHUNK`).

### P3.1.4 Exit criteria

| Criterion | Measured by |
|---|---|
| Access is closed by default | `scripts/audit_routes.py --strict`: 0 unguarded. Every route added or changed in Phase 3 has a stranger test (another member's private thing is a 404; an anonymous caller gets 401 or 404) beside its owner and admin tests (P3.9.1). |
| Members cannot make the server dial or spend | A member's seat, scenario seat, suite or probe naming a base URL, a key or a key variable is refused and nothing is dialled; a member's machine row never reaches the host's LM Studio, hardware scan or environment (patched `httpx` and `subprocess` record no call); a revoked token or a disabled machine drops its helper within a second (3-01, 3-05). |
| Every job type is owned | Benchmark suites and runs, probes and probe runs, lab experiments, scenarios, maps, bot profiles and reports have rows with an owner and a visibility; a non-owner's control call is refused, a moderator's too (moderators may only unpublish); listings are filtered by visibility (3-02, 3-03). |
| Profiles, directory, admin | Field visibility honoured for anonymous visitors, members, the owner and admins, wherever an account appears; avatars validated; the audit log readable by admins (3-04). |
| One registry | `config/servers.json` imported once, under its own ids, and retired; every machine is a row; `#/servers` redirects to the Servers page (3-05, 3-06). |
| The scheduler is fair and never busy-waits | A fake hub answering `busy` makes no sleep and no metered retry; ordering, round-robin, lending, the benchmark exclusion and drain, the loaded-model rule, deadlines, cancellation and v1 draining tested as invariants; metrics carry generation time and queue time apart, and generation time is bounded by what the server observed (3-07 to 3-09). |
| The ledger adds up | Given and used per member with self-use excluded; rollups equal the raw sums; a hard budget blocks at its limit, on an online API too (3-10). |
| Chat behaves | Timeouts enforced by the session alone, in fake time; status events reach only the two parties and god-view spectators; a suspension or a restart loses no answer; drafts and the deal builder survive updates; End Turn shows who you wait for (3-11, 3-12). |
| Phase 2's handoffs are done | The recap plays from `Delta` frames and `replay_data(Full)` is gone; a view request with a current ETag gets 304; no per-refresh view carries `trade_options` (3-13). |
| Hybrid seats honour their categories | For each of the ten categories, with that category the model's, the bot does not act in it and the model can; fallback to the bot on timeout; the veto on mixed deals; paired control games valued alike on both arms and the leaderboard (3-14 to 3-16). |
| The minimap leaks nothing and never shrinks | `node --test` on the window logic; the engine's home region holds only explored tiles (3-17). |
| A hand-set machine stays hand-set | A helper's reconnect leaves manual hardware, models and power alone and keeps the scan as "last scan" (3-18). |
| It all works together | `python scripts/qa_phase3.py all` exits 0 on a dev server and the browser checklist of P3.9.5 passes with screenshots, recorded in "As built in 3-19". The Python suite, ruff, the route audit, the link check, `mkdocs build --strict`, the front-end checks, and (where Rust changed) fmt, clippy, nextest, refcheck `--strict --with-bot` and `cargo golden check` green. |

### P3.1.5 What changed since the plan was written

Each checked against the code at `d93345a`:
1. **3E's engine half is done.** Sequence numbers, required messages, the cap, the aliases, the empty-counter refusal, the End Turn rule, `close_negotiation`, the tool text and the chat lines in `turn_progress` are all in the Rust engine. What is left in the engine is the schema's `required` list (P3.5.4).
2. **The "late reply" defect has changed shape.** A person's reply to a model's offer is no longer lost at the end of the model's turn (the turn cannot end with the chat open), but a reply that comes after the tool's own wait reaches the model only when it tries `end_turn` or asks `get_diplomacy`. The fix is to put the reply in front of the model before its next step (P3.5.3).
3. **The hybrid turn is the engine's drive.** The plan's "bot plays with LLM-owned categories off, the lock is released, the diplomat runs, the turn ends" is `drive()` → `Stop::HybridDiplomat` → the diplomat outside the lock → `drive()` again (P3.6.3). `BasicBot.diplomacy_owners` is citar-bot's `Owners` on the bot handle.
4. **"Engine changes in Python first" happened, in Rust.** Phase 3's Rust changes are small: `respond_negotiation`'s schema (3-11); `replay_data(Full)` retired and `trade_options` moved out of the per-refresh view into its own call (3-13); a binding for `citar_bot::evaluate` and `normalize_seats` keeping a seat's `hybrid` object (3-14); and the minimap block in seat views (3-17).
5. **The registry merge is a storage move, not only a page merge.** `servers.py` (LLM seats, managed model loading, quiet hours, keys), benchmarks, costing and reports all read `config/servers.json`; it moves into the `servers` table (P3.2.8).
6. **Budgets were in money** (`budgets.currency`, priced by `costing.py`). They become credits, the ledger's unit, so they can reserve and settle against `compute_events` (P3.4.6).
7. **`public` becomes `global`,** and `server` is new; the string sweep covers every place P3.0 lists.
8. **"Helpers report power"** needs protocol v2's `power` frame: Phase 4. Phase 3 keeps the host's own power sampler and the JSONL power rows Phase 4 will write into.
9. **The plan's 'server' pseudo-user** is `provider_id = NULL` (and `consumer_id = NULL` for the server's own data collection), so no fake account appears in the directory or the admin page.
10. **Saves:** seats are saved with the session record (`asdict(seat)`, `session.py:888-893`), so a seat's `hybrid` block needs no save-format change; the engine needs none either.
11. **Report narratives** bypass `make_conversation` today; they move onto it so that the scheduler sees every model call (P3.3.4).
12. **The plan's defect list is not all fixed.** "Revoking a token doesn't disconnect the helper" (plan §2) still holds, and neither Phase 3's first draft nor plan §6.7 covered it; it is fixed in the registry merge (P3.2.8). The review also found the open paths of P3.0's last list, which the plan did not name: a member's seat can make the server dial any address with any of its environment variables as the key, and several machine routes act on the host.
13. **Phase 2 left three items to Phase 3** (P3.0): the `Delta` replay in `replay.js`, view ETags and trade options on demand. They are Phase 3 work (P3.5.8), not non-goals.

---

## P3.2 3A: accounts, visibility, ownership, profiles, directory, admin, the registry merge

### P3.2.1 Today

See P3.0's database and server bullets. Also: local mode signs in a real admin account called "local" (`citar/auth/accounts.py:432`, `auth/deps.py:76-82`); capabilities are `register_servers, run_reports, publish_public, create_games, invite` with role defaults in `settings_kv` and per-user overrides in `User.caps` (`auth/policy.py:29-49`); probation denies `register_servers`, `invite`, `publish_public`; nothing can edit caps or limits after `create_user` except the database; `audit.recent()` has no route; the game sharing routes exist (`app.py:1557-1636`) and `api.js` wraps them, but no page calls them.

### P3.2.2 The schema baseline

- **One revision replaces both.** `citar/migrations/versions/` loses `f064316a6b34` and `767c824c2fcb` and gains `0016a_baseline` (down-revision none), written by hand from the metadata and checked by a test that compares it with `Base.metadata` (alembic's `compare_metadata` finds nothing). It holds **every table and column Phase 3 needs**, so later packages fill in behaviour rather than migrate, as 2-00a wrote every signature first. A later package that must change the schema adds a revision on top, with an upgrade test from the baseline; nothing re-squashes.
- **Old databases.** `server/boot.py:migrate()` today runs `upgrade head`, or `create_all` plus `stamp head` for tables without `alembic_version`. It gains:
  - a database at `f064316a6b34` or `767c824c2fcb` (a 0.1.5 or Phase 0-2 database): on SQLite, the WAL is checkpointed (`PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)`), the engine is disposed, the file and any `-wal`/`-shm` left are **renamed** (never deleted) to `citar-0.1.5-<YYYYmmdd-HHMMSS>.db` beside it, a warning names the new path, and the server starts on a fresh database. If a rename fails (on Windows, another process holding the file), the server **refuses to start**, naming the file and the error; it never carries on with the old database or with half of it renamed (a rename that failed part-way is undone before the refusal). On Postgres it refuses to start with a message saying to create a new database (`CITAR_DB_URL`) or drop the schema, since a server process should not rename someone's database;
  - any other revision the scripts do not know (a newer build's): refuse to start, as today's alembic error, with the message spelled out;
  - tables without `alembic_version` (only tests create those, with `db.create_all()`): `stamp head` only if `users.listed` exists (the baseline's), otherwise treated as an old database.
  - Saves are not in the database: a game loaded from disk gets its `games` row again (`ownership.register_existing`, now called on load).
- **Tables and columns** (`citar/db/models.py`):
  - **`users`** gains `bio` (Text, at most 1,000 characters), `links` (JSON, at most 3 `{label, url}`), `avatar_path` (String(200), relative to the data directory, null), `field_visibility` (JSON, P3.2.6), `listed` (Boolean, default true), `trust_tier` (String(16), default `new`: `new|provisional|trusted|quarantined`, set by admins in Phase 3, automated in Phase 4). `avatar_url` stays for the sign-in provider's picture.
  - **`owned_items`** (new; P3.2.4): `id` (`oi_<hex>`), `kind` (String(24), indexed), `ref` (String(120): the id on disk), `owner_id` (FK users, SET NULL; null for system items), `name`, `visibility` (default `private`), `slug` (unique, as games'), `path` (String(512), relative to the state directory), `meta` (JSON), `collection` (Boolean, default false: the server's own data collection, P3.4.2), `created_at`, `updated_at`, `deleted_at`; unique `(kind, ref)`.
  - **`reports`** is dropped: a report is an owned item of kind `report`, its `scope` and `spec` in `meta`.
  - **`games`**: `status` becomes `setup|playing|finished|closed` (P3.2.5).
  - **`servers`** gains `is_host` (Boolean: the machine running CITAR), `server_resource` (Boolean: the site provides it, so it is the server's in the ledger; true for the host and for online APIs an admin registers), `capabilities` (JSON, P3.8.2), `hardware_mode`, `models_mode`, `power_mode` (String(8): `auto|manual`; defaults auto, auto, manual), `hardware_detected` (JSON, null) and `detected_at`, `gpu_class_id` and `cpu_class_id` (FK `hardware_classes`, null), `class_override` (Boolean), `trust_tier` (as users'), `flags` (JSON: `overreports` with its count and last time, set by the scheduler, P3.3.4). `reach` (`direct|worker`) and `max_concurrent` stay; `max_concurrent` becomes the one site number for LLM slots (P3.2.8). `owner_id` becomes **nullable with `ondelete SET NULL`**, as does `server_groups.owner_id`: deleting an account must never delete the host or the site's API rows, and a row without an owner is managed by admins only.
  - **`hardware_classes`** (new; P3.4.3): `id` (`hc_<hex>`), `kind` (`gpu|cpu|api`), `name`, `rate` (Float: credits per generation-second for `gpu`, per core-second for `cpu`, per unit of the registry's currency of token cost for `api`), `match` (JSON), `priority` (Integer), `is_default` (Boolean), `updated_at`, `updated_by`. Seeded by the baseline: `GPU (unclassified)` 1.0, `CPU core (unclassified)` 0.05, `Online API` 1,000, each the default of its kind.
  - **`compute_events`** and **`compute_rollups`** (new; P3.4.2, P3.4.5).
  - **`budgets`**: `currency` becomes `unit` (String(8), default and only value `credits`); `budget_entries` amounts are credits.
  - **`acl_entries`**: `object_type` takes the item kinds; `OBJECT_TYPES` in `models.py` and `access.py` follow.

### P3.2.3 The visibility ladder

| Level | Who may view | Listed for | Needs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `private` | the owner and admins | the owner and admins | |
| `allowlist` | the owner, admins and the accounts with an ACL entry | the same | |
| `link` | the same, and anyone presenting the slug | the owner, admins and ACL accounts only (unlisted) | |
| `server` | the owner, admins, ACL accounts and every signed-in member whose status is `probation` or `active` (no slug needed) | members | |
| `global` | anyone on the internet | everyone | `publish_global` |

ACL entries (view, play or manage, as today) count at every level, so sharing with a person never needs a wider level.

- **One place.** `access.VISIBILITY` is the tuple and `access.check_visibility(value)` the validator; `app.py`'s game creation, `pool/__init__.py`'s groups, `share_api`, `ownership.to_client`, `access.set_visibility` and every new sharing route call it. A test checks the sites rather than grepping a word that `User.public()`, `public_server` and `INTENTIONALLY_PUBLIC` also use: an AST scan of `citar/` finds every assignment to a `.visibility` attribute, every `visibility=` keyword and every comparison with a `visibility` name, and fails on a string constant that is not in `access.VISIBILITY`; and `check_visibility("public")` raises.
- **`access.on`**: `server` grants `VIEW` to a signed-in viewer with an active status; `global` grants `VIEW` to anyone. ACL entries add to any level. A suspended or deleted account keeps nothing.
- **Moderators unpublish; they do not manage.** A new permission, `access.MODERATE`, is the moderator's only extra: on a `global` object, and on a `link` object presented with its slug, a moderator gets `VIEW` and `MODERATE`; on `server` objects nothing extra (members see them already), and below `link` nothing. `MODERATE` allows one action, **unpublish**: `POST /api/games/{gid}/unpublish` and `POST /api/items/{kind}/{ref}/unpublish` (MODERATE or MANAGE) set the visibility to `private`, rotate the slug and write an audit row with the moderator's reason; the owner sees the reason on the item. Every control route (run and job control, stop, delete, rerun, priority, rename, sharing, seats, pause) keeps requiring `MANAGE`, which moderators no longer get. This replaces today's `VIEW` and `MANAGE` for moderators (`access.py:162-168`), and matches plan A4: only the owner or an admin controls a thing.
- **`visible_filter`**: `global` rows for everyone; plus `server` rows for members; plus own rows and ACL rows; admins everything; `link` rows never listed.
- **Capability:** `publish_public` is renamed `publish_global` (`CAPABILITIES`, `ROLE_DEFAULTS`, `PROBATION_DENIES`, the console's policy editor); `server` needs no capability. A member's own profile fields may be `global` without it (they are the member's own data) unless the account is on probation (P3.2.6).
- **The share URL** (`ownership.to_client`, the item sharing routes) is offered for `link` (carrying its key), `server` and `global` (without one).

### P3.2.4 Owned items

- **`citar/items.py`**: `register(sdb, kind, ref, owner, name, path=None, visibility="private", meta=None)`, `rename`, `delete` (sets `deleted_at`), `get(sdb, kind, ref)`, `resolve(sdb, kind, ref, viewer, slug=None) -> (row, Access)`, `require(...)` (raises 404/403 as `Access.require` does), `visible(sdb, kind, viewer)`, `adopt_orphans(sdb, owner)`. `access.on` learns `OwnedItem` (its `object_type` is the row's `kind`).
- **Kinds and their producers**, each registering at creation and deleting at deletion:

| Kind | `ref` | Registered by | Files stay at |
|---|---|---|---|
| `benchmark_suite` | suite id | `benchmarks.save_suite` | `BENCH_DIR/suites/` |
| `benchmark_run` | run id | `create_run` (the run's owner owns its games' rows too, kind `benchmark`, the run's visibility) | `BENCH_DIR/runs/` |
| `probe` | probe id | `probes.save_probe` | `saves/probes/` |
| `probe_run` | run id | `ProbeRunner.start` | `saves/probes/runs/` |
| `lab_experiment` | experiment name | `bots_api` POST `/experiments`, `lab submit` | the lab's queue and done folders |
| `scenario` | scenario id | the scenario editor's save | `saves/scenarios/` |
| `map` | map id | `PUT /api/maps/{id}` | the maps folder |
| `bot_profile` | profile id | `bots_api` create and fork | the profiles folder |
| `report` | report id | `ReportRunner.start` (the runner now writes the row) | `saves/reports/<id>/` |

- **System items.** Shipped content (the built-in bot profiles `standard`, `classic-production`, `idle`; example scenarios and maps, if any) is registered at boot with `owner_id = NULL` and `global`; only admins manage them.
- **Adoption.** Files of these kinds without rows are registered to the first admin (in local mode, the local owner), `private`, with one audit row. A fresh server-mode database has no admin at boot (`admin_cli create-admin` or the setup page makes one afterwards), so adoption runs at boot when an admin exists, and again from `accounts.on_first_admin()`, which every path that makes an account an admin calls (`admin_cli create-admin`, `create-user` with the admin role, `set-role`, the setup flow); until it has run, `settings_kv.orphans_adopted` is unset and every route of an unregistered file answers 404 (`items.require` finds no row), so nothing is unguarded meanwhile. After the fresh start there are few or no such files, but a half-written one must not become an unowned, unguarded object.
- **Capabilities** gain `run_benchmarks` (user default true), `run_probes` (true), `run_lab` (false: the lab uses the server's CPU, today admin-only), `edit_bots` (false: today admin-only), `full_llm` and `partial_llm` (true; P3.3.7). `run_reports` stays.
- **Routes and their gates.** Every route below resolves its item through `items.require`; listings use `items.visible`. "Create" means the capability plus, where the work runs on a machine, admission (P3.3.7).

| Routes | Read | Change |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/benchmarks/status`, `/runs` | member; runs filtered | |
| `GET/PUT /api/benchmarks/settings` | member | admin (server-wide score weights) |
| `/api/benchmarks/suites[...]` | VIEW the suite | create `run_benchmarks`; update and delete MANAGE |
| `POST /api/benchmarks/runs` | VIEW the suite | `run_benchmarks`; the run is the caller's |
| `/runs/{id}/control`, `/jobs/{job}/control` | | MANAGE the run |
| `/runs/{id}/jobs/{job}/watch` | VIEW the run | |
| `/api/maps[...]`, `/maps/validate`, `/maps/generate` | VIEW the map | create `create_games`; update and delete MANAGE |
| `/api/scenario-editor[...]` | the editing session's creator or an admin | saving creates the scenario item, or needs MANAGE on it |
| `/api/scenarios[...]`, `/launch` | VIEW | delete MANAGE; launch `create_games` and the game limit |
| `/api/probes[...]`, `/probes/{id}/run` | VIEW | create `run_probes`; update and delete MANAGE; run `run_probes` |
| `/api/probe-runs[...]`, `/open/{case}/{rep}` | VIEW | stop and delete MANAGE |
| `GET /api/lab`, `/lab/report/{name}` | member, experiments filtered; VIEW | |
| `/api/bots/profiles[...]`, `/fork` | VIEW | create and fork `edit_bots`; update and delete MANAGE |
| `POST /api/bots/experiments` | | `run_lab` |
| `/api/reports[...]` (from `admin_api.py`) | VIEW | create `run_reports`; rerun and delete MANAGE |
| `GET /r/{slug}` | `global`, or `link` with its slug | |
| `GET /api/queue` | member; only machines the caller may view (P3.3.8); others' items show as "someone's <kind>" unless visible | |
| `POST /api/queue/priority` | | MANAGE the item (a game through its `games` row) |
| `GET /api/models/scores`, `/api/metrics/compare` | member; pooled data only (below) | |
| `GET/PUT /api/items/{kind}/{ref}/sharing`, `POST .../sharing/users`, `DELETE .../sharing/users/{handle}`, `POST .../sharing/rotate` (new) | MANAGE | MANAGE |
| `POST /api/items/{kind}/{ref}/unpublish`, `POST /api/games/{gid}/unpublish` (new) | | MODERATE or MANAGE (P3.2.3) |
| Benchmark suites' model entries, probe subjects, scenario seats | | model blocks as P3.2.9 |

- **`share_api`'s report routes** become the generic item routes; `/r/{id}` becomes `/r/{slug}` for reports (a `global` report is also reachable by id).
- **A report gathers only what its owner may see.** Report building was admin-only, so `citar/reports/data.py` reads every save, run and ledger row in scope. Opened to members, it takes the report's owner: per-game, per-run and per-activity sections cover the owner's own activities and the items the owner may view; machine costs (configurations, tariffs, depreciation) only the owner's machines; anything wider comes from pooled aggregates (`aggregate.scope_for`, the minimum-contributor threshold). An admin's report sees everything, as today. The report's `scope` (`own|pooled|both`) keeps its meaning within those limits.
- **The pooling opt-out.** `scoring._reports` and `/api/metrics/compare` read only games whose row allows it for the viewer: the viewer's own games, plus games with `data_sharing = pool` whose owners are not opted out, through `aggregate.scope_for` with its minimum-contributor threshold. Benchmark games are rows now, so pooled aggregates see them.
- **The sharing dialog** (`citar/web/js/share.js`): visibility select, the share link with copy and rotate, the people with access (handle, permission, remove), "add by handle"; used by games (the existing routes), reports, benchmark runs, probe runs, scenarios, maps and bot profiles. A member who opens a page they cannot see gets the router's "not found" page, never a stack trace. Every caller of the report routes `share_api` loses moves to the item routes, the phone site included (`mobile.js:190` reads `/api/shared/reports` for its reports tab).

### P3.2.5 The games index

`ownership.sync(sdb, session)` runs from the session (off the game lock, through `ownership.sync_quietly`, on the writer's thread after an autosave lands, so it costs no lock time) when the phase or the turn changes and when a session stops: `status = "playing"` while `phase == "playing"`, `"finished"` once it is `over` (with `finished_at`), `"closed"` when a running game is closed without finishing, `"playing"` again when it is loaded.
- **The game limit** counts rows with `kind = "game"` in `setup` or `playing` only: a member's benchmark and probe games are the run's, limited by the benchmark and probe queues, not by the lobby's two games.
- **No row stays "playing" without a game.** At boot, after `restore_live`, every row in `setup` or `playing` with no live session (a failed restore, a deleted save folder, a crash between registration and the first save) is marked `closed`, with a log line per row.
- **`GameSession.owner_id`** is set from the game's row wherever a session comes to life: the create and load routes, `SessionManager._register_loaded`, `restore_live`, and the benchmark and probe runners (from the run's owner). The scheduler and the ledger read it; a session without one is the server's own (P3.4.2).

### P3.2.6 Profiles and the directory

- **Field visibility.** `users.field_visibility` holds `{profile, display_name, avatar, bio, links, items, ratio}`, each `private|server|global`. Defaults: `profile`, `display_name`, `avatar`, `bio`, `links`, `items` = `server`; `ratio` = `private`. A field shows when the viewer's level reaches both the field's and `profile`'s: anonymous visitors see `global`, members `server` and `global`, the owner and admins everything. The handle is always shown wherever the account appears (seats, sharing, grants). Setting a field to `global` is refused on probation ("New accounts can show their profile to members only").
- **`User.public(viewer)`** returns `id`, `handle`, and `display_name` and `avatar` (the upload's route or the sign-in picture) **only when those fields are visible to `viewer`** under the rule above; otherwise `display_name` is the handle and `avatar` is null. `role` and `status` move to `User.account()` (the owner's own view and admins). The viewer is passed at every call site: `shared_with`, grant subjects, server owners, seat owners (seat lists of `global` games are read by anonymous visitors), the directory, the Queue page and the admin pages. A test lists the callers (`git grep "\.public("` over `citar/` against a fixed list), so a new caller without a viewer fails.
- **Search** in the directory matches the handle, and the display name only for accounts whose `display_name` is visible to the caller, so a hidden name is never revealed by its hits.
- **Avatars.** `POST /api/auth/me/avatar` (multipart, at most 256 KB) accepts PNG, JPEG and WebP, recognised by their magic bytes and with dimensions read from the header (PNG IHDR; JPEG SOFn; WebP VP8, VP8L, VP8X), at most 1024x1024; anything else is refused with the rule. Stored as `<data dir>/avatars/<user id>-<sha256[:12]>.<ext>` (the previous file removed), never under the code or the saves. `GET /api/users/{handle}/avatar` serves it with the type fixed by the validated format, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox` and a cache lifetime, when the `avatar` field is visible to the caller; else 404. `DELETE /api/auth/me/avatar`. No image library is added: nothing is decoded, only the header is read.
- **`PUT /api/auth/me`** also takes `bio` (control characters stripped, 1,000 characters), `links` (at most 3; label up to 40 characters; `http`/`https` URLs up to 300 characters), `field_visibility` (known keys, known values) and `listed`.
- **`GET /api/users`** (members only): accounts with `listed` and an active status, newest first, searchable by handle or display name, each with the fields visible to the caller; paged by 50.
- **`GET /api/users/{handle}`** (anyone): 404 unless `profile` is visible to the caller; the visible fields; the account's items visible to the caller (games rows and owned items, through `visible_filter` with `owner_id = them`, 20 per kind, newest first) when `items` is visible; the ratio block when `ratio` is (3-10). A hidden account (not `listed`) still has a page for whoever may see it; it is only missing from the directory.

### P3.2.7 The admin users page and the audit log

- `GET /api/admin/users` (filters: text, role, status; paged), `GET /api/admin/users/{id}`, `PATCH /api/admin/users/{id}` with any of `role`, `status` (with `reason` for a suspension), `caps` (`{cap: true | false | null}`, null removing the override), `invite_quota`, `max_concurrent_games`, `max_seats_per_game`, `trust_tier`, `listed`. Each change is one audit row naming the fields; the last admin cannot demote or suspend themselves. `GET /api/admin/audit` (filters: actor, action, object type and id, before; at most 200 a page). All `require_role("admin")`.
- **CLI parity** in `citar/server/admin_cli.py`: `create-user` (handle, email, password, role, status active, email verified: the QA harness uses it), `set-caps`, `set-limits`, `set-trust`, `audit`.

### P3.2.8 The registry merge

- **Every machine is a `servers` row.** `servers.py` keeps its public functions (`find`, `get`, `list_servers`, `host`, `resolve_llm`, `seat_ref`, `describe_seat`, `model_entry`, `profile`, `ensure_model`, `restricted_now`, `restricted_text`, `server_name`, `normalize_server`) with their return shapes, and reads and writes rows instead of `config/servers.json`: a row's `config` already holds the `normalize_server()` dict (`pool/__init__.py`), and `reach` says who dials (`direct`: CITAR; `worker`: the helper). `with_pooled()` becomes the identity. The electricity plans move to `settings_kv` (`electricity_plans`); `host_server_id` becomes the row with `is_host`.
- **One-time import.** When `config/servers.json` exists and `settings_kv.registry_imported` is unset, `pool.import_registry` brings every server in **under its own id** (so seats, suites, saved games, the usage ledger and the keystore entries keep resolving), in no group (sharing one is a later choice of its owner), owned by the first admin (in local mode, the local owner), `reach = direct`, `is_host` for the registry's host, `server_resource` for the host and for `api` kinds; then it sets `registry_imported` and renames the file `servers.json.imported-<date>`. API keys stay in the keystore under the same ids. A fresh server-mode database has no admin at boot, so the import runs at boot when an admin exists, and again from `accounts.on_first_admin()` (P3.2.4) once something makes the first one; until then `servers.py` reads nothing (no machine is usable) and the boot log says why. `POST /api/pool/import-registry` (`pool_api.py:572`) is retired.
- **One concurrency number.** `Server.max_concurrent` is the site's LLM slots; `config.connection.max_parallel` is read once on import and dropped from new configs (`normalize_server` keeps reading it as an alias). The helper's `hello.max_concurrent` is its local ceiling, and the scheduler takes the smaller (P3.3.2).
- **Routes, each with its own gate.** `admin_api.py`'s routes keep their paths; owner-or-admin means MANAGE on the row (`access.on(Server)`); "direct or host" means the row has `reach = direct` (admin-only to create, below) or `is_host`. A member's helper row is never dialled by the server and never reaches the host's LM Studio: its models come from its helper's hello, and a host-touching route on it answers 404, without spawning `lms`, loading the SDK or sending a request.

| Route | Gate |
|---|---|
| `GET /api/servers` | member: the caller's rows (all for admins) |
| `GET /api/servers/new`, `POST /api/servers` (create or save) | owner-or-admin; `reach = direct` admin-only unless the provider is `dryrun` |
| `DELETE /api/servers/{sid}`, `/hardware`, `/rates`, `/restricted` | owner-or-admin (they edit the row's own configuration) |
| `/detect`, `/state`, `/load`, `/unload`, `/loading`, `/loaded-config`, `/estimate` | owner-or-admin **and** direct or host (so in practice admins, and owners of direct dry-run rows, whose provider makes no call) |
| `POST /api/servers/{sid}/collect`, `GET /api/lmstudio/devices` | admin, on the host row only (they read the CITAR host) |
| `POST/DELETE /api/servers/{sid}/key` | owner-or-admin for the `keyring`, `file` and `none` backends; the `env` backend (naming one of the server's environment variables) admin-only |
| `PUT /api/servers-settings`, `POST /api/keys/unlock`, `POST /api/keys/lock`, `/api/electricity-plans*` | admin (host-wide) |
| `GET /api/hardware-collector/{name}` | member (it serves the collector script, nothing of the host) |

  **Creating or switching a machine to `reach = direct` is admin-only** unless its provider is `dryrun`: a direct machine is a URL the CITAR server dials, and letting members name one would let them make the server request addresses on its own network. For the same reason `POST /api/llm/models` (the lobby's "Detect models", which fetches a caller-supplied base URL; `app.py:321-363`) becomes admin-only except for the `dryrun` provider: a member's machine reports its models through its helper. `/api/pool/servers` remains the sharing view for everyone a group reaches (redacted by `public_server`).
- **Revoked and disabled machines are dropped.** `hub.drop(server_id, reason)` closes a helper's socket and fails its pending requests as not retryable; revoking a worker token (`pool_api.py:473`), disabling a machine and deleting one call it, after their transaction commits. The scheduler refuses requests and tickets to a disabled row (P3.3.2), so a dropped machine earns nothing more in the ledger. A helper that reconnects with a revoked token is refused at hello, as today.
- **The test registry.** `tests/__init__.py`'s `TEST_REGISTRY` file gives way to a seeded test database: a fixture builds the same machines as rows (with the same ids) through the import path, so the suites that read `servers.py` keep their machines and the import is exercised by every run; a test checks the seed against the old dict.
- **The page.** `#/pool` is the one Servers page: every machine the viewer owns or may use, direct and helper machines alike, with the sharing it has today. `servers.js`'s editors become dialogs (connection and models with load profiles; hardware and power; costs; quiet hours; API key for direct machines) opened from a machine's card; `#/servers` and `#/servers/<id>` redirect to `#/pool` (opening that machine). The nav's "Servers" goes to `#/pool`.
- Benchmarks' `resolve_group` and `_resolve_pooled` merge (every group is a row; `reach` decides whether its seats play through the helper).

### P3.2.9 Model blocks from members

A seat's `llm` block decides which address the server dials and which key it sends, so a member may only **name a machine**, never describe one:
- **`servers.member_llm(block, user) -> dict`** is the one gate. For a non-admin it keeps `server_id`, `model`, `model_id`, `profile_id` and the `SEAT_OVERRIDES` keys, and drops everything else (`provider`, `base_url`, `api_key`, `api_key_env`, `timeout`, unknown keys). The `server_id` must resolve to a row the member may use (P3.3.7 admits it); a block without one is refused (400, "Pick a machine for this seat") unless its `provider` is `dryrun`, which dials nothing. Until the registry merge (3-05) a machine of `config/servers.json` is not a row, and `pool_seats.authorize` skips it today, so a member could spend the host's GPU or the site's API key with no grant: from 3-01 such a machine is refused for non-admins unless its provider is `dryrun` (403, "only an administrator can seat this machine until it is shared"), and from 3-05 every machine is a row and admission decides. Admins (and the local owner, who is an admin) keep inline blocks, which is how tests and scripts give a raw provider.
- **Its callers:** `create_game` and `update_seat` (which now also runs `pool_seats.authorize` on the new block), the scenario editor's save and `/api/scenarios/{id}/launch` (a scenario saved by an admin with an inline block launches for a member only after the block names a machine), benchmark suites' model entries and probe subjects (both already name machines; the gate refuses anything more).
- **`resolve_llm`** keeps building the configuration from the row when a `server_id` is present (only `SEAT_OVERRIDES` come from the seat, `servers.py:774-776`), and passes an inline block through only when the session's owner is an admin or the session has no owner (tests); otherwise the seat's agent reports "this seat has no machine" and the seat is skipped as disconnected.
- **Tests** (3-01's gate): a member's seat carrying `base_url`, `api_key_env` or `provider: openai_compatible` without a `server_id` gets 400 on `create_game`, `update_seat`, a scenario save and a launch, and with `httpx`, the OpenAI and Anthropic clients patched nothing is dialled and no environment variable is read; the same block from an admin is accepted.

### P3.2.10 The web pages

- `#/users` (members: the directory, a search box, cards with avatar, display name, handle, bio's first line), `#/u/<handle>` (a profile; open to anonymous visitors when `global`; the router lets this route through without a session), `#/admin/users` (admins: a table with filters, an edit drawer for role, status, caps, limits, trust, and the account's audit rows), and a "Profile" card on `#/account` (bio, links, avatar upload with preview, the field visibility selects, the directory toggle, "view as a member" and "view as a visitor" links).
- The nav gains "People" for members and "Users" beside the admin's "Server".

### P3.2.11 Tests

`tests/test_access.py` (the ladder table: five levels by eight kinds of viewer by view, play, manage and moderate), `tests/test_migrations.py` (P3.2.2), `tests/test_items_access.py` (table-driven over every route in P3.2.4: stranger, ACL viewer, moderator, owner, admin, anonymous), `tests/test_profiles.py` (field visibility by viewer, avatar validation with a corpus of good and bad headers, links and bio limits, the directory), `tests/test_admin_users.py`, `tests/test_registry_merge.py` (a fixture `servers.json` imports to rows, under the file's ids, equal to `normalize_server()` of each entry; every `servers.*` function answers as before; a member's direct machine is refused, a dry-run one allowed; every route of P3.2.8's table by a member on their own helper row, with `httpx` and `subprocess` patched), `tests/test_seat_blocks.py` (P3.2.9).

**As built in 3-01: the schema baseline, the visibility ladder, the games index, members' model blocks** (P3.2.2, P3.2.3, P3.2.5, P3.2.9; Appendix D). One agent, 2026-10-11, from 15:26 to 16:28 (-04:00), 62 minutes, on branch `p-3-01` from `f12aa45`. No Rust changed.

- **The schema.** `citar/migrations/versions/0016a_baseline.py` was rendered from the metadata by alembic and edited by hand (types, layout, the seeds); `tests/test_migrations.py` holds it to `Base.metadata` with `compare_metadata` and renders it for Postgres offline (`upgrade head --sql`, where the seeds are plain INSERTs because a JSON value has no literal form). The ORM classes later packages fill in are `OwnedItem`, `HardwareClass`, `ComputeEvent` and `ComputeRollup`; `Report` is gone, and `share_api`'s report routes read owned items of kind `report` (by their `ref`) until 3-03 moves them. Choices the design left open: the default classes have fixed ids (`hc_gpu`, `hc_cpu`, `hc_api`; `models.HARDWARE_CLASS_SEEDS`, which the revision writes out and a test compares), and `db.create_all` seeds them too; `compute_events.machine_id` is an indexed string, not a foreign key, so an event outlives its machine; `compute_rollups` is keyed by `(user_id, hour, side, kind)` with the user `CASCADE` (rollups are per member; the server's own work has none). `Budget.currency` is `unit` (`credits`); `pool.budgets` and the grant form follow, while the estimates are still priced by `costing` until 3-10.
- **Old databases.** `boot.migrate` as P3.2.2 says, with the migrations run on the engine's own URL. The rename takes a `-journal` too, never overwrites an earlier archive (a `-2` suffix in the same second), and undoes what it moved before refusing; the notice heads the boot's warnings. `tests/data/citar-0.1.5.db` was built by the two old revisions at `f12aa45` (whose `citar/` is `d93345a`'s) with one account and one game row, checkpointed and vacuumed to 90 KB. SQLite removes a log it does not want when its last connection closes, so the test of "renamed with its `-wal` and `-shm`" makes them after the boot lets go of the file, as a crashed or another process leaves them. No Postgres was at hand: its path is the dialect unit test and the offline rendering.
- **The ladder.** As designed, with `access.check_publish` (on the ladder, and `global` needs `publish_global`) shared by `set_visibility`, `create_game` and `pool.create_group`, which before let anyone create a public group; `access.BadVisibility` is both an HTTP 400 and a `ValueError`; `access.unpublish` and `access.last_unpublished` (the game's sharing GET shows the last unpublish to whoever manages it); `Access.client()` gains `can_moderate`; `policy.can` falls back to the built-in role default for a capability a saved template does not name. The AST scan is `tests/test_access.py`'s `VisibilityScan`, with a test that it catches each kind of site. There is no visibility choice in the web client yet (the sharing dialog is 3-03's): the console's probation text and `api.unpublishGame` are what changed there. `User.public()` keeps `id`, `handle`, `display_name` and `avatar_url`; `User.account()` (the session's own view, `/api/auth/me`, login) adds the rest.
- **The games index.** As designed: the save writer syncs the row after a round's autosave counts as written (so `flush_saves` never waits on the database), the session's stop after its writer closes, and `SessionManager._register_loaded` indexes a loaded game (`ownership.index_loaded`) and gives the session its owner before its driver starts; `_close_orphan_rows` runs at boot after the benchmarks and probes are back. Found on the way and fixed: (1) the lobby's **End game** (`DELETE /api/games/{gid}`) set `deleted_at`, which hid the game's saves from their owner though the dialog says they are kept; it now closes the row (`closed`, or `finished`), and deleting every save of a game deletes it. (2) A game that ends inside a turn (a capital taken) took its last autosave only at the ten-minute close; it takes it at once, so its row is `finished` at once. (3) `create_game` started the game before its row and owner existed; it now starts once both are in place. (4) In a process that never opened its database (`citar bench`) the index does nothing, rather than opening, creating or switching to WAL the default per-user database.
- **Members' model blocks.** `servers.member_llm` as designed, on `create_game`, `update_seat` (then admission, also when only the type changes), the scenario editor's seats and save, and the launch. Beyond the letter: a model seat of a game being made or changed must name a machine (`required`), so a member's empty or unfilled block is a 400 rather than a seat with no machine; a scenario's seats are defaults its launch fills in, so its save still takes them. An inline **dry-run** block is a member's too, kept to its harmless keys, and `resolve_llm` passes it through in a member's game: the design admits dry runs from members and its QA plays members' games with dry-run seats, and "an inline block only for an admin's game" is read as the blocks that dial something. A seat whose block cannot be resolved plays `NoMachineAgent` (its turns skipped by the driver, one `agent_error`, its chats declined), never the block as it is, which the old fallback did. `pool.seats.authorize` does not name a machine the caller cannot see.
- **Also closed.** The scenario launch made a game with no row (its member could not even list it): it is now the caller's lobby game (`create_games`, the game limit, its row and owner, every merged seat through the gate, admission before anything of it reaches the disk: `SessionManager.register_new`), started once registered. The scenario editor opened on a live game or a save handed any member that game's whole state by its id; it now needs MANAGE on the game. The public report's fallback page escapes the report's name. The benchmark and probe runners set `owner_id` from `run["owner_id"]`, which 3-02 records.
- **The session's overhead (P3.9.7).** `scripts/session_overhead.py` (100 rounds of 4 bots on small, seed 5002, five runs each way, alternating): at the start, before any change, ratios **2.378 and 2.210** (two runs; mean **2.29**), with 0.27 s through `EngineGame.drive` and 0.65 s through the session; over 300 rounds 1.67. At the end, with the game's row kept once a round (the script's default now): 2.304; without a row: 2.176, 2.349, 2.309. The reference for later packages is **2.29**: a ratio above 2.87 (1.25x) fails P3.9.7.
- **Gates.** (1) `tests/test_migrations.py` 18 tests; (2) `tests/test_access.py` 43, with a table of every game route 3-01 added or changed by an anonymous caller, a stranger, an ACL viewer, the owner and an admin (P3.9.1); (3) `tests/test_games_index.py` 11; (4) `test_access.UserPublic`; (5) `tests/test_seat_blocks.py` 22, every request under a watch of `httpx`'s transports, both clients and the key variables. S1: 730 tests OK in 171 s (665 at the start, 150 s). S2 ruff clean. S3 191 routes, 0 unguarded. S4 every internal link resolves. S5 `mkdocs build --strict` and the wiki regenerated. S6 the 30 modules parse as ES modules. CI only: test.yml on its three OSes with its package, installer and Docker jobs.
- **Fix round** (2026-10-11, 16:46 to 17:12, 26 minutes). The review found a blocker, a major and seven minors; all were fixed except the last, which is left to 3-03 as recorded below.
  - *A request's own write blocked the index on its thread* (the blocker). A sign-in last seen over a minute ago has its `last_seen_at` written by the request, and its transaction kept SQLite's write lock while the route closed or loaded a game. The index's own write of the row, on the same thread, then waited out the busy timeout (10 s) and gave up, so End game left the row `playing` and a load left the session ownerless. `deps.principal` now resolves the caller without autoflush and commits what that changed before the route runs (a request that changed nothing keeps its connection: the commit costs no measurable time per request). A side effect: an idle sign-in's revocation is now kept even when the request then answers 401. The two error paths that close a game after registering it (`create_game`, the scenario launch) roll back the request's write first; for a new game the index finds no row to write, so this is defence, and the tests check the invariant itself (`_write_lock_free` at each index write). `sync_quietly` logs a failure at WARNING with the game. The stop's sync stays on the stopping thread, after the game's lock and the writer are released: moving it to the writer's thread would not help, since the stop waits for the writer, and doing it later would let an End game race the next create against the limit.
  - *A seat that plays no model was refused for its block* (the major). The lobby sends every seat's block, so a seat made LLM and then Bot again carried the lobby's default block and the game was refused. `_member_seat` now refuses a block only on a model seat; on any other seat it keeps what the gate keeps (an admin's block as it is, a member's machine) and drops what the gate would refuse. Making that seat a model seat later checks the block it then has.
  - *Minors.* A block that is not an object is a 400 for anyone (`servers.llm_block`), never a 500. `update_seat` checks only a new block or a seat newly made a model seat, so a rename succeeds while the machine is outside its window, and an ACL manager can rename an admin's inline seat. The last unpublish names a role ("you", "its owner", "an administrator", "a moderator", "someone who manages it"; `access.unpublished_by`, from the `by_role` the audit row now records), never a handle. `NoOutbound` also refuses `subprocess.run` and `Popen`. The P3.9.1 table gained `/api/saves/load` (a stranger and anonymous 404, an ACL viewer 403, the owner and an admin 200, the game staying its owner's) and the scenario launch (anonymous 401, a member 200 and then 429 over the limit, the owner and an admin 200, each owning what they launched). `index_loaded` takes the caller's kind: an opened probe case gets a `probe` row, not a lobby one. A row nobody owns pools nothing (`data_sharing = private`), and the administrator who loads such a save takes it with their own pooling default.
  - *Left for 3-03: share URLs do not open in the web client.* `access.share_url` gives `#/game/<gid>` (`?k=<slug>` for `link`), but the router (`app.js`) opens only `#/game/<gid>/<token>`, sends an anonymous visitor without `k` to sign in, and the WebSocket URL (`api.connectWS`) carries no `k`. The `link` URLs never opened either. Nothing in the client shows these URLs yet. 3-03's sharing dialog, which hands them out, adds the route: a spectator `GameScreen` with no token that keeps `?k=` in its own links, `k` on the socket, `#/game/<gid>` let through without a session, and the router's "not found" page. Browser check A1 covers it.
  - *Gates again.* (1) 18 tests; (2) 46; (3) 16; (4) `UserPublic` 3; (5) 26. S1: 742 tests OK in 185 s. S2 ruff clean. S3 191 routes, 0 unguarded. S4 every link resolves. S5 `mkdocs build --strict` and the wiki regenerated. S6 30 modules parse. The session's overhead: 2.165, against the reference 2.29 and the 2.87 limit. CI only, as before.

**As built in 3-02: owned items, every job type owned, gated and shareable** (P3.2.4; P3.2.9 for suites and probe subjects; Appendix D). One agent, 2026-10-11, from 17:28 to 18:22 (-04:00), on branch `p-3-02` from `2598a19`, rebased onto `12d14b8` (3-01's CI fix). No Rust changed.

- **`citar/items.py`** is the one writer of `owned_items` and the gate: `register` (a new item is the maker's and `private`; saving over a live one keeps its owner, visibility, sharing and slug; an id whose item was deleted is a new item with a row of its own, its old allowlist, link and unpublish gone), `rename`, `delete` (`deleted_at`), `get`, `resolve`, `require` (404 when the caller cannot see it or it has no row, administrators included; 403 when they see but may not do it), `writable` (the gate on saving under an id, before anything is written: MANAGE on a live row, a 404 for a file with no row, else the kind's capability, `CREATE_CAPS`), `listed`/`visible_refs`/`visible` (`access.visible_filter`), `permissions_by_ref` (listings carry `can_*` per item), `client` (the sharing block), system items and adoption. Its rows keep paths relative to the state directory (`relpath`, `file_path`; `share_api`'s public report reads through it). The facade gained `map_ref`, `map_file`, `map_dir`, `scenario_ref`, `scenario_file` and `scenario_dir`, so the server knows a map's or a scenario's id before the save makes it.
- **Producers.** Suites (the save route), runs (`BenchmarkScheduler.create_run(owner_id=, session=)`, the run's file recording `owner_id`), probes (the PUT), probe runs (`ProbeRunner.start(owner_id=, session=)`), lab experiments (`POST /api/bots/experiments`, and `citar lab submit [--owner]` through `items.register_offline`, which opens the server's database only when it already exists with this version's tables, never creating, migrating or renaming one), scenarios (the editor's save), maps (the PUT), bot profiles (create and fork) and reports (`ReportRunner.start(owner_id=, session=)`, the runner writing the row with the spec in its meta). A producer handed a request's session commits the row before it queues or ticks: a benchmark job's thread writes its game's row while it holds the scheduler's lock, so a request must not hold an open write while it waits for that lock. Deletes mark the row deleted after the files go.
- **Routes.** Every route of P3.2.4's table as designed, the report routes moved from `admin_api`'s admin-only router to `citar/server/reports_api.py`, and the item sharing routes and `POST /api/items/{kind}/{ref}/unpublish` in `citar/server/items_api.py`. Choices the design left open: a route that opens a game or starts work (a job's watch, a probe case's open, a run, a launch) needs an account as well as VIEW, so a `global` item's visitor reads it but loads nothing on the server; forking a profile checks VIEW before `edit_bots`, so a stranger's refusal says nothing of a private one; the scenario editor needs `create_games` to open; validating and generating maps need `create_games` and validating a probe `run_probes` (the design lists them with creation); `PUT /api/benchmarks/settings` is admin-only. Using an item in something else (a map in a game or an editor, a scenario in a probe or an editor, a bot profile in a seat, a suite, an experiment, a profile's parent, a suite in a run) needs VIEW on it (`app._may_use`), the shipped profiles and "best" excepted; a file with no row is an administrator's to use. Listings are filtered per kind; `GET /api/benchmarks/status` and `/api/status` count and log only the runs the caller may see (log lines carry their run); `GET /api/probe-runs` shows only their live cases (a live case carries the subject's thoughts); `GET /api/lab` shows a member their experiments and keeps the log and the side runs for administrators; `GET /api/bots/rankings` and the profile list drop entries of profiles the caller may not see; the report builder's options offer a member only the items they may see and the machines they may use. Another member's run or probe run shows no machine address or key variable.
- **Benchmark games** get their row at creation (`ownership.register_quietly`, from the job's thread, before the game starts): kind `benchmark`, the run's owner, the run's visibility. A reloaded or watched job's game, and a probe case opened to be looked at, takes the run's owner when its row has none (`ownership.claim_quietly`). A run's visibility change or unpublish carries to its games (`items.VISIBILITY_HOOKS`, the app's hook using the scheduler made at the start, never one made inside a request).
- **The model-block gate** on suites (every machine through `servers.member_llm`; a base URL, a key or a key variable refused with 400 rather than dropped) and probe subjects (the scripted bot, `provider: bot`, with a profile the caller may see, or a machine named, never described), checked against the caller at run time whoever saved the suite; runs are admitted to their pooled machines for `benchmark` or `probe` (`pool.seats.authorize(purpose, queued=True)`: who may is decided now, a refusal that only says "not yet" lets the run queue). A report's narrative names a machine its maker may use (`member_llm`) and is admitted to it as a run's machines are (`pool.seats.admit`, purpose `game` until 3-09 gives pooled prompts their own).
- **Capabilities** `run_benchmarks`, `run_probes`, `run_lab`, `edit_bots`, `full_llm` and `partial_llm`, with the role defaults of P3.2.4 (moderators as members) and their refusal messages; `deps.check_cap` for routes that learn in their body which one they need. The Bots page follows `edit_bots`, `run_lab` and a profile's `can_manage`; the Queue page shows priority controls only on work the caller manages, and another member's work as "someone's <kind>" (its machines are filtered in 3-09; `pool.seats.occupants` names holders with their kind and id for it).
- **System items and adoption.** The shipped bot profiles are registered at every start (`items.on_boot`, after the runners are back), owned by nobody and `global`. Adoption (`items.adopt_orphans`) runs at every start that has an administrator and from `accounts.on_first_admin`, which `admin_cli create-admin` and `set-role`, the setup wizard, `bootstrap_admin`, an invitation with the admin role and the local owner's creation call; it is idempotent, so each run adopts only what appeared since (a crash between file and row, a hand copy, `lab submit` without a database). A file that records its owner (a run's, a probe run's, a report's, an experiment's `owner_id`) goes back to that account; the rest go to the first administrator (in local mode the local owner), `private`; games with no owner go to the same account with their pooling default (`ownership.adopt_orphans`, 3-01's note). One audit row per run that adopted anything; `policy.set_marker("orphans_adopted", ...)` (markers are `settings_kv` rows the console cannot set).
- **3-01's open CSRF item, decided here.** `_gate` enforces the session's CSRF token for a caller a cookie signed in, unless the request holds a valid seat or spectator token of the game or an Authorization header (a made-up token does not excuse it), and `/api/saves/load` and `/api/saves/delete` make the same check; the web client already sent the token, and no test changed.
- **Gates.** (1) `tests/test_items_access.py`, 33 tests: the route table (73 routes, checked both ways against the app's own list) asked of the stranger, the ACL viewer, a moderator on a `global` item, the owner, an admin and an anonymous caller, every listing filtered per caller, the editor's sessions, the sharing routes for every kind; (2) the producers per kind through their routes, deletes, system items, adoption at boot and from `create-admin` and `set-role` with a fresh server-mode database waiting for the first (its stray answering 404 to everyone before), files that name their owner, `lab submit`; (3) control routes and queue priority, a lobby game's by its owner; (4) a benchmark job's game row and its following the run's visibility and unpublish; (5) suites and probe subjects that describe a machine refused under `NoOutbound`, a report's narrative machine. Updated: `test_access`'s scenario launch (the scenario shared with members first), `test_seat_blocks`' admin scenario (likewise), `test_games_index`'s probe case (the run's owner), `test_bot_profiles`' direct calls (a session and an administrator). S1: 775 tests OK in 177 s (742 at the start). S2 ruff clean. S3 197 routes, 0 unguarded (`items.require(` and `items.listed(` are body gates). S4 every link resolves. S5 `mkdocs build --strict`; ACCOUNTS, API and BOTS updated and the wiki regenerated. S6 30 modules parse. The session's overhead (P3.9.7): 2.241, against the reference 2.29 and the 2.87 limit. CI only: test.yml on its three OSes with its package, installer and Docker jobs.
- **Left for later packages.** 3-03: what a member's report gathers (until then it reads everything in scope, as an administrator's did), `share_api`'s report routes and `/r/{slug}`, the sharing dialog over `items_api`, `mobile.js`'s reports tab. 3-09: `/api/queue`'s machine filter, and a purpose of its own for a report's narrative.
- **Fix round** (2026-10-11, from 18:40 to 19:00, -04:00; the review's nine findings, every one confirmed by a test that failed before its fix).
  - *A report's narrative is admitted to its machine.* A member's narrative on a pooled machine was checked only for the machine existing (`member_llm`), so another member's private machine took the prompt with no grant. It now goes through admission as a run's machines do (`pool.seats.admit`, which `app._admit` uses too; purpose `game` until 3-09 gives pooled prompts one), on a new report and on a rerun, a machine the member cannot see refused without being named, and nothing made.
  - *Adoption never stops the server or an administrator.* A cut-short or corrupt scenario file (`EOFError`, `zlib.error`) escaped adoption, so the boot failed and so did `create-admin`, `set-role`, the setup wizard and an admin invitation. A file that does not read is now adopted under its id by the adopting administrator (private; theirs to remove through its route) and logged; one whose id or path would not fit a row is left, logged. `items.on_boot` never raises (the shipped items and adoption each in a transaction of their own), and `accounts.on_first_admin` adopts in a savepoint, so a failure rolls back the adoption alone and the next start tries again. Per-file savepoints were not used: this SQLite driver commits on releasing a savepoint that no write preceded (checked), which `on_boot`'s fresh transaction would hit.
  - *Adoption reads only what it adopts.* It lists the folders and reads a file only when its id has no row (`_on_disk`, then `_describe`), where every start parsed every run, report, experiment and whole scenario state.
  - *One audit row for an adoption.* Adopted and shipped items get no `<kind>.created` row each (`register(audit_row=False)`); the `migration.items_adopted` row carries the counts.
  - *Using an item is checked when the thing runs.* A probe shared wider than its scenario runs only for those who may see the scenario (an opened case is the scenario's state); a launch checks the profiles of the scenario's own seats as merged with the request's, not only the request's.
  - *Wrong shapes are 400s.* A profile, map or scenario named by anything but a string (`_may_use`), a suite id that is not a string, a suite whose parts have the wrong type (`normalize_suite` raises ValueError) and a report specification whose parts are not objects or lists (`normalize_spec`, applied before the narrative's gate) were 500s.
  - *Listings' permissions in two queries.* `items.permissions_by_ref` reads the rows and the viewer's allowlist entries in one query each (`access.acl_permissions_for`, `access.on(acl=)`), not two per item.
  - *"Best bot" is a profile every member may see* (`ratings.everyones_profiles`: the shipped ones, `server` and `global`), the same for a seat (`_pin_best`), the lab and the Bots page; in a process without the server's database (a command-line tool) every profile counts.
  - *Code.* The fork route uses `profiles.fork(as_id=)` and `profiles.fork_name` instead of a copy of them; `items.delete_quietly` and `items.owner_of` (unused) are gone.
  - *Gates again.* `tests/test_items_access.py` 41 tests (8 new: the bad-body table, the narrative's admission, the probe's scenario, the launch's seats, Best bot, listing permissions, damaged files at boot and at `create-admin`, a failed adoption).
