S1: 783 tests OK in 180 s. S2 ruff clean. S3 197 routes, 0 unguarded. S4 every link resolves. S5 `mkdocs build --strict`; ACCOUNTS, API and BOTS updated and the wiki regenerated. S6 30 modules parse. The session's overhead: 2.321 (reference 2.29, limit 2.87). CI only: test.yml on its three OSes.

**As built in 3-03: reports scoped to their owner, the pooling opt-out, the sharing dialog, the QA harness** (P3.2.4, P3.2.3's share URL, P3.9.4, P3.9.5; Appendix D). One agent, 2026-10-11, from 19:14 to 20:15 (-04:00), 61 minutes, on branch `p-3-03` from `3069d25`. No Rust changed.

- **Pooling in the model figures.** `aggregate.scope_for` reads the owner's account as well as the game's row: a game pools when both say `pool` and the owner has not left, so opting the account out withdraws every game it played, as the account's own docstring always promised (the route's note said the opposite; both now say this). `aggregate.publishable(scope, games_by_label)` keeps a viewer's own games and adds the others' only when at least `aggregate_min_contributors` *other* accounts are behind a label (counting the viewer would let one other account's figures be read back by subtraction). `/api/models/scores` and `/api/metrics/compare` take the caller: an administrator's figures cover every game; a member's are their own games and the pooled ones, other members' games counted (`pooled_games`) and never named, a model's pooled part withheld under the threshold (`pooled_withheld`, or a row with `withheld` and the reason when nothing of it is the member's), and the scores say what the scope held (`scope`). `scoring._reports` reads only the allowed games (a member's request never opens another member's save). The Models page tells a member what their figures are made of, counts the pooled games and lists the withheld models with the reason (`models.js`, `metrics.js`; a withheld row has no figures to draw).
- **What a report gathers** (`citar/reports/reach.py`, `Reach.of(owner_id)` read once when the report is built): everything for an administrator's report and for one with no owner (a script's); for a member's, the activities of the games, runs, probe runs, experiments and reports they may see (`visible_filter`: theirs, shared with them, members', published), with costs **on their own machines only**. The costs are worked out over the whole ledger first, so a minute a machine shared is split as it is for anyone, then cut (`Reach.cost`): an activity that also ran on the host or another member's machine keeps its outcome and usage, not its cost there, and a data-quality note counts them; the registry, the per-machine, per-day and per-hour figures and the notes keep only the owner's machines; other machines the member may see are labels only. The builder's options follow the same reach (`scope_options(reach)`), which replaced `reports_options`' own filter. "Anything wider" is the report's new **Data** choice (`spec.pooling`: `own`, `both`, `pooled`; the design names a scope `own|pooled|both` that the specification did not have): pooled figures per model in the models section, from `scoring.compare_models` over other accounts' pooled games under the same threshold. A report's traceback and owner id in its metadata are its managers' (a viewer or a link holder sees the rest).
- **`/r/{slug}`** (`share_api.public_report`): a `link` report by its key, a `global` one by its id or key, 404 for anything else, its owner and administrators included (they read it in the app); the HTML is read from the report runner's own folder, with a policy that lets it load nothing. `share_api`'s `/api/shared/reports*` routes are gone (the item routes do it all); `items.client` gives a report's `public_url`. The route audit lists `/r/{slug}` as public on purpose; its stale `_report(` body-gate marker (share_api's old helper, which `def public_report(` matched by accident) is gone.
- **Share links open** (3-01's open item). `#/game/<gid>` (and `/-/<player>`) is a game screen with no token, the account or the key deciding; the screen keeps `?k=` in its own links (`api.gameLink`, `replayLink`) and on the socket (`&k=`); `#/game`, `#/replay` and `#/reports/<id>` open without a session; a 404 anywhere is the router's **Not found** page (with the sign-in for a visitor), and a game with a person playing it says it cannot be watched until it is over. The view answers `can_manage`, so the screen offers pause, the AI delay and Save only to managers; the recap opens live for whoever may watch an AI-only game, not only the spectator token. Item listings show the item a share key opens to whoever presents it (`items.keyed_ref`, `listed(slug=)`, `permissions_by_ref(slug=)`, on all nine listings), so the other kinds' share links open on their pages; without the key a `link` item stays unlisted.
- **The sharing dialog** (`citar/web/js/share.js`): the ladder (with `global` only for `publish_global`), the link with Copy and New link, a report's public page, a game's pooling, people with access and add by handle, a past take-down and its reason; **Unpublish** with a reason for a moderator who does not manage the thing. Wired into the lobby (a game shared with you shows "shared with you" and can be watched; seats, Play and Close are its managers'), reports (Re-run and Delete for managers; the viewer carries the key into its frame and links), benchmark runs, probe runs, scenarios, maps and bot profiles. `mobile.js`'s reports tab lists `/api/reports` for everyone.
- **`admin_cli create-user`** (active, email verified, any role; `--password-stdin` for scripts, never an argument), an administrator made this way adopting strays as every path does.
- **The QA harness** `scripts/qa_phase3.py` as P3.9.4, with `dev_server.instance_env` (every path of an instance under its directory, `CITAR_STATE_DIR` and `CITAR_BENCH_DIR` included, which `dev_server.py` itself now uses) so the harness's `admin_cli` calls reach the server's database. Beyond the letter, it also refuses CITAR's own per-user data directory and any source checkout (a database in a synced folder). The fake endpoint records connections and answers `/v1/models` and a plain completion; its scripted policy is 3-05's. The `accounts` part: 38 checks (games and reports through the ladder, links and their keys on HTTP and the socket, sharing by handle, a moderator's take-down, members' model blocks with nothing dialled, a member's report without another member's private game and an administrator's with it, `/r/`, scoped scores), about 70 s, most of it waiting for the usage ledger's one-minute flush.
- **Gates.** (1) `tests/test_pooling.py` 11 tests: the routes by a member, an opted-out account (absent for others, present for itself; by its account and by its game's row), the threshold, an administrator, an anonymous and a suspended caller; `scope_for` and `publishable`; the real `_reports` opening only allowed saves. (2) `tests/test_report_scope.py` 16 tests on a made-up ledger and two members' priced machines: a member's report holds none of the other's private games, runs, probe runs or machine (names and ids), and prices only their own machine (the host's part of their own game excluded, their machine's part equal to an administrator's report's); an administrator's holds everything; shared runs reach their viewer; the builder's options; a pooled-only report; the runner builds with its owner's reach; `/r/` by key and id at each level, another kind's key, a row pointing outside the folder, the manager's `public_url`, the old routes gone, a failed report's traceback. (3) `python scripts/qa_phase3.py accounts` exit 0, 38 of 38 (evidence `qa-accounts.json`); `tests/test_qa_harness.py` 10 tests: port 8799, a port in use, an unmarked directory with files, `C:/dev/citar-play` (the owner's real directory, its files unchanged) and a part not written yet each exit 2 with nothing made or changed, the data directory and a checkout refused, marked and empty directories taken, the instance's paths, the fake endpoint, `create-user`. P3.9.1: share keys in every item listing (`test_items_access.Table`), the tokenless view and replay (`test_access.GameRoutes`). Browser A1 (screenshots `a1-1` to `a1-11` in the QA directory, kept at `C:/dev/target/p-3-03-a1-evidence/screens`, with the Models page as a member and an administrator): alice's lobby and her Share dialog setting her game to members; bob's private game "Not found" for alice; the report's sharing dialog by link with its public page; the phone site's Reports tab; bob's lobby listing alice's game "shared with you" and his tokenless watch (no pause or save offered); a visitor's "Not found" on that game and the front door; a visitor opening the link report and its `/r/` page. The signed-in steps were driven by headless Chrome over its DevTools protocol (each account signing in through the page's own `/api/auth/login`) rather than by typing the throwaway accounts' password into the browser pane; the visitor's steps were checked in the pane too, where the report's frame was blocked by the pane itself (`ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT`) and loads in Chrome. S1: 822 tests OK in 209 s (783 at the start, 195 s). S2 ruff clean. S3 192 routes: 164 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded (the five `/api/shared/reports*` routes gone). S4 every internal link resolves (37 files, 159 links). S5 `mkdocs build --strict`; ACCOUNTS, REPORTS, API, RUNBOOK and CONTRIBUTING updated and the wiki regenerated. S6 31 modules parse. The session's overhead (P3.9.7): 2.124 (reference 2.29, limit 2.87). CI only: test.yml on its three OSes with its package, installer and Docker jobs.
- **Fix round** (2026-10-11, 20:31 to 20:57, 26 minutes). The review found a major and nine minors; all were fixed.
  - *Another member's machine named in a pricing note* (the major). `Reach.cost` kept a note of `costing.compute` when the name of one of the owner's machines appeared in it, so "No token price for <model> on Bob GPU box" reached the report of an owner whose machine was "GPU". `costing.compute` now files each note under its machine (`notes_by_server`; `"unassigned"` for usage with no machine) beside the full list administrators keep, and a member's report keeps only the notes filed under their own machines, and the one about usage with no machine when an activity of theirs had some. `Reach.cost` lost its registry argument.
  - *What a report's reader is given.* `GET /api/reports/{rid}` gave anyone who may read a report its whole specification: the narrative's instructions and its machine, model and profile, and the scope's machines and items by id and name. `reports_api._spec_for` gives a reader who does not manage it the scope's items they may see (a game by its row, an owned item by its kind; an activity id never), the machines they may see (one they hold VIEW on; for a member, the site's dry-run ones) and of the narrative only `enabled`, in a specification's shape so "Edit copy" still loads it.
  - *An owner out of good standing.* `Reach.of` passed a suspended account to the member's reach, which still priced its machines; a suspended administrator got every run (the listing filter short-circuits on the role) and no game. An owner not in good standing (suspended, deleted, gone, pending) now reaches what a visitor would, an administrator's role included; probation stays a member's reach.
  - *The QA harness's failures.* A server that did not start or an account that could not be made raised `Refused` (exit 2, "touching nothing") after the directory had been emptied, and a part that raised wrote no evidence. `Refused` is now raised only before `claim`; afterwards a directory that cannot be emptied, a server that does not start or an account that cannot be made is a `HarnessError`, and a part that raises is caught: each a failed check with `qa-<part>.json` written, exit 1, the checks before it kept. `test_the_owners_dev_server_directory` no longer compares the modification times of the owner's real `C:/dev/citar-play`, which its running dev server changes: it checks that nothing of the harness's appears there and nothing is emptied, and a stand-in directory (patched in for `OWNER_PLAY_DIR`) has every file compared.
  - *Minors in the client and the policy.* The report pages' policy (`/r/{slug}` and the in-app frame) allows `img-src data:` only, so a report loads nothing, as ACCOUNTS says. The benchmark page's per-job pause, resume, skip and retry follow the run's `can_manage`. A bot profile or a map that the address names and the viewer may not see throws its 404 to the router's Not found page (with its sign-in for a visitor) instead of an error line or a toast over a blank map. `reports_api`'s module docstring describes what it does now.
  - *Tests.* `tests/test_report_scope.py` 19 (a member's API machine whose name holds the owner's machine's name with an unpriced model, and usage with no machine, absent from her report and present in an administrator's; owners suspended before the build, a member and an administrator, and probation; the reader's specification for a member with the link and a visitor; no `https:` in either policy). `tests/test_qa_harness.py` 15 (`Failures`: a directory that cannot be emptied, a server that does not start, an account that cannot be made, a part that raises; the stand-in for the owner's directory). `test_access.GameRoutes`: a moderator's view and replay of a private game (404) and of a `global` one (200, `can_manage` false), and its control (403).
  - *Gates again.* S1 830 tests OK in 209 s. S2 ruff clean. S3 192 routes: 164 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded. S4 37 files, 159 links, every internal link resolves. S5 `mkdocs build --strict`; REPORTS, ACCOUNTS, API and CONTRIBUTING updated and the wiki regenerated. S6 31 modules parse. S8 `python scripts/qa_phase3.py accounts` exit 0, 38 of 38, twice (with and without `--keep`); A1 again (screenshots `a1-1` to `a1-11`), with `a1-12` (bob holding alice's report link reads its specification as a reader: no narrative instructions, no traceback, no owner) and `a1-13` to `a1-15` (a missing bot profile and map for a member, a stale bot link for a visitor: Not found), kept at `C:/dev/target/p-3-03-a1-evidence/fix-round/screens`. The session's overhead: 2.885 right after the QA runs, then 2.473 and 2.378 (session medians 0.80, 0.68 and 0.67 s, drive 0.28 s each time; the median of the three 2.473, under the 2.87 limit; this round changed nothing on the session's path).
- **Left for later packages.** 3-04: `User.public(viewer)` at the sharing list's call sites (the dialog shows display names as `public()` gives them). 3-05: `POST /api/llm/models` still dials a member's base URL (3-01's open item; the harness's trap is ready for its check), the fake endpoint's scripted policy, and registry machines in reports (a member's report labels the registry's dry-run machines only). 3-09: the Queue page's machine filter, a narrative's own purpose. A pre-existing test-isolation fault, not 3-03's: `tests.test_costs` run alone fails one test (no tables), and after `tests.test_items_access` in one process two of its report tests wait forever (the work queue keeps the earlier module's report runner registered); the full suite's order passes.

**As built in 3-04: profiles, the directory, avatars, the admin users page and the audit log** (P3.2.6, P3.2.7, P3.2.10; Appendix D). One agent, 2026-10-11, from 21:10 to 22:07 (-04:00), 57 minutes, on branch `p-3-04` from `5a39f02`, in one sitting (the split the entry names was not needed). No Rust changed.

- **The rule lives on the account.** `User.field_levels()` (the choice over the defaults, narrowed by standing: a probation account shows nothing wider than `server`, one pending, suspended or deleted nothing but to itself and administrators), `User.viewer_rank(viewer)` (the account itself and administrators everything, a member in good standing `server` and `global`, anyone else `global`), `User.visible_fields` and `User.public(viewer, *, rank=None)` (`id`, `handle`, `display_name` or the handle, `avatar`: the upload's route with the file's hash, else the sign-in picture, or null). `viewer` has no default, so a caller that forgets it raises. `User.account()` carries the profile for its owner (`bio`, `links`, `field_visibility` as chosen, `listed`, `avatar_url` and `avatar_uploaded`). Every place an account appears passes its viewer: `access.shared_with(session, obj, viewer)`, `items.client(session, row, perms, viewer)`, `ownership.to_client(row, perms, viewer)` (which now names the game's owner, so a `global` game's list shows a visitor the handle only, and the lobby says "shared with you by ..."), the machine's, group's and grant's accounts in the pool, the inviter `/api/invites/check` names to a code holder (as a visitor sees it), the directory and the admin pages. `tests/test_profiles.PublicCallers` holds the callers of `.public(` in `citar/` to a fixed list (a file scan, so it needs no git).
- **Profiles** (`citar/auth/profiles.py`): `PUT /api/auth/me` takes `bio`, `links`, `field_visibility` and `listed`, all checked before any changes (control characters and bidirectional overrides stripped, line breaks kept; 1,000 characters; three links of `http`/`https` with a host and no account in them, labels of 40, addresses of 300; known fields and levels), one `user.profile_updated` audit row naming the fields and the level changes, never the text. Beyond the letter: making a field `global` on probation is a 403, but a field made `global` before probation may stay (the Account page sends every field, and the field shows to members meanwhile); a new account's display name is cleaned as an edit's is. `GET /api/users/{handle}` (anyone) answers 404 alike for an account that does not exist, a hidden page and an account out of good standing; it lists the fields that show, the bio and links, and the items (lobby games and every kind of owned item the viewer may list, 20 of each, newest first; benchmark and probe games are their runs'); `?as=member|visitor` narrows the view for the account's own "view as" links and never widens it. The ratio block is 3-10's (`profiles.to_client`).
- **The directory** (`GET /api/users`, members only): listed accounts in good standing, newest first, 50 a page (`next`); each card is `public(viewer)` with whether the page opens and the bio's first line when it shows. The search matches the handle, and the display name only where the caller may see it, in SQL (the levels read from the JSON column with `field_visibility[...].as_string()`, the absent ones as their defaults), so a hidden name finds nothing and the paging stays in the database. A listed account whose page the caller may not open shows its handle, unlinked (the design's "accounts with `listed`", read literally).
- **Avatars** (`citar/auth/images.py`): PNG (every chunk's CRC, IHDR first, an IDAT, IEND last with nothing after), JPEG (markers to the first SOFn, which gives the size; EOI last) and WebP (the RIFF length the file's; VP8, VP8L or VP8X), at most 256 KB and 1024x1024, nothing decoded and no image library. `POST /api/auth/me/avatar` reads the body only up to the limit, parses the one file with Starlette's own form parser (in memory), checks it, writes `<data dir>/avatars/<user id>-<sha256[:12]>.<ext>` under a temporary name of its own and renames it into place, commits, then removes only the file it replaced (a sweep of the account's other files could delete the file a concurrent upload had just pointed the row at). `GET /api/users/{handle}/avatar` serves it with the type its header proved, `nosniff`, `default-src 'none'; sandbox` and `private` (or `public` when the picture and the page are both `global`) caching for an hour, to whoever the field shows to. The corpus `tests/data/avatars/` (the gate's ten files, plus a lossless and an alpha WebP and a progressive JPEG) is marked binary in `.gitattributes`.
- **Administering accounts** (`citar/auth/user_admin.py`, shared by the routes and the CLI): `apply` takes any of `role`, `status` (`active`, `probation`, `suspended`; a suspension needs its reason and revokes the account's sessions), `caps` (`true`, `false`, `null`), the three limits (bounded), `trust_tier` and `listed`, checks everything first and writes one `user.updated` row naming the fields with their old and new values; whoever asks, the last active administrator cannot be demoted, suspended or put on probation (409); a new active administrator adopts as every path that makes one does. `/api/admin/users` (search on handle, name or email; role; status; 50 a page), `/api/admin/users/{id}` (the account, its capabilities resolved, the role's defaults, its audit rows about it and by it), `PATCH`, `/api/admin/audit` (actor by handle or id, action or a `prefix*`, object type and id, `before` an id, at most 200 a page; an account a row is about named by its handle in `object_label`). They use `require_role("admin", hide=True)`: a signed-in non-administrator gets 404, a visitor 401. `admin_cli` gains `set-caps NAME cap=on|off|default`, `set-limits`, `set-trust` and `audit [--json]`, and `set-role` and `set-status` now go through `apply` (so `set-status ... suspended` needs `--reason`, and each change is one `user.updated` row of `admin_cli`'s). The audit log is administrators' (the old docstrings and ACCOUNTS said moderators read it; no route ever let them).
- **The web client.** `people.js`: `#/users` (the directory with a search and More) and `#/u/<handle>` (open to visitors: `u` joined the router's open routes; a profile the viewer may not see is the router's Not found). The Account page's first card is now **Account** (username, email, role, status, time zone, pooling) and the new **Profile** card holds the display name, the picture (the current one, a preview of the chosen file, "Use this picture", "Remove picture"), the bio with its count, three link rows, "Who sees what" per field (`global` disabled on probation), the directory switch, and "See your profile", "View as a member", "View as a visitor". `admin_users.js`: `#/admin/users` with filters, a drawer per account (role, status and its reason, trust, listing, limits, each capability as role default, on or off with what it resolves to, Save sending only what changed) and its audit rows, and the **Audit log** card (filters, Older). The nav gains **People** for members and **Users** beside **Server** for administrators; the account menu shows `avatar`. `api.js` sends a `FormData` body as multipart.
- **The route audit.** The design asks for `/api/users/{handle}` and its avatar in `INTENTIONALLY_PUBLIC` and for `profiles.require_visible(` as a body gate; with the marker, both routes count as gated (checked in the body), and the script's own rule keeps a gated route out of `INTENTIONALLY_PUBLIC` (an entry there would hide the route if its gate were removed). So only the marker was added: 201 routes, 173 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded.
- **Gates.** (1) `tests/test_profiles.py`: every field at every level through the profile route by a visitor, a member, a probation member, the account and an administrator (and `public(viewer)` by every field level and page level), the defaults, a hidden page 404 as for no account, standing, probation holding `global` to members, view-as, items per viewer, the edits and their limits (a `javascript:` link, `JavaScript:`, `data:`, `ftp:`, credentials, spaces, a fourth link, 41 and 301 characters, 1,001 characters), the directory (members only, `listed`, standing, a hidden name never found, cards, 55 accounts in pages of 50), a private name and picture absent from a `global` game's owner for a visitor, from a game's and a report's sharing list for another member, from an item's owner and from an invitation check, and the fixed caller list; (2) the corpus accepted or refused and served as proven, the header checks, 404 when hidden, replace and delete, only the replaced file removed, nothing written outside the avatars folder, bad uploads; P3.9.1 for every new route (the profile and avatar by a visitor, members, a moderator, the owner and an administrator; the avatar routes' 401 and CSRF; the admin routes 401 for a visitor and 404 for a member, a probation member, a moderator and the account itself). 39 tests. (3) and (4) `tests/test_admin_users.py`, 16 tests: each field's PATCH and its one audit row (the same again records nothing), a suspension's reason and sign-out, `null` removing an override and the member's own capabilities following, wrong values changing nothing, the last administrator (409, then allowed with a second), adoption on promotion, the listing's filters and pages, the drawer's audit rows, the audit route's filters and 200-row pages, and every CLI command in process on the module's database seen through the API, plus the real `python -m citar.server.admin_cli` in a child on a database of its own (`create-user`, `set-trust`, `audit --json`). (5) `python scripts/qa_phase3.py accounts` exit 0, 60 of 60 (3-03's 38 and 3-04's 22: a profile, the limits, an avatar served safely and refused as SVG, the directory and its search, a public profile with a private name, unlisting, the admin routes' 404, a PATCH and its audit row, `null`, the last administrator, `set-trust`, `set-limits`, `set-caps` and `audit` on the server's database, probation's 403, a suspension and the sign-in after), about 75 s; browser A2 and A3 (screenshots `a2-1` to `a2-10` and `a3-1` to `a3-5` in the QA directory, kept at `C:/dev/target/p-3-04-a2a3-evidence`, with four phone-width looks), driven by headless Chrome over its DevTools protocol as 3-03's were: alice's Profile card, a picture chosen (its preview) and uploaded, her fields set (page, picture, bio and links `global`, name `private`), bob's directory listing her by handle, alice unlisted and bob's directory without her, a visitor opening her page signed out (picture, bio, links, no name), her page as a visitor sees it and as she does; the admin's Users page, the drawer changing a capability, the game limit and the trust tier, the saved change in the audit log as one row naming the three, the drawer's audit rows, and bob's Not found on `#/admin/users`. S1: 885 tests OK in 225 s (830 at 3-03's merge). S2 ruff clean. S3 above. S4 37 files, 160 links, every internal link resolves. S5 `mkdocs build --strict`; ACCOUNTS (profiles and the directory, administering accounts, the roles table), API (the new routes and the account block everywhere) and RUNBOOK (the new commands) updated and the wiki regenerated. S6 33 modules parse as ES modules. S8 as (5). The session's overhead (P3.9.7): 2.319 and 2.354 (session median 0.71 s, drive 0.30 s; reference 2.29, limit 2.87): this package changed nothing on the session's path. CI only: test.yml on its three OSes with its package, installer and Docker jobs.
- **Fix round** (2026-10-11, 22:21 to 22:55, 34 minutes). The review found two majors and three minors; all were fixed.
  - *Paging numbers past SQLite's integer* (a major). `GET /api/users?offset=` (any member), `/api/admin/users?offset=` and `/api/admin/audit?before=` handed the caller's number to SQLite, which raised `OverflowError` past 64 bits: a 500, and a traceback from `admin_cli audit --before`. The routes bound them (`offset` 0 to `OFFSET_MAX`, 1,000,000; `before` 1 to 2**63-1; 422 otherwise, after the role check, so anyone but an administrator still gets 404 on the admin routes), and `profiles.directory`, `user_admin.search` and `user_admin.audit_page` hold them in range themselves for the CLI.
  - *A malformed upload* (a major). `_the_file` caught only Starlette's `MultiPartException`; python_multipart's own parse errors (a boundary that is not the header's, bytes that are no form) are `ValueError`s and were a 500. Both are a 400 with the rules; the parser has already closed what it opened.
  - *Metadata published with the picture.* Pictures were stored and served byte for byte, Exif (GPS, the camera's serial number, the time), XMP and text chunks included, to everyone the `avatar` field reaches. `images.inspect` now walks each file to its end and returns the file without its metadata (`ImageInfo.data`), which `store_avatar` hashes and writes: keep-lists rather than drop-lists, so a program's private chunk is left out too. JPEG: every segment and scan to EOI (a scan's entropy-coded data passed over to the marker that ends it), JFIF's APP0 kept without its thumbnail (which can be an older crop), APP2 `ICC_PROFILE` and APP14 `Adobe` kept, APP1 (Exif, XMP), APP13 (IPTC), every other APPn and COM left out, and anything after EOI (a multi-picture file's second picture, appended bytes) left out rather than refused; a frame and a scan must come before EOI, and a second SOI is refused. PNG: critical chunks and `tRNS`, `cHRM`, `gAMA`, `iCCP`, `sBIT`, `sRGB`, `cICP`, `mDCV`, `cLLI`, `bKGD`, `pHYs`, `acTL`, `fcTL`, `fdAT` kept, the rest (tEXt, zTXt, iTXt, eXIf, tIME, private chunks) left out; a critical chunk no decoder knows, or a type that is not four letters, is refused. WebP: every chunk walked (a second VP8X refused, an unpadded odd last chunk accepted and padded), VP8X, VP8, VP8L, ALPH, ANIM, ANMF and ICCP kept, VP8X's EXIF and XMP flags cleared and the RIFF length rewritten. Still nothing decoded: Pillow (a tool here, never a dependency) found every frame's pixels unchanged on the corpus and on animated PNG and WebP, CMYK, grayscale progressive, restart-marker, palette and multi-picture files.
  - *admin_cli's changes found by no actor filter.* Its rows have no account (`actor_id` null, label `admin_cli`), and `audit_page` matched only `actor_id`. An actor that names no account now also matches rows without one by their label (`admin_cli`, `system`); an account's own label ("handle (role)") never names an actor. The page's Who box and `audit --actor` say so.
  - *A limit saved that is not the limit applied.* `max_concurrent_games` took 0, which the game route reads as its default of 2. Its range is now 1 to 100 (`create_games` is what stops an account making any; 0 invitations is none and 0 seats the ruleset's most, as the server reads them). The drawer sent `Number("") === 0` for an emptied limit; it now checks each limit's range before sending anything and says which is wrong.
  - *Tests.* `tests/test_profiles.py` 41: the corpus gains `exif-gps.jpg`, `metadata.png` and `metadata.webp` (each with GPS Exif and the text SECRET in every kind of metadata its format holds; made with Pillow), served without any of it, the ICC profile, gamma and pixel size kept, a stored picture unchanged by a second pass; seven new header refusals and two acceptances (bytes after a JPEG's EOI left out, an unpadded odd last WebP chunk padded); three malformed bodies; the directory's out-of-range offsets for a member and a visitor. `tests/test_admin_users.py` 20: the users list's offsets and the audit log's `before` (422 for an administrator, 404 for a member), the label filter (and an account named `admin_cli` getting its rows and the CLI's), the limits saved being the ones `_game_limit` applies, a game limit of 0 refused by the API and the CLI, `audit --actor admin_cli` and `--before 2**64`. Each new test fails on the code before the fix.
  - *Gates again.* S1 891 tests OK in 222 s, and 220 s on the final code (885 before). S2 ruff clean. S3 201 routes: 173 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded. S4 37 files, 160 links, every internal link resolves. S5 `mkdocs build --strict`; ACCOUNTS, API and RUNBOOK updated and the wiki regenerated. S6 33 modules parse. S8 `python scripts/qa_phase3.py accounts` exit 0, 65 of 65 (five new: metadata left out, a malformed form, the 422s, a game limit of 0, admin_cli by actor), about 76 s; A2 and A3 again on the final client (`a2-1` to `a3-5`), and the fix round's own: the drawer refusing an emptied and a 0 game limit beside Save with nothing recorded, and the Audit log's Who finding admin_cli's rows (`fix-a3-1` to `fix-a3-5`, kept at `C:/dev/target/p-3-04-fix-evidence`). The session's overhead: 2.365 and 2.304 (reference 2.29, limit 2.87).
- **Left for later packages.** 3-10: the ratio on the profile (`profiles.to_client`, when `ratio` shows; the Profile card already has its level). 3-09: if the Queue page ever names an account, through `User.public(viewer)` (the fixed caller list will ask). 3-05 might decide whether suspending an account drops its helpers, as revoking a token will (nothing in P3.2.7 asks it). Phase 4: trust tiers set automatically. An administrator who makes a `pending` account active does not confirm its address, so it still cannot sign in until it confirms it (as before 3-04).

**As built in 3-05: the registry merge, machines as rows, the routes' gates, dropped helpers** (P3.2.8, P3.2.9's interim rule retired, P3.9.4's scripted endpoint; Appendix D). One agent, from 2026-10-11 23:09 to 2026-10-12 00:18 (-04:00), 69 minutes, on branch `p-3-05` from `dec6dc2`, in one sitting. No Rust changed.

- **`servers.py` on rows.** `load()` is the registry dict built from the `servers` table (each row's `config` through `normalize_server` under the row's id, name and kind; the row's `max_concurrent` as `connection.max_parallel`; the plans and the currency from `settings_kv` `electricity_plans` and `registry_currency`, which `policy` leaves out of its cache; the host the row with `is_host`; `aliases` from the rows' `former_ids`). It is cached and read again after a commit in this process that changed a machine, those settings or an owner's time zone (an `after_flush`/`after_commit` listener on the ORM `Session`; a savepoint's release fires `after_commit` too, so only the outermost commit counts), when the database is another one (`db.epoch()`, bumped by `configure`, `dispose` and `create_all`), and every 5 s otherwise (another process's change). A process that opened no database (`citar lab`, `citar doctor`) reads the server's through a private read-only engine when the file exists with this version's tables, never configuring its own `db` (which would start the games index writing there). `with_pooled()` is the identity; `machine(id)` gives the row's facts (`reach`, `enabled`, owner and zone, group, `is_host`, `server_resource`, slots, the stored config) and `pool.seats.lookup` serves from it with no query. `reach` decides: a helper machine's dict carries no address or key, its quiet hours are its owner's clock, its seats resolve to the `worker` provider; `detect_models`, `estimate`, `loaded_state`, `loaded_config`, `ensure_model` and `unload_models` refuse a helper machine whatever calls them. Model, profile and component entries stored without an id (a helper's hello) get one made from what they are, so ids no longer change at every read. Writes: `upsert(data, actor=, reach=, session=)`, `delete`, `save_plans`/`save_currency`, `upsert_plan`/`delete_plan`, `set_settings`, `apply_row` and `stored_config` (the config without `max_parallel`, the row's number being the one), `row_registry(row)` for a row in a request's own session. The old `save()` (the file) is gone; `bootstrap()` stays as the first run's registry. The dry run reads no key whatever its configuration names.
- **The import** (`citar/pool/registry_import.py`, `pool.import_registry`). `run_once(session, owner)` imports `config/servers.json` (or, with no file, `servers.bootstrap()`, as the file's first read used to make it) unless `registry_imported` says it was: every entry a row under its own id, in no group, the owner's, `reach = direct`, the registry's host `is_host` (one host at most), the host and `api` kinds `server_resource`, `max_parallel` into `max_concurrent`, created in the file's order; plans merged by id, the currency set when none is; the marker (`{at, source, servers, owner_id}`) and one `migration.registry_imported` audit row. `on_boot()` runs it in a transaction of its own after the boot's migrations and before any game comes back, never raising; with no administrator it logs that no machine is usable until the first one is made; a file that reappears after the import is left with a warning at each start. `accounts.on_first_admin` runs it in a savepoint of its own before the adoption's (which got its own savepoint too, so either failing leaves the other and the new administrator), and `rename_after_commit` renames the file `servers.json.imported-<YYYYmmdd-HHMMSS>` (never over an earlier one) only once the outermost transaction commits; a failed rename is logged and the file is never read again. An unreadable file imports nothing and is tried again. `POST /api/pool/import-registry` and `api.importRegistry` are gone. `citar setup --local` writes through `servers.register_offline`: into the rows when the server's database exists and was imported, else into `servers.json` (from the first run's registry) for the first start to import.
- **Members' machines.** `member_llm`'s interim rule is gone: a block names any row, and admission decides (`pool.seats.authorize` on every row; a machine CITAR dials has no connection, so it is neither online nor offline to admission: `seats.liveness`). The site's own machines (the host, the APIs, the imported registry's) are administrators' until shared in a group. A suite names only machines its maker may see (403 without naming one; whether they may run there is the run's admission). A new suite starts on the host or the first machine with models the caller may use. A report's narrative and the machines a reader of a report sees go by VIEW on rows alone (the dry-run registry clause of `Reach` and `_scope_machines` is gone, which also stopped a member's report listing another member's private dry-run machine). `pool.check_reach`: a machine CITAR dials is an administrator's to create or switch to unless its provider is `dryrun` (`ReachRefused`, 403 with the reason, on `POST /api/servers` and on the pool's create and update); `pool.check_key_backend`: the `env` backend likewise (it names one of the server's own variables, and the key status showed its last characters).
- **The routes** (`admin_api.py`, no router-wide gate any more): as P3.2.8's table. `GET /api/servers` lists the caller's rows (every one for an administrator) with `reach`, `enabled`, `is_host`, `server_resource`, `owner_id`, `group_id` and `max_concurrent`, the plans' names only and the key backends without the host's paths for a member; `POST /api/servers` saves a row the caller manages (404 otherwise) or creates one with `register_servers` (a new one `direct` by default, as the registry's were; `is_host` an administrator's), in the request's transaction, committed before the answer; `DELETE` removes the key, the row and drops the helper; `/hardware`, `/rates`, `/key` owner-or-admin (`env` 403 for a member); `/detect`, `/state`, `/load`, `/unload`, `/loading`, `/loaded-config`, `/estimate` need MANAGE and a direct row or the host, and for a member a dry-run provider, else 404 before anything runs; `/collect` and `/api/lmstudio/devices` administrators' (`/collect` on the host row only); `/api/servers-settings`, `/api/keys/*`, `/api/electricity-plans*` administrators' with a 404 for anyone else signed in; `/api/servers/restricted` only the machines the caller may see. Beyond the table, by the same rule: `POST /api/llm/models` (403 before any address or variable is touched, the dry run excepted), and the first-run wizard's `POST /api/setup/scan` and `/api/setup/apply`, which probe this computer and register a machine the server dials (`require_role("admin", hide=True)`; `/api/setup/state` offers setup to administrators only). The route audit: 200 routes, 172 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded (the import route gone).
- **Dropped helpers.** `WorkerHub.drop(server_id, reason, token_id=None)` pops the connection, fails its pending requests as not retryable (`refusal: "dropped"`), sends `shutdown` with the reason (the helper stops rather than reconnect to a refusal) and closes the socket (4403); `drop_many` for an account's machines. The pool's revoke, disable and delete routes and the registry's delete commit, then drop; revoking a token drops only a connection made with it (`WorkerConnection.token_id`, from `authenticate`, which now returns it). `authenticate` also refuses a machine whose owner is suspended, deleted or pending, and suspending an account (`PATCH /api/admin/users/{id}`) drops its machines' helpers: 3-04's open question, decided as revoking a token is. The keep-alive checks every connection's token, machine and owner at each ping (`still_allowed`), so a change made from `admin_cli` or another process drops within a ping.
- **Benchmarks.** `resolve_group` is one function on rows: a direct machine's catalogue with load profiles, a helper machine (`pooled`) by key with no profiles, `max_parallel` from the row's slots; the restricted-hours unload leaves helper machines alone.
- **The test registry.** `tests/__init__.py` keeps `TEST_REGISTRY` as a dict and builds it as rows through `registry_import.run_once(..., audit_row=False)` in every database `db.create_all()` makes (a wrapper installed by the test package) and in the default one it creates for modules that open none (only when the package chose that database's path). Owned by nobody, the site's machines are administrators'; `tests.share_with_members(*ids)` puts some in a group granted to every member where a module's members used the site's dry run (`test_seat_blocks`, `test_items_access`, one reader test of `test_report_scope`).
- **The QA endpoint** (`scripts/qa_phase3.py`'s `FakeEndpoint`) is the scripted policy of P3.9.4: it answers a chat completion from the request (the negotiation it is asked about; a turn's reads, the dry run's legal orders, a `share_map` trade with a met civilization, at most `max_trades`, a vote when one is open, `end_turn`), with a delay per completion and a log of every request (tools offered, calls answered, seconds). Found on the way: the dry run never founded its city, since the engine names unit types as the ruleset does (`Settler`); it does now (`dryrun.py`), and checks `researching`, the key `get_empire` has.
- **Gates.** (1) and (2) `tests/test_registry_merge.py` (30 tests): the fixture (`tests/data/registry/servers.json`: the host's LM Studio with models and load profiles, an Anthropic server with its key in the file keystore, a LAN machine with an `env` key, a dry-run test server, two plans, EUR) imported at `create-admin` and at boot under its ids, each `get(id)` equal to `normalize_server()` of its entry, `max_concurrent` from `max_parallel`, the file renamed, once only, keys resolving, the host row; a fresh server-mode database logging why and waiting for `admin_cli create-admin`; no file, an unreadable file, a failed rename, an administrator rolled back; every answer of `tests/registry_calls.py`'s table (103 calls, two phases, a helper machine in the second, `costing.compute` over a synthetic ledger of four machines and two cost periods) equal to `tests/data/registry/expected.json`, recorded from the file-backed code before the change (the first commit); the seed against `TEST_REGISTRY`; `register_offline`. (3) the routes by alice on her own helper machine whose stored address is `http://localhost:1234/v1`, under `NoOutbound` (httpx's transports, `subprocess.run`/`Popen`, both clients, the server's variables) and a watched LM Studio SDK: the eight host routes 404 (and for bob, a visitor 401, an administrator 404 on a helper machine), nothing recorded; the host-wide routes 404 to a member and 200 to an administrator; `env` 403 for a member, accepted for an administrator; her own configuration hers and 404 to bob; a direct machine 403 with the reason on both route families and as a switch, a dry run allowed; the model probe; the setup wizard; P3.9.1's caller table for every changed route. (4) `tests/test_worker.Revocation` (8 tests) on a real uvicorn server and a real helper over a websocket: revoking the token, disabling, deleting (both route families) and suspending the owner each close the socket within a second of the request, the helper stops, its next request is refused, a request it was serving fails at once as not retryable, a hello with what was taken away is refused, a token's revocation spares another token's connection, and the keep-alive's check. (5) a suite naming the host and a helper machine resolved through rows; a run's dry-run job starting, the helper job's seat on the `worker` provider and no model loading; the full suite on the seeded database. (6) `python scripts/qa_phase3.py accounts` exit 0, 86 of 86 (3-04's 65; 3-05's 21: the import at the first administrator's creation with the boot's log line, the rows, the rename and the audit row; a member's machine the server would dial and the model probe refused with nothing connecting; the `env` backend refused; her direct dry-run machine playing her game; another member's 404 and 403; the site's dry run 403 to a member without naming it; a real helper (`python -m citar.worker`) behind the scripted endpoint connecting with its model; the eight host routes 404 with nothing asked of any model server; the host-wide routes 404; her game playing two turns through the helper with a city founded, a trade opened with the civilization it met and `end_turn`; the token revoked, the machine disabled and then deleted each dropping the helper in under a second (0.003 s from the answer) and the helper stopping, a new hello refused). S1: 932 tests OK in 233 s (891 at the start). S2 ruff clean. S3 above. S4 37 files, 166 internal links, every one resolves. S5 `mkdocs build --strict`; CONFIGURATION (the machines in the database, the import, keys under the kept ids), WORKERS (who may do what to a machine; revocation), API (the machine routes), RUNBOOK, ACCOUNTS, REPORTS, AI_PLAYERS and ARCHITECTURE updated and the wiki regenerated. S6 33 modules parse as ES modules. S8 as (6); no browser item is 3-05's (A4 is 3-06's). The session's overhead (P3.9.7): 2.327 and 2.268 (session medians 0.65 and 0.63 s, drive 0.28 s; reference 2.29, limit 2.87). CI only: test.yml on its three OSes with its package, installer and Docker jobs.
- **Fix round** (2026-10-12, 00:36 to 01:21, -04:00, 45 minutes). The review found three majors and ten minors; twelve were fixed, and the thirteenth (the old pool import's copies) is answered differently from its suggested fix, as below. Every new test fails on the code before its fix.
  - *A machine body of the wrong shape* (a major). `normalize_server` crashed (AttributeError, TypeError) on a string where `connection`, `connection.key`, `power`, `restricted_hours` or a model's `inference` belongs, on `models: [1]`, `costs: "x"`, `costs: [1]`, `windows: [1]` and the like: a 500 on `POST /api/servers` (which 3-05 opened to members) and the pool's create and update. Each part's shape is checked (`_obj`, `_items`: objects, lists of objects, a window's days a list, a cost period's plan id text) and a wrong one is a `ServerError`; whatever else the checks miss is caught and turned into one, so every route that saves a machine answers 400. `normalize_plan` likewise; `hardware_summary` and `apply_hardware` read only the parts of a collector file or a helper's hello that have the shape they expect.
  - *A helper machine's key* (a major). The key routes read the backend from the registry's view of the machine, which blanks a helper machine's key: a member could store secrets in the host's credential store or key file that no delete could find. `POST` and `DELETE /api/servers/{id}/key` are 404 on a helper machine (the server never reads its key); the key routes and both delete routes read the backend from what the row stores (`servers.stored_key`), deleting a machine removes its keystore entry once committed (`servers.forget_key`, the pool's delete too), and a save of a helper machine's view keeps the address and key its row stores (`apply_row`, `HELPER_HIDDEN`; the pool's update sets `reach` first). `servers.js` shows a helper machine a note instead of a key section.
  - *Listings that named machines* (a major). `GET /api/pool/availability` ran admission over every row and listed refused machines, so a member saw another member's private machine and the site's unshared host, APIs and dry run; the benchmark scheduler's `restricted` in `/api/benchmarks/status` and `/api/status` named every machine in its quiet hours. Both go through VIEW now (`pool.visible`, `pool.visible_restrictions`, which `/api/servers*` use too; `admission.usable_servers(only=)`).
  - *An administrator made a member kept the site's machines* (minor). The import makes the first administrator the owner of the host and the API rows, and ownership gave MANAGE (delete, key, hardware) and an owner's seat. The site's machines (`is_host` or `server_resource`, `access.is_site_machine`) are administrators' whoever is named their owner: no MANAGE, no listing as one's own and no owner's admission for anyone else (`access`, `admission.check`, `pool.servers_for`, `GET /api/servers`, reports' `Reach`).
  - *The tariff through a member's rates* (minor). A member could name the household's plan in a machine's costs and read `kwh_price` from `/rates`. The site's plans are an administrator's to put in a machine's costs (`pool.check_plans`, 403 on `POST /api/servers`, the pool's create, update and costing; a plan the row already carries stays); `/rates` on both route families leaves a member's energy unpriced (`pool.machine_rates`); a member sees only the names of the plans an administrator put in their machines; `GET /api/servers/new` fills in no plan for a member. (Reports still price a member's machine with a plan an administrator gave it: that is the administrator's choice.)
  - *A member choosing a new machine's id* (minor). `POST /api/servers` created a row under the body's id, so a removed machine's id (seats, suites, the ledger, aliases) could be taken over. A member's new machine gets an id from the server; an administrator may still choose one.
  - *Revocation races* (minor). A revoke committed between a helper's hello check and its registration dropped nothing, and the helper served until the next keep-alive. `WorkerHub.admit` registers, then checks the token, machine and owner again; a refused helper gets `shutdown` before any welcome, which now stops it too. A request opened on a connection a drop or a disconnect just closed fails at once (`open_request`, with `closed` set in `fail_all` under the same lock).
  - *The model probe read a variable for the dry run* (minor): the dry run answers before `api_key_env` is read. *Setup's dismiss and reopen* (minor) set a site-wide policy for any member with `register_servers`: an administrator's now (404 to anyone else), and a member's `/api/setup/state` has no `paths`.
  - *The import* (minors). With no file, `on_first_admin` built the first run's registry (a hardware scan and up to two `lms` calls) inside the transaction that made the administrator, holding SQLite's write lock: it now imports once that transaction commits, in one of its own (`import_after_commit`). A `servers.json` found after the import is called NOT imported when it is not the file the marker names (another instance's `config/`), never safe to remove. The power sampler follows the host (`PowerSampler(None)`, `start_power_sampler(follow_host=True)` at the server's start), so a host made by the first administrator after the boot is sampled without a restart.
  - *The old pool import's copies* (minor, fixed differently). The suggestion was to treat a registry entry as present when a row's `config.id` equals it. That would leave the registry's ids, which seats, suites, the usage ledger and the keystore name and which the copies never answered to, resolving nowhere (and the host unset). The import still brings each machine in under its own id, and names the copies (`earlier_copies`) in its result, its audit row and a warning, for an administrator to remove once nothing uses them.
  - *Gate 4's tests* (minor). The one-second bound is now timed on the helper itself (its thread stops: the socket closed at both ends) for revoke, disable, delete on both route families and suspension; the keep-alive test runs the hub's own `ping_loop` (every 0.1 s) on the server's loop against a token revoked in the database; the QA delete check measures its second through an administrator's `/api/status`. Measured: the helper stops 0.01 to 0.03 s after the request.
  - *Found on the way.* One worker token in 64 began with `-`, which the helper's command line (the Servers page's, and the QA harness's) read as an option: the first QA run of this round failed on one. Tokens never start with `-` now (`tokens.generate`), and the harness passes `--token=`.
  - *Gates again.* S1 946 tests OK in 239 s (932 before). S2 ruff clean. S3 200 routes: 172 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded. S4 37 files, 167 internal links, every one resolves. S5 `mkdocs build --strict`; WORKERS, API and CONFIGURATION updated and the wiki regenerated. S6 33 modules parse. S8 `python scripts/qa_phase3.py accounts` exit 0, 89 of 89 (three new: the key routes, bad bodies and the plan, the listings), about 77 s; the helper machine's editor checked in the browser pane against the kept QA server (the note in place of the key section). The session's overhead: 2.220 and 2.212 (reference 2.29, limit 2.87).
- **Left for later packages.** 3-06: the Servers page (`servers.js` still lists and edits every machine for an administrator, a helper machine's detection there now answering 404 and its editor showing no key section; the page still offers a member the Electricity plans and Settings buttons, whose routes answer 404, and a cost editor whose plan list is empty; the lobby already merges the caller's machines with the ones shared with them). 3-07: the scheduler's capacity from `machine(id)["max_concurrent"]` capped by the helper's hello, `hub.drop`'s refused requests and the disabled rows; a request's database session may commit after its answer reaches the client (seen in `test_worker.Revocation`, which polls for it), so a route that must act on its commit (as the drops do) commits itself first. 3-09: `/api/queue` still names every machine. 3-18: the machine settings (slots are `max_concurrent`).

**As built in 3-06: one Servers page** (P3.2.8's page; Appendix D). One agent, 2026-10-12, from 01:37 to 02:33 (-04:00), 56 minutes, on branch `p-3-06` from `7a2147c`, in one sitting. No Rust changed.

- **The page** (`citar/web/js/pool.js`, `#/pool`): every machine the viewer owns or may use, from `GET /api/pool/servers` (what anyone the machine is listed to may know, admission, the helper's live models) joined by id to `GET /api/servers` (the registry's view of the machines the viewer manages: what the editors edit), the machines CITAR dials and the helper machines alike, in sections: an administrator's "This server's machines" (the host, `server_resource` rows and any with no owner), "Your machines" and "Members' machines"; a member's "Your machines" and "Shared with you". Each card shows its kind, "runs CITAR", direct or the helper online or offline, disabled, its quiet hours, the group that shares it, its owner when not the viewer, its connection, hardware, power, rates, key status, issues, models (a connected helper's live models, loaded ones marked; LM Studio's loaded state on a machine the server drives) and its admission line. The groups and their access are below, as before (`grantDialog`, the windows editor, `groupCard`). It refreshes every 20 s without a dialog open, keeping the reader's place (`keepPlace`).
- **The editors as dialogs** (`citar/web/js/servers.js`), opened from a card: **Connection & models** (General, Connection and Models tabs: name, kind, description, notes and, for an administrator on a direct machine, "this is the machine running CITAR"; the provider, base URL, LM Link device, model loading, slots, timeout or the dry run's delay; the catalogue with each model's inference settings, token prices and LM Studio's load profiles with Estimate, Load now, Copy loaded settings and Unload now; Detect models), **Hardware & power** (not for online APIs or the dry run), **Costs** (components, cost periods; the plan select only with plans the viewer is given, "Edit electricity plans" an administrator's), **Quiet hours** (the pool's and the old Restricted hours tab merged: windows, unloading on a direct LM Studio machine, grace minutes; the owner's zone for a helper machine, this server's clock for a direct one), **API key** (a direct machine whose provider takes one: the backends the caller is offered, `env` only to administrators; unlocking the key file an administrator's), **Helper** (a helper machine's tokens), **Sharing** (the group, `PUT /api/pool/servers/{id}` with `group_id`), **Disable/Enable** and **Remove**. Every dialog edits a copy of the registry's view and saves it whole through `POST /api/servers`, except quiet hours (`PUT .../quiet-hours`). A helper machine's dialogs say what its helper owns (its address and key, its models at each hello, its hardware scan) and offer no detection, manual model or key. The head: **Add a machine** (a helper machine or the dry run for anyone with `register_servers`; the six machines the server dials for administrators), **New group**, and for administrators **Electricity plans** and **Settings**. The old `renderServers` page and the pool's `openCostEditor` are gone.
- **The gates, in one table** (`citar/web/js/machines.js`, pure, no DOM): `GATES` names each route the page calls by its `api.js` name with its gate (`member`, `view`, `register`, `save`, `manage`, `direct`, `dialled`, `admin`, `host`), and `mayCall(name, viewer, target)` decides from the viewer (`{admin, register}`) and the machine or group (`can_manage`, `reach`, `is_host`, the provider). servers.js's `call(name, target, ...args)` is the page's one way to the network (it refuses without calling when `may` says no) and `may` decides every control's visibility, so a control and its request cannot disagree. A name missing from the table is never allowed.
- **The old addresses** (`machines.legacyServersTarget`, pure): `#/servers` to `#/pool`, `#/servers/<id>` to `#/pool/<id>`, a group's `#/servers/groups/<gid>` to `#/pool/groups/<gid>`, the query kept; the router replaces the address (`location.replace`) before anything else, so Back does not return to it and a visitor's sign-in comes back to the new one. `#/pool/<id>` opens that machine (highlighted, its Connection & models dialog open when the viewer manages it), `#/pool/groups/<gid>` highlights the group, and one the viewer may not see is a notice on the page ("not here, or not shared with you"), the same for both. The pool's group listing no longer hands out a `share_url` (the fix round, below); the benchmark page's quiet-hours pills link to `#/pool`; the nav's "Servers" already went there.
- **Found on the way and fixed.** (1) *A save read back stale*: FastAPI ends a request-scoped dependency after the answer is sent, so the pool's write routes committed after their answer, and the page's redraw right after a dialog's save could read the old machine, group or grant (3-05 saw the same in its tests). The pool's routes that change something now commit before they answer (`pool_api._saved`; the drops already did). (2) *Revoking another member's grant said it exists*: `DELETE /api/pool/grants/{id}` answered 400 from `pool.revoke_grant` to a stranger; it now passes the group's gate first (404). (3) *A member was asked to pick an electricity plan* they may not name: a member's `GET /api/servers` leaves out `servers.NO_PLAN_ISSUE`; the Costs dialog says to put one's own power cost in the fixed monthly figure. (4) *The Sharing dialog could unshare by accident*: a member's machine in a group an administrator's page does not list showed "not shared" selected; the group it is in stays the choice.
- **Tests.** `citar/web/tests/machines.test.mjs` (13 Node tests, `citar/web/package.json` making the modules ES modules for Node; `node --test "citar/web/tests/*.test.mjs"`, since Node 22 takes a pattern, not a folder): the redirect cases and the addresses that are not the old page's, what an address opens, ids round-tripping through `poolLink`, and the gates (a member or a member without `register_servers` never may call an `admin` or `host` route on any machine, dials only their own dry run, manages only their own machines and keys only on a direct one; saving needs `register_servers` new and MANAGE existing; an administrator may call everything on the host and the host's own only there, never a helper machine's dialling routes; nobody signed out may call anything; a name outside the table never). `tests/test_servers_page.py` (11): **the scripted check of gate 2**: servers.js and pool.js use `api` only inside `call`, and every `call` and `may` names a `GATES` route; every `GATES` name is an `api.js` function with a route of the app; each route's gate as the server's code declares it (the `require_role("admin")` and `require_cap("register_servers")` closures, the body gate it calls: `_dialled`, `_not_a_helper`, `_row`/`_server`/`_group` with MANAGE or VIEW, `check_cap`) equals its `GATES` entry (a mutation of the table or a direct `api.lmDevices()` in pool.js fails it); the header's calls are member routes.
