---

## P3.3 3C: the prompt scheduler, task classes and the Queue page

### P3.3.1 Today

Every model call goes straight to its provider. A helper machine answers `busy` when its slots are full and the caller sleeps and retries; a direct machine has no slot accounting at all. Machines are "occupied" per activity for a whole game. There is no per-request queue, fairness or priority; `pool.queue` orders whole jobs (benchmark jobs, probe runs, reports, lobby games' make-way) by priority and age (`citar/pool/queue.py`). See P3.0.

### P3.3.2 Machines and capacity

- **`citar/pool/scheduler.py`** keeps one `Machine` per `servers.id`, made on first use and shared by the whole process: sessions, the benchmark scheduler, the probe runner and the report runner. The lab never calls models.
- **Capacity** is `Server.max_concurrent` (the one site number, P3.2.8), capped by the connected helper's `hello.max_concurrent` for a helper machine. While a helper machine is offline its capacity is unknown: lease requests pass through, so the provider's connection error drives today's reconnect window and disconnect policy, and tickets are refused as temporary. A **disabled** row refuses every lease request and ticket ("this machine is disabled"), and its helper has been dropped (P3.2.8).
- **Not scheduled:** a model configuration without a `server_id` passes through, measured by the metrics but not by the ledger. Only an admin's inline block or a session with no owner (tests, scripts) can have one; a member's cannot (P3.2.9).
- Capacity, the loaded models (a helper's reported `loaded` flags; LM Studio's model list for a direct LM Studio machine, cached; every model for an online API) and the quiet hours are cached per machine, re-read at most every 5 s and at once on a helper's connect or disconnect, so no request touches the database.

### P3.3.3 Leases, tickets and the order

- **A lease** (task class *full*) is a model seat that plays every turn, and it **belongs to the session's seat, not to its agent** (agents are made lazily, at the seat's first model turn, and a lease made with them would leave a machine looking free while a game that will want it is loaded, restored, paused or waiting on its bots). `GameSession` makes a lease for every living full seat with a `server_id` when the session is registered: created, loaded (registered paused), brought back by `restore_live`, reloaded by a benchmark, launched from a scenario, or started as a probe's turn case. It releases it when the seat changes type or machine (`update_seat` releases the old lease and makes the new one), the seat is eliminated, the game is over, closed or stopped, or the session crashes. A make-way pause (`pause_reason.kind == "queue"`) releases its leases and the resume makes them again, as `occupied()` skips such games today; every other pause (a person's, quiet hours, a budget, a disconnect) keeps them, since the game resumes where it left off. The agent binds to its seat's lease (`GameSession.lease_for(pid)`), which outlives it. Kinds: `game` (a lobby game; lends its slot while idle), `benchmark` (never lends), `probe_case` (a probe's turn case, for the case only; never lends). Every request the seat makes, in its turn or answering a chat out of turn, is a request of its lease.
- **Active and idle.** A lease is *active* while its seat is mid-turn and *idle* (lending, for a `game` lease) otherwise. The driver calls `activate()` before the seat's `play_turn` and `idle()` after it. The agent also calls `idle()` whenever its turn is blocked on something other than the machine (waiting for a chat reply in `_await_answer` or `_end_turn`, held by a pause) and `activate()` before its next step: a seat waiting on a counterpart must not keep the slot that counterpart needs to answer, which on a one-slot home GPU serving both would otherwise be every such chat.
- **A ticket** (task class *partial*) is one pooled prompt: a hybrid seat's diplomat phase or chat answer, a probe's offer or message case, a report's narrative, the Servers page's test. It carries the machine, model, activity, the consumer (a user id), its class (`chat`, `diplomat`, `probe`, `report`, `test`), a priority, a deadline (the latest start), `steps` (how many consecutive calls it may make once started; default 8) and `wait_for_benchmark`.
- **The rules,** which tests hold as invariants:
  1. Requests in flight, draining ones included (below), never exceed capacity.
  2. Lease requests are served in arrival order and before any ticket: a slot that frees goes to the oldest waiting lease request (skipping one whose consumer is at its grant's cap, P3.3.7), and to a ticket only when no lease request waits.
  3. A ticket starts only when (a) no benchmark lease is live on the machine and no benchmark drain is under way (otherwise it is refused at once, "held by a benchmark", unless `wait_for_benchmark`, when it waits); (b) its model is loaded, or the machine allows swaps (`capabilities.llm.allow_swaps`, P3.8), or a live lease uses that model (otherwise refused at once, "the model is not loaded"); (c) the machine is outside its quiet hours (otherwise refused as temporary, with `retry_at`); (d) its consumer is under its grant's concurrency (P3.3.7); (e) in flight + 1 ≤ capacity − reserved, where *reserved* counts the leases that are active or never lend, and have no request in flight.
  4. **The reservation keeps slots from tickets only.** A lease request may take any free slot, so between an active seat's steps another seat's lease request (its chat answer, its turn in another game) may take the slot, and the active seat then waits its turn in arrival order; a ticket never takes it. A benchmark's slot is never lent to a ticket.
  5. **Borrowing is bounded.** A ticket that starts while a `game` lease on the machine is idle borrows that lease's slot: it runs under its class's cap (`diplomat` 180 s, `chat` 120 s, `probe` 120 s, `report` 300 s, `test` 30 s from its start, and each step's `max_tokens` at most the class's cap), after which its next step is refused and its caller falls back or finishes. So a lobby seat whose turn comes waits for at most one capped step, and that wait is its `queue_s`.
- **Order among tickets:** priority, highest first; within a priority, round-robin across consumers (the one served longest ago first); within a consumer, first come first served. Priority = the class's base (`test` 30, `chat` 20, `diplomat` 10, `probe` 0, `report` −10) + the activity's queue priority (−100..100, `pool.queue.priority`, which its owner or an admin sets on the Queue page).
- **A started ticket** holds its slot for up to `steps` calls (and, borrowing, its class's cap) or until it is closed. States: `queued → running → done`, or `cancelled`, `expired` (not started by its deadline; the caller falls back), `refused` (rule 3, admission or a budget). A running ticket is never cut off by its start deadline: its caller's own budget stops further steps.
- **The benchmark drain.** When a benchmark lease becomes live on a machine, queued tickets that do not wait for benchmarks are refused ("held by a benchmark"), running tickets are cancelled (their callers fall back), and the lease's first request waits until no ticket request is in flight there, draining ones included, for at most `benchmark_drain_seconds` (default 120). The wait is the job's `drain_s` metric; if the bound is reached, the request goes ahead and the job's timing is marked `contaminated` in its result (shown on the run page, left out of the speed score). So no pooled request overlaps a benchmark step. (Lobby leases beside a benchmark are the job-level rule's business, P3.3.5, as today.)
- **Cancel and draining:** a queued ticket leaves the queue; a running one closes its conversation. `WorkerConversation.close()` now calls `hub.cancel(server_id, request_id)`, which sends `CANCEL` and ends the caller's wait with an error; on a direct machine the HTTP call is abandoned (closing the connection, which LM Studio and the online APIs stop on). A v1 helper ignores `CANCEL` and keeps generating (Phase 4 fixes that), so the scheduler keeps that request's slot as **draining** until the hub sees the request id's late `Done` or error, or its request timeout plus the hub's 15 s grace passes. The hub hands frames for resolved ids to the scheduler (`scheduler.late_frame(server_id, request_id)`) instead of dropping them (`workers.py:128-135`), and `submit`'s own timeout drains the same way. The scheduler's in-flight count is therefore the helper's own, and the hub has no reason to refuse `busy`.
- **A `busy` refusal anyway** (a helper restarted with fewer slots, an accounting bug) is never retried blindly: the scheduler lowers the machine's capacity to its in-flight count until the next frame frees a slot, puts the request back at the head of its queue, and logs the mismatch; the request then waits on the machine's condition like any other, with no sleep and nothing metered.
- **Position and ETA:** each machine keeps an exponentially weighted mean of generation time per (model, class); a ticket's ETA is the expected time of the work ahead of it over the slots it may use. Both are published to the ticket's status callback.

### P3.3.4 The conversation wrapper and metering

- `make_conversation(cfg, system, tools, binding=None)`: with a lease or a ticket and a scheduled machine it returns `ScheduledConversation(inner, binding)`, whose `step()` acquires the binding's slot (blocking; never with a session lock held, P3.3.9), sets the inner conversation's request timeout only then (from the caller's remaining budget, which the queue does not shorten), runs the inner step, releases, and records `last_gen_s` and `last_queue_s`.
- **Generation time is bounded by what the server saw.** On a helper machine `gen_s = min(Done.duration, observed)`, where *observed* is the server's wall time from sending the request frame to receiving its `Done` (`WorkerConversation` now keeps both); on a direct machine it is the wall time of the HTTP call after the slot was granted. Both values travel in the step event and in `compute_events` (P3.4.2). Token counts are capped at the request's `max_tokens` (output and reasoning) and at the model's context length (input). A helper whose reported duration exceeds the observed time by more than 10% plus a second on three requests within an hour gets `flags.overreports` on its machine row, an audit row and a note on the admin's view of the Servers page; Phase 4's trust tiers can read it. A modified helper therefore cannot raise its owner's given credits beyond the time the server actually waited for it.
- **Listeners:** `scheduler.add_listener(fn)` receives a `StepEvent` (machine, model, activity, consumer, task class, class, `gen_s`, the reported and observed seconds, `queue_s`, the usage delta, request id, time) after every step. The usage tracker's `llm()` is the first listener and replaces `LLMAgent._meter`, so an attempt that never reached the model meters nothing; the ledger is the second (P3.4).
- **One path for every model call.** Report narratives (`reports.complete`, which today calls the SDKs directly) and the Servers page's test go through `make_conversation` with a ticket; `_complete_on_worker`'s polling goes.
- `LLMAgent._step` loses its `busy` branch. `metrics.model_step` records `gen_s` as the step's seconds and a new `queue_s`; the stats screen shows "waited for the machine" beside generation time, and the model score's speed reads generation time only.
- **The turn's clock counts the model's own time only.** Each step's `queue_s` and every chat wait (`_await_answer`, `_end_turn`, and from 3-11 the clock-bounded waits of P3.5.2) are added to `LLMAgent._waited`, as reconnects and pauses already are, so neither counts against `max_turn_seconds` (1800); the request timeout `_step` gives the call is set after the slot is granted, from the turn's remaining time. A turn with 25 minutes of queueing and chat waits and a minute of generation ends by `end_turn`, not `time_limit`.
- `WorkerHub.submit` keeps its busy check as a safety net only.

### P3.3.5 Occupancy, quiet hours and the job queue

- `pool.seats.occupied()` returns the labels of the leases live on a machine (game, benchmark, probe case). Since leases belong to registered sessions' seats (P3.3.3), a game loaded paused, restored, or whose bots move first is listed, as today's `occupied()` lists it. Hybrid seats, chat answers and report narratives are tickets and never occupy a machine. `claim()` and `release()` go.
- `pool.queue` stays the job-level queue for work that needs a lease (benchmark jobs, probe runs, lobby games making way). Reports leave it: their narrative is a ticket. A benchmark job starts when its machine has fewer leases than its limit (`scheduler.leases(server_id)`); the benchmark scheduler also counts its own running hybrid jobs, which hold no lease (P3.6.9), against that limit, and never starts a hybrid job on a machine where a benchmark lease is live. A probe run takes a `probe_case` lease for each turn case and tickets for offer and message cases; a lobby game's `_quiet_hours` and `_make_way` apply to its full seats.
- `usage.session_servers` counts a machine as held by a game only while one of the game's leases is live on it.

### P3.3.6 Lending and the benchmark rule

A lobby lease lends its slot while its seat is idle (between its turns, and while it waits on a chat or a pause), within the borrowing caps of P3.3.3 (P3.1.2). A benchmark lease never lends; when one becomes live the machine drains its pooled requests first, and while one is live no ticket starts there, so no pooled prompt shares the GPU with a benchmark step (P3.3.3's drain). A hybrid benchmark game holds no lease: its prompts are tickets with `wait_for_benchmark` and no start deadline (P3.6.9), and the benchmark scheduler keeps hybrid and full jobs of its own off each other's machines (P3.3.5), so it never runs beside a full benchmark and never stops pooled work.

### P3.3.7 Admission and task classes

- Grant purposes gain `pooled` (partial prompts); `game` remains full seats; an empty list still means all.
- Capabilities `full_llm` and `partial_llm` (P3.2.4) are the hooks for future restrictions (plan A3). An LLM seat needs `full_llm` and a grant for `game`; a hybrid seat `partial_llm` and `pooled`; a report's narrative `run_reports` and `pooled`; a probe run `run_probes`, with `probe` for its turn cases and `pooled` for the rest.
- `pool_seats.authorize` checks every seat with a model by its class, and `update_seat` runs it again.
- **Admission decides who may, the scheduler when.** Leases and tickets are admitted with `admission.check(..., in_flight=None)`, which checks the grant, the purpose, the window and the budget and skips the capacity step, so a busy machine queues the work rather than refusing it. A ticket is admitted when it is made (`purpose="pooled"` for the consumer on that machine); a refusal is the ticket's `refused`, with the admission's reason and `retry_at`. An offline helper still refuses a ticket at once (`online=False`).
- **A grant's `concurrency`** (`AccessGrant.concurrency`, 0 meaning no cap below the machine's slots) is enforced by the scheduler as a cap on that consumer's running requests on the machine, leases and tickets together (rule 2's skip and rule 3(d)). The baseline keeps the column.

### P3.3.8 The Queue page

- `GET /api/queue` lists **only the machines the caller may view** (`access.on` on the `Server` row: its owner, members its groups reach, admins), and adds a single line "n other machines are busy" for the rest, with no names or loads; today it names every machine to every member. Per machine it adds its capacity, its leases (activity label, kind, lending or active, the member when the caller may see the activity) and its running and waiting tickets (position, class, the activity's label or "someone's <class>" when the caller may not see it, the member's handle under the same rule, model, priority, waited, ETA, deadline).
- `POST /api/queue/priority` takes `kind = game|benchmark|probe|report|lab` and needs MANAGE on the activity (P3.2.4); tickets follow their activity's priority. `POST /api/queue/tickets/{id}/cancel` (new) is for the activity's owner, the machine's owner and admins.
- `citar/web/js/queue.js` shows a "Pooled prompts" section under each machine, with priority buttons and cancel only where the caller may use them.

### P3.3.9 Threading

One lock and one condition per machine. `acquire` waits on the condition in ticks of at most a second, to notice cancellation, a stopped session and its deadline. The callers are threads already (the session driver, responders, the report and probe runners, benchmark sessions). Nothing in the scheduler takes a session lock or reads the database on the request path.
- **Only `acquire` blocks.** In test mode `acquire` asserts that the calling thread holds no session lock (sessions register their `RLock` in a weak set when `CITAR_TEST_LOCKS` is set, and an `RLock` can tell whether the calling thread owns it). `cancel`, `release`, `idle`, `activate`, `close` and the late-frame and status bookkeeping take the machine lock only to update counters, never wait, and are allowed under a session lock: `suspend`, `update_seat` and `_crashed` cancel agents there, and the chat clock cancels answers there.
- **Callbacks run outside the machine lock.** Status callbacks (`queued`, `thinking`, the Queue page's counts) are queued while the machine lock is held and delivered after it is released, by one notifier thread per process, so a callback that takes a session lock can never meet a thread that holds that session lock and waits for the machine lock.

### P3.3.10 Phase 4 hooks

Capacity and the swap rule come from one function, `servers.machine_settings(server_id)`, which Phase 4's settings frame will also feed; every request carries the id the v2 `cancel` frame will name; v2's reported `gen_s` arrives through the same listener.

**As built in 3-07: the scheduler core: leases, tickets, draining, the conversation wrapper, metering** (P3.3.2-P3.3.4, P3.3.9; Appendix D). One agent, 2026-10-12, from 03:34 to 04:36 (-04:00), 62 minutes, on branch `p-3-07` from `bd52efb`. No Rust changed.

- **`citar/pool/scheduler.py`.** `Scheduler` (one per process, `scheduler.get()`, the usage tracker its first listener) keeps a `Machine` per `servers.id`, made on first use. Capacity is `servers.machine_settings(id)["slots"]` (new here: `max_concurrent`, `capabilities.llm.enabled` and `allow_swaps` from the registry's cache; 3-18 adds the CPU limits), capped by the helper's `hello.max_concurrent`; an offline helper's capacity is unknown (lease requests pass through, tickets are refused as temporary), a machine with no row is unscheduled, a disabled row (or LLM off) refuses everything. `Lease` (`game`, `benchmark`, `probe_case`; `activate`/`idle`/`release`) and `Ticket` (`chat`, `diplomat`, `probe`, `report`, `test`; priority = the class's base + the activity's, `start_within`, `steps`, `wait_for_benchmark`, `cancel`/`close`) share one dispatcher that applies the rules of P3.3.3 in one place: lease requests FIFO before tickets (skipping a consumer at its cap), a ticket only on an unreserved slot, on a loaded model (a helper's `loaded` flags; a direct LM Studio's `/api/v0/models`, asked at most every 5 s when a ticket is made; every model for the dry run and online APIs), outside quiet hours and never while a benchmark holds the machine (a live benchmark lease, or a request of one still in flight or draining); tickets by priority, then the consumer served longest ago, then arrival. A running ticket holds its slot between its steps. A ticket that starts on an idle `game` lease's slot is `borrowing`: its steps' `max_tokens` are capped by class (`BORROW_MAX_TOKENS`), and its next step is refused once its class's time cap has passed **or a lease request waits** (so a lobby seat whose turn comes waits one capped step, not the cap). The benchmark drain: a benchmark lease made refuses queued tickets that do not wait for benchmarks and cancels running ones (closing their conversations outside the machine lock), and its first request waits until no ticket slot is left, at most `drain_seconds` (120); `drain_s` and `contaminated` are on the lease for 3-08 to put in the job's result. Position and ETA (an EWMA of generation time per model and class, 10 s for none yet) go to `on_status` callbacks, which a notifier thread delivers after the machine's lock is let go; that thread also expires drains, deadlines and a `busy` hold every second, survives an exception in its loop and is started again if it died.
- **Draining.** `WorkerConversation.close()` calls `hub.cancel(server_id, request_id)` (a `CANCEL` frame; the caller's wait ends with an `unfinished` error), and `submit`'s own timeout cancels the same way; the conversation reports `unfinished = (request id, seconds left)`, and its slot stays taken until `late_frame` (the hub now hands a frame for a resolved id to the scheduler instead of dropping it) or the request's timeout plus 15 s. A late frame that arrives before the caller has marked the slot draining is remembered and frees it at once. A `busy` refusal anyway holds the machine's capacity at what else is in flight until the next frame frees a slot (or `BUSY_HOLD_SECONDS`, 5, when nothing else is in flight to send one), puts the request back at the head of its queue with its place kept, logs the mismatch, and meters nothing; nothing sleeps.
- **`ScheduledConversation`** (`make_conversation(cfg, system, tools, binding=)`; `LLMAgent` wraps its provider's conversation the same way, so the tests that patch `make_conversation` still run under the scheduler): each step acquires (blocking, in ticks of a second, noticing a closed conversation, a released lease and the caller's `halted`), sets the request timeout from `budget(queued)` only then, runs the provider's step, frees the slot (or drains it), and emits a `StepEvent`: `gen_s` = `min(Done.duration, observed)` on a helper (the hub measures `observed_s` from sending the request frame to its answer; an absent duration falls back to it), the call's wall time on a machine CITAR dials; `reported_s`, `observed_s`, `queue_s`, the usage delta with output and reasoning tokens capped at `max_tokens` and input at the helper's reported context, and flags (`capped_tokens`, `overreported`). Three over-reports (above 10% plus a second) within an hour put `flags.overreports` (`count`, `last`) on the row with one `server.overreports` audit row, written by the notifier thread; the note on an administrator's Servers page is 3-18's (its scope).
- **Leases for seats** (`GameSession.lease_for(pid)`, made on first use by `get_agent`; 3-08 moves them to registration): kind `benchmark` in a benchmark game, `probe_case` in a probe's case (`probes.run_case` sets `lease_kind`), else `game`; consumer the game's owner, capped by the concurrency of the grant that admits the owner (`pool.seats.consumer_cap`, read once when the lease is made); a seat without a machine gets an unscheduled lease (its steps still metered, under `unassigned`). Released at `stop`, at `_crashed` and by `update_seat` when the seat's type or machine changes (non-blocking, under the lock). The driver activates the seat's lease around `play_turn`; `LLMAgent` idles it while it waits on a chat reply (`_await_answer`, `_end_turn`) or a pause and activates it after, adds the wall time of those waits and each step's `queue_s` to `_waited`, keeps the turn's budget on an injectable `_clock`, and lost its busy branch and `_meter`. `metrics.model_step(pid, gen_s, queue_s=)`: a turn's `model_s` is now generation seconds and `queue_s` sits beside it; the summary gains `avg_model_s` and `avg_queue_s`, `compare_models` carries them, and the model score's speed reads `avg_model_s` (`scoring.model_speed`; a row without it falls back to its turn time). The stats screen shows "Model/turn" and "Waited/turn".
- **Locks.** In test mode (`CITAR_TEST_LOCKS`, which `tests/__init__.py` now sets for the whole suite) every `GameSession` registers itself in a weak set and `acquire` raises when the calling thread owns one's `RLock`; nothing in the suite tripped it.
- **Choices the design left open, and departures.** (1) The row's settings are read again by the request that finds them more than 5 s old, before it takes the machine's lock and from the registry's own cache, rather than by the notifier thread: a background reader held the test suite's SQLite files open (3-01's rename test failed on it), and an idle machine needs no reads. A helper's state is still pushed by the hub at once (connect, disconnect, drop, catalog), and `Scheduler.refresh(id)` reads a row at once (3-18's settings routes). (2) The Servers page's test (`POST /api/pool/servers/{id}/test`) became a `test` ticket here, not in 3-09, because gate 8's QA needs a test ticket to cancel over HTTP: it gained `wait` (1-120 s; then the ticket is cancelled and the helper's request drains) and answers `gen_s`, `queue_s` and `cancelled`; its admission purpose stays `game` until 3-09's `pooled`, and it prefers a loaded model. (3) An administrator's `GET /api/status` lists the scheduler's machines (slots, in flight, draining, waiting, leases, tickets, counters), which the QA reads. (4) `tests/test_session.py`'s busy test names a machine: a busy refusal is the scheduler's to absorb, and a seat with no machine never meets one.
- **Gates.** (1) `tests/test_scheduler.py`: 500 seeded random schedules of 120 events each (lobby and benchmark leases, tickets of every class, cancels with v1 draining, late frames, busy refusals, step and clock times, a benchmark lease appearing mid-schedule) with every rule asserted at every grant and ticket step (`AuditedMachine`) and between events (`audit`), and a final settle that serves every lease request: 10,293 grants, 14,706 tickets, 1,589 drains, 494 benchmark leases (84 events past a contaminated drain), 51 yields, 1,934 busy refusals and 3,169 expiries over the 500, in 0.75 s; and the constructed cases (priority then round-robin then FIFO; an idle lobby lease's slot lent and given back within one capped step, `queue_s` that step, and the time cap; expiry; cancelling a queued and a running ticket, its drain freed by the late frame or its bound; a model not loaded refused unless swaps or a lease's model; quiet hours with `retry_at`; the ETA rising with position; a consumer capped at 1; the reservation; the drain and its contaminated bound; disabled and offline machines; the helper's ceiling; the busy hold; a ticket's steps). (2) `NoBusyWait`: three `busy` refusals, no `time.sleep` from the stepping thread, one event metered, done once frames free slots. (3) `tests/test_scheduler_e2e.py` `Draining`: a real server, a real helper (the worker agent in a thread, its completions by the OpenAI-compatible provider against the QA script's scripted endpoint at 20 s each): the cancelled ticket's helper logs the `CANCEL`, its slot drains, the next request reaches the endpoint only after the first completion ended, one late frame, no drain timed out, no `busy` anywhere (23.6 s). (4) `OneSlot`: two lobby games on `sv_gpu` (one slot) play five rounds with no overlapping step among the listener's events, `max_in_flight` 1, no busy refusal, every turn ended by `end_turn`; seat A opens a trade and waits, seat B's model accepts on the slot A lends, A's turn ends in seconds by `end_turn`; with a person as seat B, a test ticket started while A waits gets A's slot (`borrowing`) and answers the chat. `GrantCap`: a member's lease carries her grant's concurrency 1. (5) `Metering`: an honest helper's `gen_s` equals its duration (at most the observed time); one reporting ten times plus five seconds is credited the observed time, flagged on every step, and the row gets `overreports` (count 3) and one audit row; output tokens capped at `max_tokens` 5 with `capped_tokens`; of two requests on one slot the second's `queue_s` above 0.3 s; a game's turn on the helper machine has `model_s` and `queue_s`. `Speed`: generation 3 s with 1,500 s queued scores 100. (6) `TimeAccounting` (fake clock): 5 steps of 180 s queued and 12 s generated plus a 600 s chat wait, `max_turn_seconds` 1,200: `end_turn`, `queue_s` 900, `model_s` 60. (7) `Locks`: `acquire` under a session lock raises; `suspend`, `update_seat` and `_crashed` cancel a waiting conversation under the lock in under 0.5 s (it ends with `Cancelled`); a status callback taking a session lock completes while another thread holds that lock and takes the machine's (5 s bound); a failing callback is logged and the notifier carries on and is started again. (8) P3.9.7: an acquire-release pair with nothing queued 7.8 to 8.1 µs (median of 1,000; the limit 1 ms); the session's overhead 2.316, 2.413 and 2.359 (median 2.359; the limit 2.87). `python scripts/qa_phase3.py queue`: 9 of 9 in 34.4 s (`C:/dev/target/p-3-07-qa-queue`): two lobby games on a member's one-slot helper machine, 28 completions, 0 overlapping, no `busy` line in the helper's log, 2 leases; the test given up after 2.02 s is cancelled, the slot drains (1 in flight, 1 draining), the helper finishes it at 8.01 s and the resumed game's next completion reaches the endpoint at 8.02 s with 5.61 s more queue time, one late frame, no drain timeout. `accounts` still 95 of 95. (9) S1 995 tests OK in 278 s (958 at 3-06's merge); S2 ruff clean; S3 200 routes, 172 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded; S4 37 files, 169 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict`, the wiki regenerated; S6 34 modules parse as ES modules, 17 Node tests pass. CI-only: test.yml on its three OSes.
- **Left for later packages.** 3-08: leases made at registration and released on elimination, game over and the make-way pause; `occupied()` from `scheduler.leases`; `drain_s`/`contaminated` into the job's result; reports and probe runs onto bindings. 3-09: tickets admitted at creation with the `pooled` purpose (and `in_flight=None`), the grant's concurrency for tickets from admission, `/api/queue` and the ticket cancel route over `Scheduler.status()`-like data. 3-10: a ledger listener (`StepEvent` has every field `compute_events` needs). 3-11: `on_status` on leases and tickets for `negotiation_status`. 3-18: `machine_settings` gains the CPU limits, its routes call `Scheduler.refresh`, and the Servers page shows `flags.overreports` to administrators.
- **The fix round** (the review's five majors and eight minors, all fixed; 2026-10-12, from 04:56 to 05:41 (-04:00), on the same branch; each fix's test fails with that fix reverted, ten mutations in all). (1) **No lease after a game ends.** `stop` and `_crashed` released the leases, but a driver or a chat's responder that had looked at the game just before could make the seat's agent, and with it a lease nothing released: in a benchmark game that lease refused every pooled prompt on the machine ("held by a benchmark") until a restart. Releasing every seat's lease now closes them for good (`_leases_closed`): `lease_for` returns None once the game has stopped or crashed, `get_agent` gives a model seat no agent then, and the driver looks again. `lease_for` also reads the grant's concurrency and the machine's row before it takes the leases' lock, which `release_leases` takes under the session's, and a seat's new model on the same machine follows it (`update_seat` releases the lease when the model changes; a reused lease takes the model, `Lease.set_model`). (2) **A drain ends with its connection.** A v1 helper answers only over the socket a request went out on, so a slot drained across a helper's restart stayed taken for the request's whole timeout (a turn's, up to half an hour). Each `WorkerConnection` now has a `serial`, `helper_state` reports it, an unfinished request carries it, and `apply_helper` gives back the drains of a connection that disconnected or was replaced (`drains_dropped`); a request whose connection went while its caller unwound never drains. A helper still finishing such a request answers `busy`, which the fallback absorbs. (3) **A busy answer to a request let through** (the helper thought offline, a connection past its liveness that revived with no push) no longer loops: lease requests of a machine of unknown capacity are held while a `busy` hold lasts, the answer reads the helper's state again, and the request then waits for a real slot (a request put back is no longer marked pass-through when it gets one, which had left its slot taken). (4) **The Servers page's test is bounded per caller.** As a queued ticket it had lost the hub's back-pressure: each waiting test holds a worker thread and a database connection for up to its `wait`, and one member could queue any number. Now one test of a machine per caller (`Scheduler.ticket(one_per_consumer=)`, code `duplicate`), two waiting in all (`too_many`), at most 20 in ten minutes (`ratelimit` rule `server_test`, 429), each refused at once; the request's database connection goes back to the pool before the wait; and the test is metered under no activity (its `test:<id>` activity had no row, so costing reported it as unknown). (5) **Route tests** (`tests/test_scheduler_routes.py`, P3.9.1): the test route by a visitor, another member, a moderator, the owner, a member her grant reaches and an administrator (a connected helper with no socket), `wait` out of bounds, a model not loaded, the per-caller bounds, the drain after a cancel and across a helper's disconnect and reconnect through the hub; `/api/status`'s machines for an administrator only. (6) **The seat's wait is shown again**, as the busy branch's note was: `ScheduledConversation.on_wait`, called on the stepping thread with no lock held, sets a seat mid-turn to `queued` with its machine, place and ETA, and back to `thinking` (on that thread, so it never races the driver's own status writes); the game screen says "waiting for Test GPU box: 1st in line, about 10 s", the phone site and the benchmark page "waiting for its machine", and the benchmark scheduler's grace counts it as mid-turn (`benchmarks.MID_TURN`). (7) **The suite's hygiene.** `test_scheduler_e2e`'s server start no longer leaves the host's power sampler running (its `nvidia-smi` calls failed `test_seat_blocks`' outbound watch on this laptop) or the benchmark scheduler (`start_power_sampler` patched out; the runners put back); `NoBusyWait` counts only the stepping thread's sleeps; the expected busy and contaminated warnings are captured (`assertLogs`), so S1's log fell from 2,481 lines to 636; `test_scheduler` plays its sessions on a database of its own (`setUpModule`), which it had found only by the order of modules. Dead code went (`_ALWAYS_LOADED`, `Machine.flagged`). The QA queue part gained a check: her second tab's test of the machine while the first waits is refused at once. **Gates rerun:** (1) 500 seeds, the same totals (10,293 grants, 14,706 tickets, 1,589 drains, 494 benchmark leases) in 0.92 s, plus the new constructed cases; (2) `NoBusyWait`; (3)-(5) the e2e module; (6) `TimeAccounting`; (7) `Locks`; (8) acquire-release 7.8, 7.9 and 7.9 us; the session's overhead 2.102, 2.133 and 2.151 (median 2.133; the limit 2.87); `qa_phase3.py queue` 10 of 10 in 36 s, `accounts` 95 of 95; (9) S1 1010 tests OK in 286 s (995 before the round) (the scheduler's, the routes' and the session's modules also on `TZ=UTC0`); S2 ruff clean; S3 200 routes, 172 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded; S4 37 files, 169 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict`, WORKERS, API and ARCHITECTURE updated and the wiki regenerated; S6 34 modules parse as ES modules, 17 Node tests pass. CI-only: test.yml on its three OSes.

**As built in 3-08: leases for the session's seats: occupancy, benchmarks, probe and report leases** (P3.3.3, P3.3.5; Appendix D). One agent, 2026-10-12, from 06:01 to 06:58 (-04:00), 57 minutes, on branch `p-3-08` from `1b4bbff`. No Rust changed.

- **Leases at registration.** `GameSession.start` makes the leases (`open_leases`: every living model seat with a `server_id`), so every registered session holds its machines from then on, whoever moves first: created (`create`, the create route), launched from a scenario (`register_new` then `start`), loaded paused (`_register_loaded`), brought back by `restore_live` and reloaded by a benchmark (both through `load`). A benchmark game is started only once `benchmark` is set, so its leases are `benchmark` ones from the first. Released: per seat on a type, machine or model change (`update_seat` then makes the new lease at once, off the lock), on elimination (the engine's `eliminated` event, `_on_event`; no lease is made for that seat again), for good on game over (`victory` and `game_over` events), stop and crash; all of them on a make-way pause (`suspend` with kind `queue`), made again on `resume` or `set_paused(False)`; every other pause keeps them. A caller holding the game's lock (the control route's `set_paused`, a test's `update_seat`) cannot make a lease there (it reads the database), so `_want_leases` hands it to the driver, which makes it at its next look. `get_agent` gives a model seat no agent once its lease cannot be made (ended, stopped, crashed, eliminated).
- **Occupancy is the leases.** `pool.seats.occupied()` lists the scheduler's live leases on a machine, one label per lease (`held_text` names each holder once in a sentence), and `occupants()` their queue kind and id (`Lease.ref`: the game, its benchmark run, the probe run, the report) and session (`Lease.game`, for `exclude`); `claim()` and `release()` are gone. The benchmark scheduler's job rule (`busy + len(occupied(..., exclude=ours)) >= limit`) is therefore "fewer leases than its limit"; probe runs and reports wait on the same list. `usage.session_servers` lists the machines of the game's live leases (`GameSession.leased_machines`), and nothing while it is paused or over, as before.
- **Benchmarks.** A benchmark lease's `on_status` hears a new `drained` status when its first request goes ahead (`drain_s`, `contaminated`); the session adds the wait to `benchmark["drain_s"]` (summed over the job's leases: its start, a reload, a resume after making way; `drains` counts them) and ORs `contaminated`, under its lock on the notifier thread. `game_progress` puts both in the job's progress and result, so the game's saves carry them too; the run page shows "drained 6s" or "timing contaminated" under the job's turn time. `scoring.compare_models` leaves a contaminated job's model seat out of every timing aggregate (`avg_turn_s`, `max_turn_s`, `avg_model_s`, `avg_queue_s`, tokens per second; `timed_turns` and `contaminated_games` are new), so `model_speed` never reads it; a model with no cleanly timed turn has no speed score and its overall score is made of performance and reliability, re-weighted. The Servers page's test cancelled by a benchmark answers `code` `benchmark`.
- **Probe and report leases.** A probe run holds one non-lending lease (kind `probe_case`, label `probe run “…”`, ref `("probe", id)`) from after its wait for a free machine to its end (`ProbeRunner._lease`), and every case's subject plays under it (`GameSession.adopt_lease`: the case's session never releases it). A report whose narrative a model writes holds a non-lending lease of the new kind `report` while it is built (`ReportRunner._lease`), and the narrative goes through `make_conversation` on it (`reports.complete(cfg, system, prompt, binding)`), so it waits for its slot and is metered by the usage listener; `_complete_on_worker`'s polling is gone. Plain completions now work through every provider: the OpenAI-compatible and Anthropic ones send no empty tool list, and the dry run answers a prompt with no tools with its narrative text.
- **Choices the design left open, and departures.** (1) A game opened only to be looked at holds no machine: a finished, failed or cancelled benchmark job's game (`open_job_game`) and a probe case's end state (`probe_run_open`) are loaded with `leases=False` (`holds_machines`): a cancelled job's benchmark lease would otherwise refuse every pooled prompt on the machine while somebody watched its recap. Such a game resumed by a person still plays its model, its lease made on first use. (2) A lease made at registration, before the game had its owner (the load route gives a save of an ownerless game to the member loading it), takes the owner and her grant's cap when the seat next asks for it. (3) The probe run's lease is of kind `probe_case`, not a new kind: it is what 3-09 gives each turn case. (4) `usage.session_servers` keeps "a paused game holds nothing", which costing has always read. (5) The QA harness gained `Server.restart` (a stop as a crash, the log kept as `server-<n>.log`; a venv's `python.exe` on Windows is a launcher whose server goes a moment later, so it waits for the log and the port) and `Account.reconnect`. (6) `tests/test_worker.py`'s bare probe runner removes the made-up runs it writes: they have no probe, so in another module order the Probes page's listing failed on them.
- **Gates.** (1) and (2) `tests/test_session_leases.py` (16 tests, 10 s): a game loaded paused, one brought back by `restore_live` (its save folder patched to a temporary one) and one whose bots move first (the driver resting 5 s after each bot's turn) are each listed by `occupied()` before the model seat has an agent, and a one-job benchmark on their machine stays queued naming them; a make-way pause empties `occupied()` and the resume (and a person's resume under the lock, through the driver) brings it back, while a person's, a quiet-hours and a disconnect pause keep it; an eliminated seat's lease goes and none is made again, game over closes every lease and the model seat gets no agent; `update_seat` to another machine moves the lease at once, to a bot drops it, back makes it, and under the lock the driver makes it; a scenario launch's and a benchmark reload's leases (the reload's a `benchmark` lease while paused), a cancelled job's watched game holding none; `occupied()` equal to the scheduler's leases through a two-seat game, a report's and a probe run's leases, `exclude`, a seat change and the close; a job on a two-slot machine waits behind two leases and starts beside one; a real probe run (two cases on `sv_gpu`) holding one lease for the run with every model step metered as `probe_case` under `probe:<id>`; a report holding its `report` lease while its narrative is written, one step metered as `report`; `session_servers` following the leases (a seat changed to a bot, a make-way pause, a seat moved, a game opened to be looked at). `test_items_access`: the Queue page's `busy_with` names the owner's two-seat game once to her and an administrator, "someone's game" to another member, 401 to a visitor. (3) A one-job benchmark started while a test ticket's request is in flight on a four-slot machine cancels the ticket (`code` `benchmark`), its request drains, and the late frame 0.6 s after the job's first request began to wait gives a `drain_s` between 0.5 and 10 s and `contaminated` false; with the bound at 0.8 s and no late frame, `contaminated` true (the warning captured), kept in the game's `benchmark`, and with the seat counted as a real model's the model has no speed score (`timed_turns` 0, `contaminated_games` 1) and its overall score is performance and reliability re-weighted; a second, clean job gives it a speed score from that job alone with both games counted for performance. `SpeedScoreLeavesContaminatedTimingsOut` checks the aggregates on made-up games. Eleven mutations, each reverting one of these (leases at registration, release on elimination, on game over, on making way, the new lease after `update_seat`, the drain in the result, contaminated timings, the probe case's adoption, the owner's cap, `session_servers` on leases, a watched cancelled job's game), each fail their tests. (4) `python scripts/qa_phase3.py queue`: 19 of 19 in 56 s (`C:/dev/target/p-3-08-qa-queue`): 3-07's ten, then a lobby game whose person moves first holds its lease before its model is asked anything; the server killed and started again brings it back with its lease and the member's Queue page shows the machine in use by it; a one-job benchmark waits for it; the member's test runs on the slot the idle game lends, the game is closed, the job starts 1.2 s later and cancels the test (`code` `benchmark`), its first request waits for the helper to finish the test (`drain_s` 11.1, not contaminated), the benchmark's first completion reaches the endpoint 0.02 s after the test's ends, one late frame, no drain timeout, no busy refusal, no lease left. `accounts` still 95 of 95. (5) S1 1027 tests OK in 296 s (1010 at 3-07's merge); the touched modules also on `TZ=UTC0` (173 OK); S2 ruff clean; S3 200 routes, 172 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded; S4 37 files, 169 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict`, BENCHMARKS, WORKERS, ARCHITECTURE and API updated and the wiki regenerated; S6 34 modules parse as ES modules, 17 Node tests pass. P3.9.7: the session's overhead 2.409, 2.304 and 2.240 (median 2.304; the limit 2.87); the scheduler's acquire-release pair 8.2 us. CI-only: test.yml on its three OSes.
- **Left for later packages.** 3-09: report narratives and probe offer and message cases become tickets (the `report` lease kind and the run-long probe lease go then), turn cases take `probe_case` leases of their own, admission by task class. Pre-existing and not changed: a probe case's saved copy (`GameSession.save_copy` of a game started from a scenario) logs "This game's history did not come back whole" for every case of a run.
- **The fix round** (the review's one major and five minors, all fixed; 2026-10-12, from 07:17 to 08:13 (-04:00), 56 minutes, on the same branch; seventeen mutations, each reverting one fix, each fail their tests, and the first round's eleven still do). (1) **A made-way game's chat answer left a lease.** A person can chat with the model seat of a game paused to make way; its answer made the seat's lease again on first use and nothing released it, so `occupied()` listed the game while it still made way, and the work it made way for waited for the game while the game's watcher waited for that work. Now the answer runs on a lease of its own (`lease_for(answering=True)`, `get_agent(answering=True)` from `_run_responder`), released with its agent when the seat's last answer ends (`_answered`; a bot's answer holds no lease and keeps its agent), and while the game makes way `lease_for` and `get_agent` give the driver nothing (checked again under the leases' lock, since a pause can land while `lease_for` reads the grant). (2) **An agent made across a release.** `get_agent` cached an agent bound to a lease released while it was made (a benchmark's preemption on its own thread, a seat change), so the seat's later turns failed one after another. Now `_llm_agent` keeps the agent only if, under the game's lock, the seat still plays the block it was made from and its lease is still the seat's and live (`suspend`, `update_seat` and `_crashed` change, cancel and release in one hold of that lock), and makes it again from the seat as it is otherwise (three tries); a cached agent whose lease was released is made again. (3) **A game opened to be looked at held a machine after a restart.** The live mark keeps `leases` (`holds_machines`), and `restore_live` and `_reopen` load with it. (4) **A report on an offline helper lost its narrative.** `ReportRunner._blocked` keeps a report queued while its narrative's helper is offline ("<machine>'s helper is offline", holding nothing), and `_complete_waiting` rides out a `WorkerConnectionError` (a helper that drops mid-build) for up to `RECONNECT_SECONDS` (1,800, the old polling's bound), trying again once the helper is back and never sooner than a second; any other failure fails at once. (5) **Lease caps by purpose.** A seat lease's grant cap is read for the purpose its game was admitted for (`GameSession.CAP_PURPOSES`: `benchmark`, `probe` for `probe_case`, else `game`), and the report lease's for `reports.NARRATIVE_PURPOSE` (moved from `reports_api`). The review's single-grant example does not occur (a purpose refusal still carries its grant, so `consumer_cap` read its concurrency anyway); the difference shows when a member holds one grant for games and another for benchmarks on the machine, which the test sets up. (6) **Waiting notes per viewer.** A benchmark job's, a probe run's and a report's waiting note, and a queue item's, are kept as `pool.seats.note` (words and holders: `holders`, `queue_holder`), stored as text that names no holder ("someone's game"), and told to each caller with the names they may see by the routes (`citar/server/work_access.py`: `access_to`, the Queue page's old `_queue_access`; `namer`; `with_waiting`) in `GET /api/benchmarks/runs`, `/api/probe-runs/{id}`, `/api/reports`, `/api/reports/{id}` (its "Waiting: ..." progress too) and `/api/queue`. A made-way game's pause and a run's log name the work ahead by its kind only (`kind_text`: "making way on Test GPU box for a probe run (priority 5)"), since everybody who may watch the game or see the run reads them; the probe run's live panel says "(waiting: the machine is in use)". **Tests** (`tests/test_session_leases.py`, 16 to 29): `MadeWay` (a person's chat answered while made way: the answer's requests metered on a `game` lease, then no lease and no agent, a queued benchmark starts, the resume makes the lease again; a bot's answer keeps its agent; the driver gets no agent and no lease, nor the answer's; a pause landing while a lease is made leaves none; an agent made across a make-way pause is not kept and the seat's next turn ends by `end_turn` on its new lease; one made across a seat change, to another machine or only new settings, is made again from the seat as it is; a cached agent whose lease was released is made again; the pause names the work ahead by its kind), `LookedAtAfterARestart`, `GrantPurposes` (grants of concurrency 3 for games and 1 for benchmarks: the benchmark game's lease 1, the lobby game's 3, and a lease made before its owner takes the benchmark cap), `ReportOnHelper` (queued while the helper is offline, holding nothing, built once it is back; two dropped tries then the narrative; the bound; a refusal not retried); `test_items_access` `test_what_queued_work_waits_for_names_only_what_the_caller_may_see` (the owner's private game on the dry run; the stranger's queued job, probe run and report: the stranger told "someone's game" by every route and the Queue page, an administrator the name); `test_worker` and `test_costs` read the notes. **Gates rerun:** (1)-(3) `test_session_leases` 29 tests; (4) `qa_phase3.py queue` 19 of 19 in 55.5 s (`C:/dev/target/p-3-08-fix-qa-queue`; the member's job names her own restored game through the runs route), `accounts` 95 of 95; S1 1041 tests OK in 368 s (1027 before the round); S2 ruff clean; S3 200 routes, 172 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded; S4 37 files, all internal links resolve; S5 `mkdocs build --strict`, WORKERS, BENCHMARKS, REPORTS, API and ARCHITECTURE updated and the wiki regenerated; S6 34 modules parse as ES modules, 17 Node tests pass. P3.9.7: the session's overhead 1.709, 1.560 and 1.732 (median 1.709; the limit 2.87; session medians 0.85 to 0.88 s, the drive alone 0.49 to 0.54 s against the first round's 0.31 s, the laptop warm after the suite); the scheduler's acquire-release pair 8.3 us. CI-only: test.yml on its three OSes.

**As built in 3-09: pooled work: admission, task classes, reports and probes on tickets, the Queue page** (P3.3.3, P3.3.5, P3.3.7, P3.3.8; Appendix D). One agent, 2026-10-12, from 08:39 to 10:00, 81 minutes (-04:00), on branch `p-3-09` from `92777ae`. No Rust changed.

- **Admission by task class.** `pool.seats.seat_class(seat)` is the one classifier (a dict or a session's `Seat`): `llm` is *full*, `hybrid` *partial* (3-14's seat), any other seat nothing, whatever block it carries; `MODEL_SEAT_TYPES` (`app.MODEL_SEATS` is it) and `CLASS_NEEDS` (`full`: `full_llm` and a grant for `game`; `partial`: `partial_llm` and `pooled`). `authorize(session, user, seats)` checks each classified seat's capability (`policy.denial`, the friendly 403 text moved from `deps`) and admits the machine its block names for its class's purpose; `admit(session, user, ids, purpose)` (runs, a report's narrative, a probe run's subject) adds `partial_llm` for `pooled` (`PURPOSE_CAPABILITY`). `create_game`, scenario launches and `update_seat` (every new block or seat made a model seat) go through it. `admission.PURPOSES` gains `pooled` ("pooled prompts"), and the Servers page's grant dialog offers it. Every admission now passes `in_flight=None` (seats, the Servers page's listings and a machine's detail, the lobby's availability): a busy machine is never a refusal; `admission.check` keeps the step for a caller that gives a count. `pool.seats.ticket(...)` makes a pooled prompt's ticket admitted when it is made: the consumer's `partial_llm`, the work's own capability when its maker names one (`run_reports`, `run_probes`), a grant for `pooled` in its window and budget, a helper that is offline refusing at once (temporary); a refusal is the ticket's `refused` with the admission's reason, `code` and `retry_at` (aware UTC turned into the scheduler's naive local time), a machine the consumer may not see refused as "You may not use that machine."; an admitted ticket's `cap` is the grant's concurrency. No consumer (the server's own work), no machine or no database: no admission.
- **The scheduler's side.** A `Ticket` carries `ref` (the work it is part of, as the shared queue names it), `queue_priority` and `created`; `Scheduler.ticket(..., ref=, refusal=)`; `set_priority(ref, value)` moves every queued or running ticket of that work (the priority route calls it, so tickets follow their activity's priority); `Machine.view()`/`Scheduler.views()` give the Queue page each machine's capacity, leases (active, lending, waiting) and running and waiting tickets in the rules' order with position, ETA (`_line`, which `_positions` now uses too), waited and the time left to start; `find_ticket`, `models_changed` (a direct machine CITAR just loaded a model on). The `report` lease kind is gone (`LEASE_KINDS`: game, benchmark, probe_case).
- **Report narratives are `report` tickets.** `ReportRunner` builds each queued report on a thread of its own, four at once (`MAX_BUILDS`), best queue priority then oldest, gathering and rendering one at a time (`_cpu`); reports left `pool.queue` (no `queue_items`, `running_items`, `_blocked`, `_next`, `_lease`, `_current`). The narrative's ticket (`_ticket`): the report's owner its consumer, admitted for `pooled` with `run_reports`, its report's priority, `wait_for_benchmark`, one step, a 30-minute start deadline (`START_SECONDS`); a refusal that says "not yet" (offline helper, quiet hours, a grant's window) is waited out within that deadline unless it is known to clear later (then skipped at once), anything else skips the narrative with the reason (`NarrativeRefused`: "The narrative was skipped: The model is not loaded on ..."); a helper that drops mid-request is waited for (a new ticket) up to `RECONNECT_SECONDS` from the first drop. The model is loaded first only on a machine that allows swaps (`_load_model`; P3.10 #14). While its ticket waits the report's progress is its place in line ("Waiting for Test GPU box: 2nd in line, about 20 s"), kept in memory from the ticket's status (`_places`, overlaid by `meta`/`list`), never written from the notifier thread. `NARRATIVE_PURPOSE` is `pooled`.
- **Probes.** Each answer of an offer or message case is a `probe` ticket (`probes._answer`): admitted for `pooled` with `run_probes`, its run's priority and ref, `wait_for_benchmark`, `steps` the agent's negotiation calls, at most an hour to start (`PROBE_START_SECONDS`, temporary refusals retried within it); the subject's agent is made for prompts (`GameSession.prompt_agent`, bound to no lease) and given each answer's ticket; a refusal is the case's `error` ("The prompt was not asked: ..."). A turn case plays on a `probe_case` lease of its own for the case, named for its run (`GameSession.lease_ref`/`lease_label`), released when the case's game stops. The run-long lease (`ProbeRunner._lease`, `GameSession.adopt_lease` and `_adopted`) is gone. A probe run waits at the job level only for a benchmark that holds its machine (`_benchmarks`, `_machine_busy`, and `_wait_benchmark` before each turn case) and for work ranked ahead of it; a lobby game no longer holds it up (its prompts run on the slots the game's seat lends). Stopping a run cancels the ticket its answer waits on (`run_case(on_ticket=)`, `ProbeRunner.asking`, kept out of the live panel the routes serve). `probes.case_purposes(probe, cases)` is what `POST /api/probes/{id}/run` admits the subject for: `probe` for turn cases, `pooled` for offer and message cases.
- **The Servers page's test** is admitted for `pooled` with `partial_llm` (its refusal answers `code`).
- **The Queue page.** `GET /api/queue` lists only the machines the caller may view (`pool.visible`; every machine for an administrator), counting the other machines in use in `others_busy` and naming none; per machine `capacity`, `in_flight`, `draining`, `busy_with`, the job queue's `waiting` and `running` as before, its `leases` and its `prompts` (`running`, `waiting`), each told per viewer by `work_access.binding_rights`/`told_lease`/`told_ticket`: the work's label and member (handle) when the caller may see the work (their own prompt, or `access_to` its `ref`), else "someone's <kind>" or "someone's <class>" with no member; `can_manage` (MANAGE on the work) with its `ref`, `can_cancel` (the work's manager, the prompt's own member for a prompt of no work, the machine's MANAGE, administrators) with the ticket's `id`. `POST /api/queue/tickets/{id}/cancel` (new): 404 for no such prompt and for a caller who cannot see it, 403 for one who can but is none of those, an audit row `queue.prompt_cancelled`. `queue.js` shows the seats that play every turn, the jobs and the "Pooled prompts" (place, class, model, priority buttons where the caller may move the work, waited, ETA and start deadline, Cancel where they may), each table scrolling sideways at phone width, and "n other machines are busy".
- **Choices the design left open, and departures.** (1) Every pooled prompt needs `partial_llm`, as its capability's description says ("use pooled prompts"), besides the work's own capability that P3.3.7 names (a report's narrative `run_reports`, a probe run `run_probes`). (2) One ticket per answer of an offer or message case, not one per case: a case's rounds (the script's follow-ups) take turns with other members' prompts, and each answer's steps are the agent's own bound. (3) A probe run no longer waits for a lobby game's lease, only for a benchmark's: gate 5's probe cases run beside a lobby game. (4) Reports are built several at once: a narrative waiting up to half an hour for its machine must not hold up a report without one, and two members' narratives must be able to queue together to be served by priority and turns. (5) The lobby's and the Servers page's admission listings skip the capacity step too: a busy machine is not "unavailable". (6) The QA harness's fake endpoint can hold completions (`hold`/`gate`) and describes each request (`kind`, `head`, `marker`); `--hold-line` keeps the queue part's line waiting for the browser checklist. (7) Tests that described 3-08's leases for reports and probe runs moved to `tests/test_pooled_work.py` on tickets (`test_session_leases`' report and probe tests, its `ReportOnHelper`; `test_worker`'s probe runner tests; `test_costs`' report queue test; `test_items_access`' waiting-notes test, whose probe run now waits for the owner's benchmark). `test_scheduler_routes`' one-test-at-a-time case lets 50 ms pass before the held answer: answered within a millisecond of his arrival, bob's queue time read 0.0 once in a combined run (rounded to the millisecond by the route).
- **Gates.** (1) `tests/test_pooled_work.py` `Classifier`, `AdmissionByClass` and `UpdateSeat`: the classifier on dicts and `Seat`s of every type; gina (a grant for games) gets no ticket ("limited to ordinary games, and this is pooled prompts"), carol (no grant) "You may not use that machine.", bob, dave, the owner, an administrator and the server's own prompt do; `partial_llm` off refuses (`capability`); seats admitted by class and capability (a bot seat's block never); with the one slot taken a ticket queues and starts when it frees; dave's grant of concurrency 1 is his tickets' cap and of eight of his steps sent at once on a two-slot machine beside bob's none overlapped (the listener's events) while the machine ran two; `update_seat` to carol's machine and, for dave, to alice's (pooled only) 403 with the seat as it was, gina's to alice's 200. `ServersPageTest`: the test for dave, refused for gina (`purpose`) and without `partial_llm` (`capability`). (2) `Reports`: a seat mid-turn on the one-slot `sv_gpu`: the narrative waits in line, no narrative step starts while the seat is active, it starts borrowing once the seat idles, and the seat's next request waits for that one step (`queue_s` above 0, at most the step plus a second, under the report cap); a benchmark lease: the narrative waits and starts after its release; no report lease ever (the kinds sampled through the builds); four narratives of two members behind a seat: dave's raised one first, then turns (`dave 2, bob 1, dave 1, bob 2`); a report raised before its ticket is made carries the priority. `ReportOnHelper`: an offline helper waited for holding nothing, built once connected; two drops ridden out on new tickets, never in a tight loop; the bound; a refusal not retried; a model the helper has not loaded skips the narrative with the reason. (3) `Probes`: a run on `sv_gpu` (offer, message, turn) with bob as its owner: no lease during the offer and message cases, the turn case's `probe run “…”` lease during it and gone after, events `probe`/partial and `probe_case`/full metered to `probe:<id>` for bob, outcomes accept, reply and end_turn as the dry run gives them (`tests/test_editor.py`'s `ProbeTests` unchanged and passing); gina's answers refused with the reason, dave's answered; a stopped run cancels the answer waiting behind a benchmark and ends within seconds, its status carrying no ticket; `case_purposes`. (4) `QueuePage`: bob sees alice's machine (capacity 1, in flight 1), her game's lease as someone's game, the line test 1, someone's report 2, his report 3 with positions, waited, ETA and start deadline, his rows' buttons only, carol's private machine counted (`others_busy` 1) and named nowhere; alice (the machine's owner) sees bob's report and test as someone's, hers to cancel; ray (who may read bob's report) sees it named, not his to move or cancel; carol and the moderator do not see alice's machine; an administrator sees every name; priority: alice 404, ray 403, carol 404, a visitor 401, bob 200 and his ticket moved to the front, an administrator on alice's 200; cancel: carol, the moderator, bob on alice's, dave on bob's test 404, ray 403, a visitor 401, an unknown id 404, alice (the machine's owner), bob (his own) and an administrator 200, three audit rows. `test_items_access`'s route table names the cancel route. Sixteen mutations, each reverting one behaviour of this package, each fail their tests (`C:/dev/target/p-3-09-mutate.log`). (5) `python scripts/qa_phase3.py queue`: 29 of 29 in 70 s (`C:/dev/target/p-3-09-qa-queue`): 3-07's ten and 3-08's nine as before, then 3-09's ten: alice's one-slot helper machine shared with bob for pooled prompts and probes only; his game seat on it refused for its purpose (403); her lobby game's seat holding the slot mid-turn while bob's probe run (two message cases), her probe run and four reports (two each) queue, the line B2 (his, raised by his own button), A1, A2, B1, his probe (lowered) with positions 1 to 5, her report not his to move (404); his, her and the moderator's Queue pages as gate 4 says; released, served B2, A1, B1, A2, his two probe answers, then her probe run's (priority, then turns between members, then order), 11 of the game's turns and 7 prompts with none starting inside a turn, a turn's longest queue 0.43 s (one 0.4 s pooled step), no lease but the game's seen while they ran, 37 grants and no busy refusal, no traceback. With `--hold-line 150` (C1's run, `C:/dev/target/p-3-09-c1-qa`) 29 of 29 again; `accounts` 95 of 95 in 81 s (its shared machine's grant now covers pooled prompts, its test a pooled prompt). Browser C1 (`C:/dev/target/p-3-09-c1-evidence`): taken by headless Chrome during the held line: bob's page (alice's machine, 1 of 1 slot in use, her game's seat mid-turn as someone's game, his three prompts named with priority buttons and Cancel, her two as someone's report with neither, places, waited, ETA and the start deadline), alice's (her game and reports by name, bob's as someone's, Cancel on every row as the machine's owner), the moderator's (no machine named; "1 other machine is busy"), bob's at 390 px (the page fits; both tables scroll inside their card, his three Cancel buttons reachable), and the controls read from his page (priority boxes and Cancel on his rows only). (6) S1 1058 tests OK in 391 s (1041 at 3-08's merge); the touched modules also on `TZ=UTC0` (220 OK in 155 s) and on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` (116 OK); S2 ruff clean; S3 201 routes, 173 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded; S4 37 files, 171 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict`, WORKERS, ACCOUNTS, API, REPORTS, BENCHMARKS and ARCHITECTURE updated and the wiki regenerated; S6 34 modules parse as ES modules, 17 Node tests pass. P3.9.7: the session's overhead 1.923, 1.697 and 1.676 (median 1.697; the limit 2.87); the scheduler's acquire-release pair 7.7 to 8.1 us (22.4 us in S1's run, beside the docs build). CI-only: test.yml on its three OSes.
- **Left for later packages.** 3-10: a ticket's steps reach the listeners as a lease's do (`StepEvent` with its `consumer`, `activity`, class and task class `partial`; the work it is part of is the ticket's `ref`, not in the event); a test ticket's activity is None, as 3-07 left it. 3-11/3-14: chat answers and the diplomat phase are tickets of classes `chat` and `diplomat` made through `pool.seats.ticket` (a hybrid seat is already `seat_class` partial and needs `partial_llm` and `pooled`); give them the game's `ref` so they follow its priority. Pre-existing and not changed: a probe case's saved copy logs "This game's history did not come back whole".
- **The fix round** (the review's three majors and eight minors: ten fixed, one answered in the docs; two more found while rerunning the gates; 2026-10-12, from 10:22 to 11:45 (-04:00), on the same branch; twenty-one mutations, each reverting one fix, each fail their tests (`C:/dev/target/p-3-09-fix-mutate.log`), and the first round's sixteen still do). (1) **Narratives waiting for their machine held up every report.** A build waiting for its narrative counted against `MAX_BUILDS`, so four narratives waiting on an offline helper (or a busy machine, or quiet hours) kept every other member's report queued for up to half an hour. A build in its narrative (`_waiting`) is no longer counted; each member has at most `NARRATIVES_PER_OWNER` (2) narratives under way, their next report with one queued meanwhile; the dispatcher picks by queue priority, then the member with the fewest builds under way, then age. (2) **A probe answer the machine cut off was scored as the model's.** An answer borrowing an idle lobby seat's slot is refused its next step when the seat's turn comes (`yielded`; also `borrow_cap`, or a benchmark's drain), which was recorded as the model's `no_response`. `_answer` now asks it again on a fresh ticket while the negotiation still waits for the subject, up to `PROBE_ANSWER_TRIES` (3); an answer that never ran whole (refused, expired, cut off every time, stopped) is the case's `error`, its outcome `error` (or `stopped`) and not scored (`passed` None), so contention never counts against a model. (3) **A benchmark that took the machine mid-run failed the rest of the run.** An offer or message answer's ticket waited for the benchmark silently and expired after an hour, case after case. A probe's ticket no longer waits for benchmarks: refused because one holds the machine (or cancelled by its drain), the answer waits for it at the job level (`ProbeRunner._wait_machine`, which sets the run's status and waiting note and puts the live panel back after), before every case of a model subject and whenever a benchmark refuses an answer, and its start window begins again after; a case played without a runner tries again within its window. (4) **A skipped narrative said nothing.** The report's Analysis section now shows the note alone when there is no narrative ("The narrative was skipped: The model is not loaded on ..."), and the record keeps `narrative_note` and `narrative_written`, which the Reports list shows. (5) **A report waiting for its machine across a restart failed; one being built could not be deleted.** The runner queues a report the server restarted under again (`restarts`; after `MAX_RESTARTS`, 2, it fails as before, so a build that brings the server down does not do so for ever). `delete` of a report being built hides it at once, cancels its narrative's ticket (`_asking`; one made after the delete is cancelled at once) and removes its files once its build lets go (`_deleting`: `_set` raises `_Deleted`, and the build's thread removes the folder). (6) **The probe run route's admission by its cases** is tested over HTTP (`ProbeRunRoute`: a grant for probes only runs a turn case and is refused an offer or message case naming pooled prompts, one for pooled prompts only the reverse, `partial_llm` off refused with `policy.denial`'s text). (7) **A stop that lands while an answer's ticket is made** (the runner finds nothing to cancel) is noticed by `_answer`, which cancels its own ticket. (8) **A turn case plays only on a free slot.** Before a turn case the run also waits while the machine's leases fill its slots (`_slots_held`, the benchmark job's rule), so its non-lending lease never plays step by step against a lobby game's seat; offer and message cases still run beside a lobby game. (9) **The reports' dispatcher survives a failed pass** (logged, P3.9.2), and a queued report whose record cannot be read is failed with the reason (its title from its specification) rather than tried on every pass. (10) `ProbeRunner._prepare_model` tells the scheduler of the model it loaded (`models_changed`), as the report path does. (11) **Grants limited to games** are not migrated: a 0.1.5 database is set aside on the first start (P3.2.2), so no released grant reaches 0.1.6, and only a database of a Phase 3 development build can hold a grant made while the test and narratives were admitted for `game`; past data is not sacred (the owner's rule). WORKERS.md says that a grant limited to ordinary games does not cover pooled prompts; 3-19's CHANGELOG pass should say it too. (12) **Nothing reaches a helper before its welcome.** S1's first run failed `test_scheduler_e2e`'s Draining once: registering a helper's connection tells the scheduler its machine is online, which let a request waiting for it go before the server had sent the welcome, and the helper ("Unexpected first frame: request") reconnected, failing it; any work waiting when a helper connects met it. A frame sent before the welcome now waits on its connection and follows the welcome in order (`WorkerConnection.welcome`; `test_worker`'s `test_nothing_reaches_a_helper_before_its_welcome`). (13) A helper the hub drops (a revoked token, a disabled machine) whose socket is closed before the socket loop's next receive ends quietly, rather than printing Starlette's "not connected" traceback, which a QA part's "no traceback" check would read as a failure. **Gates rerun:** (1)-(4) `tests/test_pooled_work.py` 35 tests (22 in the first round) and `tests/test_worker.py`'s turn-case and welcome tests; S1 1073 tests OK in 364 s (1058 at the first round's end; its first run here is the Draining failure of (12)); the touched modules also on `TZ=UTC0` (216 OK in 155 s) and on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` (216 OK); S2 ruff clean; S3 201 routes, 173 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded; S4 37 files, 171 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict`, WORKERS, REPORTS, API and ARCHITECTURE updated and the wiki regenerated; S6 34 modules parse as ES modules, 17 Node tests pass. (5) `python scripts/qa_phase3.py queue` 29 of 29 in 70 s (`C:/dev/target/p-3-09-fix-qa-queue`: served B2, A1, B1, A2, his two probe answers, then hers; 11 turns and 7 prompts, none inside a turn; a turn's longest queue 0.43 s; 37 grants, no busy refusal, only the game's lease), `accounts` 95 of 95 in 81 s; C1 again with `--hold-line 150` (29 of 29, `C:/dev/target/p-3-09-fix-c1-qa`; screenshots in `C:/dev/target/p-3-09-fix-c1-evidence`: priority boxes and Cancel on bob's three rows only, at 390 px the page fits and both tables scroll).
