## P3.4 3B: the compute ledger and the ratio

### P3.4.1 Today

The usage ledger (`citar/usage.py`) is JSONL in `saves/usage/`, per activity and machine, with held, busy and CPU seconds and tokens, and no user; `costing.py` prices it by reading every file. Budgets never accrue. Nothing records compute given or used per member.

### P3.4.2 `compute_events`

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `id` | integer, autoincrement |
| `at` | when the work ended (UTC, indexed) |
| `activity` | `game:<id>`, `benchmark_run:<id>`, `probe_run:<id>`, `report:<id>`, `lab:<name>`, `test:<id>` (indexed) |
| `activity_kind` | `game`, `benchmark`, `probe`, `report`, `lab`, `test` |
| `task_class` | `full`, `partial`, `cpu` |
| `consumer_id` | the member whose activity it is (FK users, SET NULL, indexed); null for the server's own data collection |
| `provider_id` | the machine's owner (FK users, SET NULL, indexed); null when the server provides the machine |
| `machine_id` | `servers.id` (indexed) |
| `kind` | `gpu`, `cpu`, `api` |
| `model` | the model that served it, or null |
| `gen_s`, `cpu_core_s` | generation seconds (bounded by what the server observed, P3.3.4); CPU core-seconds |
| `reported_s`, `observed_s` | for a helper's model step: the helper's `Done.duration` and the server's own wall time from request to `Done`; null otherwise |
| `tokens_in`, `tokens_out`, `tokens_reasoning` | from the provider's usage, capped as P3.3.4 says |
| `credits` | priced at write time (P3.4.3) |
| `flags` | JSON: `overreported`, `capped_tokens`, `unpriced` (an `api` step whose model has no token price) |
| `self_use` | true when consumer and provider are the same member, or both are the server |
| `verification` | `unverified` for model work (attributed, never re-run; plan C2), `verified` for the host's CPU; Phase 4 writes `pending` for helpers' CPU jobs |
| `request_id` | the model request, for audit |

- **The consumer** is the activity's owner: the game's `games.owner_id`, the run's, the probe run's, the report's, the experiment's owned item. It is null when the item is marked `collection` (only admins set it: the server's own benchmarks and data gathering), which counts as given by whoever's machine served it.
- **The provider** is the machine's `owner_id`, or null when the machine is `server_resource` (the host; online APIs the site provides).
- **Given(u)** = events with `provider_id = u` and not `self_use`. **Used(u)** = events with `consumer_id = u` and not `self_use`. So a member's own games on their own GPU count on neither side; another member's hybrid prompts on their GPU count as given; their own games on the server's CPU count as used; work on a server machine for the server's own collection counts on neither.

### P3.4.3 Writers, credits and hardware classes

- **Writers.** (1) The scheduler's listener: one `gpu` (or `api`) event per model step. (2) The host's CPU, **measured per unit of work**: `time.thread_time()` deltas around each drive and engine answer on a game's driver thread, each responder task on the responder pools' threads (which serve many sessions in one window, so sampling them would misattribute), each report build and each probe case; the unit's activity is charged directly, and at each flush the usage tracker hands the ledger one `cpu` event per activity for the window, on the `is_host` machine, `provider_id` null. The 5 s thread sampling (`usage.py:_sample`) stays only for a driver thread's time outside those units (save writes and the like), charged to its session. (3) The lab: each game's `cpu_s` in its result (`lab.py:393`) becomes one `cpu` event, the experiment's owner its consumer. (4) Phase 4: helpers' CPU jobs.
- Events go through a ledger writer thread with a queue, inserted in batches (every 2 s or 200 events), never on a game's lock.
- **Credits** = `gen_s × rate` for `gpu`, `cpu_core_s × rate` for `cpu`, and for `api` the step's **token cost** (`costing.price_tokens` with the model's prices in force, in the registry's currency) `× rate`, with the rate of the machine's class of that kind. The default rates for compute are an energy proxy, one credit for roughly 300 W for a second: an unclassified GPU generation-second 1.0, a CPU core-second 0.05 (about 15 W a core). An online API's default is 1,000 credits per unit of currency, roughly the generation time a home GPU spends producing the output a unit of currency buys from an API, so a member who uses the site's paid key is charged for it in the same unit and an admin's budget on that key has something to count. An `api` step whose model has no price is credited 0 and flagged `unpriced`. Admins edit classes and rates (`/api/admin/hardware-classes`); a new rate applies from then on, and `citar admin reprice --since <date>` recomputes the credits of a period when wanted.
- **A machine's class** is the first class of its kind, by priority, whose `match` fits the machine's hardware (`gpu_name` regex, `vram_gb_min`, `cpu_name` regex, `cores_min`), else the kind's default; it is recomputed when the hardware changes, unless an admin pinned it (`class_override`).

### P3.4.4 Who sees what

- `GET /api/ledger/me?range=24h|7d|30d|all`: given and used (GPU seconds, CPU seconds, credits, tokens), the ratio (given credits over used credits; "only given" when nothing was used), by machine and by activity kind.
- `GET /api/users/{handle}/ratio`: the same totals when the account's `ratio` field is visible to the caller (P3.2.6); admins always.
- The admin users page shows each account's 30-day given, used and ratio; the Account page gets a "Compute" card; a profile shows the ratio line under the field's rule.
- Ratio-based restrictions later: an admission rule may read `ledger.ratio(user)` and set capabilities; nothing does in 0.1.6.

### P3.4.5 Rollups

`compute_rollups(user_id, hour, side, kind)` with sums of `gen_s`, `cpu_core_s`, `credits`, `tokens_in`, `tokens_out` and `events`, keyed by all four. A rollup thread recomputes, every 5 minutes, each hour touched since its last pass (delete and insert per hour, one transaction). Reads use the rollups for complete hours and the raw events for the current hour, so a total never waits for the pass.

### P3.4.6 Budgets

- A grant's budget is in credits per period (day, week, month or total, in the owner's time zone as today), hard or soft.
- **Reserve:** when a lease request or a ticket under a grant with a budget starts, `budgets.reserve(budget, activity, estimate)` holds the machine's mean credits per step for that model (at least 1; on an `api` machine, the cost of the request's `max_tokens` of output plus its input at the model's prices, times the class rate). If a hard budget's spent plus reserved would pass its amount, the request is refused with the period's end as `retry_at`: a lease's game pauses with that reason (as for quiet hours, and resumes by itself when the period turns), a ticket is refused and its caller falls back. A hard budget on an `api` machine whose model has no token price refuses every request ("this model has no price, so its budget cannot be kept"), rather than letting unpriced steps through at 0.
- **Settle:** the ledger's listener settles each step's credits into the entry for (budget, activity, period) and releases the reservation. A soft budget only warns: an event to the consumer and a note on the machine owner's Servers page.
- `budgets.refresh_from_ledger` rebuilds a period's entries from `compute_events`: the admin's repair.

### P3.4.7 What costing reads

`costing.py` keeps pricing each machine by its configuration over time (electricity, depreciation, leases, token prices). Per activity and minute it now reads generation seconds, tokens and the host's CPU seconds from `compute_events`, so queue wait is no longer charged as work; held time and power samples stay in the JSONL ledger, which keeps its activity, `held` and power rows and stops writing `busy`, tokens and requests in spans.

**As built in 3-10: the compute ledger: events, credits, rollups, budgets, the ratio** (P3.4; Appendix D). One agent, 2026-10-12, from 12:11 to 13:38 (-04:00), 87 minutes, on branch `p-3-10` from `2802103`. No Rust changed. Not split: budgets and costing are in this package.

- **`citar/ledger.py`.** One `Ledger` per process (`ledger.get()`; the prompt scheduler's listener, `ledger.listener`, which `scheduler.get()` adds in place of the usage tracker's, gone with `Tracker.llm`). A model step is **priced when the listener hears it**, on the stepping thread (no lock held) from what the process caches (the machine's row through `servers.machine_facts`, a new no-copy reader; the classes, read again every 5 s; the model's prices): `gpu` its `gen_s` x the class rate, `api` its token cost (`costing.price_tokens`, cache reads and writes included) x the API class's rate, 0 and `unpriced` with no price; its budget hold is settled in memory there and then, and the means per (machine, model) that budgets estimate from move. Events (steps, and `cpu` ones) go through a queue to **one writer thread**, which inserts them in batches (200, or what it has after 2 s; Core `insert`, no ORM objects) with the budget entries they settle in the same transaction, resolving each activity's **consumer** (`owner_of`: a games row's owner, or nobody when the run its `meta.item` names is marked `collection`; a probe run's, report's, lab experiment's or benchmark run's owned item; the scheduler's consumer for an activity with no row, a machine's test) and **provider** (the machine's owner, null for `server_resource`), `self_use` when they are equal (both null included). It survives a failed batch (logged once a minute, the events lost), is started again if it dies, and drains on shutdown (`stop`, from the app's shutdown after the usage tracker's last flush). **Classes**: `class_of` prices by the class an administrator pinned (`class_override`), else `classify` on the machine's hardware (the first non-default class of the kind by priority whose `match` fits: `gpu_name` and `cpu_name` regular expressions, `vram_gb_min`, `cores_min`; else the kind's default), so a machine follows its hardware from the next event; a flush hook writes the computed ids on the row whenever its configuration (hardware) changes (`reclassify`). **Rollups** (`rollup`): a thread recomputes, at the server's start and every 5 minutes, the hours of the events past its watermark (`settings_kv.ledger_rollups`, the last event id included), each hour's rows deleted and inserted in one transaction; `totals` reads rollups for complete hours not touched since and the raw events for the range's first partial hour, the current hour and the touched ones (all raw when more than 200 hours wait), so it equals the raw sums at any moment; `summary`, `breakdown` (by machine, by kind of work, own use; a machine the caller may not see now named only as whose it is), `ratio`, `reprice` (credits of a period again at today's classes, rates and prices, then the hours' rollups), `ingest_lab` (each finished lab game's `cpu_s` as one `cpu` event, `request_id` `lab:<experiment>:<game>`, read from the lab's results by the rollup pass and before a report is gathered: the lab runner is a process of its own without the server's database).
- **The host's CPU per unit of work** (`ledger.cpu_unit(activity)`): `time.thread_time()` around a drive (`_drive`'s `play_turn`), every engine call (`call_tool`, on whatever thread: a person's and an MCP seat's too), each chat answer (`_run_responder`), a report's gathering and rendering, and a probe case (its runner thread); a unit inside another on the same thread is the outer one's. The usage tracker sums the units per activity (`unit_started`/`unit_ended`) and, for a tracked game's driver thread, samples only its time outside them (a thread inside a unit at a sample is left for the next, so a unit straddling a sample counts once); each flush hands the ledger one `cpu` event per activity on the host (`GameSession.cpu_activity`: its usage act, else `game:<id>`, so a bots-only game's CPU is used by its owner too). The usage ledger's spans keep `held` only (P3.4.7); `usage.read` returns the compute events of the range as `work` rows, which `costing.compute` prices beside the held spans (for the activities the usage ledger knows), so a request's wait for its machine is never busy energy; spans written before keep their own work.
- **Budgets** (`citar/pool/budgets.py`): a process `Keeper` holds each budget a binding was made under (`handle`, where the lease or ticket is made: `pool.seats.consumer_terms` for a seat's lease, `_ticket_admission` for a pooled prompt, the test route for its ticket), its period in the owner's zone, the settled spend and the open holds, on an injectable clock; `ScheduledConversation` reserves once the slot is granted (`ledger.estimate`: the mean credits per step, at least 1; on an API the price of `max_tokens` of output plus the input, the last step's or the text's quarter, times the rate), and a hard budget that would pass refuses with `MachineRefused` `budget` (the period's end as `retry_at`, the slot given back), and every request of an API model with no price. `LLMAgent` turns the refusal into `GameSession.pause_for_budget` (kind `budget`, resumed by a watcher when the budget's period turns, as quiet hours are); a ticket's caller falls back as for any refusal. A soft budget's first request past its amount is told to the binding's `on_budget` (a game's log line) and an audit row (`budget.soft_passed`), and the grant's meter on the Servers page says so (`BudgetState.client()['warning']`). `settle_entries` writes one entry per (budget, activity, period); `refresh_from_ledger` rebuilds a period's entries from the events (the grant's subject, its group's machines, its purposes by `ledger.purpose_of`), `citar admin refresh-budgets`; the grant routes read a changed budget again at once. `admission.check` sees the held credits through `budgets.state`.
- **Routes** (`citar/server/ledger_api.py`): `GET /api/ledger/me` (the caller's own), `GET /api/users/{handle}/ratio` (`profiles.require_visible(field="ratio")`: 404 unless the field shows), `GET/POST /api/admin/hardware-classes`, `PUT/DELETE /api/admin/hardware-classes/{id}` (administrators, 404 to members; a kind keeps one default, 409; each change audited, the machines classified again). `/api/admin/users` carries each account's 30 days (`compute_30d`), a profile its `ratio` when the field shows. **Pages**: the Account page's Compute card (range, given and used, the ratio, by machine and by kind, own use), the profile's ratio line, the admin users page's column, a soft budget's warning on a grant (`citar/web/js/compute.js`, pure, with `citar/web/tests/compute.test.mjs`). **CLI**: `citar admin reprice --since [--until]`, `refresh-budgets [--period]`, `set-collection <kind> <ref> on|off`.
- **Choices the design left open, and departures.** (1) **An activity is the usage ledger's id**: `game:<id>` for a benchmark game too (`activity_kind` `benchmark`), `probe:<id>` for a probe run, `lab:<experiment>:<game>`, `report:<id>`, `test:<ticket>`; not P3.4.2's `benchmark_run:<id>`, `probe_run:<id>`, `lab:<name>`: costing joins each game's work to the time it held a machine per game, and a run's or an experiment's events are found by its kind and owner (the owner resolver reads `benchmark_run:`/`probe_run:` ids too). (2) **Revision `0016b_compute_cache_tokens`** on the baseline (with its upgrade test; `test_one_revision_only` became a check that the revisions are one line from the baseline): `tokens_cache_read` and `tokens_cache_write`, which an online API prices apart (Anthropic's cache reads at a tenth of the input price), else costing from the ledger would misprice API work. (3) **The listener prices and settles**, the writer persists: with the writer's two-second batches, holds settled only by the writer let a fast seat run a dozen requests past its budget on the first estimate (the integration test caught it). (4) **Costing reads the work of the activities the usage ledger knows**: a bots-only game's CPU and a machine's test are in the compute ledger (the ratio) but in no cost report, as before. (5) **`collection`** is set by an administrator with `citar admin set-collection` (no page); a benchmark game's row keeps its run as `meta.item` for it. (6) **A test of a machine** is metered now (`test:<id>`, the caller's use, the machine owner's gift, under the grant's budget); its usage record is none, so no report lists it. (7) **The hardware classes** have their administrators' API and no page yet; the pin (`class_override`) is 3-18's settings dialog's to set. (8) Each kind of work's consumer is the item's owner at write time (an owner cached a minute; an activity with no row asked again within seconds); an account the database no longer holds is nobody, so one stale id never costs a batch its other events. (9) Outside the package, found by the QA's `accounts` part on its first run here: `POST /api/items/{kind}/{ref}/unpublish` commits before it answers (the owner's read straight after saw the item as it was: a request's session commits only after the answer is sent). (10) The suite: `test_ledger` stops the process's usage tracker and makes the ledger afresh before its database, and `test_migrations` holds the ledger's writer back while it renames database files (the writer, fed by games other modules leave playing, writes to whatever database is configured).
- **Gates.** (1) `tests/test_ledger.py` `GivenAndUsed`: thirteen events and a lab game, one of each kind of activity (a lobby game on bob's machine, on alice's own and on the site's GPU, its host CPU; a benchmark game; a probe run's partial step on bob's machine and on the site's API, 1 M input, 100 k output and 2 M cache reads of `claude-opus-5`, 8,500 credits; bob's report on alice's machine and its CPU; an administrator's collection run on bob's machine, on the site's GPU and its CPU; carol's test of bob's machine; the lab game's 100 CPU seconds read from its results once): each event's consumer, provider, kind, activity kind, credits and self-use as the table says, and given and used per member (alice used 8,528, gave 4; bob gave 22, used 4.1; carol used 1; the administrator nothing) equal to it before and after the rollups; the site's machines owned by the administrator still the server's; a rate change (GPU 2.0, API 500) prices the events after it, `reprice` the period before (two of three events changed, the hour's rollups with them, a second reprice nothing); an unpriced API model 0 and flagged; through the scheduler's conversation a helper reporting 10 s that the server saw take 3 s gives an event of `gen_s` 3, reported 10, observed 3, flagged; a machine's class follows its hardware (a 32 GB RTX 5090 the fast class, a 12 GB card the middle one, an EPYC the CPU class, written on the row as it is saved) unless pinned; an account the database does not hold costs no batch. (2) `CpuAttribution`: two real sessions' chat answers interleaved on one thread (0.12 s and 0.25 s of CPU each, three and two of them) charge each game its own measured `thread_time` (within the clock's tick), the two together what the thread used, and the flush gives one `cpu` event per game with those seconds; a unit inside another is the outer one's; the driver thread's sampling charges only its time outside its units, a unit straddling a sample once. (3) `Rollups`: 600 random events over three days (four machines, four consumers, self-use, CPU on both sides): the 24 h, 7 d, 30 d and all-time totals of three members equal the raw sums before any pass (and with the rollups far behind, all raw), after it (every hour of the three days), with two events added in the current hour and in an hour already rolled up, and rolled up again; every rollup row equals its hour's events; the rollup loop survives a failing pass. (4) `Budgets` (keeper on a fake clock, scheduler on a fake clock): a hard budget of 5 admits a step (reserve 1, spends 2), a second (reserve 2, spends 4), refuses the third with `budget`, the day's end as `retry_at` and its slot given back, then admits again the next day; on the site's API under a 30-credit hard budget the first request (estimated 25) runs and spends 35, the next is refused, and with no price for the model every request is refused (no `retry_at`) until the budget is made soft; a soft budget of 3 lets four steps of 2 through and tells its `on_budget` once (and the Servers page's meter says so); `refresh_from_ledger` rebuilds the settled entries (one lost) exactly, carol's work not on alice's budget. `BudgetedGame`: alice's lobby game on bob's machine under a hard budget of 12 a day (a GPU second at 100 credits) pauses with the budget's reason, stays paused, and resumes when the keeper's day turns, its spend counted in the new day's entry. (5) `Costing`: a synthetic ledger priced from old spans (busy, CPU and tokens) and from held-only spans plus the compute events gives every total equal (the API's tokens priced, the GPU's dynamic energy); with 20 s of queue time in the old busy the busy energy and busy hours fall and the held costs stay; `tests/test_costs.py`'s metered game has its steps (with tokens) and its host CPU in the compute ledger and none in its spans, and its report prices it. Twenty-two mutations, each reverting one behaviour (self-use, collection, the site as provider, API pricing, cache tokens, the dirty hours, the first partial hour, self-use in rollups, settling at the listener, a hard budget's refusal, the unpriced refusal, the soft warning's once, the period's turn, the game's budget pause, nested units, sampling a thread inside a unit, the responder's unit, costing's work, the ratio's field, the class routes' gate, a pinned class, reprice's rollups), each fail their tests (`C:/dev/target/p-3-10-mutate.log`). - **Left for later packages.** 3-11/3-14: chat and diplomat tickets made through `pool.seats.ticket` carry the grant's budget already; a hybrid seat's fallback on a `budget` refusal is theirs to choose (the LLM seat pauses its game). 3-18: the machine settings dialog's class route sets `class_override` and the classes (the ledger prices by them from the next event), and could list the hardware classes for administrators. 3-19: the CHANGELOG (credits, budgets in credits, the Compute card). Pre-existing and not changed: the top navigation is wider than a phone (881 px at 390 px, every page; seen in B1).
- **The fix round** (the review's five majors and six minors, all fixed; 2026-10-12, from 14:08 to 15:02 (-04:00), 54 minutes, on the same branch; each fix's test fails with that fix reverted: eighteen mutations, `C:/dev/target/p-3-10-fix-mutate.log`, and the package's twenty-two again on the final code, `p-3-10-fix-mutate-orig.log`). (1) **A binding follows its grant, not the budget the grant had.** A lease or ticket carried the budget id read when it was made, so a budget added to (or replaced on) a grant while a game ran never held it, nor counted it on the owner's meter. A binding now keeps the admitting grant's id (`Binding.access_grant`; `pool.seats.consumer_terms` and `_ticket_admission` return it, the test route passes it; `Keeper.bind`), and each request reserves against the budget that grant has then (`Keeper.budget_of`), which the grant routes (`reload_grant`) and the ledger's rollup pass (`Keeper.sync`) keep current: a budget set, changed, replaced or removed holds the work already running from its next request. `Budgets.test_a_budget_added_to_a_grant_holds_the_work_already_running_under_it` (through the grant route). (2) **A budget read again counts each step once.** Holds are settled in memory and their entries written up to two seconds later, so a read of the entries lost the steps in between: an edit of the amount or behaviour now changes the terms only; a read from the database (an account loaded afresh, its period changed in kind or zone, a repair) adds the credits settled and not yet written (`Keeper._unwritten`, cleared by the writer's `Keeper.written`), in a transaction of its own begun after the writer's state was noted and read again when a batch was written meanwhile. (3) **The repair reaches the running server, and a period key fits its kind.** `refresh_from_ledger` marks `settings_kv.budgets_refreshed`; the rollup pass reads every followed grant again and, when the mark changed, every spend from the rebuilt entries. A key rebuilds only the budgets counted in its kind of period (`--period 2026-10` failed on the first daily budget, and a total budget got entries under a key nothing reads); a key of no kind, or another kind than the one budget asked for, is a `ValueError` (the CLI says so). (4) **A game paused for its budget comes back running after a restart** (`session.SERVER_PAUSES`): its watcher went with the process; its seat's next request is refused again while the budget is spent. A benchmark job paused for it says "paused (budget used up)" and runs again when its game resumes. (5) **The lab priced once and over its run.** Costing leaves out an activity's compute events up to the end of its last span that carries its own work (written before 3-10: `busy`, `cpu` or tokens, `costing.LEGACY_SLACK` a second), so the lab's past games, which the ledger reads from their results on the first start, and a game played across the upgrade are counted once (before: twice). A CPU event's `observed_s` is now the wall time its seconds were measured over (the tracker's flush window; a lab game's `seconds`), which `usage.work_rows` spreads them across (a minute when none; looked for up to a week past the range): an hour's lab game of 3,600 core-seconds had cost a seventh of its dynamic energy. (6) **Compute events are read only for costing**: `usage.read(work=False)` by default, `True` from `costing.compute` and a report's `gather`; the report builder's options read none (0.93 s and about 100 MB a visit with 200,000 events, in the review's probe). (7) **Short transactions.** Rollups commit every `ROLLUP_CHUNK` (48) hours, the watermark with the last chunk (until then `totals` reads those hours raw); `reprice` pages by `REPRICE_PAGE` (5,000) events, each page committed with its hours' rollups; the writer tries a batch the database is too busy for again (`WRITE_TRIES` 4, from half a second, doubling) rather than losing it. (8) **The lab's readers one at a time** (`ledger._LAB_LOCK`, each in a transaction of its own begun under it), their events written by the ledger's writer thread, the only one in the process that inserts events (so ids commit in order, which the rollups' watermark relies on on PostgreSQL); `ingest_lab(ledger, results_dir)` no longer takes a session. (9) The Compute card shows only its latest range's answer. (10) `tests/test_costs.py`'s metered game ends with a unit of its work that Windows' 15.6 ms thread clock surely registers (the game's own units of a few milliseconds mostly read 0; the reviewer's runs failed 2 of 8, here the unfixed test passed 4 of 4, and the round's own probe read 2 or 3 of its 6 units as one tick). The budget tests read the meter at the keeper's clock (they assumed the real day was 2026-10-12). Docstrings (budgets, the scheduler, the ledger's writer and listener) and ACCOUNTS (a probe's turn case ends with `budget`, CPU events' window, the repair's reach), WORKERS, AI_PLAYERS, REPORTS and ARCHITECTURE (the listener is the compute ledger) follow the build; the wiki regenerated (it had missed AI_PLAYERS' last edit). **Gates rerun:** (1)-(7) `tests/test_ledger.py` 33 tests (23 before: `Budgets` four more, `Costing` two, `CpuAttribution`'s window, `ShortTransactions` three, `LabReaders` one); `test_session`'s restart and `test_benchmarks`' budget-paused job. (8) `qa_phase3.py ledger` 10 of 10 in 19 s (`C:/dev/target/p-3-10-fix-qa-ledger`); B1 again on `--keep` (`C:/dev/target/p-3-10-fix-b1-evidence`: the eight shots, and `b1-9`, the Compute card switched to 24 hours and at once to all time with the 24-hour answer held back and marked: all time's figures stay, where the card before the fix showed the stale answer); `queue` 29 of 29 in 71 s, `accounts` 95 of 95 in 81 s.
