## P3.5 3E: trade chat

### P3.5.1 Today

The engine side is done (P3.0, P3.1.5). The server waits per agent (90 s for a bot's turn, 240 s per model message), starts a thread per responder and gives a model's chat answer a fresh conversation with no deadline. The web client's Diplomacy modal is rebuilt on every event, losing what was typed.

### P3.5.2 Timeouts and the chat clock

- **Lobby settings.** The engine keeps any lobby key verbatim in the game's config (`api/host.rs:270-279`), so no engine change is needed: `chat_model_reply_seconds` (default 600: how long a chat waits on a model seat, LLM, MCP or a hybrid seat's model, queue time included, except behind a benchmark in a benchmark game, below), `chat_person_reply_seconds` (default 300: how long a chat waits on a person), and the existing `diplomacy.max_chat_messages` (default 30). The lobby gets a "Chat" group (minutes, and the cap); `GameSession.info()["config"]` lists them; a game without them uses the defaults.
- **`citar/server/chat.py: ChatClock`**, one per session, started with its driver. It tracks every open negotiation as (entries, awaiting, waiting since, deadline, kind of the awaited seat); a change of entries or of the awaited seat restarts the clock; while the game is paused the clock stands still. It wakes on the session's condition, and at least once a second. At a deadline it first cancels any answer still in progress for that chat (the seat's ticket, or its lease request through the agent's `cancel_chat(nid)`), then acts once, by the awaited seat's kind, through the session (each is an engine call that a lost race turns into an `ActionError`, which it ignores):
  - **a model seat** (llm, mcp): the seat declines, `respond_negotiation(reject, "(no reply in time)")` on its behalf;
  - **a hybrid seat whose model has the chat:** its fallback bot answers (`EngineGame.answer(pid, nid, fallback)`, P3.6.5);
  - **a person:** the chat closes as expired, "(no reply in time)", whoever opened it (an AI waiting on a person declines after the per-game timeout; a person waiting on a person ends the same way);
  - **a bot seat:** never; bots answer inside drives and through their responders at once.
- **Queue time in benchmark games.** In a lobby game the model timeout includes queue time (the owner's rule). In a benchmark session (`session.benchmark`) the clock stands still for a chat whose awaited seat's answer is a ticket queued behind a benchmark lease (the ticket's status says so), and counts only once that ticket has started; so a hybrid benchmark seat whose answer waits out another benchmark is answered by its model, not by the fallback. Every fallback and decline records its cause (`timeout`, `queued_behind_benchmark`, `refused`, `failed`), and the leaderboard leaves out fallbacks not caused by the model (P3.6.10).
- **Deadlines after a restart.** When a session is loaded or brought back by `restore_live`, the clock starts every open chat's deadline afresh from the load; on resume from a pause it carries on from where it stood.
- **The clock is the chat timer, with safety nets.** `BotAgent.play_turn` waits until the chats its drive stopped on move or close; `REPLY_WAIT_SECONDS` (90) goes, and in its place it keeps a safety cap of twice the longest chat timeout of the game, after which it closes those chats as `expired` itself. `LLMAgent._await_answer` and `_end_turn` wait the same way, with the same cap. The clock's loop catches and records every exception (in `session.errors`, `where: chat clock`) and carries on; the driver checks at each turn that the clock thread is alive and restarts it if not, with a log line. The driver's force-end of a turn an agent left open (`_close_open_chats`, `session.py:796-799`) stays as the last resort.
- **An injectable clock.** `ChatClock` and `GameSession._await` take a clock (`time.monotonic` and the condition's wait by default; a fake in tests), so every timeout test runs in fake time and adds nothing to the suite's wall time.

### P3.5.3 Responders

- **`citar/agents/responders.py`:** a queue per (session, seat), so a seat answers one chat at a time, in order, and **two executors** per process. Model answers (an LLM seat's, a hybrid seat's model) run on a bounded pool (`CITAR_RESPONDERS`, default 16); bot answers (a bot seat's, and a hybrid seat's bot-first pass, P3.6.5) are instant engine calls and run on their own small pool (`CITAR_BOT_RESPONDERS`, default 2), so a pool full of model answers waiting for a busy GPU never delays a bot's answer. `_dispatch_negotiation_interrupts` submits `(pid, nid, entries)` there instead of starting a thread; `_responding` drops a key when its chat moves or closes.
- **Cancellation never declines.** `cancel_agent` (a suspension for quiet hours, make-way or a disconnect, a seat change, a crash) halts the seat's responders: a halted responder returns without calling `_reject_if_unanswered` and clears its seat's keys from `_responding`, so the chat waits for the seat's next responder or for the clock. `_reject_if_unanswered` runs only for an answer that ran and failed outright.
- **Dispatch on every return.** On resume, load, `restore_live` and an agent's creation, the session dispatches responders for every open chat waiting on a bot, LLM or hybrid seat, so a chat that was waiting when the game stopped or the seat changed is answered without anyone acting first.
- Bot seats answer as now (`EngineGame.answer`); hybrid seats as P3.6.5.
- **Model seats get one conversation per chat** (`ChatThread`, held by the agent per negotiation, dropped when it closes, rebuilt from the history after a restart). Its system prompt is the diplomacy prompt of P3.5.4 with the seat's persona; its tools are `get_diplomacy`, `get_empire`, `get_players`, `get_victory_status`, `read_notes`, `write_notes`, `log_thought`, `respond_negotiation`, `send_message`. Each exchange adds what changed since the last (the other side's entries, the proposal from the seat's side, the minutes left) and runs up to `max_negotiation_calls` steps on the seat's lease, stopping when the chat moves. `_reject_if_unanswered` stays for an answer that failed outright; a slow one is the clock's.
- **Late replies.** Before each step of its own turn, `LLMAgent.play_turn` compares the chats it is in with the last step's (`_chat_marks`) and adds a note for each that changed, worded by its state, so a model is never told to answer a chat that is closed:
  - waiting on the seat: "NEGOTIATION UPDATE: <name> answered negotiation #n: <their entry>; the proposal from your side: <...>. Answer with respond_negotiation, now or before end_turn.";
  - accepted: "NEGOTIATION UPDATE: <name> accepted negotiation #n: the deal is in effect. No answer is needed.";
  - declined, withdrawn or expired: "NEGOTIATION UPDATE: negotiation #n with <name> is closed (<declined|withdrawn|expired>: <their note>). No answer is needed."
  A person's reply reaches the model at once, not only at `end_turn`.

### P3.5.4 The engine and the model-facing text

- **Rust:** `respond_negotiation`'s schema requires `message` (`api/tools/registry.rs`), recorded in the tool list test's fixes and `tests/rules/intended.toml` as `respond-negotiation-message-required`.
- `citar/agents/prompts.py`: SYSTEM_PROMPT's DIPLOMACY section adds that a chat waits for an answer for a time the game sets and then closes or the silent side declines, and that whatever is typed in a chat is answered in it; NEGOTIATION_PROMPT becomes CHAT_PROMPT (who, what changed, the proposal from your side, minutes left).
- MCP (`citar/agents/mcp_server.py`): `wait_for_turn` reports each chat's deadline; INSTRUCTIONS say a chat waiting on you closes when its time runs out.

### P3.5.5 Status events

- `{"type": "negotiation_status", "negotiation": nid, "players": [a, b], "waiting_on": pid, "kind": "model"|"person"|"hybrid"|"bot", "state": "queued"|"thinking"|"waiting"|"closed", "position": n|null, "eta_s": x|null, "deadline": epoch seconds|null}`. The clock sends `waiting` with the deadline; the answering seat's lease or ticket callbacks send `queued` (position, ETA) and `thinking`; the session sends `closed`.
- The game socket (`app.py:1765-1830`) delivers it to a seat named in `players` and to spectators of a game whose god view is allowed, and to nobody else (a filter beside the event filter).

### P3.5.6 The web client

- **`citar/web/js/chatmodel.js`** (pure, no DOM, tested with `node --test`): merges a negotiation's entries by `seq`; derives a window's state (whose move; input enabled or not; the status line from the last `negotiation_status`; the countdown); derives the End Turn button's state from the open chats; keeps each negotiation's draft and builder state in a map that updates never touch.
- **`citar/web/js/chat.js`:** docked windows along the bottom edge, **to the left of the End Turn box** (`.turn-box`, `style.css:110`), one per civilization (the engine allows one open chat per pair), side by side from right to left, each minimisable, so End Turn, whose states the chats drive, is never covered; when they do not fit, the oldest minimise to tabs. **At 700 px wide or less** one window shows at a time, full width above the End Turn box, with a tab strip for the others; `E1` checks both widths. Bubbles and proposal cards in `seq` order, the game's close notes in grey. The textarea and the deal builder (moved out of `panels.js` into the window, collapsible) keep their state: new entries are appended and nothing is redrawn whole. A window opens straight from the socket's `negotiation` event naming you, reading `GET /api/games/{gid}/negotiations/{nid}` (new: the seat's `negotiation_view`; a seat token, or a spectator of a god-view game), without waiting for a view refresh; play goes on while it is open.
- **Strict turn-taking:** input and buttons are enabled only on your move; otherwise the window says "Rome is thinking…", "queued #3, about 40 s" or "waiting for you, 3:12 left". The quick buttons fill in default lines as today ("Agreed.", "No deal.", "How about this instead?"). Typing in an open chat sends `respond_negotiation(reply)`, which the other side answers; a broadcast `send_message` stays on the Diplomacy screen and gets no reply.
- **End Turn:** "Answer Rome first" (opening that chat) while a chat waits on you; "Waiting for Rome… 4:12" while one waits on the other side, and clicking it then arms "End turn when they answer" (cancellable), which ends the turn once every chat has closed with nothing left waiting on you.
- The Diplomacy screen keeps relations, war and peace, denouncing, the UN vote and broadcast messages; each civilization's row gets "Open chat", which opens the docked window (or starts a chat on your turn).

### P3.5.7 Metrics

`metrics.negotiation` rows gain `nid`, `gen_s`, `queue_s` and `outcome` (`answered`, `declined_late`, `fallback`, `closed`) with the fallback's cause (P3.5.2); the stats screen shows chat answers per seat.

**As built in 3-11: chat in the server: the clock, responders, status events, late replies** (P3.5.2-P3.5.5, P3.5.7; Appendix D). One agent, 2026-10-12, from 16:51 to 19:20 (-04:00), 149 minutes, on branch `p-3-11` from `47876f4`. Rust changed (the `respond_negotiation` schema only).

- **The chat clock** (`citar/server/chat.py`, `GameSession.chat`). The lobby's keys reach it through the game's configuration, which the engine keeps verbatim: `chat_model_reply_seconds` (600) and `chat_person_reply_seconds` (300), held within 1 s and 7 days, and `diplomacy.max_chat_messages` (30, the engine's); `info()["config"]` carries the three as the game plays them (`chat.settings`). The clock keeps each open chat as (the seat it waits on, its entries, the kind of that seat, the seconds counted against its timeout), brought up to date by the session's event hook on every negotiation event (`note`: a new chat, or a change of entries, awaited seat or that seat's kind, starts its count at 0) and by every look; it counts time only while the game is not paused (`settle`, called before `set_paused`, `suspend` and `resume` change the pause, then `republish`), and not for a chat whose answer is a prompt queued behind a benchmark in a benchmark game (`answer_status`: the ticket's or the conversation's `queued` with `benchmark`), which counts from 0 again once the prompt starts. Past its timeout it cancels the answer still in progress (`GameSession.cancel_chat_answer`: the seat's conversation for that chat) and acts once: a model seat (LLM, MCP) declines, "(no reply in time)" (`decline_late`), a person's chat closes as expired (`expire_chat`), a hybrid seat's goes to its fallback bot when 3-14's agent has one (`chat_fallback`, else it declines); each re-reads the chat under the lock and does nothing when it moved, so an answer and the deadline leave one outcome. A loaded or restored session has a new clock: every open chat starts afresh. Its thread (started with the driver) waits on the session's condition at most a second, records every exception in `errors` (`where: chat clock`) and carries on; the driver starts it again when it finds it dead (`ensure`, with a log line). Its time is the session's `clock` (`RealClock`; `tests/chatfake.py`'s `FakeClock` in the tests, whose waits pass at once and tick the chat clock), which `GameSession._await` waits on too.
- **Responders** (`citar/agents/responders.py`). `_dispatch_negotiation_interrupts` hands each chat that comes to wait on a bot or LLM seat to the seat's line (`SeatLine`: one chat at a time, in order) on one of two daemon pools, `CITAR_RESPONDERS` (16) for model answers and `CITAR_BOT_RESPONDERS` (2) for bot answers (`Pool`: a thread for every task no free thread takes, up to its size); `_responding` is a dict of keys to seats, and a key goes when its chat moves or closes (the event hook). `cancel_agent` halts the seat's line (a new epoch: what is queued is dropped and what runs returns as halted), and `_run_responder` declines, "(no response)", only an answer that ran and failed outright (an error, a model that used its steps without answering, a seat with no machine); `suspend`, `_crashed` and `stop` halt every line. `dispatch_waiting` hands out again every open chat no line holds: on `resume`, `set_paused(False)`, a load (`_register_loaded`; bots at once, models after the resume) and `update_seat` (the new controller answers).
- **One conversation per chat** (`ChatThread` in `citar/agents/llm_agent.py`). `LLMAgent.respond_negotiation` runs one exchange of the chat's thread: its first message is `CHAT_PROMPT` (who, what they said, the proposal from the seat's side, the negotiation's view, the empire at a glance, the minutes left), later ones `CHAT_UPDATE` (their entries since the model's last answer, the proposal, the minutes left); the system prompt is `chat_system_prompt` (static, with the seat's persona and the shared `DIPLOMACY` text), the tools `CHAT_TOOLS` (P3.5.3's nine); up to `max_negotiation_calls` steps on the seat's binding, stopping when the chat moves. Outcomes `answered`, `moved`, `unanswered`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `paused`; the thread is dropped when its chat closes (`chat_closed`) and cancelled by `cancel_chat`, and its conversation is made again from the history after a cancel or a restart. Each request's place in its machine's queue and its start reach the chat through `ScheduledConversation.on_status` (new: the scheduler tells the conversation a request is of, with a `benchmark` flag on a ticket waiting behind one), sent as `queued` and `thinking`.
- **Waits and late replies.** `BotAgent.play_turn` waits until the chats its drive stopped on move or close, with a safety cap of twice the game's longest timeout (`wait_cap`, the probes' turn case 0) past which it closes them itself; `REPLY_WAIT_SECONDS` is gone. `LLMAgent._await_answer` waits the tool's own wait (`negotiation_wait_seconds`, now 60 s by default, was 240) and `_end_turn` until every chat it opened has moved or closed, both under the same cap (`_wait_cap`), adding the wall time to `_waited`; `_chat_left` is gone. Before each step of its turn the agent compares the chats it is in with what it last heard (`_chat_marks`) and puts a note in front of the model for each that changed (`prompts.late_note`: answered, with their entry and the proposal from its side; accepted, or closed as declined, withdrawn or expired, asking for no answer); a chat another seat opened that waits on the model is its responder's, told only when it closes.
- **Status events, the route, MCP.** `negotiation_status` (`waiting` with `deadline` and `left_s`, `queued` with `position` and `eta_s`, `thinking`, `closed` with `status`; `paused` and, behind a benchmark, `held`) is broadcast by the clock and the session; the game socket gives it to the seats in `players` and to spectators while the god view is allowed (`app._sees_chat_status`). `GET /api/games/{gid}/negotiations/{nid}`: a seat token reads the chats its seat is in, a viewer of the game any while the god view is allowed (from `as_player`'s side when that civilization is in it), with the chat's `clock`; 404 otherwise. `wait_for_turn` (HTTP and MCP) lists every chat the seat is in with its deadline (`chats`); the MCP instructions and the tool's description say a chat runs out.
- **Rust.** `respond_negotiation`'s schema requires `message` (`api/tools/registry.rs`), the fix listed in `crates/citar-testkit/tests/engine/tools.rs`' `FIXES` and `tests/rules/intended.toml` (`respond-negotiation-message-required`), the rule script `negotiation_every_entry_needs_a_message` showing the schema's refusal, and the changelog's rule fixes written by `cargo refcheck changelog --write`.
- **The rest.** Prompts: `DIPLOMACY` (a chat waits a time the game sets, then closes or the silent side declines; what is said in a chat is answered in it), shared by the turn's and the chat's system prompts; `NEGOTIATION_PROMPT` became `CHAT_PROMPT` and `CHAT_UPDATE`. Probes: offer and message cases answer through the chat's conversation (`respond_negotiation`), which moves to each answer's ticket (`ScheduledConversation.rebind`) and asks a message its machine cut off again as it stood; a turn case's subject has `wait_cap` 0. Metrics: `negotiation` rows carry `nid`, `gen_s`, `queue_s`, `outcome` (`answered`, `declined_late`, `fallback`, `closed`) and `cause` (`timeout`, `queued_behind_benchmark`, `refused`, `failed`); the summary's `chat_answers`, `chat_fallback_causes`, `avg_chat_gen_s` and `avg_chat_queue_s`, which the stats screen shows. The lobby's new game form has a Chat row (minutes for an AI and a person, the message cap). The dry run answers each chat message once (the latest naming its negotiation), waits `[dry run: wait N s]` when a message says so, and with `dry_run_propose_every` opens a trade with the first civilization it has met; a seat on a dry-run machine keeps those knobs (`servers.member_llm`, `DRYRUN_LLM_KEYS`).
- **Choices the design left open, and departures.** (1) The answering side's `queued`/`thinking` come from the conversation's own requests (`ScheduledConversation.on_status`), not the lease's `on_status`: a lease's requests serve its turn and its chats alike, and a status must name the chat. A ticket's `on_status` (3-14's hybrid chats) works as it is. (2) The tool's own wait for an answer went from 240 s to 60 s: an answer after it reaches the model before its next step, so the model plays on rather than sit in a tool call. (3) A model seat's chat answers wait for the resume only while the game is paused for its machine (quiet hours, a disconnect, a spent budget: `GameSession.answers_held`), where an answer could only fail and be declined; during a person's pause or a make-way pause it answers as before (3-08's made-way answer on a lease of its own). (4) The responders' pools are daemon threads of their own (`responders.Pool`), not `concurrent.futures`, whose workers the interpreter joins at exit: a shutdown must not wait for an answer stuck behind a busy machine. (5) The status events carry `left_s` and `paused` besides P3.5.5's fields, so a client counts down without trusting its own clock, and a closed one its `status`. (6) The chat route answers 404, not 403, to a viewer while a person plays: a chat's existence is the two sides' business.
- **Gates.** (1) `tests/test_chat_clock.py`, in fake time: a chat waiting on an MCP seat is open at 599 s and declined for it at 600, "(no reply in time)" (with the defaults: no chat keys), one `declined_late`/`timeout` row; an LLM seat's answer still running at its 120 s is cancelled (its conversation closed) and declined once by the clock, never "(no response)"; a person-to-person chat and a model's chat waiting on a person close as expired at 300 s; a 10-minute pause leaves a 5-minute chat open (no deadline told while paused), 200 s left on the resume, expired 200 s later; a reply at 200 s moves the deadline to 500; an answer landing as the deadline fires (both orders) leaves one outcome; the defaults (600, 300, 30, cap 1,200) and a game's own keys (90, 45, 6, the engine's cap 6, expired at 45 s); the settings held within their bounds; a bot never timed; in a benchmark game a chat whose answer's ticket waits 30 minutes behind a benchmark lease stays open (`held`), and once the ticket starts it counts from 0 (open at 599, declined at 600); in a lobby game the queue counts; after a load every open chat has its 300 s again. (2) a bot's turn takes a person's answer at 200 s (no 90 s budget) and ends; it ends at 300 s when the clock closes a chat nobody answers; a model seat's turn waits the same way (the person's accept at 200 s, `end_turn`, the wait in `_waited`) and ends at the timeout; with the clock thread killed (`SystemExit` in its tick) the driver starts it again, with its log line, and the chat still closes; with the clock stopped for good (no ticks) the bot's and the model's safety cap (1,200 s) closes the chat and ends the turn; the clock's loop survives an exception in a tick, recorded in `errors`. (3) `tests/test_chat_responders.py`: 40 chats (32 from four people, 8 between the models) with 8 model seats on `CITAR_RESPONDERS=4` ran on 4 `model-responder` threads, one chat at a time per seat, each answered; a second exchange in each chat stayed in its one conversation (one `make_conversation` per seat and chat); `_responding` empty once all closed; with 16 model answers blocked on `sv_gpu` (one slot: 1 in flight, 15 waiting) a person's offer to a bot was answered in under a second; the pools start a thread for every task no free thread takes. (4) a suspension mid-answer leaves the chat open and the resume answers it (no "(no response)"); a chat that comes during quiet hours waits for the resume, one during a person's pause is answered; a seat changed mid-answer is answered by its new agent; a chat waiting on a model across a restart (`restore_live`) is answered at once (the model's timeout 600 s). (5) a person's reply after the tool's wait is in the model's next message ("NEGOTIATION UPDATE: ... answered negotiation #1: replied: ...; the proposal from your side: ... Answer with respond_negotiation, now or before end_turn."); accepted, declined and expired chats get notes that ask for no answer, a withdrawn one says so; a chat another seat opened is its responder's until it closes; an answer the machine cut off is asked again with the same message. (6) `tests/test_chat_routes.py`: through the app's sockets, the two sides of a person's chat with a model seat on a one-slot machine whose slot was busy heard `queued` (position 1), `thinking`, `waiting` (deadlines, on the model then the person) and `closed` (`rejected`), in that order; a third seat's socket and the spectator's (a person plays) heard none; a spectator of a game with no person heard them; `GET /api/games/{gid}/negotiations/{nid}` by both seats' tokens (200), a third seat, an unknown chat, a stranger, a moderator, the owner and an administrator while a person plays, and the spectator link (404), a visitor (401 or 404); with no person playing: the owner (the opener's side, `as_player`), an administrator and the spectator link 200, a stranger and a moderator 404, an ACL viewer 200, and published `global`, a moderator and a visitor 200. (7) Rust: the schema refuses a `respond_negotiation` without `message` ("Missing required parameter(s): message.", the rule script); S7: `cargo fmt --check`, clippy clean, nextest 1455 passed (2 skipped), the doctests, `xtask check` (0 NotPorted, 0 Pending), refcheck `--strict --with-bot` on the mini and late fixtures clean, `golden check` all 16 sets ok, the tool list test passing with its new fix. (8) `wait_for_turn` (HTTP, so MCP) lists each chat with `waiting_on`, `deadline` and `seconds_left`; the MCP instructions and tool description say a chat runs out; the probes' offer and message cases pass (`tests/test_editor.py`, `tests/test_pooled_work.py` `Probes`). (9) `python scripts/qa_phase3.py chat`: 14 of 14 in 86 s (`C:/dev/target/p-3-11-qa-chat`): model A answered after 3 s and alice answered back; model B, asked while A held the one slot, queued (position 1) and was declined for by the clock 20.6 s after the chat opened; the bot's chat expired at 20 s; the game played to its end (turn 9); on turns 3 and 6 both models proposed to alice, she accepted A's and B's expired; her socket heard queued, thinking, waiting (model and person) and closed, the bot seat's only its own chats', a spectator's none; the metrics counted A's answers and B's late decline (`timeout`); no traceback. `queue` 29 of 29 (70 s), `ledger` 10 of 10 (20 s), `accounts` 95 of 95 (81 s). (10) S1 1155 tests OK in 470 s (1114 at 3-10's merge); the touched modules under `TZ=UTC0` (246 OK in 213 s) and on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` (246 OK in 212 s); S2 ruff clean; S3 208 routes, 180 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded; S4 37 files, 187 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict`, AI_PLAYERS, PLAYING, ARCHITECTURE and API updated and the wiki regenerated; S6 35 modules parse as ES modules, 23 Node tests pass. Twenty-one mutations, each reverting one behaviour (the pause, a reply's restart, a halt that never declines, the bot pool, the benchmark hold, the socket's filter, one conversation per chat, the late notes, a closing answer's outcome, the pools' threads, the safety cap, dispatch on resume, a person's expiry, the decline's note, the closed status, the wait's deadlines, the dry-run knobs, a retry's message, the responder's chats, the conversation's queued status, the route's side check), each fail their tests (`C:/dev/target/p-3-11-mutate.log`). P3.9.7: the chat clock's tick with 20 open chats, median 0.04 to 0.09 ms (the limit 10 ms); the session's overhead 2.251, 2.010 and 2.005 (median 2.010; the limit 2.87); the scheduler's acquire-release pair 10 to 12 us. CI-only: test.yml on its three OSes, rust.yml and determinism.yml.
- **Left for later packages.** 3-12: the chat window reads `negotiation_status` (fields above) and `GET /api/games/{gid}/negotiations/{nid}` (its `clock`); the lobby's Chat row is in; the stats screen's chat line is in `metrics.js`. 3-14: hybrid seats join `_dispatch_negotiation_interrupts`' seat types (their bot-first pass on the `bot` pool, the model's answer on the `model` pool, as a `chat` ticket made through `pool.seats.ticket` with `ref=("game", gid)` whose `on_status` is `ChatClock.answer_status`), `KINDS` already maps `hybrid`, `chat_fallback` calls `EngineGame.answer` with the agent's `fallback_bot`, and `CAUSES` names the fallbacks' causes. 3-19: the CHANGELOG (the chat timeouts and the lobby's Chat row; `negotiation_wait_seconds` now 60 s; `respond_negotiation` requires `message`).
- **The fix round** (the review's five majors and four minors, all fixed; 2026-10-12, from 19:46 to 20:55 (-04:00), 69 minutes, on the same branch; each fix's test fails with that fix reverted: forty-two mutations, this round's twenty-one and the package's twenty-one with their texts brought up to date, all caught on the final code, `C:/dev/target/p-3-11-fix-mutate.log`). (1) **A machine's chat answers take no more threads than it has slots.** A model answer waits for its machine's slot on the thread that asks, so sixteen answers queued on one busy one-slot machine held every `model-responder` thread and every other machine's seats went unasked until their chats timed out. A seat's line now takes a model thread only while its machine has fewer of the responders' answers in flight than its slots, and never more than half the pool (`responders._Machines`, `machine_limit`); otherwise it is parked, holding no thread, its chat told its place as the machine tells its own line (`queued`, behind what waits on the machine, `benchmark` when one holds it; `Machine.line_info`), and the next of that machine's answers to end hands its place to the first parked line, which is told again as it moves up. A halted parked line leaves the queue, so a seat moved to another machine answers there at once. One pool with places per machine rather than a pool per machine: the threads stay bounded by `CITAR_RESPONDERS`. A member's dry run is held to what it may be (`servers.dry_run_knobs`: its delay within the 60 s a dry-run machine's own field already allowed, `DRY_RUN_MAX_DELAY`; an answer it knows; a whole period), and the provider holds every delay, a chat's `[dry run: wait N s]` included, to the same bound. Each answer in a line names its pool, so a line whose next answer is of the other kind moves there (a hybrid seat's bot-first pass, then its model's answer: never a model's answer on the bot pool's two threads). (2) **A loaded game asks its models nothing until it is resumed.** `_register_loaded` handed every seat's chats out, so the Load route's game (paused "so its model configuration can be checked before anything spends tokens") and a probe case's end state opened to be looked at asked their models at once, and the answer changed the end state being inspected. Handing out only the bots' was not enough: a bot's answer is an action, and every action hands out the chats waiting on models while a person's pause holds none. So a loaded game holds its models' answers until it is first resumed (`GameSession.held_from_load`, read by `answers_held`, cleared by `resume` and `set_paused(False)`), its bots answer at once, and a game opened only to be looked at (`load(leases=False)`) answers nothing. The chat clock looks at the open chats when it starts (`ChatClock.start`), and `deadline_of` looks first at a chat it has not seen, so an answer's first message tells its real deadline, never "no time limit". (3) **A chat's conversation closed by the scheduler is made again.** A benchmark that takes the machine while a probe's answer runs cancels its ticket and closes the conversation on it; the retry rebound that closed conversation, whose every step refused, and the case was scored as the model's own non-answer. `ScheduledConversation.closed` says so, and the next exchange starts a new conversation told the whole chat. (4) **A tool's result marks only the chat it reports.** `_await_answer` and `_end_turn`'s refusal marked every chat as heard, so a chat that moved meanwhile (accepted while the model generated the step that opened another) was never told; each now marks the chat it reports (`LLMAgent._heard`), at the moment it is reported. (5) **A game that made way answers every chat in its seat's line.** `_answered` let the answering lease go, with a halt that dropped the line's next chats, after each answer; the lease now goes after the line's last answer (`SeatLine.pending`), the agent without a halt. (6) **The message cap reported is the one the engine plays** (`EngineGame.max_chat_messages`, the game's or its ruleset's), and the lobby's cap field is empty by default (placeholder "rules"): sent only when filled in, so a lobby game keeps its ruleset's cap. (7) **A failed action at a deadline is tried again**, after 1 s, doubling up to a minute, while the chat stands where the clock saw it (a chat that moved is a new record at the next look): a chat between two people has no agent's safety cap. (8) **Dead state gone:** `_Chat.answer`, the clock's unbounded `acted` list and its `ticks`. The fourth minor, each answer on its own pool, is the last sentence of (1). Gates again: (1) `tests/test_chat_clock.py` adds the engine's cap reported, a failed expiry tried again at 301 and 303 s, the retries' pauses 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32 and then 60 s, and a chat the clock had not looked at timed when asked. (3) `tests/test_chat_responders.py`: a model seat on `sv_dryrun` answered a person in under a second while sixteen answers waited on `sv_gpu` (one in flight, fifteen parked, every other `model-responder` thread idle; the bot's answer, as before, on the bot pool); eight seats on a four-slot machine answer four at once with the default pool, two with a pool of four; a parked chat hears `queued` 2, then 1, then `thinking`; a parked seat moved to another machine answers there at once; places go to the parked lines in order; each answer of a line runs on its own kind's pool. (4) a made-way seat's two chats are both answered, on one lease let go once; a loaded game's bot answers at once and its model only on the resume, its first message telling the minutes left, and a look-only load answers nothing. (5) a chat accepted while the model opened another, and one accepted before `end_turn`'s refusal, are each told before the next step. `test_pooled_work` `Probes`: a benchmark taking the machine mid-answer leaves the model's own answer (accept, passed). `test_chat_routes` `DryRunKnobs`: a member's knobs held to 60 s, a known answer and a whole period. S1 1171 tests OK in 479 s; the touched modules under `TZ=UTC0` (305 OK in 236 s) and on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` (305 OK in 240 s); S2 ruff clean; S3 208 routes, 180 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded; S4 37 files, 187 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict` (AI_PLAYERS, PLAYING, ARCHITECTURE, API), the wiki regenerated; S6 35 modules parse, 23 Node tests pass; S7 again although no Rust changed (fmt, clippy, nextest 1455 passed and 2 skipped, the doctests, `xtask check`, refcheck strict with the bot clean, `golden check` all ok). S8: `qa_phase3.py chat` 14 of 14 in 86 s (model B queued at place 1 behind A, then declined for after 20.6 s), `queue` 29 of 29 (70 s), `ledger` 10 of 10 (20 s), `accounts` 95 of 95 (81 s). P3.9.7: the clock's tick with 20 open chats, median 0.045 to 0.138 ms; the session's overhead 2.129, 2.042 and 1.978 (median 2.042, the limit 2.87). For 3-14: each answer submitted names its pool (a hybrid seat's bot-first pass `bot`, its model's answer `model`), and a hybrid seat's machine is its block's `server_id` (`responders.machine_of`); its parked answers are told their place with `benchmark` when one holds the machine, which holds the chat in a benchmark game.

**As built in 3-12: the chat window** (P3.5.6; Appendix D). One agent in two sittings: 2026-10-12 from 21:33 to about 22:20 (-04:00), when the session ended with two files uncommitted, then 2026-10-13 from 00:50 to 01:20 (-04:00), on branch `p-3-12` from `14b07f9`, with `origin/v0.1.6` (`cd33f45`: the readers of replaced files, the probe copy's history, the chat clock's restart test) merged in before the gates. No Rust changed in the package.

- **`citar/web/js/chatmodel.js`** (pure: no DOM, no network, no clock of its own; `now` is the caller's monotonic milliseconds). A chat's history only grows and each entry has a `seq`, so the number of entries is the chat's version: `mergeEntries` keeps one entry per `seq`, in order; the view's `diplomacy` block (`applyView`), the chat route's answer with its `clock` (`applyNegotiation`) and the socket's `negotiation_status` (`applyStatus`) each say what they know, and whatever names fewer entries than the client has seen only adds entries it lacks (a stale view never takes a chat back, a late status is dropped, a closed chat never opens again, a status ahead of the entries asks for the chat). A chat the client holds open that a view does not list is read again (`missing`), never closed for its absence. `windowState(civ)` gives a window's mode (`new`, `your_move`, `their_move`, `closed`), what it allows (strict turn-taking: the text box and the answers on your move, Withdraw on theirs, opening a chat on your own turn) and its status line from the last status ("Rome is thinking…", "Rome: queued #3, about 40 s", "queued behind a benchmark", "Waiting for you, 3:12 left", "(the game is paused)"), counted down from the server's `left_s` at its arrival, never the client's wall clock. `endTurnState` gives End Turn's state from the open chats: `answer` (the oldest waiting on you), `waiting` (the one that may take longest, a bot's or a paused one counting as longest), `armed`, `clear` (`fire` when armed on your turn); `arm`/`disarm`, and `syncTurn` disarms at a new turn. Each civilization's draft (the text and the deal builder: what each side gives, open or folded) lives in `drafts`, which only `draft()` writes; `builder` edits a draft (add, take out, load what is on the table, clear). The windows (`openWindow`, `notify`: a new open chat pops up and is the one shown at narrow widths unless you are answering another; news for a minimised window marks its tab; `minimise`, `restore`, `closeWindow`) and `dockLayout`: wide, side by side from right to left along the bottom edge, the first-opened next to the End Turn box (`.turn-box`) and never over it, minimised ones as tabs at the left end, the oldest but the active one folded into tabs when they do not fit; at 700 px or less (or with no room beside the box) one window at a time, full width above the End Turn box, under a strip with a tab per window.
- **`citar/web/js/chat.js`** (`ChatDock`, `ChatWindow`) draws what the model decides, in the game screen's map area (`GameScreen.chat`). Nothing is redrawn whole: entries are inserted in (chat, `seq`) order with a divider per chat, each once; the proposal on the table, the status line and the buttons change in place; the text box and the deal builder are built once per window, so a status, a refresh or the chat moving never wipes what is typed or built; the log follows its newest entry unless the reader scrolled up. A `negotiation` event naming the seat reads `GET /api/games/{gid}/negotiations/{nid}` at once (one read at a time per chat, and once more if asked meanwhile); `negotiation_status` moves the status line and End Turn. Bubbles (yours on the right), proposals as cards, the game's close notes in grey, a decline by the side that spoke last shown as a withdrawal. The quick buttons send the default lines when the box is empty ("Agreed.", "No deal.", "How about this instead?", "I propose this deal."); Enter sends a reply or opens a chat; what was sent leaves the draft. The deal builder moved out of `panels.js` into the window, folded by default, with the view's trade options as one-click chips and "Load into the builder". End Turn's states draw the button (`endTurnButton`, `GameScreen.onEndTurn`, Shift+Enter too): "Answer Rome first" opens that chat; "Waiting for Rome… 4:12" arms, after the turn's other decisions are checked as for any end of turn (Ctrl+click skips them), "End turn when they answer ✕", which a click disarms; armed, once every chat has closed with nothing waiting on you the turn ends, once, as the click that armed it would have (a plain arming checks the other decisions again, since a deal may bring one). `negotiation` left the turn's blockers (End Turn's own states hold the turn for a chat), and a negotiation alert opens its chat window. A spectator gets no windows.
- **The Diplomacy screen** keeps relations, war and peace, denouncing, the United Nations vote and messages to everyone (a message's draft now survives its redraws); each civilization's row and its page have **Open chat**, which closes the screen and opens the docked window with the cursor in it. The old Diplomacy popup on a negotiation waiting on you (`checkNegotiations`) went: the window pops up instead.
- **The server, for the window** (`citar/server/chat.py`). Each `negotiation_status` carries `entries`, the chat's length when it was said (P3.5.5's fields plus this), so a client tells a late status from a current one. The clock tells a chat `waiting` when it begins to wait and never over an answer's own `queued` or `thinking`, which stand until the answer ends (before, its next look turned a model still generating back to "waiting"). A pause or a resume tells each chat again where it stands, with its new deadline: an answer that goes on through a person's pause keeps its `queued` (with its place and machine) or `thinking` (`_Chat.answering`, `_said`), and a suspension, which halts every answer until the resume dispatches them again, says `waiting` (`republish(answers_halted=True)`).
- **Tests and CI.** `citar/web/tests/chatmodel.test.mjs` (the model and the layout) and `citar/web/tests/chat.test.mjs` (`chat.js` over a few stand-ins for the DOM, as `modal.test.mjs` does: a window opening from the socket, a draft and an open builder through 100 seeded updates of every kind, entries arriving out of order, the log's following, sending, End Turn's states driving the turn, minimising and folding, the narrow layout, a spectator). `citar/web/package.json` and test.yml's Node step came with 3-06; the step's comment now names the chat modules, and CONTRIBUTING the same. `tests/test_chat_clock.py` `AnswerStatuses` and `tests/test_chat_routes.py` `StatusEvents` cover the server's part.
- **Docs.** `docs/PLAYING.md` "Chatting" (with `docs/assets/chat.png`, from E1 on a QA server) and the Diplomacy screen's new scope; `docs/API.md` (the status's `entries`); `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the statuses through a pause; the client's chat modules); `CONTRIBUTING.md`; the wiki regenerated (`scripts/build_wiki.py` now points an image at the repository's raw file, since a wiki page cannot show a `blob` page as a picture).
- **Choices the design left open, and departures.** (1) The quick-reply line for an open is "I propose this deal.", as the old popup had. (2) A window is per civilization and shows every chat with it, the latest last, each under a divider; the draft is the window's, so it carries over to the next chat with them. (3) Close (✕) hides a window and the chat goes on: End Turn, an alert, Diplomacy or the other side's next word bring it back. (4) The design's gate 1 names `node --test citar/web/tests/`, the folder form, which Node 22 and later run as one file and fail (3-06's note); the gate runs the pattern form test.yml uses, `node --test "citar/web/tests/*.test.mjs"` (or `npm test` in `citar/web`). (5) The server change above is the window's: without it a model's "thinking" could not survive the clock's next look.
- **Gates.** (1) `node --test "citar/web/tests/*.test.mjs"`: 49 tests pass (26 the chat window's, 15 in `chatmodel.test.mjs` and 11 in `chat.test.mjs`; 23 before): entries merged by `seq`, duplicates once and out-of-order arrivals in order, from views, the route and statuses in every order (a stale view, a late status, a status ahead of the entries, a close against a view of the same length); drafts and the builder intact through 100 seeded random updates of the model and 100 updates of every kind through the dock (the same text box and builder nodes, each entry drawn once and in order); window state for your move, their move, closed and no chat yet, and for each status (queued with its place and ETA, thinking, waiting with its countdown, held behind a benchmark, paused); End Turn's states (answer first, waiting with its countdown ticking in place, armed, clear, firing once, as the arming click would: plain or Ctrl) from constructed sets of chats; the dock's layout over 500 seeded sizes from 320 to 2,620 px (a window or a tab never meets the End Turn box or another; at 700 px or less one window with a tab for each), the oldest folding first, and a folded window brought up by its tab. (2) S6: 37 modules parse as ES modules (`node --check` on `.mjs` copies, as test.yml does). (3) S8: `qa_phase3.py chat` 14 of 14 in 86 s (`C:/dev/target/p-3-12-c-qa-chat`), and again with `--keep` (`C:/dev/target/p-3-12-c-qa-e1`, 14 of 14), against which E1 ran step by step in headless Chrome over the DevTools protocol, signed in as the harness's alice, on a game of her own (a person, two dry-run models on her one-slot machine, one proposing every second turn, and a bot; models answer within 30 s, people within 240 s), at 1280 by 800 and at 700 by 800, 22 of 22 checks each, 18 screenshots each in `C:/dev/target/p-3-12-c-qa-e1/e1/`: [x] the Diplomacy screen keeps relations and messages, with Open chat for each civilization and no deal builder; Open chat docks the window with the cursor in it; [x] a reply shows "Siam is thinking… · 0:30 left", then the answer, waiting on alice with her 4:00; [x] the window docked left of End Turn (above it at 700 px), which stays visible and clickable; [x] a half-typed message and an open deal builder with an item survive two updates (the game paused, its status saying so, and resumed): the same text box, the same item; [x] minimised to a tab (at 700 px: only its tab), restored with its draft; [x] End Turn "Waiting for The Maya… 0:30", a click arms "End turn when they answer 0:30 ✕", and the turn ended by itself when the answer came (accepted, 8 s later); [x] a model too slow for the 30 s timeout declined for, "(no reply in time)"; [x] an AI's chat pops up docked during its turn, and on alice's next turn End Turn is visible and clickable beside the windows; [x] three windows at 1280 px, End Turn "Answer Iroquois first" uncovered; [x] at 700 px one window with a tab for each, a tab bringing its window up, End Turn visible and clickable, and its "Answer … first" bringing that chat up. The kept server's log has no traceback; it was stopped. (4) S1 1192 tests OK in 416 s (1171 at 3-11's merge, plus the merged `test_fsutil` and probe-copy tests and this package's); `test_chat_clock` and `test_chat_routes` under `TZ=UTC0` (35 OK in 13 s) and on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` (35 OK in 12 s); S2 ruff clean; S3 208 routes, 180 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded (no route added); S4 37 files, 189 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict` (PLAYING, API, ARCHITECTURE; CONTRIBUTING), the wiki regenerated. Thirty-four mutations, each reverting one behaviour (thirty of the window, four of the server's statuses), all caught on the final code (`C:/dev/target/p-3-12-c-mutate.log`). P3.9.7: the chat clock's tick with 20 open chats, median 0.016 ms; the session's overhead 2.072, 2.064 and 2.029 (median 2.064; the limit 2.87). No Rust changed, so no S7. CI-only: test.yml on its three OSes, with its front-end job's Node step now running the chat window's tests.
- **Left for later packages.** 3-13: the deal builder's one-click chips read the view's `diplomacy.players[].trade_options` (`ChatWindow.drawHints`, redrawn only when they change); fetch them from `GET /api/games/{gid}/trade-options/{other}` when the window opens and after each entry in its chat instead, and E2's "the deal builder lists the trade options it fetched" is that window's; the recap's chats as threads can reuse `mergeEntries` and `CLOSED_TEXT`. 3-15: a hybrid seat's statuses (`kind: "hybrid"`) already read as a model's in the window ("thinking", "queued"); "diplomat thinking" on the game screen is the turn box's, not the window's. 3-19: the CHANGELOG (the docked chat windows, End Turn's chat states and the armed end of turn, the trimmed Diplomacy screen, the statuses' `entries`).
- **The fix round** (the review's four majors and five minors, all fixed; of the fourth major's two cases one, a new chat first heard of as a status, already popped up (the reply test in `chat.test.mjs` opens its window from a status); 2026-10-13, from 01:38 to 03:02 (-04:00), on the same branch; each fix's test fails with that fix reverted: fifty-seven mutations, the package's thirty-four with their texts brought up to date and this round's twenty-three, all caught on the final code, `C:/dev/target/p-3-12-f-mutate.log`; an armed End Turn never disarmed loops through its fresh views and hangs the Node tests, which the runner stops after 180 s and counts as caught; the scheduler's two again after their test closed its waiting steps, `p-3-12-f-mutate-place.log`). (1) **An armed End Turn ends the turn on a view fetched after the last chat closed.** It fired on the view in hand, fetched before the deal (a `negotiation_status` closes the chat; the view refreshes only on `update` and `turn`), so a decision the deal brought (a technology received that finishes the research, a city) never stopped a plain arming, as `PLAYING.md` said it would. `ChatDock.fire` now disarms, waits for `GameScreen.syncView` (any refresh in flight, then a fresh one), and ends the turn only if nothing holds it then, as the arming click would: a plain arming stops at the new decision (shown, as End Turn shows it), a Ctrl one ends anyway. No fresh view came: armed again, tried a second later at a tick (never in a loop). A chat opened meanwhile: armed again, for that one. (2) **A chat's clock after a page load.** The view's `diplomacy` carries no clock and the server sends a status only on a change, so after a reload a chat waiting on you read "Your move" (no countdown) and End Turn "Waiting for Rome…" with none; the chat tests' fake view had a clock the real one lacks. `ChatDock.onView` reads, once at each version, every open chat it holds no clock for (`clockAsked`); the fake view now has none, and only the route's answer does. (3) **The windows keep clear of the game screen's panels.** `dockLayout` takes the panels shown (`obstacles`: the unit panel, the city panel, the side panel, the minimap; `GameScreen` passes them, a `ResizeObserver` and `renderPanels` place the windows again when one is shown, hidden or grown): a panel that comes down into the row's lowest 200 px takes its columns from the row, which is the free stretch nearest End Turn; one higher up lowers the windows under it. Where they cannot keep clear (the narrow layout), an open unit or city panel lies over the dock (`z-index` 8 over the dock's 7) and End Turn over everything (9). (4) **A window closed with ✕ comes back with the other side's news.** It came back only when the chat turned to you, so their accept or decline, or the clock's close, went unseen. `consider` now brings it back for their entries or the chat closing as well (at 700 px or less as a lit tab, as news of a known chat lights its tab rather than take the screen); a chat first seen closed (a page load's recent chats) is not news. (5) **The narrow layout's tab strip keeps its buttons** (`drawStrip`, keyed on the tabs' civilizations, names and colours; each tab's look changes in place), so a tap is not lost to the redraws that run on every status and top bar. (6) **The wide layout never folds every window**: with no room for one window beside a tab for each of the others it falls back to the narrow layout (the review's probe: 1000 px, End Turn 280 px, six windows). (7) **A late place in the line no longer stands on a chat.** `ScheduledConversation.heard` dropped a late `queued` only once the step had started; a wait cancelled before its slot came (a suspension's halt, a seat change) never started, so the notifier's late `queued` reached the chat after the answer's `done` and read "queued #2" through the suspension. A conversation now passes its place on only while a step waits for its slot (`_waiting`, around `acquire` and the busy re-wait). (8) **Each window counts its messages against the cap** ("12/30 messages (18 left)", warning with two or fewer left; "…: the next closes the chat" at the cap; `ChatModel.messageCount`), as the old Diplomacy popup did. (9) **End Turn's click is the dock's** (`ChatDock.endTurnClick(force, blockers)`: `open`, `disarm`, `blocked`, `arm`, `end`, `none`); `GameScreen.onEndTurn` only calls it, so the Node tests exercise every branch. Gates again: (1) `node --test "citar/web/tests/*.test.mjs"`: 60 tests pass (37 the chat window's, 19 in `chat.test.mjs` and 18 in `chatmodel.test.mjs`): End Turn's click in every branch; an armed End Turn stopped by a decision the deal brought (plain), ending anyway (Ctrl), on a view fetched after the close, once; tried again a second later when no view came, held by a chat opened meanwhile; a reload's clocks read once per version; a window closed with ✕ brought back by the close and by their word, a page load's closed chats not; the strip's buttons kept through redraws and a tap made before them landing; the count; the panels kept clear of; the 500 seeded sizes now with random panels (no window or tab over one when wide; the active window never folded, never every window), the panels' cases, the review's probe. (2) S6: 37 modules parse. (3) S8: `qa_phase3.py chat` 14 of 14 in 86 s (`C:/dev/target/p-3-12-f-qa-chat`), and with `--keep` (`C:/dev/target/p-3-12-f-qa-e1`), against which E1 ran again at 1280 by 800 and 700 by 800 (28 of 28 checks at 1280 px and 29 of 29 at 700 px, 20 and 21 screenshots; screenshots in `C:/dev/target/p-3-12-f-qa-e1/e1/`), with this round's checks added: [x] the window counts the chat's messages; [x] after a reload mid-chat it still counts down ("Waiting for you, 3:5x left") and End Turn says to answer it; [x] ✕ while the model thinks, and the window comes back with its accept as the armed turn ends (on a view fetched after the close; at 700 px its tab lit, which brings it up); [x] a city's panel open: the three windows keep clear of it at 1280 px, and at 700 px it lies over the dock, End Turn over both; closed, the windows are back beside End Turn. (4) S1 1193 tests OK in 422 s on the final tree (1192 before this round, plus its scheduler test; 476 s at an earlier run before that test closed its steps); `test_scheduler`, `test_chat_clock`, `test_chat_responders`, `test_session_leases`, `test_ledger` and `test_chat_routes` under `TZ=UTC0` (151 OK in 61 s) and on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` (151 OK in 63 s); S2 ruff clean; S3 208 routes, 180 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded; S4 37 files, 189 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict` (PLAYING, ARCHITECTURE), the wiki regenerated. P3.9.7: the chat clock's tick with 20 open chats, median 0.016 ms (S1's own measurement); the session's overhead 1.994, 1.979 and 2.126 (median 1.994; the limit 2.87). No Rust changed, so no S7. CI-only: test.yml on its three OSes.

### P3.5.8 The client's payloads (Phase 2's handoffs)

Phase 2 left these to Phase 3's web work (P3.0, P3.1.5). They come after the chat window, since the deal builder moves there.
- **The recap reads `Delta`.** `replay.js` decodes the `Delta` replay format (keyframes plus deltas, as the journal stores them: changed tiles as 8-byte records, newly explored tiles varint-delta coded per major, the cities list, units at 8 bytes each, the event range; `crates/citar-engine/DESIGN.md` "Replay data"), with the unit palette `unit_ids` the engine already sends for it, in a pure module (`citar/web/js/replaydecode.js`, tested with `node --test` against a committed fixture, `citar/web/tests/fixtures/replay-delta.json`: a 100-turn game with a reload, its `Delta` replay and its `Full` frames, recorded once before `Full` is retired). `/api/games/{gid}/replay` serves `Delta`; `replay_data(Full)` and its encoder are retired from the engine and the facade (the 8.2 table's row), and the server tests that compared `Full` frames compare `Delta` bytes. The recap also shows chats as threads, with their statuses and close notes.
- **View ETags.** `/api/games/{gid}/view` answers with an `ETag` built from everything its body is made of: the engine's `rev()`, the session's `version`, a counter the session bumps whenever `info()` would change (pause, agent status, seats), and the perspective (the seat, or the spectator's `as_player`). A request with a matching `If-None-Match` gets `304` without building the view; the client sends it on every refresh. A test holds that two answers with the same ETag have identical bodies, and that every kind of change moves it.
- **Trade options on demand.** The client view's `diplomacy` block stops carrying `trade_options` for every met civilization (`api/views/players.rs:370`; Rust: a flag on `diplomacy_info`, with the `get_diplomacy` tool keeping them, since a model reads them there); `EngineGame.trade_options(pid, other)` (a new binding of `views::players::trade_options`) and `GET /api/games/{gid}/trade-options/{other}` (a seat token for its own seat; a spectator of a god-view game) serve them, and the chat window's deal builder fetches them when it opens and after each entry in its chat. `refcheck/intended.toml` gains `client-view-trade-options-on-demand` (group `views`).

**As built in 3-13: the client's payloads** (P3.5.8; Appendix D). One agent, 2026-10-13, from 03:30 to 05:09 (-04:00), 99 minutes, on branch `p-3-13` from `bce8d36`. Rust changed.

- **The fixture first.** `citar/web/tests/fixtures/replay-delta.json.gz`, recorded by `record_replay_delta.py` beside it on the tree at `bce8d36` (the one build with both formats): three bot civilizations, a city-state and barbarians on a 22 by 14 map played by the compiled bot to its turn limit of 100, reloaded in place at turn 70 (the `reload` test operation), so its 100 frames hold keyframes at rounds 1, 65 (64 frames on) and 70 (the reload), with deltas between; its `Delta` records and its `Full` frames, and the replay's palettes. Gzip with mtime 0: the full frames repeat every layer, 417 KB of JSON stored as 19 KB.
- **`citar/web/js/replaydecode.js`** (pure). `decodeRecord` reads a record as `save::journal` lays it out (keyframes with their palettes, deltas of changed tiles and LEB128 gaps of newly explored land, the cities, the units, the event range), refusing what does not decode with its reason (another version or kind, a record and bytes that disagree, a delta before any keyframe or from another base turn, counts past the bytes left, positions past the palette, a capital flag that is neither 0 nor 1, bytes left over). `ReplayFrames` finds the records that decode in one pass (a failed one and the deltas after it until the next keyframe are left out, as the engine's decoder leaves them) and serves any round by walking from the nearest keyframe, at most 64 records (`steps`), or on from the frame in hand, updating one frame in place: memory stays at one frame however long the game. `fullShape` gives a frame as `Full` did (improvements and features renumbered by name into the replay's palettes, units named), which the tests compare. `chatThreads` gives the chats a round shows (each with an entry that round, as it stood at its end: its entries in `seq` order, its final status once the entry that closed it is in), `describeTerms` a stored proposal in words, `plainMessages` the round's messages less the chats' own words (the engine records each chat entry as a message to the other side too).
- **The recap** (`replay.js`) reads the frames through it and names what a frame shows by the frame's own palettes. Its Diplomacy tab shows the round's messages and its chats as threads (bubbles, the opener's on the right, proposals as cards, the game's notes in grey, earlier rounds' entries dimmed, the status at the end of the round from `CLOSED_TEXT`).
- **`Full` is retired.** `Game::replay_data()` and `replay_json()` take no format and send the records as stored, only those that decode (`replay::decodable_frames`): a delta names its base by a hash the browser does not compute, so the server sends a chain that holds. `ReplayFormat`, `full_frame_json` and the renumbering went; the binding's `replay_data(format="delta")` refuses `"full"` ("The 'full' replay format was retired in 0.1.6: ...") and anything else; the facade's `replay_data()` is the `Delta` replay with `config` as `EngineGame.config` gives it. The testkit's replay test decodes the records with `FrameDecoder` against the captured frames and the state read round by round (the deltas sent are under a third of the frames expanded); the determinism test reads `replay_data()`; `test_saves`' autosave chain compares the frames' `Delta` bytes after the reload, and `test_engine_module` a reloaded game's records.
- **View ETags.** `/view` answers with an `ETag` and `Cache-Control: private, no-cache`; an `If-None-Match` naming it (or its weak form, or in a list, or `*`) gets an empty 304 before the view is built, after the game's gate (a stranger with the tag is a 404). The tag (`app.view_etag`) is a blake2b over the game object (`EngineGame.instance`, random per object: a loaded game counts again from 0), the commands it has taken (`EngineGame.writes`; a query tool is a read and never counts, as the fix round made it), the perspective and the keys the server splices in, hashed as they are written (the seat or the spectator flag, `info()`, `version`, `can_manage`). A view built while a command came in (the count moved during it) carries no tag. The client (`api.view(gid, token, asPlayer, etag)`, `cache: "no-store"` so the 304 reaches it) sends the tag of the view in hand on every refresh and keeps that view on a 304, skipping the map model's rebuild; `GameScreen.viewSeq` counts both kinds of answer.
- **Trade options on demand.** `diplomacy_info(g, pid, limit, with_trade_options)`: the client view passes `false`, `get_diplomacy` `true`; `views::players::trades_with` (a living major other than itself that it has met, or any once the game is over, as the view's own list) and `trade_options_with`; `Game.trade_options` in citar-py (None for anything else, players the game lacks included) and `EngineGame.trade_options`; `GET /api/games/{gid}/trade-options/{other}` gives `{player, with, trade_options}` to a seat token for its own seat (`as_player` ignored) and to a viewer of a game whose god view is allowed through `as_player`, and 404 otherwise. `refcheck/intended.toml`'s `client-view-trade-options-on-demand` (group `views`, `rust = { is = "absent" }`, cited where the flag is read) explains the trade options each civilization's diplomacy no longer lists; the changelog's rule fixes were written again (`cargo refcheck changelog --write`). The chat window's deal builder (`ChatWindow.wantOptions`, `fetchOptions(civ)`) fetches them while it is open: on each opening, after each entry in its chats, and at each turn; one fetch at a time and once more for what came meanwhile; a 404 clears the chips, a blip keeps them.
- **Choices the design left open, and departures.** (1) The fixture is gzip (`replay-delta.json.gz`, not `.json`): 19 KB stored instead of 417 KB. (2) The ETag is not built from the engine's `rev()`: that moves on writes to the state, and a message, a thought or a host's event is history, which the view shows (the test sends a message: the revision stands, the tag moves). The binding counts its own write calls instead, under the game's lock and before each call (`Game.writes`; `Game::rev`'s and `Heads.revision`'s documentation now says what they count). Nor is it a counter the session bumps whenever `info()` would change: `info()` is hashed itself (0.034 ms, the agents' `last_error` included, which changes outside the session), so the tag is right by construction. (3) The decoder names units (and improvements and features) by each keyframe's own palette, not by the replay's `unit_ids`, which are the current ruleset's: a frame recorded under another ruleset still names what it showed. `unit_ids` stays in the payload. (4) The server sends only the records that decode, so the browser never needs the base hash. (5) The deal builder fetches while it is open rather than whenever its window is, and also at each turn: a folded builder shows nothing, and what either side could give moves with the turn as well as with the chat. (6) An armed End Turn reads `viewSeq`, not the view object: a 304 keeps the object, and the old identity check would have re-armed for ever on a quiet game. (7) The recap leaves the chats' words out of the round's messages, since its threads show them. (8) `tests/test_engine_boundary.py` lets `citar/web/tests` reach the extension, as `tests/` may (the recorder reads both formats). (9) Gate 1's folder form (`node --test citar/web/tests/`) still fails on Node 24 (3-06's and 3-12's note); the pattern form test.yml uses runs.
- **Gates.** (1) `node --test "citar/web/tests/*.test.mjs"`: 73 tests pass (13 new: 11 in `replaydecode.test.mjs`, 2 in `chat.test.mjs`): every frame of the fixture's `Delta` replay decodes to exactly its `Full` frame, in order, and again read at random (213 reads, backwards and across both keyframes and the reload), a jump decoding from the nearest keyframe; made-up frames round-trip, a frame of another ruleset is renumbered by name, a record cut short or lost drops itself and the deltas after it, malformed records are refused with their reasons; the chats as threads and the messages without their words; the builder's fetches and an armed End Turn on a 304. (2) `tests/test_client_payloads.py` `Replay`: the route's and the facade's frames are the stored records (a keyframe, then deltas, each a version 1 record), a reloaded game sends the same; `replay_data("full")` raises ValueError; `test_saves` and `test_engine_module` compare `Delta` bytes. (3) `ViewETags`: one tag, one body throughout; a move, a message (the revision standing), a thought, a resume, a pause, an agent's status and a seat's change each move it from where it was (a pause back to the state after the thought gives that state's tag and body again); another seat's view, another civilization's eyes and the god view each their own tag (an administrator and the owner see the same god view: one tag, one body; the spectator link's `can_manage` differs); `If-None-Match` (strong, weak, in a list, `*`) gets an empty 304 with `view_json` never called, a stale tag 200, a stranger with the tag 404; a view a command overtook carries no tag. (4) `TradeOptions`: four civilizations, all met: no seat's view carries `trade_options` (not even the key's name in the bytes); the route gives seat 0 exactly what `get_diplomacy` lists for each; while a person plays it 404s for a civilization not met, itself, one the game lacks, past a player id, a seat that met nobody, a seat token with another `as_player`, a stranger, a moderator, the owner and an administrator as viewers and the spectator link, and a visitor gets 401 or 404; in a game no person plays the owner (both sides), an administrator and the spectator link read through `as_player` (404 without it, or for an unknown seat), a stranger and a moderator get 404 and a visitor 401 or 404, an ACL viewer 200, and published `global` a moderator and a visitor 200; the engine's own test (`api::views::tests`) and the facade's hold the same rule, eliminated and game-over cases included. (5) S7: `cargo fmt --check`, clippy clean, nextest 1456 passed (2 skipped; the renumbering test went with `Full`, the trade options test came), the doctests, `xtask check`, refcheck `--strict --with-bot` on the mini and late fixtures clean with the new entry used (12 entries, 7 used; views 35 explained), `golden check` all 16 sets ok (unchanged). (6) E2, step by step in headless Chrome over the DevTools protocol against `qa_phase3.py chat --keep` (`C:/dev/target/p-3-13-f-qa-e2`), signed in as the harness's alice, at 1280 by 800 and 700 by 800, 13 of 13 checks each, 8 screenshots each in `C:/dev/target/p-3-13-f-qa-e2/e2/`: [x] the recap of the harness's finished game opens on its last turn from the replay's `Delta` records (a keyframe and 7 deltas, decoded in the page, none dropped, the last frame with cities, units and owned land); [x] scrubbed to turn 1, and Play moves through the rounds; [x] turn 1's chats as threads (alice's three: model A's declined, model B's "(no reply in time)" and the bot's expired), no message repeating them; [x] turn 3's trades as threads, one accepted, one expired; [x] the map as one civilization knew it; [x] in a fresh game the view lists the civilizations met without trade options, and its ETag sent back gets 304 through `api.js`; [x] the deal builder, opened, fetched the trade options and lists them as chips; a chip adds its item; after the chat moved the open builder asked again; [x] the game screen's refreshes send the ETag of the view in hand, every one after the first. (7) S1 1203 tests OK in 416 s (1193 at 3-12's merge); the whole suite on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` 1203 OK in 406 s; S2 ruff clean; S3 209 routes, 181 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded; S4 37 files, 191 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict` (PLAYING, API, ARCHITECTURE), the wiki regenerated; S6 38 modules parse as ES modules. Thirty mutations, each reverting one behaviour (the tag's three parts, the 304 and its order, weak tags, the overtaken view's tag, the route's two side rules, the facade's format and instance, the builder's six fetch rules and its hints' source, End Turn's `viewSeq`, the decoder's base check, its dropping, renumbering, gaps and walk, the threads' status and cut, the client view's flag, `trades_with`'s rule, the decodable filter and the binding's count), all caught on the final code (`C:/dev/target/p-3-13-mutate.log`, `p-3-13-mutate-rust.log`; three were first missed and their tests strengthened). - **Left for later packages.** 3-17: the minimap block joins the seat view, and the view's ETag covers it with nothing to do (any write moves `writes`). 3-19: the CHANGELOG (the recap decodes `Delta` frames and shows chats as threads; `replay_data("full")` refused; views answer with ETags and 304s; trade options left the view and have their own route, which the deal builder reads while open).
- **The fix round** (the review's major and two minors, all fixed; 2026-10-13, from 05:35 to 06:11 (-04:00), on the same branch). (1) **A query no longer moves the view's ETag.** The binding's `Game::execute` ran every tool through `write`, which counts `writes` before the call, so `get_unit`, `get_city` or `get_diplomacy` moved the tag while changing nothing (the review measured the revision standing and the next body byte-identical, yet a 200). The game screen asks for its selected unit or city after every refresh (`refreshDetails`), and models query freely, so nearly every refresh after one was a full view: the double refresh after a person's own order and `syncView` most of all. A tool the registry calls a query (`tools::kind`) now runs under `read` through `execute_query` (property P8: a query changes nothing), a poisoned game refusing it in the same words as a command; an unknown tool and every action, refused or not, still go the command's way and count. Tests: `test_engine_module` (`writes` and the revision stand on five queries by either player and on refused ones: an unknown unit, a missing argument, no such player, arguments that are not JSON; a refused action counts one, a move moves both; a poisoned game refuses `get_empire` and `get_unit` with the command's words and counts nothing); `test_client_payloads.ViewETags` (through the tool route, `get_unit`, `get_diplomacy`, `get_empire`, `get_tile` and a refused `get_unit` between two looks each leave an empty 304 with `view_json` never called; a refused action gives the same body under a new tag, a move a new body); `qa_phase3.py chat` gains a check (alice's view, `get_unit` through the route, her view with the first look's tag: 304); E2 gains a step (a unit selected, the page's own socket handed the server's `update` twice: each refresh a 304, each followed by its `get_unit`). The facade's, the stub's, `view_etag`'s and ARCHITECTURE's words on `writes` say a query never counts. (2) **`crates/citar-engine/DESIGN.md`** no longer lists `replay_data(ReplayFormat)` among the host methods or a `ReplayFormat` row in the types table (now `replay::decodable_frames`). (3) **A record that is no frame at all is a `FrameError`.** `atob`'s error on bytes that are not base64 is a `DOMException`, which `ReplayFrames` rethrew, so one malformed record failed the whole recap; `recordBytes` now refuses bytes that are not base64, a record with no bytes (or bytes that are no text) and anything that is no object, each as a `FrameError`, and `ReplayFrames` leaves it out with the deltas after it. Tests: each refused with its reason; garbage before a keyframe drops alone with every frame around it served, garbage in a run of deltas with the deltas after it. Mutations: the query's read path reverted (both new tests fail), its poisoned check removed (the poisoned test fails on the words), `recordBytes` reverted (both new Node tests fail). **Gates rerun:** (1) `node --test "citar/web/tests/*.test.mjs"` 74 of 74 (one new); (2)-(4) `tests/test_client_payloads.py` and `test_engine_module` OK; (5) S7: `cargo fmt --check`, clippy clean, nextest 1456 passed (2 skipped), the doctests, `xtask check`, refcheck `--strict --with-bot` clean (12 entries, 7 used; views 35 explained), `golden check` all 16 sets ok with the same hashes; (6) E2 at 1280 by 800 and 700 by 800, 14 of 14 each (`C:/dev/target/p-3-13-fixr-qa-e2/e2/`, 9 screenshots each), the new step's requests `view 304, get_unit, view 304, get_unit` at both widths; (7) S1 1205 tests OK in 416 s; the whole suite on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` 1205 OK in 408 s; S2 ruff clean; S3 209 routes, 181 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded; S4 37 files, 191 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict`, the wiki regenerated (Architecture); S6 38 modules parse as ES modules.
