## P3.6 3D: hybrid seats

### P3.6.1 Today

The engine stops for a hybrid seat's diplomat and lets its bot defer a chat (P3.0); the bot has its switches, its advice and its valuation; the facade can make a bot with owners and drive it. The server has no hybrid seat type, and `BotAgent` drives a `hybrid_diplomat` stop straight on.

### P3.6.2 The seat

```
{"type": "hybrid", "name": "...", (civ_name, nation, leader, color, difficulty as for any seat)
 "handicap": "ai" | "human",                                                   the seat's existing key
 "bot": {"profile": "standard", "aggression": 0.4},
 "llm": {"server_id": "...", "model": "...", "persona": "...", ...},          as an LLM seat's (P3.2.9)
 "hybrid": {"owners": {"trades": "llm", "chat": "llm"} | "trade_only" | "all_but_war" | "everything",
            "advisor": true, "prompt_every": 10}}
```
- **Difficulty and handicap** use the keys every seat already has, which `normalize_seats`, `create_from_scenario` and `set_controller` read: `difficulty` is the level (`set_difficulty`), `handicap` (`ai|human`) is whose numbers the seat plays with. A hybrid seat's `handicap` defaults to `ai`; the lobby labels it "AI bonuses, like the bot" or "a person's values, like a model". There is no third spelling inside `hybrid`.
- **Validation** (`citar/agents/hybrid.py: normalize_hybrid`): owners through `engine_api.bot_set_diplomacy` on a scratch bot (its messages are the errors); at least one category the model's ("a hybrid seat with nothing for its model is a bot seat"); the seat's `handicap` `ai` or `human`; `prompt_every` 1..50 (default 10); `advisor` (default true in lobby games; always false in benchmark games).
- **Presets:** trade only = `trades`, `chat`; all but war = every category but `war`; everything = all ten.
- `Seat` gains `hybrid`; `SEAT_TYPES` gains `hybrid`. Saved with the session record.
- **The engine's seat:** `{"controller": "hybrid", "handicap": seat.handicap or "ai", "auto": {"un_vote": owners.un == "bot", "conquest": owners.captured_cities == "bot", "free_picks": true}}` at creation; `update_seat` and scenario launches call `set_controller` the same way. With `un` the model's, the engine no longer votes for the seat: the model votes with `un_vote`, and a vote it does not cast is an abstention, as for a person. With `captured_cities` the model's, a captured city stays a puppet until the model decides with `city_status`.
- **Scenarios:** `normalize_seats` keeps a seat's `hybrid` object as it keeps `llm` and `bot` (Rust, 3-14).

### P3.6.3 The turn

- **`HybridAgent`** (`citar/agents/hybrid_agent.py`) holds the seat's bot handle (its profile, its owners), a **fallback** handle (the same profile, every category the bot's; both use the seat's one memory in the game, so the fallback carries on the bot's plans), the model's configuration (`servers.resolve_llm`), and the diplomat's state (the last phase's turn and standings, the last event id read, the chats' threads).
- **`play_turn`** (on the driver thread):
  1. under the lock, `session.drive_bots(pid)`, which now drives every seat with a bot (bot and hybrid seats): the bot plays and leaves the model's categories alone;
  2. the drive stops at `hybrid_diplomat` and the lock is let go;
  3. if a trigger fired (P3.6.4) and no phase ran this turn, the diplomat phase (P3.6.6) runs, outside the lock, on one ticket;
  4. under the lock, drive again: the turn ends, or the drive stops at `awaiting_reply` for chats that wait on others (waited for as a bot seat waits, P3.5.2) or on the seat's own model (a chat it deferred, which its responder or the clock's fallback answers).
- Bot seats keep `BotAgent`; only hybrid controllers stop at `hybrid_diplomat`.

### P3.6.4 Triggers

From the events since the last phase and the standings:
- first contact with a major civilization; war declared on the seat; peace made; the seat denounced;
- a deal of the seat's expired or cut (`deal_expired`, `deal_cut`), or ending next turn (from `get_diplomacy`'s until-turns);
- a UN vote open, when `un` is the model's; a city the seat captured, when `captured_cities` is; a city-state event about the seat (`cs_ally_lost`, `cs_meet`, `cs_quest`), when `city_states` is; a `spy` event, when `espionage` is;
- the seat's score, or its military strength against a met civilization, changed by more than 25% since the last phase;
- `prompt_every` turns since the last phase.

At most one phase per turn per seat. A chat that comes in is not a trigger: the responder answers it whenever it comes (P3.6.5).

### P3.6.5 Answering chats

- A chat waiting on a hybrid seat goes to its bot first (inside drives, or `EngineGame.answer` from the responder): the bot answers what it owns and defers the rest.
- A deferred chat (its proposal touches a category of the model's, or it is talk and `chat` is the model's) goes to the seat's model through its responder queue (P3.5.3): a `ChatThread` on a `chat` ticket whose deadline is the clock's, with the diplomat prompt and the chat tools of the model's categories.
- When the ticket is refused or expires, the model's answer fails, or the clock runs out, the fallback bot answers (`EngineGame.answer(pid, nid, fallback)`) and the metrics record a fallback with its cause. On a machine held by a benchmark, the bot therefore answers at once in a lobby game (P3.3.3); in a hybrid benchmark game the ticket waits instead (P3.6.9).
- **One voice per seat at a time.** While a seat's diplomat phase runs, the seat's chat responder holds: chats that come back to the seat during the phase are given to the phase (they appear in its next user message as late replies do, P3.5.3), not to a separate `ChatThread` on its own ticket, which would race the phase's model on the same negotiation. When the phase ends, the responder dispatches whatever is still waiting.

### P3.6.6 The diplomat phase

- **One `diplomat` ticket:** at most 30 s to start (a phase that cannot start soon is skipped and the bot's defaults stand), a 180 s budget once started (the plan's default; the seat's `hybrid.phase_seconds` overrides), at most 8 steps.
- **The system prompt** (`DIPLOMAT_SYSTEM` in `citar/agents/prompts.py`, the same for every call of a seat, so a provider can cache it): "You are the diplomat of {civ} in CITAR, a turn-based strategy game in the style of Civilization V. A scripted bot runs everything else for your civilization: its cities, research, units and the fighting of any war. You decide only these kinds of diplomacy: {the model's categories, one line each}. The bot decides {the bot's categories}. A deal that includes anything the bot decides must be one the bot would accept as a whole: if it would not, your offer or acceptance is refused and you are told why. You are called when something happens (first contact, a declaration of war, a deal running out, a United Nations vote, a large change in strength), every {n} turns, and whenever another civilization talks to you. Each call is short: read what you are given, act with the tools, then stop by answering without a tool call. Every negotiation entry needs a message. You have {model_minutes} minutes to answer a chat that waits on you; the other side has {their_minutes} (a person) or {model_minutes} (another AI) to answer yours, and a chat closes when its time runs out or at {cap} messages. Keep your agreements and suspicions in your notebook (write_notes): it is shown at every call." Then the persona, if any.
- **The user message:** the triggers in words; the diplomatic situation (the seat's `get_diplomacy`, compacted: met civilizations with relations, treaties and their until-turns, open chats, recent messages, the UN); the empire at a glance (`empire_summary`); the notebook's last 2,000 characters; with the advisor on, "THE BOT'S VIEW" (`bot_advice`: deal values in gold from your side, war readiness per rival, spare luxuries, what it would ask for).
- **Tools:** `get_diplomacy`, `get_players`, `get_empire`, `get_victory_status`, `read_notes`, `write_notes`, `log_thought`, and per category the model owns: trades, agreements, peace → `open_negotiation`, `respond_negotiation`; war → `declare_war`; denounce → `denounce`; un → `un_vote`; city_states → `get_city_states`, `city_state_action`; espionage → `get_espionage`, `move_spy`, `stage_coup`; captured_cities → `get_cities`, `city_status`; chat → `send_message`, `respond_negotiation`. The session refuses any other tool the seat's model calls ("That is the bot's to decide.").
- **The veto on mixed deals** (plan 6.1). Before the seat's model opens a negotiation, or answers one with `counter` or `accept`, the session works out the proposal's categories. If any is the bot's, it asks `EngineGame.bot_evaluate(pid, bot, other, give, receive)` (a new binding of `citar_bot::evaluate`, 3-14); a value below zero refuses the call: "The bot decides <categories> and values this deal at <v> gold for you, so it will not agree. Leave those items out, or make the deal better for <civ>." What lies in the model's own categories is the model's to give.
- Tool calls go through `session.call_tool`, taking the lock per call; model calls are outside it.

### P3.6.7 Every place that asks "is this an LLM seat?"

`Seat` gains `model_seat` (llm, or hybrid with a model), `task_class` (`full` for llm, `partial` for hybrid, none otherwise) and `has_bot` (bot or hybrid).

| Place | Today | Phase 3 |
|---|---|---|
| `Seat.public`'s `llm_info` (`session.py:48`) | llm | model seats |
| `_track_turn`, `metrics_report` (`:212-221`) | llm's model | model seats; hybrid rows marked |
| `drive_bots` (`:257-261`) | bot seats | `has_bot` |
| `_dispatch_negotiation_interrupts` (`:378-384`) | bot, llm | bot, llm, hybrid, through the responder queues |
| `get_agent` (`:450-462`) | bot, llm | and hybrid: `HybridAgent` |
| `_quiet_hours`, `queue_machine`, `_make_way` (`:606, 651, 664`) | llm | full seats; a hybrid's tickets meet quiet hours themselves |
| `SessionManager.track` (`:1513`) | any llm | any model seat |
| `pool/seats.occupied` (`:58`) | llm | leases only (P3.3.5) |
| `pool/seats.authorize` (`:211`) | llm | by task class (P3.3.7) |

### P3.6.8 The lobby and the game screen

- The seat types gain "Hybrid (bot plays, model negotiates)", with both pickers (the bot's profile and aggression; `llmForm` for the model), the category grid (ten bot/model toggles and the three presets), the seat's `handicap` ("AI bonuses, like the bot" or "a person's values, like a model") beside its difficulty level, the advisor toggle and "prompt every N turns". The same in the Join/Seats dialog (`lobby.js:172-195`) and the scenario seats (`scenario.js`).
- The game screen's player list shows a hybrid seat's state: "bot playing", "diplomat thinking", "queued #n".

### P3.6.9 Benchmarks, probes and paired control games

- **Suites:** a scenario gains `mode: "full" | "hybrid"`, and for hybrid `owners` (a preset or a map) and `bot_profile` (default the standard profile). A hybrid job seats the model as a hybrid seat with the advisor off. Its prompts (diplomat phases and chat answers) are tickets with `wait_for_benchmark` and no start deadline, so a phase waits for its slot instead of being skipped after 30 s: a benchmark waits for its model rather than let the bot answer. The chat clock does not count a chat's time while its answer's ticket is queued behind a benchmark lease (P3.5.2), so a wait for another benchmark never turns into a fallback. The clock's model timeout still applies to a model that is reachable but silent once its ticket has started; that fallback is recorded with its cause and counts against the model. The benchmark scheduler counts a running hybrid job against its machine's job limit and never starts one on a machine where a benchmark lease is live (P3.3.5), so a hybrid job's diplomat phases do not sit behind a full benchmark for hours.
- **The paired control game:** every hybrid job has one, with the same configuration and seed and the hybrid seat played by the same bot profile with every category the bot's. The control seat **copies the hybrid seat's `handicap` and difficulty level**, so the pair differs only in who decides the model's categories; its automatic decisions are a bot seat's (the engine votes and annexes for it), since those are the very categories the model takes over in the other arm. Bot-only, it runs headless on the server's CPU (a Python loop over `EngineGame.drive` with the diplomacy recorder), once per (scenario, seed, build id, the bots' fingerprints, the handicap and level), cached in the benchmark directory and shared by every model.
- **Probes:** a probe run gains `mode: "hybrid"` with `owners`. Offer and message cases go to the subject's bot first and to its model when the model owns them, and record which answered; turn cases play the bot's turn, then a diplomat phase with every trigger forced.

### P3.6.10 The diplomacy leaderboard

- **`citar/diplomacy_stats.py: DiplomacyRecorder`** follows one seat through a game's events: proposals made and received; deals made; the acceptance rate of the seat's own proposals; wars declared by and on it; cities captured and lost; peace made; final score, alive, winner. Hybrid sessions attach it to their hybrid seats; control games to the same seat.
- **Both arms are valued the same way.** Many negotiations open and close inside one `EngineGame.drive` (bot to bot, and every proposal the control seat makes, since its game drives all its bots together), so they never reach Python while open and `bot_advice`'s `deal_value` would be null for them. The recorder therefore values every proposal entry the seat made or received with `EngineGame.bot_evaluate(pid, fallback, other, give, receive)` (3-14's binding) on the entry's recorded terms, read from the game's negotiation history after each drive, answer or tool call that produced it, against the state at that moment, in either arm alike. An accepted deal keeps the value of the proposal that was accepted.
- A hybrid job's result carries the recorder's rows and its control's id. A pair's line: the score difference (hybrid minus control) at the end and at turns 100, 200 and 300 where reached, deals made, total and mean deal value, acceptance rate, wars and their outcomes.
- **`GET /api/models/diplomacy`** (members): per model and owners preset, the pairs, the mean score difference with a 90% bootstrap interval (2,000 seeded resamples), deals per game, mean deal value, acceptance rate, wars declared per game, captures and losses. Only benchmark pairs count (a lobby game has no control), only runs the caller may see, and pooled data under the minimum-contributor rule (P3.2.4). Fallbacks whose cause is not the model (`queued_behind_benchmark`, `refused`) are listed beside the line and not counted as the model's.
- The Models page gets a "Diplomacy" tab beside the model scores.

### P3.6.11 Metrics

`metrics.data["diplomat"]`: one row per phase (pid, turn, triggers, steps, `gen_s`, `queue_s`, tokens, outcome `done|timeout|skipped|refused`, the tools called). A hybrid seat's turn rows carry the bot's action counts, as a bot seat's do.

**As built in 3-14: hybrid seats: the agent, the diplomat, the veto, the seat sites** (P3.6.2-P3.6.7, P3.6.11; Appendix D). One agent, 2026-10-13, from 06:47 to 08:25 (-04:00), 98 minutes, on branch `p-3-14` from `95b4944`, the whole package in one sitting (no split). Rust changed: the binding, `citar_bot::evaluate_offer`, `normalize_seats` and their tests.

- **The seat** (`citar/agents/hybrid.py`). `Seat.hybrid` (saved with the session record), `SEAT_TYPES` with `hybrid`, and `Seat.model_seat`, `task_class` and `has_bot`. `normalize_hybrid`: `owners` a preset (`trade_only`, `all_but_war`, `everything`) or a map, read through the engine's own parser on a scratch bot (its messages are the refusals) and kept as the whole map, with `preset` when it is one; at least one category the model's; `prompt_every` 1..50 (10); `advisor` (true; off in a benchmark game); `phase_seconds` 10..600 (180). The seat's `handicap` is `ai` unless it says `human`. The engine's seat: `set_controller(pid, "hybrid", handicap, engine_auto(owners))` at creation (`SessionManager.create`), scenario launch (`create_from_scenario`) and `update_seat` (which now takes `hybrid` and `handicap`, checks the block before it changes anything, and gives a seat that stops being hybrid its new controller's handicap and automatic decisions explicitly, since the engine keeps what was set explicitly). `normalize_seats` keeps `hybrid` (Rust; `scenario-seats-keep-hybrid` in `tests/rules/intended.toml`, its testkit test, the changelog's rule fix).
- **The turn** (`citar/agents/hybrid_agent.py: HybridAgent`). Under the lock `drive_bots` (every seat with a bot: bot and hybrid seats); at `hybrid_diplomat`, off the lock, the triggers and, when one fired, the phase; under the lock the drive again, and at `awaiting_reply` a wait for the chats it stops on (those waiting on another seat, and those its bot left to its model, whose responder answers them) that wakes on any move of them, past the safety cap closing another seat's as expired and having its fallback bot answer its own. The bot handle carries the owners; the fallback handle (the same profile, every category the bot's) shares the seat's one memory in the game. The model's configuration is read the first time it is needed, off the lock; a seat with no model block dials nothing (its bot answers everything).
- **Triggers** (`_triggers`, P3.6.4): from the events since the agent's last look (its mark starts at its creation) and the turn's `get_diplomacy` and statistics: first contact with a major, war declared on the seat, peace, a denunciation of it, a deal of its that expired or was cut, a deal's term or a treaty that ends next turn, the United Nations vote open and not cast (`un` the model's), a city it captured (`captured_cities`), a city-state's news about it (`city_states`), a spy's (`espionage`), its score or its strength against a met civilization changed by more than a quarter since the last phase that ran, and `prompt_every` turns since that phase (or the seat's first turn). At most one phase a turn; a seat whose model owns nothing is never called.
- **The phase** (`_phase`): one `diplomat` ticket (`pool.seats.ticket`, admitted for `pooled` for the game's owner, the game's queue priority, `ref` the game; 30 s to start, eight steps, `phase_seconds` once started; in a benchmark game it waits for benchmarks with no deadline, P3.6.9), the static `DIPLOMAT_SYSTEM` (both chat timeouts and the cap, the model's categories one line each, the bot's listed, then the persona), the message `DIPLOMAT_PHASE` (the triggers in words, `get_diplomacy` compacted without trade options, the empire at a glance, the notebook's last 2,000 characters, and with the advisor `bot_advice` as THE BOT'S VIEW), and the tools of `hybrid.diplomat_tools`. Each call goes through `GameSession.diplomat_call`. Its row is `metrics.data["diplomat"]` (turn, triggers, steps, `gen_s`, `queue_s`, tokens, outcome, cause, the tools called with `ok`); the seat's summary adds `diplomat_phases`, `diplomat_outcomes`, `avg_diplomat_gen_s`, `avg_diplomat_queue_s`, `avg_diplomat_steps`, `diplomat_tools`, `diplomat_refused`. The game screen hears `agent` statuses `diplomat` and `queued` (with its place) during a phase.
- **Chats** (P3.6.5). A chat waiting on a hybrid seat goes on its line as a `bot` answer (its bot's pass, `EngineGame.answer`), which, deferred, queues the `model` answer on the same line; the model answers in a `ChatThread` (one conversation per chat) on a `chat` ticket whose start deadline is the chat clock's, through `DiplomatModel` (the diplomat's prompt, `hybrid.chat_tools`, `diplomat_call`, and with the advisor the bot's value of the deal in each message). A refused or expired ticket, or a model that fails or uses its steps without answering, has the fallback bot answer at once (`GameSession.fallback_answer`), with a `fallback` row and its cause (`queued_behind_benchmark`, `timeout`, `refused`, `failed`; `llm_agent.cause_of`, which now maps an expired ticket to `timeout`); the chat clock's `chat_fallback` makes the seat's agent when it has none. **One voice:** while a phase runs, a model answer of the seat returns `held` at once and the phase is told the chat (`DIPLOMAT_CHAT`, before its next step); an answer already under way when the phase starts is waited for 30 s at most, then cancelled; when the phase ends the line is dispatched again. A hybrid seat's chats are dispatched on its own turn too (the turn is its bot's), and a loaded game's hybrid bots answer at once, their models after the resume.
- **The veto and the filter** (`HybridAgent.veto`, `GameSession.diplomat_call`). A tool none of the seat's categories gives it: "That is the bot's to decide.", counted (`metrics.diplomat_refusal`, `why` `category`). An `open_negotiation`, or a `respond_negotiation` counter or accept, whose deal touches a category the bot decides: the bot's value of the whole deal from the seat's side, below zero refused with P3.6.6's message, counted (`veto`). The deal is the engine's own reading of the call (`EngineGame.bot_evaluate_call`, fix round): its arguments coerced and checked as `execute` does, so no spelling of the model's (a key the engine drops, an amount as text, `Accept`) reaches the engine as a deal the veto did not value; a call the engine would refuse is left to it. The veto and the call share one hold of the game's lock, so the deal accepted is the one valued.
- **Rust.** `citar_bot::evaluate_offer(g, spec, pid, other, give, receive)` is `evaluate` of the proposal `open_negotiation` would make of written items (`deals::make_proposal`), 0 for none; citar-py's `Game.bot_evaluate` binds it (read-only, the GIL released) and the facade's `EngineGame.bot_evaluate(pid, bot, other, give, receive)` wraps it; `_engine.pyi` has it. Fix round: `Game::offer_of_call(pid, tool, args)` (citar-engine, `api/tools/execute.rs`) is the deal a tool call would put on the table or conclude, read and checked as `execute` would (the tool's coercion, its guard, the action's own check: an opening's proposal, a counter-offer, the proposal accepted; none for a reply, a rejection, a refused call), and `citar_bot::evaluate_call` values it with its categories, bound as `Game.bot_evaluate_call` and wrapped as `EngineGame.bot_evaluate_call(pid, bot, tool, args)` -> `(categories, value)` or None.
- **The seat sites** (P3.6.7, Python rows): `Seat.public` names a model seat's machine (`llm_info`) and gives a hybrid seat's block; `_track_turn` and `metrics_report` file model seats under their model, a hybrid row marked (`hybrid`, `bot`, `owners`) and the report's `diplomat`; `drive_bots` by `has_bot`; the dispatch and `get_agent` with hybrid; quiet hours, `queue_machine` and make-way stay the full seats' (a hybrid seat's prompt in its machine's quiet hours is refused and its bot answers); `SessionManager.track` any model seat; `occupied` leases only (a hybrid seat holds none); `authorize` by class (3-09); the probes' live thoughts and the reports' seats are the model seats' (the reports with `controller` and `class`); the game summary gives a model seat's `class` and a `label` ("Hybrid: standard + dry-run"); scoring leaves hybrid seats' rows out of the model figures (live and saved); usage lists every seat with its `class`.
- **The dry-run diplomat** (`providers/dryrun.py`): in a phase it looks (diplomacy, empire, and its categories' views), then acts once in each category it decides where it can (from its system prompt's lines): a trade (maps) or an agreement the trade options allow, or peace when at war; an abstention when a United Nations vote is open; an annexation of a puppet; a war when at peace with everyone; a denunciation; 100 gold to a city-state; a spy moved to its capital; a message; and answers the chats the phase was told of as configured.
- **Choices the design left open, and departures.** (1) Military strength is the turn's statistics' `military`, each side's plus 20, and a score swing is measured against at least 20: the first turns' armies and scores would swing by half at every unit. (2) The triggers of a phase that did not run (refused, skipped) wait for the next; a phase that did not run still uses the turn's one try. (3) Outcomes `failed` (the model's error) and `cancelled` (the seat halted) join P3.6.11's four. (4) The dry run abstains at a United Nations vote: a vote for anyone can end a short game with a diplomatic victory. (5) A chat answer whose borrowed slot was taken back (`yielded`, `borrow_cap`) falls back rather than retrying (3-09's note left either). (6) `respond_negotiation` is always among a hybrid chat's tools: a chat its bot left to it is its model's to answer, whatever the category (a war item's included). (7) The diplomat's state is the agent's, not the save's: a load, a restart, a suspension or a seat change starts a new agent, whose triggers read the events from its creation and whose `prompt_every` counts from its first turn. (8) `evaluate_offer` lives in citar-bot, so the bot's code id (and the build id) changes, as any engine change does. (9) `update_seat` takes `hybrid` and `handicap`; the route's `SeatUpdate` fields are 3-15's (P3.6.8's "the seat route accepting hybrid"). (10) `ChatThread` asks its agent for its system prompt, tools, calls and an extra note (`chat_system`, `chat_tool_names`, `chat_call`, `chat_note`), which `DiplomatModel` overrides; an LLM seat's chats are as before.
- **Gates.** (1) `tests/test_hybrid.py` `Categories`: for each of the ten categories a seeded 4-seat small game, 60 rounds, the hybrid seat's dry-run model owning only it, every civilization met: the bot proposed and accepted nothing of `trades`, `agreements` or `peace` (every entry of the seat's that its model did not make, an open's or counter's proposal and an accept's table) while the model proposed it; the seat declared exactly the wars its model declared; no `declare_war`, `denounce`, `city_state_action`, `move_spy`/`stage_coup`, `city_status` or `send_message` among its bot's actions while the model's happened (a gift of gold, a spy moved, a captured city annexed after it stayed a puppet, messages); the United Nations' counts with the model abstaining equal those with a model that never votes, and with `un` the bot's the first count has the seat's vote more; no fallback in any. With every category the bot's (a test-only seat) the hybrid seat's game equals a bot seat's with the same handicap, event for event (60 rounds, more than 500 events). (2) `Fallbacks`: a chat ticket refused while a benchmark lease holds `sv_gpu` (the fallback answered in under 5 s, `queued_behind_benchmark`, the model never asked); a phase refused the same way on three turns (each turn ended, no phase conversation); a ticket that does not start before the chat's 2 s deadline (`timeout`); a model that fails (`failed`, never "(no response)"); in fake time the chat clock's fallback answered in the tick that found the chat due (open at 599 s, answered at 600, the model's answer cancelled); 100 rounds with a model that never answers: every turn ended `end_turn`, every chat it was given a `fallback`/`failed`, every phase `done`. (3) `Veto`: the model's offer of 300 gold and an embassy for an embassy (agreements the bot's), valued below zero, refused with the message (its value in it, nothing opened, counted); an embassy for an embassy and gold, above zero, opened; 400 gold for nothing (trades only, the model's) opened though below zero; an accept and a counter of a mixed deal below zero refused, a better counter sent. (4) `Triggers`, a person and the hybrid seat (all but war, `prompt_every` 50) and two bots: each of first contact, a war declared on the seat, peace (its model accepting the person's), a denunciation, a deal's term ending next turn then its expiry, a resource deal cut short, a city-state's meeting, a spy, a captured city (a puppet), a score swing and a strength swing fired a phase that named it; two at once fired one phase naming both; the United Nations vote on turn 15; never two phases a turn. `Schedule`: `prompt_every` 3 calls on turns 4, 7, 10 and so on, its trigger named exactly when three turns have passed. (5) `Tools`: `declare_war`, `un_vote`, `move_unit` and `end_turn` from a trades model, and `send_message` in its chat, refused "That is the bot's to decide." and counted (`diplomat_refused` 5); over three turns' phases and chats the seat's system prompt is one text, naming 1.5 minutes for a model and 4 for a person (the game's 90 and 240 s), the cap, its categories' lines and the bot's. (6) `OneMachine`: an LLM seat and a hybrid seat on `sv_gpu` (one slot): the LLM seat's trade answered by the hybrid's model (accepted, `answered`, one conversation, no fallback); a counter that came back during the seat's phase was held for the phase and answered by it (no conversation of its own, no chat row); one that came back after its phase was answered by its model on its own ticket, and only then did the seat's turn end. (7) `crates/citar-refcheck/tests/bot_evaluate.rs`: `evaluate_offer` equals `citar_bot::evaluate` to the bit, from each side, on every recorded deal of the committed states' `deal_checks` (92 recorded deals, 85 of them with a proposal), refuses as the engine refuses those that do not read, and equals the recorded `bot_value`; S7 below. (8) `SeatSites` (every Python row above) and `tests/test_hybrid_routes.py` (a hybrid seat made through `POST /api/games`, refused with the reason for bad settings, refused a member without a machine (400) or a grant (403); the summary's `class` and `label`; a stranger 404, a visitor 401 or 404). (9) `python scripts/qa_phase3.py hybrid`: 14 of 14 in 50 s (`C:/dev/target/p-3-14-qa-hybrid`): bob's dry-run machine shared with alice for pooled prompts only (a full seat refused her, 403), the seat labelled, a trade answered by its model, a declaration of friendship by its bot, a slow trade answered by the chat clock's fallback bot at 20.4 s (`timeout`), 30 rounds played, 10 phases on turns 4 to 30, all done, 9 on schedule, the seat's summary, 6 statuses of kind `hybrid` on alice's socket, no lease on bob's machine, no traceback. `chat` again: 19 of 19 (85 s). (10) S1 1248 tests OK in 430 s (1205 at 3-13's merge); Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` (the package's and the chat modules): `test_hybrid`, `test_hybrid_routes`, `test_controllers`, `test_chat_responders`, `test_chat_clock` and `test_metrics`, 101 OK in 45 s; S2 ruff clean; S3 209 routes, 181 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded (no route added); S4 37 files, 196 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict` (AI_PLAYERS, ARCHITECTURE, API), the wiki regenerated; S6 38 modules parse, 74 Node tests pass; S7 `cargo fmt --check`, clippy clean (`-D warnings`, after a float comparison in the new test), nextest 1458 passed and 2 skipped, the doctests, `xtask check`, refcheck `--strict --with-bot` on the mini and late fixtures clean (12 states, every group 0 unexplained; intended 12 entries, 7 used), `golden check` all 16 sets ok; `cargo refcheck changelog --write` added the rule fix. Mutations: nineteen, each reverting one behaviour (the veto, the filter, the bot's first pass, two fallbacks, the phase's hold and its notes, the engine's vote, the drives' bots, the schedule, two triggers, scoring, a chat on the seat's own turn, leaving hybrid, the clock's fallback, usage's class, the summary's label, the dry-run diplomat's trade), all caught (`C:/dev/target/p-3-14-mutate.log`). P3.9.7: the session's overhead 2.126, 2.175 and 2.121 (median 2.126; the limit 2.87); the chat clock's tick with 20 open chats, median 0.017 ms. - **Left for later packages.** 3-15: the seat route's `SeatUpdate` gains `hybrid` and `handicap` (the session takes them); the lobby's form, the Join/Seats dialog and the scenario editor's seats send `{"type": "hybrid", "handicap", "difficulty", "bot", "llm", "hybrid": {"owners", "advisor", "prompt_every"}}`; the 22 JavaScript seat-label places ("Hybrid: <bot> + <model>", as the summary's `label`); the game screen's states from `agent` statuses (`thinking` while the bot plays, `diplomat`, `queued` with `position`); the stats screen's diplomat rows from the summary's `diplomat_*` keys and `metrics_report()["diplomat"]`. 3-16: the recorder values each proposal with `EngineGame.bot_evaluate(pid, agent.fallback_bot, other, give, receive)`; hybrid tickets already wait for benchmarks with no start deadline in a benchmark game (`session.benchmark`), and the advisor is off there; a control game is a bot seat with the hybrid seat's handicap and level. 3-19: the CHANGELOG (hybrid seats, the presets, the veto, the fallback, the scenario seats' `hybrid`).
- **The fix round** (the review's five majors and six minors, all fixed; 2026-10-13, from 08:46 to 09:42 (-04:00), 56 minutes, on the same branch, rebased onto `v0.1.6` at `bc7ae9d`; each fix's test fails on the code before it, and 28 mutations, the first round's nineteen and nine of the round's own, are all caught: `C:/dev/target/p-3-14-fix-mutate.log`, `p-3-14-fix-reverse.log`). (1) **The veto values the engine's own reading of the call.** It had read the items with the strict per-item reader (`item_category`), which refuses keys the engine drops, amounts written as text or floats and terms an item has none of, and left such an item uncategorised, so a mixed deal the engine then opened was never valued (an embassy with a `note`, `"amount": "300"` while trades are the bot's). `Game::offer_of_call(pid, tool, args)` (citar-engine) is the deal a call would put on the table or conclude, read and checked as `execute` would read it (the tool's coercion, its guard, the action's check: an opening's proposal, a counter-offer, the proposal accepted; none for a reply, a rejection or a call the engine refuses); `citar_bot::evaluate_call` values it with its categories, bound as `Game.bot_evaluate_call` and wrapped as `EngineGame.bot_evaluate_call(pid, bot, tool, args)` -> `(categories, value)` or None. `hybrid.items_categories` is gone; `Categories` reads a model's deal from the engine's record. Tests: the testkit's `a_call_is_valued_as_the_deal_the_engine_would_make_of_it`; `Veto.test_the_items_are_read_as_the_engine_reads_them` (a dropped key, text and float amounts, a term on an embassy, the partner as text, trades the bot's) and (2) `Veto.test_the_action_is_read_as_the_engine_reads_it` (`Accept`, `ACCEPT`, ` accept `, `COUNTER`, `Counter`, a string id), since the engine reads a response in any case, stripped. (3) **A pause holds the phase** as `set_paused` promises for an AI mid-turn: before its next model call and before each tool call; its ticket goes back for the pause (a pooled prompt on another member's machine must not hold its slot through a pause of hours) and is taken again on the resume, with the steps left and 30 s to start (a phase whose new ticket does not come ends `skipped` or `refused`, its triggers kept); paused time does not count against `phase_seconds`; the seat's status is `paused` meanwhile. `PhaseHolds` (a pause in the first step: no tool call and no step while paused, the one-slot machine free, the call made and the phase `done` after the resume, with a second of phase time against a pause of 1.5 s; a pause before the first call; a seat halted while paused ends its phase `cancelled`). (4) **Every change of controller sets the engine seat's settings explicitly.** The engine keeps a handicap or an automatic decision once set explicitly, and a hybrid seat's are, so they stayed behind through later changes (bot, hybrid, bot, LLM left an LLM seat voting and annexing for itself; hybrid, LLM, bot left a bot with a person's numbers and no free picks). `Seat.engine` keeps what the seat's own entry chose (its lobby or scenario entry's `handicap` and `auto`, or a `handicap` given to `update_seat`; never a hybrid seat's, which follow its owners), saved with the session record; `update_seat` sets the new controller's handicap and all three decisions from it and the controller's defaults at every change. `Creation.test_nothing_of_a_hybrid_seat_stays_behind_through_later_changes`. (5) **One agent per seat.** `get_agent` made a bot or hybrid seat's agent off the lock with no re-check, so the driver and a responder (or the chat clock) asking at once after a seat change, a resume or a load could each get their own, splitting a hybrid seat's diplomat state and its one voice; it is now made and kept in one hold of the lock (its constructor reads no database). `Creation.test_concurrent_askers_get_the_seat_s_one_agent` (four threads, a slowed constructor). (6) `prompt_every` and `phase_seconds` must be finite numbers: `Infinity` (which Python's JSON reads) raised `OverflowError` through `POST /api/games`, a 500; now 400 with the reason (`Settings`, and the route with `Infinity`, `-Infinity`, `NaN` and `1e400`). (7) 3-13's flaky test: v0.1.6 fixed it (`_stand_still`, `bc7ae9d`), so the branch was rebased onto it without this package's own fix of it (`f284577`), which conflicted. (8) **Pending triggers are kept by what they are about** (`pending`, a map): a swing is measured again at every look (replaced, or dropped when the standing came back), a dated trigger (a term or treaty ending next turn, the vote open) is dropped once its date has passed or the vote closed, and the events' triggers are the latest twenty with a count of the rest ("and 11 earlier events not listed"); `fired` holds only the dated triggers a phase that ran was told, until their date has passed. `PendingTriggers` (a look at a fake game through four turns of phases that did not run, then one that did). (9) **The phase takes the seat's voice before its ticket**, so its 30 s to start no longer run while a chat answer of the seat's own model finishes on their one-slot machine. `PhaseHolds.test_the_seat_s_own_chat_answer_does_not_use_up_the_phase_s_start` (a 2.5 s answer, 1 s to start: `done`, not `skipped`). (10) **The metrics keep codes, not words**: a diplomat row keeps `Metrics.DIPLOMAT_FIELDS` only and a refusal no `detail`, since the game's viewers read its metrics and a ticket's refusal names another member's machine and group; the model's error is the seat's `last_error` and the session's errors, as before. `PhaseHolds.test_the_rows_keep_codes_not_words` and `test_hybrid_routes`' ACL viewer reading `/metrics` (200, no note, a stranger 404). (11) AI_PLAYERS says the game's metrics carry the diplomat's calls (the stats screen is 3-15's), and says what a pause does; ARCHITECTURE names `bot_evaluate_call` and `offer_of_call`. **Gates rerun:** gates 1-6 and 8 in `test_hybrid` (50 tests, 40 before) and `test_hybrid_routes` (4); gate 7 and S7: `cargo fmt --check`, clippy clean, nextest 1459 passed and 2 skipped (the new testkit test among them, and `bot_evaluate_is_the_bots_evaluate_on_every_recorded_deal`), the doctests, `xtask check` (249 files), refcheck `--strict --with-bot` clean (every group 0 unexplained; intended 12 entries, 7 used), `golden check` all 16 sets ok (`C:/dev/target/p-3-14-fix-s7.log`); gate 9 `qa_phase3.py hybrid` 14 of 14 in 50 s (10 phases on turns 4 to 30, all done, 9 on schedule; the clock's fallback at 20.4 s), `chat` 19 of 19 in 86 s; S1 1260 tests OK in 449 s (the known seven tracebacks); Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` on the package's and the chat modules, 112 OK in 33 s; S2 ruff clean; S3 209 routes, 181 gated, 28 public, 0 unguarded; S4 37 files, 197 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict`, the wiki regenerated; S6 38 modules parse, 74 Node tests pass. P3.9.7: the session's overhead 2.159, 2.161 and 2.142 (median 2.159; the limit 2.87); the chat clock's tick with 20 open chats, median 0.016 ms.
