**As built in 3-15: hybrid seats in the lobby, the seats dialog, scenarios and the game screen** (P3.6.8 and the JavaScript half of P3.6.7; Appendix D). One agent, 2026-10-13, from 10:17 to 11:15 (-04:00), 58 minutes, on branch `p-3-15` from `51deaa4`, the whole package in one sitting (no split). No Rust changed.

- **`citar/web/js/seats.js`** (new, pure: no DOM, no network). The ten kinds of diplomacy in the engine's order with their words, the presets, the handicap's two choices ("AI bonuses, like the bot", "A person's values, like a model"); `ownersMap`, `presetOf`, `modelCategories`, `newHybrid`, `hybridState` (a seat the server sent, back into the form's shape), `hybridBlock` (the owners as a preset's name when they are one), `hybridProblem` (the server's refusals, said first); `seatPayload` (the lobby's seat for `POST /api/games`: a hybrid seat with its `handicap`, `ai` unless chosen, and its `hybrid` block; any other seat as before) and `seatUpdate` (the seat route's body: what the chosen type plays, no handicap for a seat leaving hybrid); `isModelSeat`; `seatLabel` ("Hybrid: <bot> + <model>", the bot marked "(best)" as a bot seat's is, "no model" while it has none; the other seats as the lobby named them); `agentState` (a hybrid seat's `thinking` "bot playing", `diplomat` "diplomat thinking", `queued` "queued #n", `paused` "diplomat paused"; an LLM seat's as before); `queuedText` (moved from `game.js`); `controllerLabel`, `diplomatText`, `outcomeText` for the stats screen.
- **The lobby** (`lobby.js`): the seat kinds gain "Hybrid (bot plays, model negotiates)"; `hybridForm` holds the bot (profile and aggression, shared with the bot seat's `botFields`), the seat's handicap labelled with the level it applies at ("Handicap at King", redrawn when the level changes; the difficulty stays in the seat's row), its model (`llmForm` with `purpose: "pooled"`, so the machines are listed with the admission a hybrid seat's pooled prompts get, and without the per-turn settings a diplomat has no use for), the grid of ten Bot/Model toggles with the three presets (the matching one lit, "a choice of its own" otherwise, and the warning when the model would decide nothing), the advisor and "Call the diplomat at least every N turns". Create checks `hybridProblem` first. `llmForm` now shows a shared machine's refusal for the form's purpose on any machine, not only a helper's. The Join/Seats dialog (`showConnectInfo`) offers the same kinds and form (its handicap read from the engine through `GET /api/games/{id}`'s `players[].handicap`), sends `seatUpdate`, shows a refusal's reason and relabels the seat. `playHuman` keeps a loaded game with any model seat (a hybrid one included) paused.
- **Scenarios** (`scenario.js`): the editor's default seats and the launch dialog take hybrid seats (the editor's without a model: the launch dialog names it); `scenarioSeat` sends a hybrid seat as the lobby does, and drops the hybrid settings and handicap of a seat that is no longer hybrid (keeping a non-hybrid seat's own loaded handicap). The editor keeps the seats being edited across tab switches.
- **The game screen** (`game.js`): the Civs list labels each seat (`seatLabel`) and says what an AI seat is doing (`agentState`), redrawn on each `agent` message; the turn box's "waiting for" note uses the same words; statuses the socket has not spoken of are seeded from the view's `session.agent_status`; the quiet-hours pill covers every model seat, a hybrid seat's worded as what it means (its bot decides until they end). **The stats screen** (`metrics.js`): `controllerLabel` for every row, the first AI row a model seat's, and for a hybrid seat a Diplomat section (who decides what, `diplomatText`, the tools, and the last fifteen calls: turn, outcome with its cause code, what called it, steps, model and queue time, tools with refused ones struck). The phone site counts hybrid seats and says what their diplomat does; the recap labels seats with `seatLabel`.
- **The server.** `SeatUpdate` takes `hybrid` and `handicap`, passed to `update_seat`; the session's `ValueError` is answered with its reason (400) instead of "Invalid seat type."; a model seat made at the route (hybrid included, through `MODEL_SEATS`) is admitted again by its class at every such change, as before. `update_seat` pins "Best bot" (off the lock) as creation does. `info()` gives each major's `handicap` beside its `difficulty`. The scenario editor checks a hybrid seat's settings and handicap when they are set or saved (`_scenario_seat`, as the fix round made it: 400 naming the seat), answers a seat the engine cannot read with 400 (it was a 500: `normalize_seats`' `ActionError` was not caught), and seats a hybrid controller in a new scenario's game; a scenario made from a running game keeps its hybrid seats' settings, bot and handicap (no machine). `create_from_scenario` leaves a default hybrid seat's handicap and settings behind when the launch makes it another type (it had played a bot with a person's numbers). The summary's hybrid label and `metrics_report` (`bot_best`) mark a "Best bot" as the client does.
- **Choices the design left open, and departures.** (1) The handicap select sits in the hybrid form beside the bot, labelled with the seat's level, rather than in the seat row's difficulty cell, whose 120 px cut its words ("A person's val…"). (2) The "22 places": every seat label and seat check in `citar/web/js` reads `seats.js`; `probes.js`'s subject (the first LLM seat) stays as it is, since probes' hybrid mode is 3-16's. (3) A bot seat's agent status still reads "thinking…" (only the hybrid seat's states are new). (4) The scenario editor's hybrid seats carry no model; the launch dialog asks for it, as it does for an LLM seat.
- **Gates.** (1) `tests/test_hybrid_lobby.py` `LobbyPayloads`: each hybrid seat of `citar/web/tests/fixtures/hybrid-seats.json` (trade only, all but war, everything, two grids of their own; `ai`, `human` and the default), as a member granted pooled prompts on the shared dry-run machine, starts with the engine's hybrid controller, the handicap, the automatic decisions (worked out in the test from the owners), the owners, the level and civilization the form chose, and `GET /api/games/{id}` lists them back; without the grant, 403. `SeatRoute`: a bot seat made hybrid (all but war, a person's values) and back to a bot (the bot's numbers and decisions, nothing of the hybrid seat left), then refused once her grant is gone and refused an LLM seat (no grant for games), each refusal leaving the seat and its controller as they were, then hybrid again: `pool.seats.authorize` called with classes `partial, partial, full, partial`, never for the bot; settings and handicap changed through the route and five bad ones refused with the reason, nothing changed; another seat's own handicap; "Best bot" pinned, the summary's label and the metrics' `bot_best`; P3.9.1 (another member 404, anonymous 401 or 404, an ACL viewer 403, a moderator on a global game 403, the owner and an administrator 200); a member's seat describing a machine refused with nothing dialled (`NoOutbound`). (2) `Scenarios`: a scenario saved with a hybrid seat launches with it, from its defaults and from the launch dialog's seats, and with a grid and the AI's bonuses at launch; launched as a bot it keeps none of the handicap (unless the launch names one); bad settings refused at save naming the seat, nothing saved; a member names a machine at launch (400 without, 403 without the grant); a scenario made from a running game keeps its hybrid seat. 14 tests in all. (3) `citar/web/tests/seats.test.mjs`, 13 tests: the label for eighteen seat shapes, the fixture's payloads, the presets, the states, the stats labels. Browser D1 on `qa_phase3.py hybrid --keep` (`C:/dev/target/p-3-15-d1`, 13 screenshots and `d1-wide.json`, 15 of 15, driven over DevTools by the session's `p315/d1.py`): [x] in the lobby P1 made hybrid from a preset (all but war, then trade only, the grid following), bob's machine offered with no refusal, a person's values at King (the form redrawn keeping its choices), a call every turn; the game created so; the Civs list's "Hybrid: standard (best) + dry-run"; a trade (gold for its world map, built in the chat window's deal builder) answered by the seat's model (a model answer in the metrics), a declaration of friendship answered by its bot at once (no model answer); "bot playing" then "diplomat thinking" in the Civs list and the turn box on its second turn, its call done; the stats screen's Diplomat section with the call's row; the Join/Seats dialog showing the seat as it is, and making another bot seat hybrid and back; a scenario's default seat made hybrid (everything, a person's values, every 7 turns), saved, and launched through the launch dialog with it; no script error. (4) S1 1274 tests OK in 458 s (1260 at 3-14's merge; 5 tracebacks, all of the known set); the package's modules on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` (`test_hybrid_lobby`, `test_hybrid_routes`, `test_seat_blocks`, `test_editor`) 57 OK; S2 ruff clean; S3 209 routes, 181 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded (no route added; the seat route changed, its P3.9.1 tests above); S4 37 files, 202 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict` (PLAYING, API, AI_PLAYERS, ARCHITECTURE; CONTRIBUTING), the wiki regenerated; S6 39 modules parse as ES modules, 87 Node tests pass; S8 `qa_phase3.py hybrid` 16 of 16 in 50 s (3-15's two checks: the seat route there and back with an LLM seat refused between, a scenario's hybrid seat launched; no traceback in the server's log, D1 included), `chat` 19 of 19. Mutations: twenty-three, each reverting one behaviour (twelve of the server's, eleven of `seats.js`), all caught (`C:/dev/target/p-3-15-mutate.log`). P3.9.7: the session's overhead 2.164, 2.117 and 2.153 (median 2.153; the limit 2.87). CI-only: test.yml on its three OSes (its Node step included).
- **The fix round** (the review's six minors: five fixed, the sixth a reporting correction; 2026-10-13, from 11:36 to 12:12 (-04:00), 36 minutes, on the same branch; no Rust changed). (1) **The "(best)" mark is the server's.** "Best bot" was recorded only as the seat bot's `chosen_as`, which a client could send and which survived a profile change: the Join/Seats dialog sent a pinned seat's bot back with another profile and the mark, so the lobby, the summary's label and the metrics' `bot_best` said "X (best)" of a bot never chosen as best. `session._clean_bot` drops a `chosen_as` sent, at creation, at a scenario's launch and at the seat route; `_seat_bot` (was `_pin_best`) sets it when it pins "best"; `update_seat` keeps a seat's own mark only while the bot sent names the profile it was pinned to (the dialog's unchanged bot). `profileSelect` deletes `chosen_as` when its profile changes, and the bot fields of a pinned seat say "Chosen as the best bot when the seat was set up" (the select shows the profile pinned). A scenario made from a game asks for `"best"` again, pinned at launch. Found while fixing it: a seat's bot that is not an object was a 500 at creation, and an aggression that is no number was taken and refused only by the bot when its turn came; both are now a 400 (`clean_aggression`, held to 0..1), at creation, the seat route and the scenario editor. (2) **Machines listed with their own admission.** 918038e showed a shared machine's refusal on any machine, but the probe run dialog and the report narrator listed machines with their admission for games: a member granted pooled prompts saw a refusal on a machine that would take the work. The narrator asks for `pooled` (`NARRATIVE_PURPOSE`); the probe run dialog for its probe's `purposes`, which `GET /api/probes` now lists (`case_purposes`: `probe` for turn cases, `pooled` for offer and message cases; not `pooled` alone, as the review suggested, since a turn case holds a probe's lease); `seatServers` takes several purposes, each machine's admission the first refusal among them (`seats.js` `firstRefusal`). (3) **Seats named as the engine names them.** The server's own checks (`_seat_settings`, was `_engine_seat`; `_scenario_seat`) said "Seat 2" for P1 while the engine's `normalize_seats`, answering the same editor route, said "Seat 1": they now say "Seat {id}" as the engine does, whose number is the "P1" the lobby and the editor show. The engine's wording stays: `refcheck/query_tools.json.gz` records Python's "Seat 0: ..." and the query tools test compares it, and "P1" rather than "Seat 1" was not worth an intended difference and a Rust change. (4) **The scenario editor refuses blocks that are not objects.** `normalize_seats` keeps only objects, so a hybrid seat sent with `"hybrid": "everything"` (or a model block or a bot that is not an object) was saved with the defaults the caller never sent; `_scenario_seat` checks what was sent and refuses it as a game's seat does (400 naming the seat). A non-hybrid seat's `hybrid`, which nothing reads, is still ignored, as a game's is. (5) `game.js` no longer re-exports `queuedText`, which nothing imported. (6) test.yml (three OSes, its Node step) is CI-only and is reported as not run, not as passed. Tests: `SeatRoute.test_the_best_mark_is_the_server_s` and `test_a_bot_that_is_not_one_is_refused`; `Scenarios`' refusals at `meta` and `save` (ten seats, each named "Seat 1"), a non-hybrid seat's `hybrid` ignored and its bot kept clean, and a scenario from a game with "Best bot" re-pinned at launch; `test_hybrid`'s seat naming; `ProbeRunRoute.test_the_list_says_what_a_run_is_admitted_for`; the Node test of `firstRefusal`. Each fails on the code before the round (the tests run with the round's code stashed: 17 failures and an error), and 36 mutations, the first round's twenty-three and thirteen of the round's own, are all caught (`C:/dev/target/p-3-15-fix-mutate.log`). Gates again: S1 1277 tests OK in 455 s (5 tracebacks, the known set); the package's modules on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` (`test_hybrid_lobby`, `test_hybrid_routes`, `test_seat_blocks`, `test_editor`, `test_hybrid`, `test_pooled_work` and the Best bot tests) 152 OK; S2 ruff clean; S3 209 routes, 181 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded (no route added; `GET /api/probes` gains a field, its listing's access tests unchanged); S4 37 files, 205 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict` (API, PLAYING), the wiki regenerated; S6 39 modules parse as ES modules, 88 Node tests pass; S8 `qa_phase3.py hybrid` 16 of 16 in 49 s and `chat` 19 of 19, no traceback in either server's log; D1 again on the final code, 19 of 19 (`C:/dev/target/p-3-15-fix-d1`, 17 screenshots), with the round's four: [x] a lobby bot seat chosen as Best bot made hybrid in the dialog says so and keeps "(best)"; [x] another profile picked drops the note and the mark; [x] the probe run dialog shows no refusal for an offer-only probe on a machine granted for pooled prompts and the right refusal ("limited to pooled prompts, and this is probes") for one with a turn case; [x] the report narrator shows none. P3.9.7: the session's overhead 2.308, 2.290 and 2.369 (median 2.308; the limit 2.87). CI-only, not run: test.yml on its three OSes (its Node step included).
- **Left for later packages.** 3-16: `probes.js` picks its subject as the first LLM seat; a hybrid probe's subject needs `isModelSeat`. The Models page's Diplomacy tab can label pairs with `seatLabel`/`controllerLabel`. 3-19 (CHANGELOG): hybrid seats in the lobby, the Join/Seats dialog and scenarios; the game screen's and stats screen's hybrid states; a scenario's seat the engine cannot read is a 400 (was a 500); a scenario's hybrid seat launched as a bot no longer plays with the hybrid seat's handicap; "Best bot" pinned at a seat change, and its "(best)" mark kept only while the seat plays the profile it was pinned to; a seat's bot that is not an object, or whose aggression is no number, is a 400 (was a 500, or taken); the scenario editor refuses a seat's blocks that are not objects instead of saving defaults; the probe run dialog and the report narrator show a machine's refusal for the work they ask for.

**As built in 3-16: hybrid benchmarks and probes, control games, the diplomacy leaderboard** (P3.6.9, P3.6.10; Appendix D). One agent, 2026-10-13, from 12:39 to 14:25 (-04:00), 106 minutes, on branch `p-3-16` from `1a5a6d5`, the whole package in one sitting (no split). No Rust changed.

- **The recorder** (`citar/diplomacy_stats.py: DiplomacyRecorder`). It follows one seat. After every drive, answer and tool call (the session's `_after_action`, before its autosave; the control's loop after each `EngineGame.drive`) it reads the seat's negotiations that moved since its last look: new negotiation ids are found by probing `negotiation_head` past the last id seen (ids are sequential), the open ones are watched, and only a negotiation whose head shows new entries is read whole. Each new entry is a row (`nid`, `i`, `turn`, `by`, `with`, `action`, `proposal`, `value`, `categories`): a proposal's `value` is `EngineGame.bot_evaluate(pid, bot, other, give, receive)` on the entry's recorded terms (`proposal[str(pid)]` given, `proposal[str(other)]` received) against the game as it stands then, `bot` the seat's own profile with every category the bot's (`hybrid_agent.valuation_bot`, the hybrid seat's fallback bot); an accept's is the value of the proposal on its table when that proposal was recorded (never valued again). The game's events (`on_event`, a subscription) add rows for wars declared by and on the seat, peace, cities captured and lost, and eliminations. Rows are append-only; a session keeps them in `metrics.data["diplomacy"]` (encoded once per row by `Metrics.json_parts`, like the turn rows), so a save keeps them and a loaded game's recorder carries on from them (`_sync`: the entries recorded per negotiation, the value on each table). Every hybrid seat of every session has one (`GameSession._recorder`), dropped and remade (from its rows) at a seat change; they are not in `metrics_report()`, so a game's viewers do not read them. A recorder's failure is the session's error (`where: diplomacy record`), never the action's nor the event's (an elimination still lets its seat's lease go). `summarize` gives one arm's figures: proposals made and received, accepted own proposals and the rate, the deals with the value each was accepted at, their total and mean, wars with how each ended (`peace`, `eliminated them`, `eliminated`, `ongoing`) and the captures and losses inside each, peace, and with `final_of` the final score (0 once eliminated), alive, won and the scores at turns 100, 200 and 300. Measured on a 200-turn control game: 11.8 µs a look, 3% of the game's time (`C:/dev/target/p-3-16-recorder-cost.log`).
- **Control games** (`citar/server/controls.py`). `control_spec(s.engine_config, s.seats, pid, ops)` is the hybrid game's own engine configuration (`SessionManager.create` now keeps it as `GameSession.engine_config`), the seed drawn by the job when the scenario gives none so both games share it, the control seat's automatic decisions a bot seat's (`BOT_AUTO`) and its handicap and difficulty level the hybrid seat's, every seat's bot as the server pinned it ("Best bot" included). `control_key` hashes the spec, the build id and every bot's fingerprint. `run_control` plays it headless (one `drive(bots, seat_limit=1)` at a time, as a session drives its bot seats, the recorder looking after each) and returns the record: the rows, the summary with `final`, the events' count and SHA-256, drives and seconds. `ControlGames` (one per benchmark scheduler, `<benchmark dir>/controls/`) keeps each record once played (the last 64 in memory), plays the queue on one thread, writes the spec beside its record so a control under way when the server stops is played again when its job next asks (`_check_controls`, at every tick), and counts the games it played (`played`).
- **Hybrid suites and jobs** (`citar/server/benchmarks.py`). A scenario's `mode` is `full` (the default) or `hybrid`, with `hybrid`: `{"owners", "bot_profile", "handicap", "prompt_every", "phase_seconds"}` (`normalize_scenario_hybrid`: through `hybrid.normalize_hybrid`, the owners kept as the preset's name when they are one; a bad block is a 400 naming the scenario). A hybrid job seats the model as a hybrid seat with the advisor off (its tickets already wait for benchmarks with no start deadline in a benchmark game, 3-14), its bot the block's profile at the scenario's aggression, its game's `benchmark` marked `mode: hybrid`, and asks for its control at launch. `_mode_wait` keeps the modes apart: a hybrid job does not start (or resume after making way) where a full job of this scheduler is active or anyone's benchmark lease is live, and a full job does not start beside an active hybrid job; hybrid jobs count against the machine's job limit as any job does (`busy`). `MID_TURN` gains `diplomat`, so a pause's grace waits out a diplomat phase. A finished hybrid job's result adds `diplomacy` (the rows and their summary) and `fallbacks` (`fallbacks_of`: the seat's chats its fallback bot answered and its phases that did not run, by cause; `model` counts what the model caused, `other` the rest, departure 5), (a record that cannot be read leaves the job done with no pair line, and a log line), and once its control is played its `pair` (`pair_line`: the score difference at the end and at turns 100, 200 and 300 where both games reached them, the hybrid arm's figures and the control's beside them). `GET /api/benchmarks/runs` gives each job without the rows (`_job_view`) and each scenario's `mode` and `hybrid`. A run whose suite has an enabled hybrid scenario is admitted for `pooled` as well as `benchmark` on its machines, and a hybrid scenario's bot is one the member may use (`_suite_gate`). Hybrid jobs stay out of the model scores, live and saved (`scoring._reports`, `_benchmark_from_state`).
- **The leaderboard** (`diplomacy_stats.leaderboard`, `board`; `GET /api/models/diplomacy`, members). One line per model and owners preset (`owners_label`: the preset's name, or `custom: <kinds>`): pairs, the mean score difference with its 90% percentile bootstrap interval (2,000 resamples drawn by `random.Random(316)`, positions `round(q * 1999)`), deals per game, the mean value of the deals valued (their totals over their count), the acceptance rate over all the seat's own proposals, wars declared, captures and losses per game, the control's deals per game and mean value, `fallbacks_per_game` (the model's) and `other_fallbacks` by cause, the caller's own pairs named and `pooled_pairs` counted. The pairs are the runs' the caller may see (`items.listed`), and for a member only those of their own games and of games that pool (`aggregate.scope_for`), another member's part of a line withheld under the minimum-contributor rule (`aggregate.publishable`, `withheld` with the reason).
- **Probes' hybrid mode** (`citar/probes.py`). A run's body takes `mode: "hybrid"`, `owners` (trade only by default) and `bot_profile` (`hybrid_settings`; the scripted bot as a hybrid subject is a 400). The subject is a hybrid seat, the advisor off. An offer or message goes to its bot first (`EngineGame.answer`) and, when the bot leaves it to the model, to the model on a `probe` ticket through its chat machinery (`HybridAgent.model`: the diplomat's prompt, its kinds' chat tools, the veto); each result records `answers` (round by round: `bot`, `model`, `none`) and `answered_by`, and the run's summary counts them per case. A turn case plays the bot's turn, then a diplomat phase with every trigger forced (`HybridAgent.force_phase`: the phase is told "you are called now, whatever happened"), recorded as `diplomat`; it holds no lease (`case_purposes(..., mode)` is `pooled` alone, and the runner does not wait for a free slot before it).
- **The web client.** `citar/web/js/diplomacy.js` (pure; `diplomacy.test.mjs`, 7 tests) words a line, a pair line, a control's status and a probe case's answering side. The Models page gains its tabs, "Model scores" and "Diplomacy" (`#/models?tab=diplomacy`): the lines, each opening on the caller's pairs, the withheld lines under "Not enough pooled data yet". The suite editor's scenarios gain "Mode" and, for a hybrid one, who decides what (the presets and the grid, `seats.js`), the seat's bot, its handicap and how often its diplomat is called; a hybrid job's row shows "hybrid: All but war", its control game's state and its pair line ("+2 vs its control · 12 deals at 4.2 (control 4 at 6.3)"). The probe run dialog gains the mode, the kinds and the seat's bot (the machines listed for pooled prompts), its results say "answered by its bot/model", and a new probe's subject may be a scenario's hybrid seat (3-15's note).
- **Choices the design left open, and departures.** (1) The scenario's hybrid settings sit in a `hybrid` object (`owners`, `bot_profile`, ...) rather than beside `mode`: the scenario's own `bot_profile` already names the opponents' bot. (2) The control seat stays the engine's `hybrid` controller with every category its bot's (its automatic decisions all the engine's, as a bot seat's): 3-14's gate 1 shows the engine plays such a seat exactly as a bot seat with the same handicap, and its drives then stop where the hybrid game's do (its `hybrid_diplomat` stop, then the drive that ends its turn), so the recorder values both arms at the same points of the game; a `bot` controller would have valued the seat's own proposals only after its whole turn. (3) Controls play while their hybrid job plays (from its launch), on one thread; a job is done when its game is, and its pair line follows when its control is (at a later tick). (4) The leaderboard reads pairs that are both visible to the caller and theirs or pooled: a member who shares a run but opted out of pooling feeds nobody's lines. (5) Fallbacks counted against the model are its chats' fallbacks with cause `timeout` or `failed` and its diplomat phases that `failed`; a phase whose prompt never started within its 30 s (`skipped`, which a benchmark game's prompts, with no start deadline, never are) is the machine's (`not_started`, beside the line with `queued_behind_benchmark` and `refused`), and a phase that ran out of its budget (outcome `timeout`) ran, so it is no fallback. (6) A hybrid probe answer the model could not give is the case's error, not a fallback bot's answer: a probe measures the model. (7) Every hybrid seat has a recorder, lobby games' included, as P3.6.10 says; only benchmark pairs reach the board.
- **Gates.** (1) `tests/test_hybrid_bench.py` `Suite`: a hybrid suite (one scenario, the dry-run diplomat, seeds 21 to 23, 60 turns, three bots) runs 3 hybrid jobs and plays 3 controls (`played` 3, three distinct ids), each job's pair line its score minus its control's; the second model's run of the suite reuses the 3 (same ids, `played` still 3); a control played twice from its spec gives the same events (more than 300, equal lists and hashes, the cached record's hash); a restart asks for a control that was never played and the pair follows; normalization and refusals; hybrid jobs out of the model scores; the runs' list without the rows. (2) `Recorder`: on a scripted 4-player game (a counter accepted, a friendship declined, a map bought, a war declared and the city taken, a war declared on the seat and peace made) the rows and the summary equal a hand-made table, values computed at each proposal, an accept keeping its proposal's value with `bot_evaluate` replaced meanwhile (never called again); in a control every proposal and deal of the negotiations opened and settled inside one drive is valued; a pair whose hybrid seat has every category its bot's (test-only, at Emperor so the handicap matters) gives rows equal to its control's, a zero difference and equal deal values; the control keeps the hybrid seat's handicap and level and a bot seat's decisions; a hybrid seat's rows survive a save and a load, and recording carries on with no entry twice; a failing record never stops an event's own effects, and a job whose record cannot be read is done without a pair. (3) `tests/test_diplomacy_board.py`: pair lines and per-model lines on fixtures equal hand-computed values (mean difference, deals per game, mean deal value, acceptance, wars, captures, losses, the control's, fallbacks), the bootstrap interval its definition written out again (ten differences; not the 80% one, not another seed's); a fallback queued behind a benchmark or refused, or a phase whose prompt never started, is not counted against the model; the route: a run the caller cannot see left out, pooled pairs under the minimum withheld, an opted-out account feeding nobody else's lines, an administrator every pair named, a visitor 401; `test_items_access`' route table has it. (4) `Scheduling`: a hybrid job waits while a benchmark lease is live on its machine and starts once it goes; a full job waits beside an active hybrid job, and a hybrid job beside a full one, each starting when the other is skipped; hybrid jobs count against a one-slot machine's job limit. `BenchmarkQueue`, in fake time: a hybrid job's chat answer queued behind a full benchmark's lease holds the chat clock (`held: benchmark`) through 1,800 s, no fallback; the lease released, the model answers (`answered`, its own words). (5) `tests/test_pooled_work.py` `HybridProbes`: a hybrid run (trade only) on alice's one-slot machine records `answered_by` model for the gift and the message, bot for the friendship, model for the turn case's forced phase (`done`), every model call a partial-class step, no lease at any moment; `ProbeRunRoute`: a hybrid run admitted for pooled prompts only (gina's probes-only grant refused, dave's pooled-only admitted, its owner and an administrator 200), its settings refused with the reason (400), another member's private probe 404, a visitor 401; `HybridSuiteRoutes`: a hybrid run needs `pooled` as well as `benchmark` (403 naming the purpose; owner and administrator 200), a suite's hybrid settings and its seat's bot checked at save. (6) `qa_phase3.py hybrid` 23 of 23 in 60 s (`C:/dev/target/p-3-16-qa-final`): 3-16's seven: bob's second machine shared with alice for benchmarks and pooled prompts; her hybrid suite's two jobs (seeds 3361, 3362), each with its control; a job's seat hybrid with the advisor off; the other model's run reusing both controls; her board (two lines, two pairs each, hers named, the interval); bob none of hers, the administrator all, a visitor 401; a hybrid probe run's sides (gift model, friendship bot, message model, turn model). `chat` 19 of 19 (85 s). Browser D2 (`C:/dev/target/p-3-16-d2`, 7 screenshots and `d2-wide.json`, 11 of 11, driven over DevTools by the session's `p316/d2.py` on `qa_phase3.py hybrid --keep`): [x] the Models page's Diplomacy tab with her two models' lines (`d2-wide-01-diplomacy-tab.png`), [x] a line opened on her pairs (`-02`), [x] the Benchmarks page's hybrid jobs with their controls and pair lines (`-03`), [x] the suite editor's hybrid scenario (`-04`), [x] the probe run dialog's hybrid mode (`-05`), [x] the hybrid probe run's results, who answered each case (`-06`), [x] bob's Diplomacy tab without her runs (`-07`), [x] no script error. (7) S1 1310 tests OK in 556 s (1281 at 3-15's merge; 7 tracebacks, all of the known set: the worker's injected "disk on fire" twice, the registry import's JSON error and its `ImportFailed`, the helper's "the local model is not loaded"); the package's modules on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` (`test_hybrid_bench`, `test_diplomacy_board`, `test_pooled_work`, `test_hybrid`, `test_benchmarks`, `test_items_access`, `test_metrics`, `test_hybrid_lobby`) 200 OK in 164 s; S2 ruff clean; S3 210 routes, 182 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded (one route added, `GET /api/models/diplomacy`; the probe run, suite save and run start routes changed, their P3.9.1 tests above); S4 37 files, 211 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict` (BENCHMARKS, API, SCENARIOS, AI_PLAYERS), the wiki regenerated; S6 40 modules parse as ES modules, 95 Node tests pass (88 before); S8 above. Mutations: thirty, each reverting one behaviour (the recorder's sides, values, accept and sync, the capture's war, the session's look, the control's handicap, level, decisions, ops, key and cache, the mode rules both ways, the restart, the runs' list, the scores, the tickets' benchmark wait, the board's causes, pooling and bootstrap, acceptance, the probe's bot first and forced phase, the purposes, the run's admission, the suite's checks, the two guards), all caught (`C:/dev/target/p-3-16-mutate.log`); one more, the board's own scope filter, is equivalent (`publishable` keeps only the scope's games) and is not counted. P3.9.7: the session's overhead 2.165, 2.153 and 2.141 (median 2.153; the limit 2.87). CI-only, not run: test.yml on its three OSes (its Node step included).
- **The fix round** (the review's nine minors, all fixed; 2026-10-13, from 14:45 to 15:37 (-04:00), 52 minutes, on the same branch; no Rust changed; each fix's test fails on the code before it (with the round's code reverted, 13 of its tests fail, one in three subtests, and 2 error, `C:/dev/target/p-3-16-fix-before.log`; the three that pass hold the new rule's bounds, a started model's timeout, a lobby game's and a told phase's, and each is caught by its mutation), and 54 mutations, the first round's thirty (one re-anchored) and twenty-four of the round's own, are all caught: `C:/dev/target/p-3-16-fix-mutate.log`). (1) **A chat whose model never started is not the model's timeout.** In a hybrid benchmark game the chat clock holds only behind a benchmark lease (P3.5.2, unchanged), so a chat whose answer's ticket still waited behind other pooled work (a report's narrative, another game's hybrid prompts) ran out as `timeout`, which the board counts against the model, though P3.6.9 counts the timeout only once the prompt has started. The clock now works out the cause at the deadline (`ChatClock._cause`): in a benchmark game a hybrid seat's chat on which its model was never seen at work falls back as `not_started` (`chat.CAUSES`, `diplomacy_stats.OTHER_CAUSES`), listed beside the line; at work means its answer's own `thinking`/`running` status, or a diplomat phase step that ran with the chat told to it (the phase calls `ChatClock.model_told` after each step, since a chat that comes during a phase is the phase's). A lobby game's stays `timeout` (the owner's rule: its queue time counts), and a full seat's (its answer waits only on its own lease). The clock itself is unchanged: the review's other option, holding it for any queue in a benchmark game, would have made a benchmark wait without bound for work that is not a benchmark, which the design does not ask. `BenchmarkQueue`: a chat queued behind a report's running ticket on the one-slot machine (`not_started`, the model never asked, `fallbacks_of` model 0 and `not_started` 1); a model that started and hangs (`timeout`, model 1); the same queue in a lobby game (`timeout`); a chat held for a phase still waiting for its slot (`not_started`); a chat a running phase was told of and left past its time (`timeout`); the phase marks only the chat as it was told (a chat that moved since, or another seat's word, is not started); `test_diplomacy_board`'s fixture gains a chat's `not_started`. (2) **A record that does not read is no record.** `ControlGames.status` said `done` whenever the file existed, so a damaged record or one of another layout showed "control game played" for ever with no pair line; it is `done` only for a record `get` reads, else `missing`, asked for again and played again (written over), and the pair follows (`Suite.test_a_control_whose_record_does_not_read_is_played_again`, damaged JSON and another `format`; the reviewer's `rv316/ctl_status.py` now prints `missing`). (3) **Controls nobody can pair with are let go.** `_check_controls` follows only the jobs that can still pair (`_awaits_control`: active, or done with a whole record and no pair line; a retry asks afresh) and returns the controls they want; each tick `ControlGames.keep_only(wanted)` drops the queued ones nobody wants (a skipped, cancelled or failed job's, a deleted run's; the one under way plays on and is cached), so no stat per dead job every 2 s, and no control played, or failed again, after a restart for a job that will never pair (`Suite.test_a_job_that_cannot_pair_lets_its_control_go`). (4) **The control's bots are pinned.** `control_spec` stores each seat's bot as `pin_bot` resolves it at the job's launch (`profiles.resolve(ref, pin=True)`: the profile and its revision, the bot version, its parameters, the profile's fixed aggression and the seat's; a deleted profile the standard bot, as the game plays it), `_bots` makes the bots from the pins alone (`bot_handle`), and the key hashes the spec with them; a pin this build cannot make (a version no longer compiled in, a spec of the layout before the pins) fails the control rather than play another bot (`Recorder.test_a_control_plays_the_bots_pinned_when_its_hybrid_game_started`: the fingerprints the hybrid game's agents play, unchanged after the profile is edited and after it is deleted, the key the same, the control played; `basic-999`, no engine and the old layout refused). (5) **A probe case whose model's prompt did not run was answered by nobody**: `_hybrid_answer` returns `none` with the refusal (the case's error), as for a seat with no model (`HybridProbes.test_a_case_whose_model_prompt_did_not_run_was_answered_by_nobody`). (6) **The mode rule reads the machine a job really plays on**: `_mode_wait` compares each other job's `_machine` (its game seat's machine), as the resume path's `machine` is, not its suite's `server_key` (`Scheduling.test_the_mode_rule_reads_the_machine_each_job_really_plays_on`). (7) **A hybrid job loads its model beside the machine's work**: `_ensure_model` passes `shared=True` for a hybrid job, and `servers.ensure_model(..., shared=True)` unloads nothing, neither the other models (whatever the profile's `exclusive`) nor its own to reload it with a larger context (it is used as loaded); a full job's load is as before (`test_a_hybrid_job_loads_its_model_beside_the_machine_s_work`, `test_a_shared_load_unloads_nothing` over a mocked LM Studio). (8) **A record that fails stops once.** `DiplomacyRecorder.fail(turn, why)` marks it `broken` with a `broken` row (the error's kind only: the save keeps it, and a loaded game's recorder is broken from its rows), after which `observe` and `on_event` read nothing; `GameSession._record_failed` writes the traceback to `errors` once and a log line, from `observe`, `on_event`, or a recorder whose valuation bot could not be made. `_hybrid_result` marks a broken record `incomplete` and `_pair` pairs nothing then, as a control whose record fails fails; the job's row says "no pair line: its diplomacy record stopped (KeyError)" (`diplomacy.js: recordText`, its Node test). `Recorder.test_a_record_that_fails_stops_once_and_pairs_nothing` (a failure at the third look of a 12-turn game: one error, nothing read after, the row, a reloaded recorder broken, the result `incomplete`, no pair, its control not followed), and the first round's `test_a_failing_record_never_stops_an_events_own_effects` now says it once. (9) **Reports.** The Benchmarks section shows a hybrid job as "<model> · hybrid: <owners>", its Performance its pair line's score against its control and its deals ("+12 vs control · 3 deals", "control pending", "no pair (record incomplete)"), its average turn "–", and a note under the tables. Found while fixing it: `config_rollup` counted a hybrid seat's performance, wins, turns, turn time, error rate and clean ends among its model's game figures (the model comparison, "Best game performance", the cost per point and per win); a hybrid seat (`class: partial`) is now left out of them, its game still counted for its configuration (`Reports`, two tests). **Gates rerun:** gates 1 to 4 in `test_hybrid_bench` (31 tests, 16 before) and `test_diplomacy_board` (9); gate 5 in `test_pooled_work` (`HybridProbes` 2, `ProbeRunRoute`, `HybridSuiteRoutes`); gate 6 `qa_phase3.py hybrid` 23 of 23 in 61 s (`C:/dev/target/p-3-16-fix-qa`, no traceback in the server's log) and D2 again on it, 11 of 11 (`C:/dev/target/p-3-16-fix-d2`, 7 screenshots and `d2-wide.json`); `chat` 19 of 19 in 86 s; S1 1326 tests OK in 471 s (1310 before; 7 tracebacks, the known set); the package's modules with the chat clock's and the reports' on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0`, 262 OK in 138 s; S2 ruff clean; S3 210 routes, 182 gated, 28 public on purpose, 0 unguarded (no route added or changed); S4 37 files, 212 internal links; S5 `mkdocs build --strict` (BENCHMARKS, API, SCENARIOS), the wiki regenerated; S6 40 modules parse as ES modules, 96 Node tests pass (95 before). P3.9.7: the session's overhead 2.119, 2.115 and 2.081 (median 2.115; the limit 2.87); the recorder 12.1 µs a look, 1.8% of a 200-turn control game played with pinned bots (the same game as before: 113 rows, 3,546 events; `C:/dev/target/p-3-16-fix-recorder-cost.log`). CI-only, not run: test.yml on its three OSes (its Node step included).
- **Seen and not changed (fix round).** A game's seat agents, and with them its bots, are made again from their profiles whenever a new agent starts (a load, a restart, a make-way pause, a seat change: 3-14's departure 7), so a profile edited while a long benchmark job plays changes that game's bots from then on, while its control keeps the pins taken at its launch. Every game does this (a full job's bot opponents too); pinning a game's own seat bots is a change to the session's seats, left for later.
- **Left for later packages.** 3-19 (CHANGELOG): hybrid benchmark suites (a scenario's mode, its hybrid settings), control games and pair lines, the Models page's Diplomacy tab and `GET /api/models/diplomacy`, hybrid probe runs and who answered; a hybrid job's run needs pooled prompts as well as benchmarks on its machine; hybrid jobs stay out of the model scores; and the fix round's: a chat whose model never started falls back as `not_started`, not counted against the model; a control whose record does not read is played again, and one no job can pair with is not played; a control plays the bots pinned at its job's launch; a hybrid job loads its model beside the machine's other work; a diplomacy record that fails stops once and its job pairs nothing; a hybrid probe case whose model's prompt did not run says "answered by nobody"; reports show a hybrid job's pair line, and a hybrid seat stays out of its model's game figures. 3-19's `all` can reuse `_hybrid_benchmarks`' suite and board checks.

## P3.7 3F: the minimap

### P3.7.1 Today

`game.js` draws the whole map into a 200x130 canvas with independent x and y scales (not hex-correct), the explored tiles where they lie, and maps a click to a tile without wrapping (`game.js:58-72, 998-1030`).

### P3.7.2 The engine's block (Rust, 3-17)

A major civilization's own view (`api/views/client.rs`, the seat's part) gains `minimap`:
- `stage`: `early`, `renaissance` or `information`, from the seat's era: at or past the era named "Renaissance era" it is `renaissance`, at or past "Information era" `information`. A ruleset without those names uses positions: the era at index ⌈n/3⌉ for the Renaissance and the second-to-last for the Information era (indexes 3 and 7 of the shipped nine).
- `capital`: `[x, y]`, or null.
- `home`: the box of the explored tiles of the home continent (the capital's; without a capital, that of the seat's first city or settler; none: null), `{x0, y0, x1, y1}` inclusive, with `x0 > x1` (or `y0 > y1`) when the shortest covering interval crosses a wrapping seam. Only tiles the seat has explored count, so nothing leaks.
- One pass over the continent's tiles, in seat views only (about 10k tiles on gargantuan). The god view and spectators are unchanged.
- `refcheck/intended.toml` gains `minimap-view-block` (group `views`, path `civs[*].view.minimap`, python `{ is = "absent" }`). Views are not in the digests, so `cargo golden check` is unchanged; the build id moves.

### P3.7.3 The window (`citar/web/js/minimap.js`, pure)

`computeWindow({explored, home, stage, capital, width, height, wrapX, wrapY, prev})`:
1. `explored` is the explored tiles' box (`exploredBox(tiles, width, height, wrapX, wrapY)`: the shortest circular interval on a wrapping axis), padded by 3 tiles, clamped on an axis that does not wrap;
2. capped by stage: `early`, on a map larger than 44x28, the 44x28 box centred on the capital (or the explored box's centre) intersected with the explored box; `renaissance`, the explored box intersected with `home` padded by 3; `information`, the whole map;
3. united with `prev` (the smallest box covering both, circularly), so it never shrinks. `prev` is kept in `localStorage` per game and seat (`citar:minimap:<gid>:<pid>`, every access in try/catch).

`fit(window, widgetW, widgetH)` gives one scale for both axes (a hex is √3 wide, rows are 1.5 apart) and the letterbox offsets; `toTile` and `toScreen` convert both ways, wrap-aware.

### P3.7.4 Drawing and moving

- The widget keeps its size (200x130) and zooms out as the window grows, never back in. The tile layer is drawn on an offscreen canvas when the view's tiles or the window change; the viewport box is drawn over it when the camera moves.
- A click centres the main map, a drag pans it, both through `toTile`.
- Human seats only: the client uses the bounded window when its seat is a person's (`view.seat.type == "human"`); a model seat's view carries the block too, harmlessly, and spectators and the recap keep today's whole-map minimap.

### P3.7.5 Tests

`citar/web/tests/minimap.test.mjs` (`node --test`): the shortest interval across a seam, the cap of each stage, never shrinking, the letterbox maths, a click mapped to a tile and back on a wrapping map. `citar/web/package.json` (`{"type": "module", "private": true}`, which lets Node load `js/*.js` as modules) and test.yml's `node --test citar/web/tests/` step arrive with the chat window (3-12). Rust: testkit tests for the stage thresholds (the shipped ruleset and a renamed-eras one), the home box on wrapping and non-wrapping maps, a tile explored on another continent left out, a seat with no capital and no city.

**Moving a live game through the eras.** The QA script talks to a dev server over HTTP only, and the debug route takes named actions only (`app.py:688-705`), so 3-17 adds `POST /api/games/{gid}/debug/ops` (body: a list of scenario operations, applied with `EngineGame.apply_ops` under the lock, then `_after_action`), gated as the existing debug route is: 404 unless `CITAR_DEBUG=1` (the dev server sets it), then MANAGE on the game. It gets its stranger test (another member: 404; anonymous: 401 or 404), and the route audit sees its `_gate(` body gate.

## P3.8 3G: the machine settings dialog

### P3.8.1 Today

A helper's hello overwrites a machine's hardware and replaces its model list; there is no auto/manual choice, no record of the last scan, no capability model beyond LLM slots (three numbers, P3.0), no trust tier and no hardware class.

### P3.8.2 Data (in the baseline, P3.2.2)

- `servers.capabilities`: `{"llm": {"enabled": true, "allow_swaps": false}, "cpu": {"enabled": false, "threads": 0, "cpu_percent": 0, "memory_mb": 0, "work": []}}`. LLM slots are `max_concurrent`, edited in the same dialog. `cpu.work` is a subset of `lab_game`, `ranking_game`, `reference_game` (plan C1).
- `hardware_mode`, `models_mode`, `power_mode`; `hardware_detected` and `detected_at`; `gpu_class_id`, `cpu_class_id`, `class_override`; `trust_tier`.
- `servers.machine_settings(server_id)` returns what Phase 4's welcome and settings frames will carry (slots, swaps, the CPU limits). Phase 3's v1 hub sends nothing new; the scheduler reads slots and swaps from it.

### P3.8.3 A helper's hello

`record_connection` keeps the scan in `hardware_detected` and `detected_at` every time, then:
- **hardware** `auto`: applied (with `apply_hardware`'s source tag), and the machine is reclassified unless pinned; `manual`: `config.hardware` is left alone;
- **models** `auto`: merged (the reported models' labels, context and `loaded` updated; models not reported kept, marked not reported); `manual`: only the `loaded` flags of listed models are updated;
- **power** `auto`: the scan's suggested figures applied; `manual` (the default): left alone.

### P3.8.4 Routes

`GET/PUT /api/machines/{id}/settings` (owner or admin: capabilities, slots, the three modes), `POST /api/machines/{id}/hardware/apply` (owner or admin: copy the last scan, or one section of it, into the configuration), `PUT /api/admin/machines/{id}/trust` and `PUT /api/admin/machines/{id}/class` (admins).

### P3.8.5 The dialog

"Machine settings…" on each machine's card (P3.2.8), three tabs:
- **Capabilities:** LLM on or off, slots, "allow model swaps for pooled prompts"; CPU sharing with threads, CPU %, memory and the kinds of work, with a note that it takes effect with the 0.1.6 helper, that limits are hard where the OS allows, and that the helper's local settings always win.
- **Hardware, models and power:** an auto/manual toggle each; the last scan with its time and its differences from the configuration; "Apply".
- **Class and trust:** shown to the owner, editable by admins.

## P3.9 Cross-cutting rules

### P3.9.1 Access

- Every route added or changed in Phase 3 gets, in the package that touches it, a stranger test (another member on a private object: 404 for reads and writes; an anonymous caller: 401 or 404), an owner test and an admin test; routes of shareable objects add an ACL viewer's test (reads 200, writes 403) and a moderator's (a `global` object: reads 200, control 403, unpublish 200).
- **Nothing a member sends makes the server dial or read its own environment.** Every route that takes a model block, a base URL or a key backend is tested with `httpx`, `subprocess`, the OpenAI and Anthropic clients and `os.environ` reads patched to record calls; a member's request records none (P3.2.8, P3.2.9).
- `scripts/audit_routes.py --strict` passes at the end of every package. A route meant to be public goes in `INTENTIONALLY_PUBLIC` with its reason (Phase 3 adds `/api/users/{handle}`, `/api/users/{handle}/avatar` and `/r/{slug}`); new body gates join `BODY_GATES` (`items.require(`, `profiles.require_visible(`).
- 404, not 403, whenever the caller cannot see the object (`Access.require`).

### P3.9.2 Locks, threads and the database

- No model call, disk write or database write under a session lock. The scheduler's one blocking call, `acquire`, is never entered with a session lock held (an assertion in test mode); its non-blocking calls (cancel, release, idle, activate, close) are allowed there (P3.3.9). The ledger, the rollups and `ownership.sync` write from their own threads.
- Every wait has a timeout and notices a stopped session. Every timer thread (the chat clock, the ledger writer, the rollups, the scheduler's notifier) survives an exception in its loop, and its owner restarts it if it dies.

### P3.9.3 The standard gates

Every package's gates include, besides its own:
- **S1** the Python suite (`python -m unittest discover -s tests`), with the dev loop's extension built in the worktree's venv (`cargo xtask develop`) when Rust changed; its count recorded.
- **S2** ruff. **S3** `python scripts/audit_routes.py --strict`. **S4** `python scripts/check_links.py --strict`. **S5** when `docs/` changed: `mkdocs build --strict`, and `python scripts/build_wiki.py` with `wiki/` committed.
- **S6** the front-end checks: test.yml's ESM parse run locally (`node --check` on `.mjs` copies of `citar/web/js/*.js`), and from 3-12 on `node --test citar/web/tests/`.
- **S7** when Rust changed: `cargo fmt --check`; clippy `--workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -D warnings`; nextest `--workspace --all-features --locked`; the doctests; `cargo xtask check`; `cargo refcheck run --fixtures refcheck/fixtures-mini --fixtures refcheck/fixtures-late --strict --with-bot`; `cargo golden check`.
- **S8** from 3-03 on, the package's part of `scripts/qa_phase3.py` (P3.9.4) exits 0, and its browser checklist items (P3.9.5) pass.
- **CI-only** (a worktree never pushes): test.yml on its three OSes with its package, installer and Docker jobs; rust.yml and determinism.yml when Rust changed. Nobody labels or dispatches nightly.yml or docs.yml without the owner's OK.

### P3.9.4 The QA script

- `scripts/qa_phase3.py` (3-03 writes the harness; each later package adds its part): `python scripts/qa_phase3.py <part>|all [--port N] [--dir D] [--keep]`.
- It starts `scripts/dev_server.py --server --registration invite` as a child on a free port, or `--port`, refused when it is 8799 or in use. The state directory must be new, empty or marked `.qa-phase3`, and `C:/dev/citar-play` is always refused, as `scripts/server_soak.py` does. It makes an admin and two members (three for `all`) with `admin_cli create-user`, signs them in through `/api/auth/login` (cookies and CSRF), runs the part's checks over HTTP and the game socket, writes `<dir>/qa-<part>.json` (each check, its result, its numbers), stops the server (unless `--keep`), and exits 1 on any failed check.
- Model seats use the dry-run provider, taught what a part needs (a diplomat that proposes, votes and annexes; scripted chat answers). Helper machines use a real Python helper (`python -m citar.worker`) pointed at a small fake OpenAI-compatible endpoint the script serves. The endpoint is a **scripted policy, not canned text**: it reads the request's tools and its last messages and answers with valid tool calls, reusing the dry-run diplomat's logic (it answers the negotiation id it is asked about, opens a valid trade with a met civilization, votes, ends the turn), with a configurable delay per completion (to make queues, drains and cancels observable) and its own log of every request it served and when. So the hub, the hello, `Done.duration`, `CANCEL` and the drain are exercised without a GPU.
- Parts: `accounts` (3-03 to 3-06), `queue` (3-07 to 3-09), `ledger` (3-10), `chat` (3-11 to 3-13), `hybrid` (3-14 to 3-16), `minimap` (3-17), `machines` (3-18), and `all` (3-19).

### P3.9.5 The browser checklist

Run in the browser pane against the QA script's server (`--keep`), each item with a screenshot in the QA directory and ticked in the package's as-built note:
- **A1** a member's game shared with members appears for another member and not for a visitor; a private one is "not found" for the other member; the sharing dialog on a report; the phone site's reports tab lists the member's reports.
- **A2** the profile card: bio, links, an avatar upload, field visibility; the directory lists the member, and stops after "hide from directory"; a global profile opens signed out.
- **A3** the admin users page changes caps, limits and trust, and the audit log shows each change.
- **A4** one Servers page with a direct and a helper machine; an old `#/servers` link lands there.
- **C1** the Queue page shows leases and pooled prompts with positions; priority buttons only on your own work; another member's private machine is not named.
- **B1** the Account page's compute card; the profile's ratio line follows its visibility.
- **E1** an AI's chat pops up docked, left of End Turn, which stays visible and clickable; a reply shows "thinking…" then the answer; a half-typed message and an open deal builder survive two updates; minimise; End Turn shows the wait and, armed, ends the turn when the answer comes; a timed-out chat shows "(no reply in time)"; at 700 px wide one window shows at a time with tabs for the rest, End Turn still visible.
- **E2** the recap plays a finished game from `Delta` frames, chats shown as threads; the deal builder lists the trade options it fetched.
- **D1** a hybrid seat set up in the lobby from a preset; the game screen shows "diplomat thinking"; a trade the model owns gets the model's answer, one the bot owns the bot's.
- **D2** the Models page's Diplomacy tab.
- **F1** the minimap: hex-correct proportions, the explored area only, growing and never shrinking, click and drag panning, across a wrapping seam.
- **G1** the machine settings dialog: a manual hardware entry survives a helper's reconnect; "Apply" from the last scan; the trust tier shown.

### P3.9.6 Docs

Each package updates the documents its behaviour touches, named in its scope: `docs/server/ACCOUNTS.md` (3-01 to 3-04, 3-10), `docs/server/WORKERS.md` (3-05 to 3-09, 3-18), `docs/CONFIGURATION.md` (3-05, 3-10, 3-18), `docs/PLAYING.md` (3-11, 3-12, 3-13, 3-15, 3-17), `docs/AI_PLAYERS.md` (3-01, 3-11, 3-14), `docs/BENCHMARKS.md` (3-08, 3-16), `docs/API.md` (every new route), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (3-07, 3-11, 3-13, 3-14), `docs/REPORTS.md` (3-03, 3-10), `CONTRIBUTING.md` (3-03); the wiki is regenerated with them.

### P3.9.7 Performance

This laptop throttles, so a timing is never compared with a number recorded on another day: each check measures its baseline **in the same run**, alternating with the measured path, five times each, and compares medians.
- **The session's overhead:** a 4-bot small game through the session driver (`tests/test_server_drive.py`'s) against the same game driven by `EngineGame.drive` alone; the ratio may grow by at most 25% over the ratio 3-01 records in its as-built note (measured the same way at its start).
- **The minimap block:** its own time (a testkit timing of the block on the late fixture) at most 5% of the seat view's time, measured in the same run.
- **The scheduler:** under 1 ms per step with nothing queued (the median of 1,000 acquire-release pairs).
- **The chat clock:** under 1% of a core: with 20 open chats, one tick of its loop (it wakes at least once a second) takes under 10 ms, the median of 100 ticks.

### P3.9.8 Process

As `ops/phase1-common.md`: a worktree per package (`.claude/worktrees/p-3-xx`, branch `p-3-xx`); `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=C:/dev/target/citar-p-3-xx` before any cargo command; files written with the Write tool (Git Bash mangles backslashes in heredocs); small commits ending with the Co-Authored-By line; never `ops/`, `saves/` or `.claude/launch.json`; never a push. Every package ends with its gates, then a review and a fix round (the owner's standing rule), and its as-built note records its start and end wall-clock times.

---

## P3.10 Decisions taken in this design

1. **Schema first.** One baseline revision holds every Phase 3 table and column; later packages fill in behaviour, and add a revision only if they must.
2. **Old databases are renamed aside** on SQLite (the WAL checkpointed first; a rename that fails refuses the boot) and refused on Postgres; newer unknown revisions are refused.
3. **The ladder** is `private | allowlist | link | server | global`; `public` is gone; `server` needs no capability; `global` needs `publish_global` (the renamed `publish_public`). **Moderators only unpublish:** `MODERATE` on `global` objects and on `link` objects presented with their slug; every control needs `MANAGE`, which only owners, ACL managers and admins have.
4. **One `owned_items` table** for every file-backed job type, with `games` kept for live sessions and `reports` folded in; ACL entries keyed by kind. A report gathers only what its owner may see.
5. **Capabilities** `run_benchmarks`, `run_probes`, `run_lab`, `edit_bots`, `full_llm`, `partial_llm` join the five; lab and bot editing stay admin-only by default, as today.
6. **Profiles:** per-field visibility with `profile` as the page's own gate; members-only defaults; the ratio private by default; any non-probation member may make their own fields global. `User.public(viewer)` applies the display name's and the avatar's visibility wherever an account appears, and search matches only visible names.
7. **Avatars** are validated by header only (no image library), stored under the data directory, served with a fixed type and a sandboxing CSP.
8. **Members name machines; they never describe them.** A member's model block keeps a `server_id`, a model, a profile and the seat overrides; an inline block (a base URL, a key, a key variable) is an admin's or a dry run's only.
9. **The registry merge** moves `config/servers.json` into the `servers` table behind `servers.py`'s unchanged functions, under the registry's own ids, once an admin exists. Direct-reach machines, `POST /api/llm/models`, and every machine route that touches the host (the hardware scan, LM Studio control, model detection, the `env` key backend, host-wide settings) are admin-only or limited to direct and host rows; a member's helper row is never dialled.
10. **Revoked and disabled machines are dropped** from the hub at once, and the scheduler refuses them.
11. **One LLM-slot number:** `Server.max_concurrent`, capped by the helper's local ceiling.
12. **The scheduler** is in-process, per machine. Leases (full seats) belong to registered sessions' seats; tickets are partial prompts; lease requests are served in arrival order and before tickets; the reservation of active and non-lending leases keeps slots from tickets only; tickets go by priority, then round-robin across members, then FIFO.
13. **Lending:** a lobby lease lends its slot while its seat is idle (between turns, waiting on a chat, paused); a ticket on a borrowed slot runs under its class's cap. A benchmark lease never lends, drains pooled requests before its first step (bounded, with the wait recorded), and while one is live no ticket starts there; tickets fail fast there unless they wait for benchmarks (reports, probes, hybrid benchmark jobs).
14. **Loaded models only** for tickets unless the owner allows swaps, or a live lease uses the model.
15. **Generation time** is the smaller of the helper's `Done.duration` and the server's observed time, or the HTTP call's time after the slot was granted; queue time is recorded apart and never counted as work, nor against a model's turn; token counts are capped; a helper that over-reports is flagged.
16. **A cancel on a v1 helper drains:** the slot stays taken until the late frame or the timeout; a `busy` refusal lowers the machine's capacity and requeues without sleeping.
17. **Admission decides who, the scheduler when:** admission runs without the capacity step; a grant's `concurrency` is the scheduler's per-consumer cap.
18. **Every model call goes through `make_conversation`**, report narratives included.
19. **The ledger:** `compute_events` with consumer and provider, null for the server; self-use (both the same, or both the server) excluded; credits at write time from hardware classes; host CPU measured per unit of work; hourly rollups.
20. **Default rates:** an energy proxy for compute (GPU 1.0 per generation-second, CPU 0.05 per core-second) and an online API's token cost at 1,000 credits per unit of currency; admins edit them.
21. **Budgets are in credits** and reserve and settle per request against the ledger; an API machine's budget is priced from tokens, and a hard budget on an unpriced model refuses.
22. **Costing** reads work from `compute_events` and held time and power from the JSONL ledger.
23. **The chat clock is the chat timer;** lobby keys `chat_model_reply_seconds` (600) and `chat_person_reply_seconds` (300); at a deadline a model declines, a hybrid's bot answers, a person's chat closes as expired. Agents keep a safety cap of twice the longest timeout; the clock survives its own errors and the driver restarts it; in benchmark games it does not count time queued behind a benchmark; every fallback records its cause.
24. **Responders** run on two executors (model answers bounded, bot answers apart) with a queue per seat; a cancelled responder never declines; responders are dispatched again on resume, load, restore and agent creation; a model seat keeps one conversation per chat; a diplomat phase holds its seat's responder.
25. **Late replies** are put in front of the model before its next step, worded by the chat's state.
26. **`negotiation_status`** reaches the two parties and god-view spectators only.
27. **The chat window** is docked left of End Turn (one at a time at 700 px or less) and built on a pure state module tested with `node --test`; drafts and builder state live outside any redraw.
28. **Phase 2's handoffs are Phase 3's:** `replay.js` reads `Delta` and `Full` is retired; views answer with ETags; trade options are fetched on demand.
29. **The hybrid turn** is drive, stop, diplomat outside the lock, drive; a fallback bot handle with every category the bot's answers whatever the model cannot. Its handicap is the seat's existing `handicap` key.
30. **The veto** on mixed deals is the bot's `evaluate()` of the whole deal through a new binding.
31. **One diplomat phase per turn at most,** on triggers and every N turns; 30 s to start, 180 s to run, 8 steps.
32. **The advisor** is on by default in lobby games and always off in benchmark games.
33. **Hybrid benchmark jobs** use tickets that wait for benchmarks and have no start deadline; the benchmark scheduler keeps its hybrid and full jobs off each other's machines; each job has a cached bot-only control game with the same seed, handicap and level.
34. **The leaderboard** counts benchmark pairs only, values every proposal on both arms with the bot's `evaluate()` on its recorded terms, leaves out fallbacks the model did not cause, and honours visibility and pooling.
35. **The games index** counts lobby games only against the limit, closes rows left without a session at boot, and takes a session's owner from its row wherever a session comes to life.
36. **The minimap's home region** is computed in the engine from explored tiles only; the window logic is a pure JS module; the previous window lives in `localStorage`; a debug-only route moves a live game through the eras for QA.
37. **Machine modes:** hardware and models auto, power manual by default; the last scan always kept.
38. **QA** is a script on a dev server per feature (never port 8799 or `C:/dev/citar-play`) whose helper machines answer from a scripted policy, plus a browser checklist with screenshots; timings are compared within a run.

## P3.11 Risks

1. **Scheduler starvation or deadlock.** Unit tests with a fake clock for every rule, checked as invariants over random schedules; every wait in ticks with a timeout; only `acquire` blocks, and never under a session lock (asserted); callbacks delivered outside the machine lock.
2. **Races between the chat clock and the agents.** The clock acts through engine calls that refuse a chat that has moved; agents only wait, with a safety cap; a cancel never declines.
3. **The registry merge breaking benchmarks, costing or managed loading.** `servers.py` keeps its functions and shapes; the registry's ids are kept; an import fixture test; the test suite runs on a seeded database built by the import; costing tested on a synthetic ledger before and after.
4. **Prompt volume on small GPUs** (KV-cache reuse lost, plan risk 6). A slim, static diplomat prompt; one phase per turn at most; triggers; tickets only on loaded models; borrowing caps.
5. **Ledger write volume on SQLite.** A writer thread with batched inserts; rollups for reads.
6. **Avatar uploads as an attack surface.** Header-checked formats only, a size cap, a fixed served type, `nosniff` and a sandboxing CSP, storage outside the code and saves.
7. **Churn in `game.js` and `panels.js`.** The chat, replay and minimap logic in pure modules with Node tests; the browser checklist.
8. **v1 helpers ignore `CANCEL`.** The scheduler counts a cancelled request's slot as draining until its late frame or its timeout, so capacity stays right; Phase 4 makes cancel real.
9. **The automatic archive of an old database.** Rename only, after a WAL checkpoint, logged with the new path; a failed rename refuses the boot; Postgres refuses; the owner's port-8799 instance gets a fresh database on its first Phase 3 start (its saves on disk are untouched and re-indexed on load).
10. **Members turning the server against its own network or keys.** Closed by P3.2.8 and P3.2.9 in the first and fifth packages, each with tests that patch every outbound path and record nothing.
11. **Self-reported work.** Helpers' generation time is bounded by the server's own observation and over-reporting is flagged; plan C2's attribution holds without trusting the helper's clock.
12. **Nineteen packages in sequence.** Each sized like 2-11 or smaller (three, 3-04, 3-10 and 3-14, at the upper bound, name where to split if they run long), with gates a reviewer can rerun locally, and wall-clock recorded.

## P3.12 Work breakdown at a glance

Nineteen packages, strictly in this order, one agent at a time. Each ends with its gates, a review and a fix round, and its as-built note.

| # | Package | Feature |
|---|---|---|
| 3-01 | The schema baseline, the visibility ladder, the games index, members' model blocks | 3A |
| 3-02 | Owned items: every job type owned, gated and shareable | 3A |
| 3-03 | Reports scoped to their owner, the pooling opt-out, the sharing dialog, the QA harness | 3A |
| 3-04 | Profiles, the directory, avatars, the admin users page, the audit log | 3A |
| 3-05 | The registry merge: machines as rows, the routes' gates, dropped helpers | 3A |
| 3-06 | One Servers page | 3A |
| 3-07 | The scheduler core: leases, tickets, draining, the conversation wrapper, metering | 3C |
| 3-08 | Leases for the session's seats: occupancy, benchmarks, probe and report leases | 3C |
| 3-09 | Pooled work: admission, task classes, reports and probes on tickets, the Queue page | 3C |
| 3-10 | The compute ledger: events, credits, rollups, budgets, the ratio | 3B |
| 3-11 | Chat in the server: the clock, responders, status events, late replies | 3E |
| 3-12 | The chat window | 3E |
| 3-13 | The client's payloads: `Delta` replays, view ETags, trade options on demand | Phase 2's handoffs |
| 3-14 | Hybrid seats: the agent, the diplomat, the veto, the seat sites | 3D |
| 3-15 | Hybrid seats in the lobby, the seats dialog, scenarios and the game screen | 3D |
| 3-16 | Hybrid benchmarks and probes, control games, the diplomacy leaderboard | 3D |
| 3-17 | The minimap | 3F |
| 3-18 | The machine settings dialog | 3G |
| 3-19 | Phase 3 exit: the QA end to end, docs, evidence | all |

The order follows the plan's dependencies (3B after 3A; 3D and 3E after 3C) and three of its own: the ledger needs the scheduler's generation time, so 3C comes before 3B; the hybrid seat answers chats through the chat responders and clock, so 3E's server half comes before 3D; and trade options on demand need the deal builder in the chat window, so Phase 2's handoffs follow it. The security fixes come first (members' model blocks in 3-01, the host-touching routes in 3-05), before members get more surface. The minimap depends on nothing else and the machine settings only on the merged registry, the scheduler and the classes, so they come last, where they cannot hold up the rest.

## P3.13 Review log

**The design review** (2026-10-11) raised 2 blockers, 19 majors and 10 minors. Each was checked against the code at `d93345a` and the plan; all were found valid and are fixed in this revision, one of them in part (the recorder finding's control-seat clause, the last row).

| Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Blocker: a member's seat without a `server_id` makes the server dial any address with any of its environment variables as the key, or spend the site's API key | Members name machines only; inline blocks for admins and dry runs; registry machines admin-only until the merge; `update_seat` admitted | P3.0, P3.2.9; 3-01 |
| Blocker: "owner-or-admin" on `/api/servers*` hands members host access (`collect`, LM Studio control, `detect`, the `env` key backend, host-wide routes) | A gate per route; host-touching routes admin-only or direct/host rows only; a member's helper row never dialled | P3.2.8; 3-05 |
| Leases made with lazily created agents leave loaded, restored and paused games unseen by benchmarks | Leases belong to registered sessions' seats | P3.3.3, P3.3.5; 3-08 |
| Running tickets keep running when a benchmark lease appears | The benchmark drain, `drain_s`, `contaminated`. The drain covers pooled requests, which the owner's rule names; a lobby lease beside a benchmark stays the job rule's business, as today | P3.3.3, P3.3.6; 3-07, 3-08 |
| An LLM seat waiting on a chat reserves the slot its counterpart needs | Agents lend while blocked; the reservation keeps slots from tickets only; lease requests FIFO | P3.3.3; 3-07, 3-14 |
| v1 cancels free the slot while the helper still generates | Draining slots; late frames to the scheduler. A `busy` that still happens lowers the capacity and requeues the request to wait on the machine's condition (no sleep, no meter, no failure), rather than failing it into the agent's retry loop | P3.3.3; 3-07 |
| Self-reported `Done.duration` feeds credits without bound | `gen_s = min(reported, observed)`, both kept, tokens capped, over-reporting flagged | P3.3.4, P3.4.2; 3-07 |
| Hybrid benchmark chats fall back after queueing behind a benchmark; hybrid jobs start on held machines | The clock stands still while queued behind a benchmark; fallbacks by cause; the job rule | P3.5.2, P3.3.5, P3.6.9; 3-11, 3-16 |
| The recorder cannot value negotiations settled inside a drive; the control's handicap is not pinned | `bot_evaluate` on recorded terms after each drive, both arms; the control copies handicap and level | P3.6.9, P3.6.10; 3-16 |
| Moderators would get `MANAGE` on members' published runs, probes and reports | `MODERATE` (unpublish) only | P3.2.3; 3-01, 3-02 |
| Benchmark rows count against the game limit; orphan rows stay "playing"; restored sessions have no owner | Limit on `kind = game`; orphans closed at boot; owner from the row | P3.2.5; 3-01 |
| Suspensions decline the model's chats; cancelled keys block dispatch; restores leave chats waiting | Cancels never decline and clear their keys; dispatch on resume, load, restore and agent creation | P3.5.3; 3-11 |
| Bot answers wait behind blocked model answers in one pool | Two executors | P3.5.3; 3-11 |
| Queue and chat waits count against a model's turn; the request timeout is set before the slot wait | Both added to `_waited`; the timeout set after the grant | P3.3.4; 3-07 |
| Budgets in credits never block an API machine at rate 0 | API credits priced from token cost; unpriced models refused under a hard budget | P3.4.3, P3.4.6; 3-10 |
| Admission refuses tickets on busy machines; grant concurrency silently dropped | `in_flight=None`; concurrency a per-consumer cap in the scheduler | P3.3.7; 3-07, 3-09 |
| Phase 2's handoffs (`Delta` replays, ETags, trade options) listed as non-goals | A package of their own | P3.0, P3.1.5, P3.5.8; 3-13 |
| Revoking a token does not disconnect the helper | `hub.drop` on revoke, disable and delete; the scheduler refuses disabled rows | P3.2.8, P3.3.2; 3-05 |
| The exit QA misses benchmarks, a full seat beside the hybrid, leak checks and a fake endpoint that can play | `all` extended; the fake endpoint a scripted policy | P3.9.4; 3-19 |
| 3-02, 3-04 and 3-05 too large; the test registry migration hidden | 3-02 and 3-05 split (3-02/3-03, 3-07/3-08), the registry page split off (3-06), the seeded test registry in 3-05's scope | Appendix D |
| `User.public()` shows private display names and avatars; search reveals them | `User.public(viewer)`; search on visible fields | P3.2.6; 3-04 |
| No route applies scenario ops to a live game | `POST /api/games/{gid}/debug/ops` (debug only, MANAGE) | P3.7.5; 3-17 |
| No admin at a fresh boot for the import and adoption; CASCADE owners; Windows rename; new ids on import | Import and adoption when an admin exists, and from `accounts.on_first_admin()`; owners nullable SET NULL; WAL checkpoint and refusal on a failed rename; ids kept; the import route retired | P3.2.2, P3.2.4, P3.2.8; 3-01, 3-02, 3-05 |
| A third spelling of the handicap in `hybrid.difficulty` | The seat's `handicap` key | P3.6.2; 3-14 |
| Late-reply notes ask for answers to closed chats; one `{minutes}` for two timeouts; a phase and a chat thread race | Notes by state; both timeouts named; the phase holds the seat's responder | P3.5.3, P3.6.5, P3.6.6; 3-11, 3-14 |
| Lending gates contradict rule 4; a borrowed slot unbounded in time | Gates on starts and on the seat's wait; per-class caps for borrowing tickets | P3.3.3; 3-09 |
| Pooled responder threads misattribute CPU | `thread_time()` per unit of work | P3.4.3; 3-10 |
| The Queue page names every machine to every member | Only machines the caller may view | P3.3.8; 3-09 |
| Docked windows cover End Turn; no narrow layout; `mobile.js` on removed routes | Docked left of End Turn; one window at 700 px; `mobile.js` moved | P3.5.6, P3.2.4; 3-03, 3-12 |
| Gates that cannot fail or are noise (a busy grep, a "public" grep, 10% timings, real-time waits) | A behavioural busy test, an AST visibility scan, same-run baselines, an injectable clock | P3.2.3, P3.5.2, P3.9.7; Appendix D |
| A blanket lock assertion would fire on legitimate cancels; a single clock thread as the only timer | Only `acquire` asserts; cancels non-blocking; callbacks outside the machine lock; agents' safety cap; the clock restarted | P3.3.9, P3.5.2; 3-07, 3-11 |
| **In part:** "the control seat copies the hybrid seat's handicap, auto decisions and difficulty" | Handicap and level copied; the automatic decisions stay a bot seat's, because they are the categories the model takes over in the other arm (copying them would measure an abstaining control, not the bot) | P3.6.9; 3-16 |
