## Appendix D: Phase 3 work breakdown

Each package is sized for one implementation session (2-11's size or smaller: 2-11 was about 1,800 lines), starts from the previous package's merge, and ends with its gates passing, then a review and a fix round. The standard gates S1-S8 (P3.9.3) are named where they apply; every other gate is local and can be rerun by a reviewer. The QA script's parts and the browser checklist items are P3.9.4 and P3.9.5. Every route a package adds or changes gets the tests of P3.9.1 whether or not its gates name them.

### 3-01: The schema baseline, the visibility ladder, the games index, members' model blocks

- **Depends on:** nothing (`v0.1.6` at `d93345a`)
- **Estimated size:** ~1,000 Python (models 350, the baseline revision 450, access, boot and the seat gate 200) + ~650 tests + docs
- **Scope:** `citar/migrations/versions/`: delete `f064316a6b34` and `767c824c2fcb`; write `0016a_baseline` with every table and column of P3.2.2 (users' profile columns, `owned_items`, games' statuses, servers' new columns, `servers.owner_id` and `server_groups.owner_id` nullable with SET NULL, `hardware_classes` with its three seeds, `compute_events` with `reported_s`, `observed_s` and `flags`, `compute_rollups`, budgets in credits, `reports` dropped) and `citar/db/models.py` to match, including the ORM classes later packages fill in. `server/boot.py:migrate()` with the cases of P3.2.2 (an old revision renamed aside on SQLite after a WAL checkpoint, a failed rename refusing the boot; refused on Postgres; an unknown newer revision refused; a version-less test database stamped). The ladder of P3.2.3: `access.VISIBILITY`, `access.check_visibility`, `server` and `global` in `access.on` and `visible_filter`, `access.MODERATE` and the moderator rule, `POST /api/games/{gid}/unpublish`, the share URL, every validation site of P3.0 moved onto it, the AST visibility scan; `publish_public` renamed `publish_global` in `CAPABILITIES`, `ROLE_DEFAULTS`, `PROBATION_DENIES` and the console (`console.js`). `User.public()` without `role` and `status`, `User.account()` for the owner and admins, every caller checked. The games index of P3.2.5: `ownership.sync` wired off the lock, statuses `setup|playing|finished|closed`, `_game_limit` on live lobby games only, orphan rows closed at boot, `register_existing` on load, `GameSession.owner_id` from the row in the routes, `_register_loaded`, `restore_live` and the benchmark and probe runners. Members' model blocks (P3.2.9): `servers.member_llm` and its callers, `update_seat` running `pool_seats.authorize`, registry machines admin-only for now, `resolve_llm`'s passthrough limited. The visibility choices in the web client where they exist. Record the session-overhead ratio of P3.9.7 in the as-built note. Docs: `docs/server/ACCOUNTS.md` (the ladder, moderation, the fresh baseline and what happens to an old database), `docs/AI_PLAYERS.md` (a seat names a machine).
- **Gates:**
  1. `tests/test_migrations.py`: `upgrade head` on an empty SQLite database, then alembic's `compare_metadata` against `Base.metadata` finds nothing; the three hardware class seeds exist. A database made by the two old revisions (a small SQLite file built at `d93345a` before they are deleted, committed as `tests/data/citar-0.1.5.db`) is renamed aside with its `-wal` and `-shm`, and the boot comes up on a fresh one; with the rename made to fail (`os.replace` patched to raise `PermissionError` on the second file) the boot refuses, naming the file, and the old database is whole and in place; a database at a made-up newer revision refuses to boot, naming it; a version-less database made by `create_all` is stamped; the Postgres refusal (a unit test on the dialect branch).
  2. `tests/test_access.py`: the ladder table (five levels by anonymous, member, probation member, suspended member, owner, ACL viewer, moderator and admin, by view, play, manage and moderate) and `visible_filter` per viewer; `global` needs `publish_global`, `server` needs nothing; `check_visibility("public")` raises; the AST scan finds no visibility constant outside `access.VISIBILITY`. A moderator unpublishes a `global` game (200: private, slug rotated, one audit row with the reason) and gets 404 or 403 on its pause, seat, delete and sharing routes; on a `server` game a moderator has nothing a member lacks.
  3. The games index: a game ended with the `end_game` test operation is `finished` and no longer counts (a member with a limit of 1 starts another); closing a running game marks it `closed`; loading it marks it `playing`; a loaded save without a row gets one; a member with three benchmark-kind rows playing can still create 2 lobby games; a `playing` row whose save folder was deleted is `closed` after a boot; a game brought back by `restore_live` has its row's owner as `owner_id`.
  4. `User.public()` carries no role or status; `/api/auth/me` still gives the owner both.
  5. `tests/test_seat_blocks.py` (P3.2.9): a member's seat carrying `base_url`, `api_key_env` or `provider: openai_compatible` without a `server_id` gets 400 on `create_game`, `update_seat`, a scenario save and a scenario launch, with `httpx`, the OpenAI and Anthropic clients and `os.environ` reads patched recording nothing; a registry machine (the test registry's `sv_api`) in a member's seat gets 403, in an admin's is accepted; a member's inline `dryrun` block is accepted; `update_seat` to a pooled machine the member has no grant on gets 403 and leaves the seat as it was.
  6. S1-S6.

### 3-02: Owned items: every job type owned, gated and shareable

- **Depends on:** 3-01
- **Estimated size:** ~1,000 Python + ~650 tests
- **Scope:** `citar/items.py` and `access.on` for owned items (P3.2.4); registration in every producer of P3.2.4's table (reports included: the runner writes the row); system items; adoption (at boot when an admin exists, and from `accounts.on_first_admin()`, called by every path that makes an admin; `settings_kv.orphans_adopted`); the capabilities `run_benchmarks`, `run_probes`, `run_lab`, `edit_bots`, `full_llm`, `partial_llm` with their role defaults; every route of P3.2.4 gated and every listing filtered (the report routes' gates included; their scoping and the `share_api` move are 3-03's); queue priority gated; benchmark games registered as rows with the run's owner and visibility; the item sharing routes and `POST /api/items/{kind}/{ref}/unpublish`; the model-block gate (P3.2.9) on suites' model entries and probe subjects. Docs: `ACCOUNTS.md` (ownership, sharing, moderation of items), `docs/API.md`.
- **Gates:**
  1. `tests/test_items_access.py`, table-driven over every route of P3.2.4 (the table is checked against the app's route list, so a gated-prefix route missing from it fails the test): another member on a private item gets 404 for reads and writes; an ACL viewer 200 for reads and 403 for writes; a moderator on a `global` item 200 for reads and unpublish, 403 for every control route; the owner and an admin 200; an anonymous caller 401 or 404; a `global` item's reads 200 anonymously where P3.2.4 allows, its writes 401.

### 3-07: The scheduler core: leases, tickets, draining, the conversation wrapper, metering

- **Depends on:** 3-06
- **Estimated size:** ~1,150 Python + ~750 tests
- **Scope:** `citar/pool/scheduler.py` (P3.3.2-P3.3.4, P3.3.9): machines, capacity (disabled rows refused), leases (`GameSession.lease_for`, made on first use in this package; 3-08 moves them to the session's registration), active and idle with the agent lending while it waits on a chat or a pause, tickets, the rules (lease requests FIFO before tickets, the reservation for tickets only, the per-consumer cap from the grant, borrowing caps), the order, position and ETA, the benchmark drain as a rule, cancel and draining (`hub.cancel`; late frames handed to the scheduler; `submit`'s timeout draining), the `busy` fallback (capacity lowered, requeue, no sleep), listeners, status delivered by the notifier thread. `make_conversation(..., binding)` and `ScheduledConversation` (the request timeout set after the grant); `WorkerConversation` keeping `duration`, the observed time and its request id, `close()` calling `hub.cancel`; `gen_s` bounded, token caps, the over-report flag. `LLMAgent` on leases: its busy branch removed, `queue_s` and chat waits added to `_waited`, `_meter` replaced by the usage tracker's listener; `metrics.model_step` with `gen_s` and `queue_s`; the speed score on generation time. The lock rules of P3.3.9 (the `acquire` assertion, non-blocking cancels). Docs: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the scheduler), `WORKERS.md` (a busy machine queues; cancels drain on today's helper).
- **Gates:**
  1. `tests/test_scheduler.py` with a fake clock and fake providers: 500 seeded random schedules of leases (lobby and benchmark), tickets, cancels with v1 draining, step times and benchmark leases appearing mid-schedule, with P3.3.3's invariants checked after every event: in flight, draining included, never above capacity; lease requests served in arrival order (but for a consumer at its cap) and never behind a ticket that started after them; no ticket takes a reserved slot; no ticket starts while a benchmark lease is live or draining; no ticket request in flight during a benchmark step unless the drain bound was reached and the job marked `contaminated`; a borrowing ticket refused a step past its class's cap. Constructed cases: priority, then round-robin, then FIFO; an idle lobby lease's slot lent and given back within one capped step; expiry; cancelling a queued and a running ticket; a model not loaded refused unless swaps are allowed or a lease uses it; quiet hours refused with `retry_at`; the ETA rising with position; a consumer whose grant has concurrency 1 never has two requests running.
  2. No busy-wait, by behaviour: a fake hub answering `busy` three times makes no `time.sleep` call (patched), meters exactly one request, and completes it when a frame frees a slot.
  3. Draining, through a real Python helper and the scripted endpoint taking 20 s a completion: cancelling a running ticket sends `CANCEL`; the next request waits for the drain; the hub records no `busy` refusal; the late `Done` reaches the scheduler.
  4. On the dry-run provider through a row with `max_concurrent = 1`: two lobby games with an LLM seat each play 5 rounds with never more than one request in flight (a listener records it) and no busy refusal; with two LLM seats of one game on that machine, seat A opens a trade on its turn and waits, and seat B's answer (a lease request) gets the slot while A is idle, with no timeout; a test ticket answering A's chat gets it too.
  5. Metering, through the scripted endpoint: an honest helper's `gen_s` equals its `duration`; one reporting ten times the observed time is credited the observed time and flagged after three such requests; token counts above `max_tokens` are capped; `queue_s` is recorded; a turn's model rows carry `gen_s` and `queue_s`; a model with long queue times and short generation scores as fast.
  6. Time accounting (fake clock): a turn with 25 minutes of queueing and chat waits and one minute of generation ends by `end_turn`, not `time_limit`.
  7. Locks: `acquire` under a session lock raises in test mode; `suspend`, `update_seat` and `_crashed` cancel a seat's scheduled conversation under the lock without blocking; a status callback that takes a session lock, while another thread holds that lock and waits for the machine, completes (bounded by a 5 s test timeout).
  8. P3.9.7: the scheduler's step overhead and the session-overhead ratio. `qa_phase3.py queue` (its lease and cancel checks: two lobby games on one helper machine, a cancelled test ticket draining) exits 0.
  9. S1-S5; S8.

### 3-10: The compute ledger: events, credits, rollups, budgets, the ratio

- **Depends on:** 3-09
- **Estimated size:** ~1,150 Python + ~300 JavaScript + ~650 tests (at the upper bound: if it runs long, budgets and costing split off as a package of their own after it)
- **Scope:** P3.4: `citar/ledger.py` (the writer thread, owners of activities, credits, the classifier, the rollup thread, totals, the ratio); the scheduler listener with the reported and observed seconds and the flags; host CPU measured per unit of work (`thread_time()` around drives, answers, responder tasks, report builds and probe cases), the 5 s sampling kept for a driver thread's other time; the lab's results; hardware classes (admin API, the classifier, `class_override`) with `api` priced from token cost; budgets in credits reserved and settled per request, an API machine's reserve from its token prices, the unpriced refusal, the lease's pause and the ticket's refusal, `refresh_from_ledger`; `costing.py` reading `compute_events`, `usage.py` no longer writing busy, tokens and requests in spans; `/api/ledger/me`, `/api/users/{handle}/ratio`, `/api/admin/hardware-classes`; the admin users page's ratio column, the Account page's Compute card, the profile's ratio line; `citar admin reprice`. Docs: `CONFIGURATION.md` (credits and classes), `docs/REPORTS.md` (where costing's work figures come from), `ACCOUNTS.md` (the ratio), `docs/API.md`.
- **Gates:**
  1. `tests/test_ledger.py`: consumer and provider for each activity kind (a lobby game, a benchmark run, a probe run, a report, a lab experiment, a `collection` run) on a member's machine, the host and an online API; given and used per member equal a hand-computed table; self-use (the same member; the server on its own machine) on neither side; credits are seconds times the class rate at write time, and an API step's credits its token cost times the rate; a rate change applies from then on; `reprice` recomputes a period; an over-reporting helper's events carry `observed_s` as `gen_s` and the flag.
  2. CPU attribution: two sessions whose answers run on the same responder thread within one window are each charged their own `thread_time()`; the sums equal the measured totals.
  3. Rollups: after random events over three days, every rollup equals the sum of its raw events; the 24h, 7d, 30d and all totals equal raw sums; a read in the current hour includes events not yet rolled up.
  4. Budgets (fake clock): with a hard budget of N credits on a grant, requests are admitted until spent plus reserved would pass N, then refused with the period's end; a hard budget on an API machine refuses the request that would pass it, and every request when the model has no price; a lobby game on that grant pauses with the budget's reason and resumes when the period turns; a soft budget only warns; `refresh_from_ledger` rebuilds entries equal to the settled ones.
  5. Costing: a synthetic ledger priced before and after the switch gives equal totals with no queue time, and less busy energy with queue time; the report tests pass.
  6. The ratio's visibility: `private` gives another member 404 and an admin the totals; `server` gives members; `global` gives anonymous callers.
  7. 10,000 events written from 8 threads in under 2 s with no session lock held; the writer drains on shutdown.
  8. `qa_phase3.py ledger` exits 0: member B's helper machine serves member A's report narrative and lobby game, B's given and A's used match the events, and A's own machine's work counts on neither side; browser B1.
  9. S1-S6; S8.

### 3-11: Chat in the server: the clock, responders, status events, late replies

- **Depends on:** 3-10
- **Estimated size:** ~1,000 Python + ~30 Rust + ~150 JavaScript + ~650 tests
- **Scope:** P3.5.2-P3.5.5 and P3.5.7: the lobby keys and `info()`; `ChatClock` with its injectable clock, the benchmark-queue rule, fallback causes, its exception-proof loop and the driver's restart; `citar/agents/responders.py` with its two executors, the halt that never declines, and dispatch on resume, load, `restore_live` and agent creation; `ChatThread` for model seats on their leases (lending while they wait); `BotAgent` and `LLMAgent` waiting on the clock with their safety caps; the late-reply notes by state; `negotiation_status` from the clock, the scheduler's callbacks and the session, and the socket's filter; `GET /api/games/{gid}/negotiations/{nid}`; Rust: `respond_negotiation` requires `message`, with the tool list test's fix and the intended entry; the prompts; MCP; the probes on the new responder API; the negotiation metrics. Docs: `docs/AI_PLAYERS.md` (chats and their timeouts), `docs/PLAYING.md` (the lobby's chat settings), `ARCHITECTURE.md` (the chat clock), `docs/API.md`.
- **Gates:**
  1. `tests/test_chat_clock.py`, in fake time: a chat waiting on a model seat past `chat_model_reply_seconds` is declined on its behalf with "(no reply in time)"; on a person past `chat_person_reply_seconds`, closed as expired; a 10-minute pause does not expire a 5-minute chat; a reply restarts the clock; an answer landing as the deadline fires leaves exactly one outcome; a game without the keys uses the defaults; in a benchmark session a chat whose answer's ticket waits 30 minutes behind a benchmark lease does not expire, and the clock counts from the ticket's start; after a load every open chat's deadline starts afresh.
  2. Waits, in fake time: a bot's turn that opened a chat with a person waits until the person answers or the clock closes the chat (an answer after 200 s is taken: no 90 s budget), then ends; a model seat's turn waits the same way; a session with a scripted person who never answers ends the turn at the timeout. With the clock thread killed, the driver restarts it at the next turn and the chat still closes; with the clock stopped for good, the bot's safety cap ends the turn.
  3. Responders: 40 chats opened at once with 8 model seats run on at most `CITAR_RESPONDERS` threads, one chat at a time per seat, each answered; `_responding` is empty once all have closed; a model seat's second exchange in a chat reuses its conversation (one `make_conversation` per chat, counted by a fake provider). With 16 model answers blocked on a capacity-1 machine, a person's offer to a bot is answered within a second.
  4. Cancellation and return: suspending and resuming the game while a model is mid-answer leaves the chat open and the model then answers it (no "(no response)"); a seat changed mid-answer is answered by its new agent; a server restart (`restore_live`) with a chat waiting on an LLM seat is answered without waiting for the timeout.
  5. A person's reply during an LLM seat's turn, after the tool's wait, is in the model's next user message (a scripted conversation records it); an accepted, a declined and an expired chat give notes that ask for no answer.
  6. Each party's socket receives `negotiation_status` (queued with a position, thinking, waiting with a deadline, closed) for its chat; a third seat's socket and a spectator of a game with a person in it receive none; a god-view spectator does.
  7. Rust: a `respond_negotiation` without `message` is refused by the schema check; S7 (refcheck clean, the golden sets unchanged, the tool list test passing with its new fix).
  8. MCP: `wait_for_turn`'s results carry deadlines; the probes' offer cases still pass.
  9. `qa_phase3.py chat` (the server half: chats between a person, a dry-run model and a bot, both timeouts set to 20 s, in a short game played to its end) exits 0, with every status event seen in the evidence.
  10. S1-S7; S8.

Docs: `PLAYING.md` (the recap), `ARCHITECTURE.md` (the replay format, ETags), `docs/API.md`.

  2. The server tests that compared `Full` frames compare `Delta` bytes; a call for `Full` fails.
  3. ETags: two answers with the same ETag have identical bodies; a move, a pause, an agent status, a seat change and another perspective each change it; a matching `If-None-Match` gets 304 without the view being built (a counter on `view_json`).
  4. No seat's view carries `trade_options` (a 4-civilization game where all have met); the route gives them to a seat token for its own seat and to a spectator of a god-view game, and 404s otherwise (stranger tests); `get_diplomacy` still lists them.
  5. S7 with the new intended entry used, refcheck `--strict` clean and the golden sets unchanged.

### 3-14: Hybrid seats: the agent, the diplomat, the veto, the seat sites

- **Depends on:** 3-13
- **Estimated size:** ~1,300 Python + ~120 Rust + ~700 tests (at the upper bound: if it runs long, the seat sites of P3.6.7 and the diplomat metrics split off as a package of their own after it)
- **Scope:** P3.6.2-P3.6.7 and P3.6.11: `Seat.hybrid`, `SEAT_TYPES`, `normalize_hybrid` and the presets, the seat's `handicap` key; the engine's controller, handicap and automatic decisions at creation, seat update and scenario launch; `HybridAgent` (the turn, the triggers, the diplomat phase holding the seat's responder, chats through the responders, the fallback); `DIPLOMAT_SYSTEM` (with both timeouts) and the diplomat's message; the tools per category, the session's tool filter and the veto; Rust: `Game.bot_evaluate` in citar-py, `EngineGame.bot_evaluate` in the facade, `normalize_seats` keeping `hybrid`; `Seat.model_seat`, `task_class`, `has_bot` and the Python half of P3.6.7's table; the diplomat metrics; the dry-run diplomat (proposes a trade to a met civilization, votes when a vote is open, annexes a captured city, answers chats as configured). Docs: `AI_PLAYERS.md` (hybrid seats), `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/API.md` (the seat).
- **Gates:**
  1. For each of the ten categories, a seeded 4-seat small game with one hybrid seat whose model owns only that category (the dry-run diplomat acting in it where it can), 60 rounds: the bot takes no action of that category (from the drives' action counts and the events: no trade offer, no war declared, no spy moved, no city-state gift, no engine UN vote, no automatic annexation, as the category goes) and the model's actions in it happen. With every category the bot's (a test-only configuration) and no phase triggered, the hybrid seat's events, turn by turn for 60 rounds, equal a bot seat's with the same handicap in the same game.
  2. Fallback: a diplomat or chat ticket refused (a benchmark holds the machine) or expired, or a model that fails: the fallback bot answers within the same drive or clock tick, and the metrics record it with its cause; a 100-round game whose model never answers ends every turn.
  3. Veto: the model's offer of a trade item plus an embassy (agreements the bot's), valued below zero by the bot, is refused with the message; valued above zero it goes through; a deal wholly in the model's categories is never vetoed.
  4. Triggers: each trigger of P3.6.4 fires one phase (a scripted game per trigger, `prompt_every` large); never two phases in a turn; `prompt_every` fires on schedule.
  5. Tools: a diplomat call outside its categories is refused with the message and counted; the system prompt is byte-identical across a seat's calls and names both timeouts.
  6. One machine, two kinds of seat: an LLM seat and a hybrid seat on one capacity-1 machine, where the LLM seat opens a trade the hybrid's model owns, gets the model's answer with no fallback. A chat that comes back to a hybrid seat during its diplomat phase is answered by the phase, never by a parallel chat thread (a fake provider counts conversations per negotiation).
  7. Rust: `bot_evaluate` equals `citar_bot::evaluate` on the deals of refcheck's `deal_checks` for the committed states; `normalize_seats` keeps `hybrid`; S7.
  8. Every Python row of P3.6.7 has a test (`occupied` ignores hybrid seats; scoring leaves them out; usage lists them with their class; the summary labels them).
  9. `qa_phase3.py hybrid` (the server half: a hybrid seat on a dry-run machine shared with `pooled`, two bots and a person, 30 rounds, `prompt_every = 3`) exits 0, with phases, chat answers by the model and by the bot, and a fallback in the evidence.
  10. S1-S7; S8.

### 3-16: Hybrid benchmarks and probes, control games, the diplomacy leaderboard

- **Depends on:** 3-15
- **Estimated size:** ~1,000 Python + ~300 JavaScript + ~550 tests
- **Scope:** P3.6.9 and P3.6.10: suites' `mode: "hybrid"`; hybrid jobs (the advisor off, tickets that wait for benchmarks with no start deadline, the clock's benchmark rule); the benchmark scheduler counting hybrid jobs against the machine's job limit and never starting one beside a live benchmark lease; the control games' runner and cache, the control copying the hybrid seat's handicap and level; `DiplomacyRecorder` valuing every proposal with `bot_evaluate` on its recorded terms; fallback causes in the jobs' results; `/api/models/diplomacy`; the Models page's Diplomacy tab; probes' hybrid mode. Docs: `docs/BENCHMARKS.md` (hybrid suites and the leaderboard), `docs/API.md`.
- **Gates:**
  1. A hybrid suite (one scenario, the dry-run diplomat, 3 seeds, 60 turns) runs 3 hybrid jobs and 3 control games; a second model's run of the suite reuses the 3 cached controls; a control game run twice gives the same event list.
  2. The recorder on a scripted game: proposals, deals, values, acceptance, wars, captures and peace equal a hand-made table; an accepted deal's value is its proposal's when made. In a control game the deal values of proposals made and settled inside one drive (bot to bot) are not null. A pair whose hybrid seat has every category the bot's (a test-only configuration) gives a zero score difference and equal deal values.
  3. The leaderboard on fixture results: pair lines and per-model aggregates (the mean score difference, the bootstrap interval with a fixed seed, deals per game) equal hand-computed values; a run the caller cannot see is left out; pooled data under the minimum is withheld; a fallback caused by a benchmark queue is not counted against the model.
  4. Fake clock: a hybrid job's chat ticket waiting 30 minutes behind a full benchmark is answered by the model, not the fallback; a hybrid job is not started on a machine with a live benchmark lease; a full job is not started beside a running hybrid job of the same scheduler.
  5. A hybrid probe run records which side answered each case.
  6. `qa_phase3.py hybrid` with its benchmark checks exits 0; browser D2.
  7. S1-S6; S8.

### 3-19: Phase 3 exit: the QA end to end, docs, evidence

- **Depends on:** 3-18
- **Estimated size:** ~500 (the `all` part and its evidence) + docs
- **Scope:** `qa_phase3.py all` on one dev server: an admin and three members; a direct dry-run machine and a helper machine (a real Python helper and the scripted endpoint, two slots) shared by member B with `game` and `pooled` to member A and with `benchmark` to the admin; one game of member A's, shared with members, on a standard wrapping map: A's person seat (played by the script through the API and the socket), a hybrid seat (the standard bot and B's helper machine's model, `all_but_war`, `prompt_every = 3`), a **full LLM seat on the same helper machine**, an **MCP seat** played by the script through `/api/games/{gid}/wait` with its seat token, and two bots, 40 rounds, with A opening chats with the hybrid, the LLM seat and a bot and answering their offers, and the timeouts exercised; member B's report narrative and A's probe on the same machine interleaving with the game's prompts; a **one-job benchmark** of the admin's started mid-game on that machine (its second slot, beside the game's LLM lease, as the job rule allows), during which pooled tickets are refused or drained and fall back in the lobby game, and no pooled request overlaps a benchmark step (from the endpoint's request log and the scheduler's listener); member C's socket and a spectator's socket without the god view receiving no `negotiation_status`, while A's does; the MCP seat's `wait_for_turn` deadlines; a member's seat with a base URL refused and nothing dialled; the ledger's given and used checked against the events; the minimap's stages through the debug ops route; a manual hardware entry surviving a helper restart; the server killed mid-game with a chat waiting on the LLM seat, the game brought back by `restore_live`, that chat answered without its timeout, and the game played on; the evidence JSON. Every item of P3.9.5's browser checklist in one pass with screenshots. The docs pass: `ARCHITECTURE.md` (Phase 3 in one section), `AI_PLAYERS.md`, `PLAYING.md`, `ACCOUNTS.md`, `WORKERS.md`, `BENCHMARKS.md`, `CONFIGURATION.md`, `API.md`, `REPORTS.md`, `KNOWN_ISSUES.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` (the Phase 3 features and the fresh database), `CONTRIBUTING.md` (the QA script), the wiki regenerated. "As built in 3-19" with each criterion of P3.1.4 and its evidence. **A real model (the owner's decision, 2026-10-11):** `qa_phase3.py` gains a part `real`, run by `all` after the scripted parts. In it the helper machine's upstream is the laptop's LM Studio (`http://localhost:1234/v1`, OpenAI-compatible) serving `google/gemma-4-e2b`, which the owner keeps loaded (131,072-token context). A hybrid seat (`all_but_war`, `prompt_every = 3`) and a full LLM seat on that machine play 20 rounds beside a person seat and two bots. The person seat opens a chat with each model seat and answers their offers, and a member's pooled report narrative runs on the same machine. The part first checks that the model is listed and loaded (LM Studio's `/api/v0/models`, `state == "loaded"`) and fails with a clear message otherwise. It never loads, unloads or swaps a model, changes LM Studio's settings, or uses another model. The model is small, so the part tests the plumbing, not the quality of play. It records: the turns each model seat played and how; the tool calls made and refused; the chat replies and their times; the generation seconds metered against the ledger's events for the machine; and every fallback to the bot with its cause.
- **Gates:**
  1. `python scripts/qa_phase3.py all` exits 0, its evidence listing every check of every part.
  2. Every item of P3.9.5's browser checklist passes, with the screenshots listed in the as-built note.
  3. Each row of P3.1.4 is measured and recorded in "As built in 3-19".
  4. S1-S7 at the head; `mkdocs build --strict`; the wiki regenerated and committed.
  5. CI-only: test.yml, rust.yml and determinism.yml green at the merge push (the orchestrator's).
  6. The `real` part passes against `google/gemma-4-e2b`. Every model turn ends, played or fallen back with its cause recorded, never hung. Each model seat plays at least one turn through its own tool calls. Every chat is answered by the model or ended by the chat clock's rule, with the cause recorded. The ledger's events for the machine match the metered generation seconds. Its numbers are in "As built in 3-19".
